# korbat sub-project 3f: authorities' orders, the alert channel, records to authorities, and recovery

Date: 2026-10-09. Status: design, written without the user with the lighter process of the 3e design: one architect's model with its lab, three drafters, one review round by three reviewers (the ledger and SQL; the people, the law and the words; fit to the code, the services and the tasks: 59 findings, all but one applied, that one overridden by a ruling), and a consistency pass. What that round did not find is found when the code is built; the dry run's amendments will be added at the end as 3c's were. It expands the model `korbat-scratch/research-3e/model-3f.md`, which is decided; what the owner or counsel may overturn is flagged in section 2.

It builds on the 3c design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-02-moderation-cases-3c-design.md`, cited as "3c" with its section) and on the 3e design (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-09-account-sanctions-3e-design.md`, cited as "3e" with its section), and changes neither's migrations. The code it describes is the scratch clone's (`korbat-scratch/repo`, read at HEAD `a5d3b99`: 3c's Tasks 1 to 7 committed, migrations 0000 to 0016 built; 0017 and 0018 of 3c and 0019 to 0023 of 3e designed and not built at that commit; `0017_appeals.sql` is in the clone's working tree, uncommitted, with 3c's Task 8).

Research, in `korbat-scratch/research-3e/`: `inputs.md` (cited IN: scope items S24 to S37, disagreements P3-1 to P3-25, gaps E1 to E28); `gap-E-orders-law.md` (OL); `gap-E-authorities.md` (AU); `gap-E-alerts-supply.md` (AS); the lab `lab-3f/` (LAB with its letter). In `korbat-scratch/research-3c/`: `gap-G8.md` and `gap-G6.md` (G8, G6); `threats-3c.md` (threats T, controls C). TCO is Regulation (EU) 2021/784; DSA is Regulation (EU) 2022/2065.

## 1. Context

3c gave korbat cases, decisions and a ledger of measures; 3e added the measures on an account. Every one of those measures is korbat's own judgement under its own rules or on a notice. Five things that the law asks of a hosting service in the EU, and that one or two people must be able to do at night, are still missing:

- **Nothing receives an order.** A removal order under TCO Art. 3 must be carried out "in any event within one hour of receipt". korbat has no contact point (TCO Art. 15, DSA Art. 11), no record of when a message arrived, and no way to wake anyone: 3c's alerts are mails to staff accounts (`apps/api/src/moderation/alerts.ts:21`), and the 3c design moved the phone channel here (3c 7.12, 3c 16).
- **No measure hides a post from its author.** 3c's strongest level is `removed`, which the author still sees with its statement. An order may forbid telling the author anything for six weeks (TCO Art. 11(3)), and an order leaves korbat no assessment to state: a statement of reasons under DSA Art. 17 is not owed for it (Art. 17(5)), and korbat has no reasons of its own to give.
- **Nothing preserves.** TCO Art. 6 obliges korbat to keep what it disabled for six months, against its own retention rules, the author's deletion and the account's purge.
- **Nothing records what korbat tells an authority.** A report of suspected child sexual abuse material after a seal, a notification under DSA Art. 18 or TCO Art. 14(5), an answer to an order to provide information and a hand-over of sealed files are made today by hand, from 3c's runbook, with no record, no timer and no check that the right files left.
- **Nothing recovers the ledger.** After a restore from yesterday's backup, every decision made since is gone: a post disabled for an order is public again and a sealed image is served again. 3a keeps the audit log's head off the server (README, The kept head); nothing keeps what the lost rows said.

korbat's promise is that limits are narrow and public, that every restriction cites a public rule or the law, that the author sees exactly what happened, and that nothing is removed quietly (main design, Context). An authority's order is the one case in which the law itself may order silence. This design keeps the promise as far as the law leaves it: the silence is as long as the order's non-disclosure period and not an hour longer than the database can prove safe; while it lasts, nothing reaches the author on any path, not a chip, not another decision's sentence, not a mail, an export or an error code; the author is then told everything korbat knows; and from the console a person can only add to the silence what the order itself shows: a "must not be told" that was missed, or, as an admin under a key, the one extension an authority sent.

This design delivers, on 3c's ledger and 3e's standing ledger and beside nothing else:

1. **The contact point's watcher.** A mailbox at an EU mail host is the contact point. A small program, `notify`, an entry point of the API image, watches it over IMAP, reads no header, body or attachment, and writes one row a message with the mail host's delivery time.
2. **The alert channel.** A self-hosted ntfy carries fixed-text alerts to the on-call phones; `notify` publishes them, and the database escalates and reminds. 3c's and 3e's staff mails stay as the second leg of every alert, so a dead channel fails open to mail; a mail wakes nobody at night, which row 22 states as a cost.
3. **Order records.** `legal_orders`, their items and their append-only events, with the one-hour clock of a TCO removal order and the facts that a DSA Art. 9 or Art. 10 order carries.
4. **Giving effect to an order.** One confirmed action, "Disable for an order", usable from a phone: the ledger measure `disable_by_order` under a decision of the new kind `order`; the post level `disabled`, which every reader refuses to everyone, the author included; and the reversal `reinstate_by_order`.
5. **The author's notice and its hold.** What the author of disabled content is told, held while a non-disclosure decision runs and released by the database: for a removal order at an end computed so that it is never early, for a DSA order at the time its issuing authority states. Whatever else korbat would tell the author about the same post is held with it.
6. **Preservation.** The disabled post, its images and its snapshot kept for six months from the disabling (from the new one, when a post is disabled again after a reinstatement), longer on a recorded request, and erased at the end.
7. **Records to authorities.** One record of every notification korbat makes to an authority, of the authority's answers, and of every production: account data, access to preserved content, and the hand-over of sealed image files.
8. **The report hold.** While a report of sealed images is owed, a deletion of the account keeps its address, and nothing else, until the notification is recorded, declined or seven days old.
9. **Recovery.** A daily export of the audit log's tail for the keeper of 3a's head; after a restore, a command that lists what the restored database lacks, and a confirmed action that takes each lost measure again under a staff key.
10. **The yearly counts** of TCO Art. 7(3) and Art. 21(1), from rows, by a command.
11. Their console screens at phone width, commands, mails and pushes, notices, terms, runbook, step-up actions and tests.

Three rules hold through all of it:

- **Nothing enters the ledger without a key.** An order is given effect by a decision that a staff key confirmed, after a restore as before. No job, no trigger, no command and no import adds a measure. The database alone decides whether a row can stand, what level a post has, when a held notice is released and when a preservation ends. A clock's event, an order's closing and a gap's closing are bound to the audit row a key confirmed, as a decision is.
- **korbat parses nothing an outsider wrote.** The watcher asks the mail host for two numbers a message. The order itself is read by a person, in the mail host's own app, never in korbat.
- **A person is told nothing korbat does not know and nothing the order forbids.** A notice says which authority the order names, what it ordered and under which act, and that korbat has confirmed the authority only once it has; it never says that korbat judged the content. While a notice is held, the post reads to its author, everywhere, exactly as a post that no order touched would read in that state.

And one rule for the night: **the hour never depends on a shell, and never on a record being right the first time.** The console opens an order without a message whenever its message is not listed, a person corrects what was typed wrongly, an admin reopens what was closed wrongly, and the reminders follow every item of an order until each is disabled or closed with a reason.

## 2. Decisions taken without the user

Each row gives the decision, what was rejected with its cost, what it rests on and its flag. The mechanics are stated once, in sections 5 to 7, and the rows point to them. Flags: **owner** means the owner may overturn it; **counsel** means it rests on a reading of law that counsel must confirm. Every number is a constant of one SQL function, `order_rules()` (6.1), mirrored column for member in the contracts as `ORDER_RULES`, so each is one line to change. "Cautious" means: among the choices a source supports, the one that never tells early, never exposes and never destroys.

The operator's Member State is not fixed. Every rule below is the EU-level rule; a state's facts are configuration (row 41) or runbook entries, and Germany is the worked example.

**For the owner** (each is a choice with a cost, made so that the work could go on; what the owner must do before going public is the list of section 18):

- korbat declares that it is offered in its home state only, so that no e-Evidence order reaches it directly; the claim must stay true as the user base grows (row 6).
- A post's page answers 404 whenever the post is not public, for whatever reason, and never 451 (row 17).
- The on-call phone is an Android phone with the F-Droid build of ntfy; no iPhone, no voice call (rows 19, 20).
- **Anyone who knows the public address can ring the on-call phone at night** (row 20): every message rings it through Do Not Disturb, and an unhandled one rings every phone at 15 and 45 minutes. `quiet_minutes` (15) spaces the first pushes and drops no message; nothing stops a flood, because a throttle that dropped mail could drop an order.
- **A watcher or a database that stops at night wakes nobody** (row 22): the alarm for it is an ordinary mail. The hour then depends on the mail host's own app on the on-call phone, signed in to the contact mailbox and allowed to ring at night, which the owner sets up and tests (section 18).
- A DSA Article 9 order is given effect only after it is verified, unless an admin signs that it is urgent (row 12); an order against an account as such is not built, and its hour depends on the operator (row 43).
- **The notice held on a console tick** (row 25; the decision most likely to be overturned). A Section C tick made in the console holds the author's notice to the computed end at once, before anyone has verified the order. Cost: a forged order, believed at night and never verified, silences one post for six weeks, with every staff account alerted daily. The alternative, a cap of 48 hours on an unconfirmed tick (G8 8), bounds that, and tells the author of a true order early whenever nobody reaches the server's shell for two days. It is one constant, `unconfirmed_hold_hours`, null today.
- During a hold the author sees what everyone sees: the post is gone and nothing accounts for it until the release; korbat's own decisions about that post, an answered appeal among them, are told only at the release (rows 26, 47; the window of row 45, item 2).
- A missed "must not be told" set after effect withdraws the notices already shown until the hold's end; an author who had read one sees it go and return (row 25). A correction after effect that changes the order's kind or its withholding, an admin's under a key, derives again the posts' level, the preservation and the measures' `withhold_author` (6.2, `order_correct` step 5). Every correction of an order's record, with or without a key, alerts every staff account (`order_corrected`, 6.2).
- Account data is not preserved with the content, and the author's own deletion of the account proceeds (row 30).
- A hand-over of sealed files is made by a one-shot service without network and recorded from the command line (row 34).
- **The report hold** (row 35) changes 1b's promise that the address goes within the purge round, and reverses 3e's row 27 ("no hold"). It is a setting, `REPORT_HOLD`, on by default; switched off, a deletion overwrites the address at once and an urgent report must be made, or the address copied off the server by hand, before anyone has judged the case.
- The yearly TCO report is published by hand in every year in which any figure is not zero (row 40), and the periods of row 42.
- The mail host of the contact point (row 1), under the requirements of section 5.

**For counsel** (the questions as the research words them are OL E9.4, E12.4 questions 1 to 7, E15.4, E20.4, E21.4 and AU E16.4 questions 1 to 7, E17.4, E22.4 questions 1 to 6):

- **First: whether the sealed files go with a notification of suspected child sexual abuse material** (rows 33, 34). The tool serves either reading, and the runbook says which is configured; in Germany the sources lean toward sending them (AU E16).
- Which instant is "receipt" under national law, and an order in a language the contact point did not declare (row 4).
- That korbat's home-state-only declaration keeps Directive (EU) 2023/1544 from obliging a designated establishment (row 6).
- That an order limited to one territory may be given effect everywhere, and that a `dsa_art9` order's content may stay visible to its author unless the order says otherwise (row 16).
- How the six weeks of TCO Art. 11(3) are counted, in which zone, and that a notice up to 28 hours late is on the lawful side (row 24); the hold on an unverified tick (row 25); the author's view during it (row 26).
- **A DSA Article 9 order's silence** (row 46): that its end is the time the issuing authority provides and nothing else; an order whose time has no end ("until further notice"), which korbat holds until the command line ends it; and how an extension reaches a host, and what korbat must do if none has arrived when the first period ends (OL E12.4 question 3).
- **During a hold** (rows 26, 47; the window of row 45, item 2): whether korbat's own statement about the same post is itself "information on the removal or disabling" (OL E12.4 question 1; the design holds it); and whether the copy of the text under an earlier decision may still be shown to its author while Art. 6 preserves the post. If it may not, that screen needs a sentence of its own that is true, and the hold cannot be kept silent for such a post.
- That korbat's appeal may be closed to a decision that gives effect to a TCO order (row 27).
- Preservation: its start, also after a reinstatement and a new disabling, its end with erasure while the order stands, and that account data is outside it (rows 28, 30).
- GDPR Art. 10 for a record of a notification, and where it is kept (row 31); when a report is owed (row 33); the hold of the address under GDPR Art. 17(3)(b) and the sentence that tells the person, in particular which words carry the reason that Art. 12(4) asks for while nobody has judged the report (the variant for a seal taken while deciding), and whether any ground gives a right to object (row 35).
- An order against an account as such (row 43); when the person named in an order to provide information is told, and under which provision korbat may stay silent (row 44); whether usage data (sign-in times and device types) is produced at all, in Germany under another provision than TDDDG s. 22 (row 34); which national laws oblige korbat to carry out a DSA Article 9 order, since the DSA itself is not their basis (recital 31).
- Whether the yearly report is owed in a year without an order (row 40), and the retention periods (row 42).

| # | Topic | Decision | Rejected, and its cost | Rests on | Flag |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | How an order reaches korbat | One mailbox at an EU mail host is the contact point for DSA Art. 11 and TCO Art. 15. Every message to it is a possible order until a person has read it, in the mail host's own app, never in korbat. `notify` records that a message arrived and when. When the order's message is not listed (the watcher is down, the mailbox cannot be read, the mail host filed it in a third folder, or the order arrived another way), the console opens the order without a message, at any time, so the hour never depends on a shell (row 4; 6.2). | An inbound mail server: impossible behind the tunnel (G8 4). A classifier on headers: nothing public tells an order from other mail. | G8 4; OL E9.3, E9.5 | owner (the host) |
| 2 | What the watcher reads | For each message its UID and the IMAP internal date, from INBOX and the junk folder, read-only, by polling every 20 seconds. No header, sender, subject, body or flag: it parses no byte an outsider wrote and stores no personal data. | 3c 16's "headers only" with the sender's domain and a DMARC result: a parser for attacker-written literals, a stored sender, two booleans a forged mail can make true. IMAP IDLE: a silently dead connection is found only by a timer, which is a poll; polling costs up to 20 s of the hour. | OL E9.5; AS E14 (unfinished); RFC 9051 2.3.3 | - |
| 3 | The IMAP client | Written for this job on `node:tls`: `LOGIN`, `EXAMINE`, `UID FETCH n:* (UID INTERNALDATE)`, `NOOP`, `LOGOUT`. No dependency. It reads four kinds of line (a completion of the command in flight, the folder's `UIDVALIDITY`, a `FETCH` with a UID and an internal date, `BYE`), none of which has a literal, and ignores every other line without one: the mailbox is shared with a person's mail app, and a server may send unrequested lines at any time. A line that announces a literal is an error and closes the connection. | `imapflow` (section 5): a general client and ten new packages for a five-command need. | AS E14.2; korbat's own TOTP as precedent | - |
| 4 | Which instant is receipt | `received_at` is the internal date of the message the staff member picks ("the date and time of final delivery"). The console never takes a typed time. A sending time the channel states is recorded from the command line as `sent_at`; the clock runs from the earlier, and Annex II states `received_at`. An order opened without a message has `received_at` null and the console says "deadline unknown: act now" until the command line records the instant. The console opens such an order itself at any time, whatever the watcher's state (6.2): its form always offers "The message is not listed", since a watcher that looks healthy may still not have seen the order, and "Disable for an order" needs no deadline. | The time someone opened the mail: the deadline shown is too late. A typed local time: on the night the clocks go back the database silently picks one of two instants (G8 8). | TCO Art. 3(3); OL E9.3, E9.5 | counsel |
| 5 | Which instruments are recorded | Three kinds of `legal_orders`: `tco_removal`, `dsa_art9` (act against content), `dsa_art10` (provide information). Any other message is closed as `referral`, `first_order_notice` (TCO Art. 3(2)), `preservation_request`, `scrutiny_decision`, `clarification` or `not_an_order`. A referral that staff act on is a moderator's finding, an ordinary 3c decision. | One kind `other`: clock, notice and preservation are per kind, and an unclassified order has no cautious default. | OL E15.3 | - |
| 6 | e-Evidence orders | Not built. The terms and the README say that korbat is offered in its home state only, so Directive (EU) 2023/1544 obliges no designated establishment and no EPOC reaches korbat directly. | Designate korbat and build a second intake with 10-day and 8-hour clocks, a 60-day preservation of account data and a ban on telling: a design of its own. Cost of the choice: the claim must stay true as the user base grows. | OL E15.3, E15.4 | owner, counsel |
| 7 | Who opens an order record | Any staff member, from the console, without a step-up: the message, the kind, the issuing state, the authority, the reference, up to 10 links, Section C and the emergency flag as the order shows them. It restricts nothing, copies nothing, tells nobody; every staff account is alerted. At most 20 records a staff member a day. **"Must not be told" is a required choice with no default**: ticked, not ticked, or "I cannot tell", and the third holds like the first, so an untouched box is never the path that tells. **What a person entered can be corrected** (6.2, `order_correct`): before any effect, by any staff member, every field (the kind, the state, the authority, the reference, Section C, the links, of which a wrong one is closed with a reason), with its own audit row; after effect, a missed "must not be told" is set at once by whoever is on call, and anything else, unsetting it among it, by an admin under a key. A closed order is reopened by an admin. So a record typed wrongly at night never stands in the way of the hour, and `order_exists` never protects a wrong record. | Only together with the disabling (G8 9): Annex III must be recordable before any effect. Only from the command line: the shell would be inside the hour (IN P3-6). A record nobody can correct: a missed Section C tick, seen with 35 minutes left, could then only be given effect as recorded, which mails the author within seconds, or cost the hour. | 3c 16; G8 9 | - |
| 8 | What is recorded of an order | The row (kind, state, authority, reference, channel, message, `received_at`, `sent_at`, Section C, emergency, cross-border, who opened it and how), its items (the link as named, the post, when it was added, the decision, or the reason it was closed), its events (row 10). From the command line only: the verification, the order's own time stamp, the authority's reasons and redress text and, for a DSA order, its territorial scope in the authority's words (DSA Art. 9(5)), the end of a DSA order's silence with its basis, when the person named in an order to provide information is told (row 44), the document's SHA-256 and length. | G8's lab shape with `e_evidence` and `other`: rows 5, 6. | G8 8, 9; OL E15.5 | - |
| 9 | The order's document | Not stored: it stays in the mailbox and the operator's records; korbat keeps its digest and length. TCO Art. 11(2) lets korbat hand a copy or inform of the reasons and rights: korbat informs, from the recorded texts; a copy goes out by the operator's hand on the author's request, to the address of the author's account and to no other: the notice asks the author to write from that address, and a command that answers yes or no and prints no address checks the sender (the runbook's entry "An author writes about an order's notice"). | A file store: 3c takes no attachment (T45), a file from a public address may hold the material at issue, and a second copy would outlive the mailbox's. | TCO Art. 11(2); OL E12.2 (b) | - |
| 10 | The clock | Only a `tco_removal` has one. It starts at the earlier of `received_at` and `sent_at`. An `annex3_sent` event stops it: no deadline. A later `grounds_ceased` (after an Annex III for force majeure) or `clarification_received` (after any other ground) starts a fresh 60 minutes from its effective time, never the rest of the old hour. The console also shows start + 55 minutes as the target until 3d purges edge copies. The database records times; it does not judge an Annex III. **The hour is the order's, and it is met only when every item is disabled or closed with a recorded reason**: one item given effect ends no reminder and does not make the hour read as met while other posts the order names are still public (6.2, the view). A DSA order has `due_at` as its text states it, or null. | "The clock pauses": against Art. 3(7), 3(8). A deadline per item: the hour is the order's. | TCO Art. 3(3), 3(7), 3(8); G8 8; LAB B | - |
| 11 | Clock events | `annex3_sent` (with the form's ground: force majeure, a manifest error, or insufficient information), `grounds_ceased`, `clarification_received`, `annex2_sent`, `receipt_confirmed` (DSA Art. 10) and `closed_without_effect` are console actions under the step-up `order_event_recorded`, as are an admin's `reopened` and the one `hold_extended` (rows 18, 24). Each is bound to its audit row as a decision is: the database refuses the commit of an event without the row a key confirmed, so what the key confirmed is what is stored (6.2). An effective time is a picked message's internal date, or now. Annex II and III themselves are sent by the person from the mailbox, to the address in the order's Section F and the home state's copy address; the console shows the form's fields prefilled as text. | korbat sending Annex II itself: mail to an authority from an address that is not the contact point's. | 3c 16; G8 9; OL E9.3 | - |
| 12 | Verification | **By the kind of order.** A removal order with its hour: at night the person checks it against the offline copy of the Commission's register and gives effect even when in doubt: the measure is reversible and preserves everything. The verification (which register entry, how checked) is recorded from the command line by the next working day. **An order that is not a removal order with an hour (a `dsa_art9`) is verified before effect**: no clock forces the night, and no register lists who may issue one (the cross-check is korbat's Digital Services Coordinator, which holds a copy of a genuine order: Art. 9(4)). The database refuses its effect while `verified_at` is null, unless an admin signs that it is urgent, and the console says so. While an order is given effect and unverified, every staff account is alerted daily and the console's home lists it; **an item added after the verification is unverified until `order-verify` runs again**, and the same alert covers it, missing texts and a DSA order's hold without a recorded end. A signature is neither required nor proof. | Verify before effect for a removal order: a call-back at 3 a.m. loses the hour. Effect before verification for every kind: a forged "court order" with "do not inform" would silence a lawful post through the reflex the hour has trained, for the one kind where korbat had time (T57: a gag is what a forger wants). | OL E9.3, E15.2 (a); G8 7; T57 | - |
| 13 | Who may give effect, and which checks apply | Any staff member on duty (`needs_admin` false); for a `dsa_art9` order that is not yet verified, an admin alone, who signs `urgent` (row 12). Recusal's relation test is waived, for the order decision and for the correction that reinstates: an order leaves no discretion in either direction, and with one person on call the hour, or the "immediately" of Art. 4(7), would be lost. `staff_target` is waived: an order about a staff member's post is given effect like any other. The decision caps neither count nor bind an order decision; its own cap does (row 15). Ground `illegal`, source `order`, no rule, no text of korbat's; a snapshot, which the same transaction takes. Every staff account is alerted of every order given effect. | Recusal as usual: nobody may act when the one person on call follows the author. Admin only: a moderator on call cannot act. The decision caps: after a busy day the cap refuses the order. Cost: a staff member can disable a post of someone close to them, under their key, with every colleague told; and a staff member whose own post an order names reads that order in the mailbox and in the console whatever Section C says, which cannot be prevented while every person on call reads the contact mailbox (section 11 names it, and the runbook has the person tell the authority with Annex II). | IN E11; `0016_decisions_and_ledger.sql:317, 339, 348, 604`; LAB D | - |
| 14 | The decision's kind | A new `decision_kind` `order`: it answers only its own intake item, which the same transaction inserts (intake kind `order`, channel `authority`); other open items of the case stay for korbat's own decision on its own rules. It holds exactly one `disable_by_order`. So no order item waits in the queue, and 3c 16's "lane 0 for an order's item" and "outcome phrase for notifiers" have nothing to apply to (section 15 amends them). | A `final` decision with source `order`: it answers every item in its range, so a notice would be answered by an order korbat never weighed it against, and under a non-disclosure decision that answer could not be released. An `interim` one: its measure would end with the case. | IN P3-22; `0016:256, 417`; OL E12.5 | - |
| 15 | One action, several items | "Disable for an order" is one confirmed action on one order: one transaction, one audit row `order_effected` whose params hold the order's facts and each item, one `order` decision a post. At most 10 items an action and 50 a staff member a day; an order with more items, or one given effect in part, stays due, with its reminders, until every item is disabled or closed with a recorded reason (row 10). The transaction takes the authors' profiles in id order, then the cases in target order (through its intake items' inserts), then the decisions in post id order (section 5, the lock order). | One step-up a post: five posts need five key touches inside the hour. No limit: a stolen key and session could disable without bound. | IN P3-21; 3c 2 row Lock order; LAB D | - |
| 16 | The measure and what it hides | `disable_by_order` gives the post the new level and state `disabled`: every reader answers as for a post that does not exist, to everyone, the author included; its images answer 404; the export and the access draft leave it out. For a `dsa_art9` order the signed params may say `withholdFromAuthor: false` (that kind's default): the level is then `removed`, shown to its author, who is told at once. For a `tco_removal` it is forced true. **"Must not be told" always hides from the author**, for every kind of order: the database forces it on the row, and signed params that say otherwise are refused (6.2, 6.3). So a notice is held only on a post its author cannot see. Such a post stays `disabled` after the release, which the notice says. An order limited to one territory is given effect everywhere, since korbat has no geography; the notice says so, beside the order's own scope once recorded. | A seal-like move of the body (G6 8.5): rejected by 3c. A boolean beside the level: a reader that forgets it shows the post to its author; a new enum value fails closed in `postAccess` and `serve`. A new image state: `serve` already refuses every image of a post in a state it was not taught. Two independent ticks for a DSA order: with "must not be told" alone the notice is held while the author reads the chip "Removed: an authority's order". | 3c 16; OL E12.3 (d), (e); `visibility.ts:20`; `serve.ts:35`; LAB C | counsel |

An order not yet given effect is closed by an admin's `closed_without_effect` with the same grounds or `not_addressed`. **A closed order is reopened by an admin** (`reopened`), with its items, events and clock as they were: an order closed by mistake, or one a court restored, can be given effect again, and nothing closed ever blocks a reference. | 20 | How a person is woken, and what escalates | A message at the contact point (junk included) raises at once a priority-5 push to the on-call topic and a mail to every staff account. A clock that starts again rings the on-call phone again. A throttle that drops messages: an order in a flood would be lost. **Cost of the choice:** whoever knows the public address can ring the on-call phone through Do Not Disturb once every 15 minutes, and every phone at 15 and 45 minutes for a message nobody closes, night after night, until the alarm is what people learn to dismiss (T58). The bound spaces the first pushes; it cannot stop the flood. | 3c 16; G8 5.3, 7; AS E13.5; T58 | owner |

Priority 3: the rest. 3c 7.12's throttle keys and fail-open rule stand. | Push in place of mail (3c 7.12's wording): a dead phone channel would be silent. The API publishing itself: it would hold the token and a route to ntfy. | IN P3-8; `alerts.ts:21` | - |
| 22 | When the channel is down | `notify` writes a heartbeat every poll. A heartbeat older than 3 minutes, or an outbox row that is still owed its push after 3 minutes: the API mails `alert_channel_down` daily while it lasts and the console's home says that alerts arrive by mail only. A push is owed for 60 minutes and tried every round, so a two-minute restart of the push server loses no alert, and a row past that bound no longer reads as an outage. An unreadable mailbox is alerted by push and mail. A daily test push at priority 3 shows the chain to a person who looks. A watcher that exits or hangs is started again by Docker. While the channel is down the console opens an order without a message, as it does at any time (row 4). | No heartbeat: a dead watcher looks like a quiet night. | 23 | What the author is told, without a non-disclosure decision | At once, in the decision's transaction (`informed_at` = `decided_at`, the mail pending): a notice, not a statement of reasons. Until then it says that they follow and that a copy of the order can be asked for. That is at or after the end of the same weekday six weeks later in any zone of the EU's European territory: never early there and at most 28 hours late (LAB E). An overseas region's zone is not covered. The authority's own written end replaces it when earlier. One extension adds 42 days. | 25 | A Section C tick nobody has confirmed, and what the console may do to a hold | The tick made in the console holds the notice at once, also before the command line has recorded the order's time stamp and the verification. One constant (`unconfirmed_hold_hours`, null). | 30 | Account data during preservation, and the author's deletion | Not held: preservation covers the content. The account's deletion proceeds; the preserved copy outlives it. A `disabled` post cannot be deleted alone by its author: the route answers as for a missing post, so no route tells more than the read does. **The deletion mail never says that everything is gone while something is kept.** It says to every account, since it is a rule and not a finding about this one, that the posts, images and profile are gone except what the law obliges korbat to keep for a time, and the terms' "Your data" names preserved content beside the held address, the purpose in the general words the law allows, and until when. A seal that korbat itself found mistaken leaves no report owed and no address held. Owed for more than 24 hours, it is alerted daily. On the daily visit on which the keeper copies the head, the keeper downloads from the console the rows since the place kept last, as JSON lines. **The download is a confirmed action**: it holds every row's metadata, which the audit page never shows, and names every held order, so a session without its key (the on-call phone, left unlocked) cannot take it; every staff account is alerted when anyone but the last downloader takes one. **The mark is the keeper's confirmation, not the stream's end**: the console posts the last line's place and hash once it holds the whole body, and only then does the database record a copy, so a download that broke off leaves the copy overdue and no silent hole. | 37 | What "apply again after a restore" means | Nothing enters the ledger without a key, after a restore as before. `staff.js restore-import` reads the keeper's files as the migrator, whole and oldest first: the restored head lies inside a file, never at its edge. Lines at or below the head are checked and not imported (the line at the head's place must have the head's hash); the first line above it must continue the restored chain. A changed file refuses everything; a hole between two files is reported, and the unbroken run from the head is imported (section 7). It then (1) inserts again the plain records the lost rows describe (orders, items, events, notification and production records) with their original times and an `imported` mark; (2) writes one row of `restore_gaps` for every lost decision. A person then takes each lost measure again, under a newly enrolled key, as a new decision that names the lost one; lost reversals likewise. The app's site stays closed (`APP_SITE=closed`) until no gap that holds an order's disabling or a seal is open. What stays lost: the texts of lost decisions, and everything after the keeper's last download, for which the mailbox and the operator's notes are the record. | An import of ledger rows with the triggers off: a restriction no key confirmed. A decision kind "restored": a branch in every function for a path used once in years. | IN P3-18, E18; `0016:513, 778`; LAB F | - |
| 38 | Order of the re-application | As the import prints it and the console shows it: order disablings, seals for good, seals taken while deciding, terminations and suspensions, other restrictions, reversals, then decisions without a measure (for reference). Before any order's disabling is taken again, the operator compares each imported hold with the mailbox since the keeper's last download: a lost extension or a lost "must not be told" would make the new decision tell at once (the runbook's step). The author is told of a re-applied decision as of a new one, with a sentence naming the lost decision's day, which the statement reads from the gap for as long as the decision stands; under a running hold nothing is told. | In time order: a seal lost yesterday would wait behind last week's removals. | 3c 2; 3c 17 | - |

So the record of a `dsa_art10` order has a field, from the command line: when the person is told (`at_effect`, `at_instant` with the instant, `never`) and the provision or the order's sentence it rests on. `production-account` refuses until it is recorded. Closing (2) needs the author's lists to know which decisions are told. | the review round's rulings | owner; counsel (item 2) |
| 46 | The end of a DSA order's silence, and the warning before any release | For a `dsa_art9` or `dsa_art10` order the end of a hold is what the issuing authority states ("at the time provided by the issuing authority", DSA Art. 9(5), 10(5)): recorded from the command line with its basis, of any length, earlier or later than six weeks, and with no end while the authority has named none; the Regulation's weeks do not cap it, and its one extension is not this kind's. The console says which rule an order's hold follows. **Somebody is warned before any held notice is released**: from 7 days before a hold's end (`hold_warning_days`) every staff account is alerted daily (`hold_ending`), so that an extension that reached the mailbox meanwhile is recorded first; a hold without an end is alerted daily while it lasts (`order_unverified`). | The TCO's six weeks for every kind: a prosecutor's order that says "inform the recipient on 1 March" would be told on day 43, or on day 85 at the latest, earlier than the order allows. No warning: the design accepts being up to 28 hours late so as never to be early, and would then let a weekend make it early. Cost of the choice: a hold with no stated end is one only the command line ends, the case row 24 rejects for the TCO ("a forgotten release is a silent removal"); the daily alert is what stands against forgetting it. | DSA Art. 9(5), 10(5), recital 34; OL E12.2 (c), (e), E12.4 question 3 | counsel |
| 47 | What else is held with a notice | Whatever korbat would tell the author about the same post. **korbat's own decisions about a post under a held notice are untold until the release** (6.3 step 10): a removal under a rule would say "another decision already keeps this post from being shown", and a reversal on appeal that another decision "listed in Settings, Cases" applies, where none is listed. The reporter's own outcome line moves as today. **An earlier decision's screen is unchanged**: it shows the content line it showed before the order, because `snapshot_withheld` is not widened for preservation (6.3); with a preservation test it would say "Content erased when the R4 decision became final"; the post's images on that screen are served to nobody while an order disables it, the author included, and each is drawn exactly as the app draws an image that could not be loaded, in the words it already has for that case and no other, so nothing there says why while the notice is held (section 7's table of readers). **An access request**: the operator's draft leaves the order and the post out of what is sent and marks the case for the operator alone. | Telling korbat's own decision at once: its statement names another decision or promises that the post is back. A neutral content line on earlier decisions ("korbat does not show this content at present"): a change on the author's own screen at the moment of the effect. An unmarked draft: the operator sends an incomplete answer without knowing it. Cost of the choice: an appeal answered during a hold reaches its author only at the release, up to twelve weeks late or, for a DSA order, later; and the text under an earlier decision stays readable to its author while Art. 6 preserves the post. | OL E12.3 (d), E12.4 question 1; `statement.ts:50`; `author.ts:79` | owner, counsel |

## 3. Research (facts the design depends on)

**Where the lab ran.** The lab is `korbat-scratch/research-3e/lab-3f/` (`RUN.txt` has every command, `out-*.txt` the raw outputs), run on 2026-10-09. It did not run in a container: Docker's engine was down, so it ran on a throwaway cluster of the PostgreSQL 18.1 binaries installed on the machine (a Windows build), READ COMMITTED. The earlier labs ran on the 18.6 alpine image; minor version and platform differ, the migrations (the clone's 0000 to 0016) and the trigger code are the same files. **The first step of the task that builds the ledger's migration (Task 3) is to run `lab-3f/` again on the 18.6 image**, with the migrations up to 0016 as `RUN.txt` lists them (by then the migrations' folder holds 3c's, 3e's and two of 3f's files) (`reset.sh` and the scripts; only the connection lines change), and to compare the outputs. Task 1 copies the lab into the repository as `apps/api/test/lab/orders/`, since `lab-3f/` lies outside it.

The lab's schema is the clone's migrations 0000 to 0016, then `20-proto.sql`: a prototype of 6.2 and 6.3 that copies eight objects of 0015 and 0016 and patches 3f's branches in by exact text replacement (`make_proto.py`, `20-tables.sql`). So what ran is 3c's own trigger code with 3f's branches, as `korbat_api`, through `decisions_record`, the ledger's triggers, `append_confirmed_audit` and the deferred `decision_holds`. It sits on 0016: 0017 to 0023 are not built.

**The code that 3f changes** (scratch clone, read 2026-10-09; line numbers are the working tree's and may move).

- The ledger. `measures_in_force` (`0015_greedy_zuras.sql:679`), `frozen_for` (`:700`), gate K's `post_media_keep` (`:735`); `snapshot_withheld` (`0016_decisions_and_ledger.sql:79`), `moderation_apply` (`:104`), `posts_guard` (`:147`), `decisions_record` (`:203`), `decisions_apply` (`:401`), `decision_actions_check` (`:495`), `decision_actions_apply` (`:674`), `decision_holds` (`:759`), `append_confirmed_audit` (`:880`). Six of the seven enums that gain a value are created at `0015:2` to `:12`; `post_state` is `0000_parallel_umar.sql:4`.
- `decisions.informed_at` is NOT NULL (`0015:140`), and two CHECKs ask every restriction for korbat's facts and reason and every decision on the law for a legal reference (`decisions_restricted`, `decisions_ground_law`, `0015:157, 158`). An order decision has none of the three texts.
- The API role cannot lock a case: it holds no UPDATE on `cases`. A case is taken by the attach trigger of an intake item (`intake_items_attach`, `0015:539`), which runs as its owner. LAB C's first run failed on a direct lock.
- The readers. `postAccess` switches over every stored state and does not compile with a state it was not taught (`apps/api/src/social/visibility.ts:20`); `serve`'s `isShown` refuses, for the author too, every post and image state it does not name (`apps/api/src/media/flows/serve.ts:23, 35`).
- **The purge tombstones posts; it does not delete them.** `purgeProfile` sets every post of the account to `deleted_by_author` with an empty body, as the API role (`apps/api/src/social/repo.ts:1233`), and deletes image rows last, where gate K keeps those of a frozen post. The model assumed that the purge deletes post rows; 6.3's `posts_guard` and section 5 are written for the code as it is.
- `finishPurge` overwrites the address as the last step of a deletion (`apps/api/src/auth/repo.ts:511`).
- Staff alerts are mails and nothing else: `staffAlert` hits its throttle key and calls `mailStaff` (`apps/api/src/moderation/alerts.ts:21`).
- The stack has three networks (`edge`, `app`, `data`), none internal; Caddy serves the app on `:8080` and the console on `:8090`; the image worker runs with `network_mode: none` (`infra/compose.yml`). `infra/postgres/init/01-roles.sh` creates three roles on a fresh data directory only.
- **What the review round read in the code**, each a fact a fix above rests on.
  - `decisions_record` forces `informed_at := decided_at` on every decision, also on those whose author is told nothing (`0016:229`); today's author reads keep those out by `author_mail <> 'none'` (`moderation/author-repo.ts`, `authorDecisions`), and `chipFacts` tests neither.
  - The author's decision screen reads `snapshot_withheld` (`author-repo.ts:135`) and words "withheld and not erased" as an R4 redaction (`moderation/author.ts:79`).
  - A `remove` that changes nothing is worded "Another decision already keeps this post from being shown" (`packages/rules/src/statement.ts:50`).
  - The correction's `decisions_record` reads the corrected decision without a lock (`0016:261`).
  - `auth.staff_sessions.last_used_at` is "the person's last action in the console, never a request the page sent by itself" (`packages/db/src/schema/auth.ts`).
  - The migrate service is given four settings and no more (`infra/compose.yml:65`); the command line may enter a module only through its index, and no file outside the media module may name `post_media` (`apps/api/test/architecture.test.ts:94, 117`).
  - Every route of the social module is mounted after the session's resolution; the image files' router is mounted before it (`apps/api/src/app.ts:67` to `:81`).
  - The job lock is in the limit store (`apps/api/src/index.ts:23`; `lib/jobs.ts:88`), and the API listens on every interface (`index.ts:116`).
  - `korbat_migrate` holds its privileges on the schema `public` without the grant option (`01-roles.sh:16`).

**Measured** (LAB, with the model's letters).

| Lab | Measured | Result |
|---|---|---|
| A | The whole prototype (8 `ADD VALUE`, the tables, two CHECKs swapped with `kind::text`, `informed_at` nullable, a view, eight replaced functions) in one transaction on a database at 0016 | exit 0 |
| B | The clock | Received 70 minutes ago: overdue. `annex3_sent`: stopped, no deadline. A clarification effective after it: a fresh 60 minutes from the clarification. One dated before the Annex III left the clock stopped. The design does not refuse that order of events, which would leave an Annex III recorded late without any restart: `order_event_record` takes the later of the two times as the restart's (6.2), and the task's lab runs that case, and an order with two items of which one is given effect (still due, the hour not yet judged) |
| C | The measure against `moderation_apply`, and the readers | A moderator gave effect to a TCO order on a user's post and on a staff member's own post: both `disabled`; decisions of kind and source `order`, ground `illegal`, keyword forced, `needs_admin` false; preserved 6 months + 1 day; cases closed with `answered_seq = intake_seq`. `postAccess` mirrored in SQL: `hidden` for the author, another account and signed out; `isShown` false; image states unchanged. A later `remove` under R5: still `disabled`, its `no_effect` `already`. Reinstatement by a moderator: `admin_required`; without a ground: refused; by the admin with one: `removed` (the R5 removal stands), shown to its author; that removal corrected: `visible`. The other order of the same four decisions: `disabled` until the reinstatement. A `dsa_art9` order without withholding: `removed`, shown to its author alone, not frozen |
| C | Hiding and keeping | `post_frozen` true while preserved: a delete of its image rows returned no row and left both; after the reinstatement not frozen. The API role's own update moved a disabled post to `deleted_by_author` (hence `posts_guard`'s clause); its snapshot stayed. Refused to the API role: a cli-only event, any update of an order, of an item's ledger columns, of `informed_at`, and an order without a message (the lab's rule; the design now accepts one at any time, 6.2 and the list below) |
| D | One action, ten items | 10 decisions and measures in one transaction with one audit row: 279 ms (one item: 108 to 136 ms; quiet machine, first calls). Refused, leaving nothing behind: no audit row; `case_decided` in its place; a forged reference in the params; an item the row does not name; a second measure; an account that is not staff. Accepted where an ordinary removal was refused: by a moderator whose linked personal account is the author (`staff_recused`), and with the decision caps at zero (`decision_cap`) |
| D | The held notice | With Section C: `informed_at` null and `author_mail` `none` on all ten; a read with `informed_at is not null` returns none. With an authority's end 3 seconds ahead, by the real clock: the release returns 0 before and 0 one second before the end, 10 after it (`informed_at` later than `decided_at`, mail `pending`, 10 events), then 0. At a computed end the predicate is false one microsecond before and true at it |
| E | `order_hold_end` against Reg. 1182/71 Art. 3 | 314,408 instants (300,000 random over 2026 to 2030; every minute around four clock changes and 43 days before them) in eight zones from the Azores to Nicosia: early in none; late by 0 to 25 h (Azores), 2 to 27 h (Berlin, Paris), 3 to 28 h (Helsinki, Athens, Nicosia). The hold lasts 42 d 4 h to 43 d 4 h; an extension adds exactly 42 days; the value is the same in three session time zones, where `+ interval '42 days'` moves by an hour across a clock change and ends about a day before the period's last hour |
| F | The import after a restore | A copy of the database as the backup; then, lost: an order opened and given effect, and a removal. The tail (3 audit rows as JSON lines) with a changed `prevHash`: refused (`tail_not_continuous`). The import re-created the order with its original `opened_at` and `received_at`, wrote 3 gaps and no ledger row; the imported `preserve_until` froze the post at once. The migrator's own copy of the lost decision and measure: the trigger forced `decided_at` and `xact` to the present, and the commit was refused both with no audit row and with an unconfirmed one. Re-applied by the moderator under a key: `disabled`, `disabled_at` and `preserve_until` unchanged, the notice still held; the removal's gap left open |

**Not measured.** Contention: one session at a time, so 3c's contention lab must gain the ten-item transaction against intakes, a seal and the purge (Task 3). The TypeScript readers themselves. `audit-verify` against a forged confirmation, which is what catches a migrator writing around the triggers. `notify`, ntfy, Caddy's sites, the tunnel, any phone, any security key on a phone (AS E13.4 L1 to L5: Task 1 runs L2, L3 and L5 as its gate and starts L1, which idles for an hour, and L4, which holds a connection for twelve hours, beside the task, their numbers due before Task 3; the owner's checks P1 to P8 and section 18's check M, since the research's P9 is the iPhone's). Anything as the role `korbat_notify`: the lab made three roles, and the build review measured on PostgreSQL 18.1 that a migration cannot give that role the schema's USAGE (6.1). Caddy's page status for `/post/*` (Task 4's first step). 3c's 0017 and 0018 and 3e's 0019 to 0023: every function this design replaces is written "as 3e leaves it" or "as 3c leaves it", and the task that replaces it first compares the built text with the lab's patch (6, Replacing a function).

**What the review round changed after the lab ran**, so that no measurement above covers it and the task's lab on the real code must (section 12):

- a disabling after a reinstatement, with the item aged past its first end: the body is still there after the retention round (6.3; LAB C's C5, then `preserve_until` set a day back, a second effect and the preservation's end);
- a release beside an uncommitted reinstatement, in two sessions, then one more round: the correction is told (6.3);
- a `dsa_art9` order with "must not be told": `disabled`, no chip, nothing on any author-side read; and its hold with no recorded end, with one, and with one later than six weeks (6.2);
- an earlier told decision about a post that is then disabled: its screen on the author's side is unchanged (6.3);
- a decision of korbat's own on a post under a held notice: untold, and told by the release (6.3);
- the author-side read with both halves of `toldToAuthor`: the lab took the short form (`informed_at is not null`), which would list a 3c decision whose author is told nothing;
- an order opened from the console without a message, accepted while the watcher's beat is old and while it is fresh; a correction under each of its three grounds; a console event without its audit row, refused at commit (6.2);
- an order with two items of which one is given effect: still due, `within_hour` null (6.2).

**The law** (OL, AU; each with what the design takes from it).

- A removal order is carried out "in any event within one hour of receipt" (TCO Art. 3(3)). Receipt is arrival at the contact point; the authority's record and Europol's check count from sending, so the safe clock runs from the earliest time korbat has a record of (OL E9.3). An order may lawfully arrive unsigned; the register is the host's check (OL E9.3). Nothing public tells an order from other mail by its headers (OL E9.3, E9.5).
- A manifest error or missing information is answered with Annex III, and the deadline then runs anew from the clarification, or from the moment the grounds of force majeure cease (TCO Art. 3(7), 3(8)). An order from another Member State can be put to scrutiny within 48 hours (Art. 4(4)), and content is reinstated at once when the order falls (Art. 4(7)).
- The author is informed of the removal and, on request, of the reasons and the rights, or given a copy of the order (TCO Art. 11(1), (2)); not while the authority has decided on non-disclosure, "not exceeding six weeks from that decision", extended once "by a further six weeks" (Art. 11(3); OL E12.2). Reg. (EEC, Euratom) 1182/71 Art. 3 counts a period in weeks to the end of the same weekday; in whose zone is counsel's (OL E12.4).
- **The six weeks are the TCO's alone.** Under DSA Art. 9(5) the recipient is informed "at the latest when effect is given to the order or, where applicable, at the time provided by the issuing authority in its order", with a statement of reasons, the possibilities for redress and a description of the territorial scope of the order; silence needs a source outside the Article, and none gives a maximum (OL E12.2 (c), (e)). Art. 10(5) says the same for the person an order to provide information concerns, and recital 34 lets national law delay it (OL E15.2 (b), E15.3). So a record needs a field for which rule applies and from which provision, with the cautious default for its kind (OL E15.5).
- No register lists who may issue a DSA Art. 9 order; the one cross-check in the text is that the issuing authority sends the order to its Digital Services Coordinator, which transmits a copy to all others (Art. 9(3), (4)), and the DSA sets no deadline for it (OL E15.2 (a)). The DSA is not the legal basis of such an order: the national law behind it is (recital 31).
- The time of being informed is the time the notice is really made available: the author's 48 hours (TCO Art. 4(4)) and the issuing state's court deadline run from it (OL E12.3 (b)).
- An answer to a request that korbat does not carry out in full says the reasons, the possibility of a complaint to a supervisory authority and the judicial remedy (GDPR Art. 12(4); AU E22.2 (f)); a notice about a held address "must not say more than the seal's statement already said" (AU E22.5), and that statement speaks of the police only for a seal for good (3c 10, the statement's words).
- An order is no decision of korbat's under DSA Art. 17: no statement of reasons is owed, and korbat's complaint system is not the remedy (Art. 17(5); OL E12.3 (c)).
- What was disabled is preserved for six months from the disabling, longer only on an authority's or court's request for review proceedings, opened only for review, complaints or an authority (TCO Art. 6; OL E20.3). No source asks korbat to republish a post that its author deleted in the meantime (OL E20.3). A DSA Art. 9 order preserves nothing under EU law (OL E20.3).
- Five instruments differ in channel, deadline, what is owed back and what the person is told; European Production and Preservation Orders reach a service only through a designated establishment (OL E15.3).
- A notification of a suspicion under DSA Art. 18 goes to the authorities of the state concerned; in Germany the intake for it, for a TCO Art. 14(5) notification and for a report of suspected child sexual abuse material is the BKA's portal (AU E17). A hand-over of sealed files follows a demand under criminal procedure, goes to an authority alone and ends nothing on korbat's side (AU E16). An address may be held against a deletion only where a report is owed, only for what the notification carries and only until it is sent or declined (AU E22; GDPR Art. 17(3)(b)).
- A self-hosted ntfy can be private and can wake an Android phone without Google; an iPhone cannot carry the hour; whether an alert wakes a person depends on phone settings no server sees (AS E13.3).

**Corrections between the research and this design.** 3c 16's "IMAP IDLE, headers only" becomes a poll that reads two numbers (rows 2, 3). G8's `decided_at` + 42 days becomes `order_hold_end` (row 24), and its 48-hour cap on a console tick is not adopted (row 25). 3c 16's lane 0 and outcome phrase for an order's item fall away with the decision kind `order` (row 14). `reverse-window` is not built (row 39). 3e's "no hold" becomes the report hold (row 35). The model's sentence that the purge deletes rows is corrected above.

**Corrections between the model and this design after the review round** (the rulings of `review-3f-rulings.md`). The model's one end for every hold becomes an end by the kind of order (rows 24, 46). Its `snapshot_withheld` with a preservation test becomes a predicate of its own, `post_preserved` (6.3). Its "a second disabling starts no new period" holds only where no preservation has ended (6.3). Its two independent facts "Section C" and "withheld from the author" become one rule: the first forces the second (row 16). Its record that nobody can change gains a correction and a reopening (row 7), and its order that needs a message can be opened without one at any time (row 4). Its clock events on the caller's word are bound to an audit row (row 11). Its one-statement `finishPurge` becomes two (6.5).

## 4. Scope

**In (3f).**

- Six migrations (section 6 numbers them): the database role `korbat_notify`, which the superuser creates and the first migration checks; eight enum values; thirteen tables (twelve in `public`, `auth.report_holds`); one column of `decisions` and two of `decision_actions`; the constants `order_rules()`; one new view; the functions and triggers of 6.1 to 6.6, two deferred checks among them; one view and ten functions of 0015 to 0023 replaced (`snapshot_withheld` is not).
- Two services and one one-shot service (section 5): `notify`, ntfy, `handover`, each behind a Compose profile; a network of the watcher's own; a third Caddy site for the phones; the page status of `/post/*` and the switch `APP_SITE` on the app's site.
- The contracts and the rulebook (section 10): `ORDER_RULES`, `STATE_FACTS`, `ALERT_KINDS` and `ALERT_TEXTS`, the step-up actions `order_effected`, `order_event_recorded`, `order_corrected`, `gap_closed` and `audit_exported`, `case_decided`'s `reinstate_by_order`, the new error codes, a new redress version with the notice's texts.
- The API: the orders routes with the correction of an order, the effect transaction, the readers of the level `disabled`, the release of held notices in the dispatch round, the alert tick, the retention job's part, the report hold in the purge, the audit export and its mark, the gaps, the status router (sections 5 and 7).
- The command line: the commands of section 8, among them everything that records what only the operator may record (verification, the order's time stamp and texts, holds, preservation, notifications, productions, the import after a restore, the yearly counts).
- The console's screens at phone width (section 8); the app's Cases rows and notice (section 9); the mails and pushes, the terms' sections, the export's members and the README's runbook (section 18); the tests and the labs on the real code (sections 12 and 13).

**Out, by decision.**

- European Production and Preservation Orders (row 6): korbat declares that it serves its home state only; the alternative is an owner's choice with a build of its own.
- A store for an order's document (row 9); a parser for mail (row 2); an inbound mail server (row 1).
- `reverse-window` (row 39); any import that writes the ledger (row 37).
- A voice-call gateway (row 20); an iPhone as the on-call phone (row 19).
- A console screen for preserved content, for a notification's record or for a production (rows 29, 31).
- Any automatic writer of a measure: no job, no trigger and no command gives effect to an order or takes a lost measure again.
- A hold of account data during preservation (row 30).
- A measure on an account as such for an order (row 43); korbat itself mailing the person named in an order to provide information (row 44).
- The known limits of row 45.

**What sub-project 3d receives** (transparency and automation; 3c 17, with 3f in place of 3e in its paragraph "Which sub-project owns what").

- Publishing the yearly report and the counts on a public page: 3f prints the counts (`staff.js tco-counts`) and the runbook publishes by hand until then.
- The Transparency Database export. An `order` decision is never exported (DSA Art. 17(5)); the export leaves the kind out by name.
- The Cloudflare purge on every restriction, an order's disabling included. Until then an image may be served from an edge copy for up to five minutes, so the console's target is 55 minutes (`order_rules().target_minutes`).
- The quarantine and the release of sealed images. The release reads 3f's notification record through `seal_report(action)` for the authority's reference and answer (6.5); the `handover` service then mounts the quarantine volume in place of the store.
- The hash match; the head's publication.
- `frozen_for` keeps its name and now means "frozen or preserved": its fourth arm is 3f's (6.3), written once in `post_preserved`. 3d's release adds its clause to the seal's arm only, and every caller of the predicate that 3c 17 lists for 3d meets preserved posts as frozen ones. `snapshot_withheld` is as 3c built it: 3f adds nothing to it, so 3d finds it meaning what it meant.

**The going-public gate stands** (3c's README, Before going public): korbat is not opened until 3f's order path and 3d's handling of sealed images have both shipped, and until the list "For the owner before going public" (section 18) is done.

**Later sub-projects.** 2d and 3b add write paths and read paths: each new reader of a post or an image must answer a `disabled` post as a missing one, which `postAccess` and `isShown` enforce for whoever goes through them. Sub-project 4 has the reviewed terms with counsel; 3f writes its sections under the draft.

## 5. Architecture

3f adds two long-running services, one one-shot service and no new dependency of korbat's own programs. Inside the API it adds no module: orders are moderation, and the division of 3c 5 stands. The API decides which rows to write; the database decides whether they can stand and what they mean; the command line, as the migrator, records what only the operator may record.

**Who may write what.** Three writers, three sets of rights.

| Writer | Database role | Writes |
|---|---|---|
| The console's routes | `korbat_api` | an order's row as the order shows it, its items, the event `opened`; a message's disposition; through definer functions: the six clock events, an order's reopening, the one extension of a hold, a correction of what a person entered, the effect (a decision and its measure), a gap's closing; outbox rows; a copy mark |
| `notify` | `korbat_notify` | a message's four columns, the outbox's push columns, its own state |
| The command line (`staff.js`) | `korbat_migrate` | every command-line column of an order, the cli-only events, an order's reopening, a hold's end and extension, a preservation's extension, every row of the records to authorities, the import after a restore, the outbox row of a command whose console twin alerts |

The ledger is written by none of them directly: a measure exists only as a row of a decision that a staff key confirmed (6.3, `decision_holds`), and the migrator's own inserts are refused at commit like anyone's (LAB F). The same holds one level down: a clock event, an order's closing or reopening, a correction after effect and a gap closed as not needed stand only with the audit row a key confirmed, checked at commit (6.2, 6.6).

**What each module gains.**

| Module | Files | What they hold |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/db` | `src/schema/moderation.ts`, `safety.ts`, `auth.ts`; the six migrations | Section 6. |
| `packages/contracts`, `packages/rules` | `orders.ts` (new), `moderation.ts`, `staff.ts`; `rules/src/index.ts`, `statement.ts` | Section 10: `ORDER_RULES`, `STATE_FACTS`, `ALERT_KINDS` (priority, scope, legs, the facts' names) and `ALERT_TEXTS` (title, sentence, the mail's line), the step-up params, the codes; the notice's texts in a new redress version. |
| moderation | new `orders.ts`, `orders-repo.ts`, `gaps.ts`; `alerts.ts`, `decide.ts`, `dispatch.ts`, `retention.ts`, `author-repo.ts`, `export.ts`, `statement.ts`, `routes.ts`, `index.ts` | `orders.ts`: opening an order, correcting it, the clock events, the effect transaction, the lists the console reads; its SQL in `orders-repo.ts`. `alerts.ts`: `staffAlert`'s second leg and the alert tick. `decide.ts`: `reinstate_by_order` and the gap a decision closes. `dispatch.ts`: `order_notices_release()` before the statements, and no mail for an untold decision. `retention.ts`: the calls of 6.4. `author-repo.ts`: the one fragment "decisions the author was told of". `index.ts`: three plain functions for the command line (below). |
| social | `visibility.ts`, `repo.ts`, `flows/posts.ts`, `routes.ts`, `index.ts` | `StoredPostState` gains `disabled`; `deletePost` answers `not_found` for it; the purge's tombstones (the lock order, item 4); the small router `statusRoutes` behind `GET /api/posts/:id/status`, over the repository's read. |
| media | `repo.ts`, `files.ts`, `index.ts` | `reach`'s `served` leaves out a `disabled` post. `serve` needs no change. `imagePost(id)`, which says to which post an image's address belongs, for opening an order (row 43). Two read-only functions for the command line and the store's naming rule as one pure function (below). |
| auth | `repo.ts`, `flows/deletion.ts`; `apps/api/src/mail/templates.ts` | `finishPurge`'s two statements, the second waiting while `auth.address_held`; the sentence added to `account_deleted`, in the variant `auth.address_hold_final` picks, with the day of `auth.address_held_until`; the new templates. |
| staff | new `orders.ts`; `audit.ts`, `routes.ts` | The routes under `/api/staff/orders`, `/contact` and `/restore`; the audit export, its confirmation and its copy mark; the audit page's filter by key and period. |
| `apps/api/src/cli` | `staff.ts`; new `orders.ts`, `records.ts`, `restore.ts`, `counts.ts` | The commands of section 8, each with the 10-second lock timeout and its audit row last. Each reads only the settings it uses, as `ENROLL_ENV` does. |
| `apps/api/src/app.ts` | | `social.statusRoutes` mounted after the global limit and before `auth.resolveSession`, as `media.fileRoutes` is mounted before both. |
| `apps/api/src/notify` (new; the entry point `notify`) | `main.ts`, `imap.ts`, `push.ts` | The loop, the IMAP client, the publisher. It imports the contracts and nothing of the API's modules. |
| `apps/api/src/handover.ts` (new; the entry point `handover`) | | The hand-over's copy and its stored-entry zip writer. It opens no database and no socket, and imports no module: it keeps its own copy of the store's naming rule. |
| `apps/api/build.mjs`, `infra/`, `scripts/` | | Two more entry points (`dist/notify.js`, `dist/handover.js`); Compose and `compose.dev.yml`, the Caddyfile and Caddy's Dockerfile, `01-roles.sh`, `infra/ntfy/server.yml.example`, the env examples, `.gitignore`; `scripts/gen-dev-env.mjs`, `scripts/test-db.mjs`, `scripts/dev-mailbox.mjs` (new), `scripts/smoke-auth-stack.mjs`. |
| `apps/admin`, `apps/app` | | Sections 8 and 9. |

**Interfaces that change.** Modules still reach each other only through their indexes (3c 5).

- **`ModerationForStaff`** gains the order functions (open, read, list, correct, record an event, give effect), the contact messages' list and closing, the gaps' list and closing, and the home screen's banners (section 7).
- **The moderation module's index exports three plain functions for the command line**, each taking the caller's transaction and no dependency of the module, as the media module's `imageModerationStates` does (3c 13 Task 9): `parseLink`; `insertOrder(tx, facts)`, the rows of opening an order, which the route, `order-open` and the import all call; and the statement's data for `access-request`. The command line may enter a module only through its index, and the built module needs the API's whole configuration, which a command does not have.
- **The media module's index gains two read-only functions** that take the caller's transaction: `preservedImages(tx, postId)` (the image rows' ids, sizes and creation times; `post_media` holds no digest) for `order-access`, and `sealFiles(tx, sealActionId)` (for each post its image rows' stored names and the row count) for `handover-list`, which compares the count with `seal_posts.images` itself. The architecture test lets no file outside the media module name `post_media`, the command line included. A third read-only function, `imagePost(id)`, answers which post an image's address belongs to: opening an order resolves an image's link to its post through it (row 43). The store's naming rule is exported from the media module as one pure function; `handover.ts` keeps its own copy, and a unit test feeds both the same names and fails when they differ. The architecture test gains the pin: `handover.ts` imports nothing under `./`, and no other file outside `media/` names the store's directory setting, apart from `config.ts` and `index.ts`.
- **`staffAlert(deps, key, windowMs, alert)`** keeps its throttle and its fail-open rule (3c 7.12). After the throttle it does two things in this order: it inserts one row of `alert_outbox` (the kind, the priority and scope of that kind, the counts and times as `facts`), and it mails every staff account as today, with the caller's full message. For a kind whose mail says more than its push (the six kinds of 3c and 3e: `mail: "own"` in `ALERT_KINDS`) it then sets the row's `mailed_at`, whether or not an address took the mail: the row is there for the push alone, and an unsent mail stays 3c's case (the throttle's hit is given back and the next occasion sends it again, `alerts.ts:31`). For a kind of 3f's own that goes through it (`mail: "sentence"`: `order_opened`, `order_corrected`, `order_effected`, `order_reinstated`, `copy_taken`) it sets `mailed_at` when an address took the mail, and a row left without it is mailed by the tick, so no such mail goes twice. It answers `sent` when either leg took the alert. The time-driven alerts are not sent through it: `alert_steps()` writes their rows (6.1), and the alert tick mails them.
- **The alert tick**, a job of the moderation module under the job lock, every 30 seconds. It always runs, whatever `NOTIFY` says: an order's reminders, the unverified alert and the owed report are not about the mailbox.
  1. `select alert_steps()`.
  2. The mail leg: for every outbox row with `mailed_at` null **whose mail is its push sentence** (`mail: "sentence"`: every kind 3f adds, whoever wrote the row: the trigger, `alert_steps()`, `staffAlert` or a command), `mailStaff` with the row's kind and facts, then `mailed_at = now()`. It never mails a 3c or 3e kind: those need a handle or a list that `facts` cannot hold (`alert_outbox_facts`), so their mail cannot be rendered from the row.
  3. The heartbeat's check of 6.1: the tick's own inserts of `alert_channel_down` and `mailbox_unreachable`, under the outbox's once-in-24-hours rule and no limit-store key. With `NOTIFY=off` step 3 alone is skipped.
- **`AuthForModeration.mailStaff`** and `mailAccount`: their closed template unions gain the kinds of section 18. Each alert's push sentence is one entry of the contracts' `ALERT_TEXTS`, and its priority, scope and legs one entry of `ALERT_KINDS`; `notify` imports both and renders the sentence, and the mail template of the same kind begins with it. A mail to staff may say more than its push (3c's `moderation_burst` names a staff handle, and 3c's and 3e's kinds keep their mails word for word); a push never carries more than counts and UTC times.
- **The status route is the social module's own small router**, `social.statusRoutes`, built over the repository's read with no interface member: nothing outside the module calls it, so `SocialForModeration` gains nothing. `app.ts` mounts it after the global limit and before `auth.resolveSession`: Caddy's question for a page view carries the visitor's cookies, and a route mounted with the others would read a session for every page view. It parses the id itself and answers 204 exactly when the post's state is `visible`, and 404 with no body for anything else, a malformed id included.
- **The author's side.** Every read of `moderation/author-repo.ts` (the chip's `chipFacts`, the Cases screen, the decision's screen, the export's cases, the access draft) goes through one exported fragment, `toldToAuthor(d)`: `<d>.author_mail <> 'none' and <d>.informed_at is not null`. Both halves are needed. `informed_at` alone is set on every decision of 3c, also on those whose author is never told (a report answered with "no breach"), which today's reads keep out by `author_mail <> 'none'`; the short form would list on the Cases screen every report against the author that korbat dismissed. The architecture test pins that the fragment holds both halves and that no other file of the module selects from `decisions` for an author.
- **`AuthForAccount.finishPurge(userId)`** runs its two statements (6.5) and answers `"held"` when the second wrote nothing because `auth.address_held` is true; the purge round leaves the account pending (section 7).

**Wiring** (`apps/api/src/index.ts`, `app.ts`): `moderation.startJobs` starts the alert tick beside the dispatch and retention jobs, and `app.ts` mounts the status router ahead of the session; nothing else changes in shape. New configuration of the API: `HOME_STATE`, `CONTACT_POINT`, `PUSH_ORIGIN`, `NOTIFY`, `REPORT_HOLD` (below).

### The services

**New dependencies: none in korbat's programs; one separate service.** Before pinning, the build's supply-chain check confirms for each: a version at least 7 days old, no AGPL code linked into korbat's programs, the lockfile's trust policy unchanged.

| Need | Chosen | Candidate checked | Facts (source) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Push server | ntfy as a separate self-hosted service, never linked | `binwiederhier/ntfy:v2.28.0@sha256:6ef4b819f722fccdc036af611c4774cfdc2de821ab74fdd48bbf4c9d6f8973da`, the release read on 2026-10-05 | Released 2026-08-27; Apache-2.0 or GPLv2 at the operator's choice; not AGPL; one maintainer who supports only the latest release; 36 MB image (AS E13.3). **The pin that is built is the newest release at least 7 days old on the build's day**, by tag and digest, with its release notes read from v2.28.0 to it for the settings and paths this section uses (Task 1's first step); the runbook moves it once a month and at once on a security release (section 18). Not verified: its memory, a read-only root, its listening port under a set user with no capability, its answers through Caddy (AS E13.4 L1 to L5: Task 1 runs L2, L3 and L5 as its gate and starts the hour-long L1 and the twelve-hour L4 beside the task) |
| IMAP client | none: `apps/api/src/notify/imap.ts` on `node:tls` (row 3) | `imapflow` 2.1.0 (MIT, eight direct and ten new packages, one maintainer; AS E14.2) | The fallback if the mail host needs a capability the five commands lack; then the pin is the newest version seven days old on the build's day |
| Phone app | ntfy for Android, F-Droid build, 1.25.2 or later | the Play build; the iOS app | AS E13.3 points 5, 6; the owner's checks P1 to P8 and check M (section 18) |
| Zip writer | none: stored entries with `node:zlib`'s `crc32`, in `apps/api/src/handover.ts` | | Node 24; the form of `apps/api/src/account/zip.ts`, not imported (an entry point imports no module) |

**The Compose profile `notify`** holds the watcher and the push server. It is off in development and in CI unless a task's test needs the two services, and it is switched on in one place: `COMPOSE_PROFILES=notify` in `infra/.env`, which Compose reads for every command, so the README's commands stay as they are and an upgrade recreates the two containers with the rest. Three rules hold for every profiled service (`notify`, `ntfy`, `handover`), because a profile does not hide a service from Compose's parser:

- No setting of a profiled service is required by Compose: the file writes `${NAME:-}`, never `${NAME:?...}`, and the program refuses to start on an empty value, naming the setting (as `enrollSettings` does for the console). A `:?` in a profiled service fails `config`, `up`, `build`, `run` and `exec` for the whole file while the variable is unset, profile on or off: CI's check, `scripts/test-db.mjs`, and a production stack that has no mailbox yet.
- No profiled service has a bind mount whose source is a variable: an empty source is an invalid file for every command (`handover`, below).
- The `api` service has an image name, which `migrate`, `notify` and `handover` use, so none of them is built a second time on the server.

**`notify`**: a Compose service of its own, the API image with `node dist/notify.js`, under the profile `notify`.
