- Networks: `watch` and `push`. `watch` is a network of its own that holds PostgreSQL and `notify` and nothing else; it is not `internal`, so the mail host's port 993 is reached through its gateway. `notify` does not join `data`: there it would share a network with the API, which listens on every interface, and could send it requests past Caddy with any client address it liked, and reach the limit store's port. No published port.
- Database role `korbat_notify` (6.1): it can insert a message's four columns, read and mark outbox rows, and write its own state; nothing else.
- How it is held: the common block of the Node services (`restart: unless-stopped`, logging, `read_only`, `cap_drop: [ALL]`, `no-new-privileges`); `depends_on` `migrate` with `service_completed_successfully` (on a first start its tables do not exist before that) and `ntfy` with `service_started`. Its memory limit starts at 128 MB (the API has 512 MB, the worker 768 MB); Task 1 measures the bundle idle and after the 10,000-message first read, and the limit is set from that.
- **It stops itself rather than hang.** It exits 1 when PostgreSQL is gone for longer than a round, and a watchdog timer exits 1 when no round has finished for two minutes; Docker then starts it again, and until it is back the beat is old. A Compose health check reads the same. A watcher that stops at night still wakes nobody by itself (row 22).
- One loop, three duties:
  1. **Poll**, every 20 seconds, INBOX and the junk folder: `EXAMINE` (read-only), `UID FETCH <last+1>:* (UID INTERNALDATE)`, one insert a message. A changed UIDVALIDITY resets the folder's mark and every message of the folder is read again as a new row (the unique key holds `uidvalidity`); those received more than `stale_hours` ago are stale and raise no push. TLS with the roots compiled into Node, the certificate's name checked. `NOOP` between polls keeps the connection; any error closes it, and the next poll logs in again. The client reads four kinds of line and ignores every other line that holds no literal (section 7): the mailbox is shared with a person's mail app, and a server may send unrequested lines at any time (RFC 9051 2.2.2).
  2. **Publish** every `alert_outbox` row that is still owed its push (`pushed_at` null and younger than `push_owed_minutes`, 6.1), oldest first, every round, to `http://ntfy:2586/<topic>` (`korbat-oncall` for the scope `on_call`, `korbat-all` for `all`), with the fixed sentence of the row's kind rendered from `ALERT_TEXTS` and the row's `facts`, the row's priority and a tag. There is no cap on attempts: a push given up after five rounds would be lost to a two-minute restart of ntfy, on the night it carries the hour. A row past its bound is left to its mail.
  3. **Beat**: `notify_state.beat_at`, `mailbox_ok_at` and `push_ok_at` after each success, and the closed-list `error` of the last failure.
- It parses no mail content, holds no SMTP credential, and reads no table but its own four.
- **Two development-only settings**, refused when `NODE_ENV` is `production`: `NOTIFY_IMAP_PORT` (default 993) and `NOTIFY_IMAP_CA` (a PEM file's path, added to the roots for this one connection through `tls.connect`'s `ca`). The development stack has no mailbox (its mail catcher speaks SMTP only), so Task 1 builds `scripts/dev-mailbox.mjs`, without a dependency: the test fixture `fakeImap` with a second caller, serving an inbox and a junk folder on a local port with a key and certificate it makes on first run, and taking "drop a message" on standard input. The dry run starts `notify` on the host against it (`pnpm --filter @korbat/api notify:dev`), with ntfy in Compose and its port published by `compose.dev.yml`.

**What the mail host must offer** (the watcher's requirements; the owner chooses the host, section 18):

- IMAP over implicit TLS on port 993 with a certificate from a public authority.
- An application password, or a second login, that can only read mail: it cannot send, delete or change settings. No interactive or OAuth sign-in is forced for IMAP.
- A junk folder reachable over IMAP under a stable name, which is the setting `NOTIFY_IMAP_JUNK`; no server-side rule that moves or deletes mail before delivery to one of the two folders.
- The IMAP internal date is the time of final delivery at the host (RFC 9051 2.3.3), not the message's own `Date` header.
- Mail stored in the EU; remote content switched off in the host's own app, in which the person reads the order.

**`ntfy`**: network `push` only, which is `internal: true`: no route to the internet, so nothing can be forwarded upstream (hence no iPhone). `read_only` root, a volume `ntfy-data` for its cache and auth files, `user` set, `cap_drop: [ALL]`, the common block's restart and logging, and the limits Task 1's lab L1 measures.

- **Its configuration is a mounted file, not variables**: `infra/ntfy/server.yml`, git-ignored, with a committed `server.yml.example`, mounted read-only. A user's line holds a bcrypt hash (`$2a$10$...`), and Compose reads `$2a` and `$10` in an env file's value as variables and blanks them; a mounted file's content is not interpolated.
- The file sets: `auth-default-access: deny-all`; sign-up and login off; `web-root: disable`; `behind-proxy` with the header Caddy passes; `listen-http: ":2586"`, a port above 1024, since with `user` set and no capability the default port 80 depends on the engine letting an unprivileged user bind it; cache 12 hours (it is how a phone gets an alert published while its connection was down); no attachments; no metrics.
- Users, declared in that file (`auth-users`, `auth-access`, `auth-tokens`): `publisher`, write-only on `korbat-oncall` and `korbat-all`, whose token is `notify`'s; and one user a phone, read-only on both topics. Rights belong to users, never to tokens (AS E13.3 point 2). The publisher's token is therefore in two places, this file and `NTFY_PUBLISH_TOKEN` for `notify`, which the README says.
- Who is on call is which phone subscribes to `korbat-oncall`; every staff phone subscribes to `korbat-all`. Revoking a phone removes its user from the file and restarts ntfy. A real user is made with ntfy's own commands (`docker compose --profile notify run --rm ntfy user hash`, `ntfy token generate`): a token must start with `tk_` and be 32 characters long, which `gen-dev-env.mjs` cannot make, and Node has no bcrypt.
- **Development**: the example file carries one fixed development publisher and one development phone user, a token and a hash committed as examples (as Turnstile's test keys are in `infra/.env.example`); `gen-dev-env.mjs` copies the example when the file is missing; and `notify` refuses the example token when `NODE_ENV` is `production`, as the API refuses test keys.

**Caddy and the tunnel.**

- A third site, `:8091`, for the push hostname (`PUSH_ORIGIN`). It proxies to ntfy (`ntfy:2586`) only the paths a phone needs, the two topics' subscription and replay paths (AS E13.2 (b)), and answers 404 to `/v1/*` and everything else: ntfy's account API and its `/v1/stats` and `/v1/config` answer any caller otherwise. The tunnel maps one more public hostname to it. Caddy joins `push`. Caddy's Dockerfile exposes the third port, and `compose.dev.yml` publishes it for development (`DEV_PUSH_PORT`).
- **`push` gets a fixed subnet, and the app's and the console's sites refuse a client from it** (`@push remote_ip <subnet>`, answered 403). On `push`, ntfy and the watcher share a network with Caddy; without the rule both could reach `:8080` and `:8090`, and with the tunnel on Caddy keeps `CF-Connecting-IP` as it arrives, because until now only `cloudflared` could reach it. Task 1's smoke step checks the refusal from inside the ntfy container. So the stack's rule still holds with two additions: `cloudflared` reaches only Caddy; Caddy reaches only the API and ntfy; the API alone reaches the data services, but for the watcher's one network to PostgreSQL, on which its role can touch four tables.
- The console's site and policy do not change otherwise: the console never talks to ntfy.
- The app's site gains two things, and the caddy service its first `environment` entry (Task 4).
  - **`APP_SITE`** (`open`, the default, or `closed`), read when Caddy starts: `closed` answers 503 with a fixed plain text to every path, the API included (section 7, the restore).
  - **The page status of `/post/*`**: Caddy asks the API `GET /api/posts/<id>/status` and serves the app's shell under the status it got, 200 for 204 and 404 for 404, and the shell with 200 when the API cannot be asked or answers anything else, with the page's security headers the same in every case. Nobody has tried the directive on the pinned Caddy image, so Task 4's first step does, on ports of its own: the shell with 200 after a 204, the same bytes with 404 after a 404, 200 when the API is stopped and when it answers 429, the headers equal in all four. If the image's own directives cannot do it, the status is not built, row 17 goes back to the owner, and only `APP_SITE` is added.

**`handover`**: a Compose service under the profile `handover`, the API image with `node dist/handover.js`, `network_mode: none`, `media-store` read-only, `cap_drop: [ALL]`, `read_only`. **It has no bind mount in `compose.yml`**: the runbook's command passes the operator's directory, `docker compose --profile handover run --rm -T -v <directory>:/handover handover`, where the directory is owned by uid 1000 (the image's user `node`, which under `cap_drop: [ALL]` can write nowhere else) and lies on an encrypted volume. It reads the list from standard input, refuses a name outside the store's layout, copies byte for byte into one zip of stored entries, prints each name, size and SHA-256 and the archive's, and never decodes a file. It has no database credential: the command line makes the list and records the digests (row 34).

**The API** gains the alert tick, the status route, the routes under `/api/staff/orders`, `/api/staff/contact` and `/api/staff/restore`, and the audit export. It holds no mailbox credential, no ntfy token and no route to `push`.

**Configuration** (`infra/.env`; `scripts/gen-dev-env.mjs` generates what korbat makes). In `infra/.env` a setting of the API is written `API_<NAME>` and Compose maps it, as the stack's other API settings are (`API_MODERATION_RETENTION`); the README and every sentence that tells the operator what to set use that form. The table gives the name in `infra/.env` and, where it differs, the name the program reads. The console names no variable: its page cannot know the spelling, and says "switched off on this server".

| Name in `infra/.env` (read as) | Read by | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `COMPOSE_PROFILES` | Compose | holds `notify` once the mailbox and the phones exist: it starts the watcher and the push server with every command |
| `API_NOTIFY` (`NOTIFY`) | API | `on` or `off` (the default in development and tests): `off` skips the heartbeat's check alone; the tick runs, and outbox rows are still written and mailed. Until the mailbox exists the operator sets it `off`, and the console's home says that phone alerts are switched off on this server; otherwise a stack without a watcher would mail `alert_channel_down` daily |
| `KORBAT_NOTIFY_DB_PASSWORD` | `01-roles.sh`, `scripts/test-db.mjs`, `notify` (in `DATABASE_URL_NOTIFY`) | the password of `korbat_notify`, named as the three it joins; Compose passes it to the `postgres` service with `${...:-}`. `gen-dev-env.mjs` knows `notify` as a fourth role, and the test environment's check refuses a `DATABASE_URL_NOTIFY` that does not name a `_test` database |
| `NOTIFY_IMAP_HOST`, `NOTIFY_IMAP_USER`, `NOTIFY_IMAP_PASSWORD`, `NOTIFY_IMAP_JUNK` | `notify` only | the mailbox; the read-only application password is in no other service. A value that holds a line break is refused at start; the password and the folder's name are sent as quoted strings |
| `NOTIFY_IMAP_PORT`, `NOTIFY_IMAP_CA` | `notify`, development only | a local stand-in's port and certificate authority; refused in production |
| `NTFY_PUBLISH_TOKEN` | `notify` | the `publisher`'s token, which is also in `infra/ntfy/server.yml` |
| `API_PUSH_ORIGIN` (`PUSH_ORIGIN`) | API (section 10), README | the phones' hostname. Caddy's sites are named by port and do not read it |
| `API_HOME_STATE` (`HOME_STATE`) | API; the migrate service, for the command line | an ISO code; required once `CONTACT_POINT` is set (row 41) |
| `API_APP_ORIGIN` (`APP_ORIGIN`) | the migrate service, for the command line | the app's origin, against which `order-open` resolves a link as the console's route does |
| `API_CONTACT_POINT` (`CONTACT_POINT`) | API | the published address; empty until the owner's list is done, and the terms' and README's contact-point sentences print only when it is set (the terms through the web build's copy of it, the next row) |
| `WEB_CONTACT_POINT`, `WEB_HOME_STATE` (`EXPO_PUBLIC_CONTACT_POINT`, `EXPO_PUBLIC_HOME_STATE`) | the web build (Task 7) | the same two values for the app's terms: the app's page is built and cannot read the API's settings, so they reach it as build arguments, as `WEB_CONTACT_EMAIL` does today; the terms' contact-point section prints only while the first is set |
| `API_REPORT_HOLD` (`REPORT_HOLD`) | API | `on` (the default) or `off` (row 35) |
| `APP_SITE` | Caddy | `open` or `closed` |

The migrate service gets `HOME_STATE: ${API_HOME_STATE:-}` and `APP_ORIGIN: ${API_APP_ORIGIN:-}`, not required by Compose, since `enroll` and the migrations must run without them; `order-open` refuses with one sentence when either is missing. A command that reads standard input (`order-texts`, `order-document`, `address-check`, `handover-record`, `restore-import`) is run with `docker compose run --rm -T migrate ...`: without `-T` Compose allocates a terminal and a redirected file fails or arrives changed. There is no `HANDOVER_DIR`: the hand-over's directory is an argument of its command.

**When something is down.**

| Down | What still happens | Who learns, how |
|---|---|---|
| `notify` | Mail to the contact point is unread by korbat; no push. Staff alerts still go by mail. The console can open an order without a message (6.2), so the hour does not wait for the shell | `beat_at` older than 3 minutes: the API mails `alert_channel_down` and the console's home shows it. That mail is an ordinary mail: **at night it wakes nobody**. What is left is the mail host's own app on the on-call phone, signed in to the contact mailbox and allowed to ring through Do Not Disturb, which the owner's list and the runbook's On call section set up and test (section 18; row 22). Docker restarts a watcher that exited or hung (above) |
| ntfy, Caddy's push site or the tunnel | `notify` records messages and tries every row that is still owed its push, each round; the API mails every alert | a row owed its push for 3 minutes: the same mail and banner, which clear by themselves once the rows are published or past their bound; the phone shows "Connection lost" |
| The mailbox | Nothing arrives. The console can open an order without a message | `mailbox_ok_at` older than 3 minutes: push and mail `mailbox_unreachable` |
| The API | `notify` still pushes step 0 of a contact mail, which the database writes (6.1); no escalation, no mail, no console | the operator's own monitoring of the stack (unchanged from 3a) |
| The limit store | `staffAlert` goes without its throttle (3c 7.12). **The tick does not run**, since its job lock is in the same store: the first push of a contact mail still goes (`notify` uses no lock); there is no mail of it, no escalation, no reminder and no channel alarm until the store is back | the log |
| PostgreSQL | Nothing | everything above fails; the phones stay silent, which the daily test shows the next day. Nobody is woken: the mail host's app is again what is left |

### The lock order

3c's order stands as 3e leaves it (3e 5): profile rows first, sorted by id; then cases; then, inside a decision's triggers, the `auth.users` rows `FOR SHARE` and the cap's advisory lock; then the case's own post; then image rows in id order; then inserts, the audit row and the deferred checks. 3f adds:

1. **The effect transaction** ("Disable for an order", up to ten posts). The API role cannot lock a case, so the cases are taken by the attach trigger of the intake items. In this order:
   1. the authors' profiles `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, in id order, in one statement;
   2. one intake of kind `order` and its items, inserted in post id order: each insert takes its case (opening one where none is open), so the cases are taken in their targets' order;
   3. the order's items' rows, in post id order: the API writes each one's `intake_item_id`, and holds the row from there;
   4. then, post by post in id order: the snapshot, the decision and the measure. Inside the decision's triggers come the case `FOR UPDATE` again (free), **the order's row `FOR SHARE`**, the `auth.users` rows `FOR SHARE`, the cap's advisory lock, the post `FOR UPDATE` and its image rows;
   5. `append_confirmed_audit`, last.

   A decision or a seal about one of those authors waits at the profile and never meets the rest. One retry on `40P01`, and lock waits bounded by the challenge's life, as 3c 7.5; the bound counts one more wait an item, for its row.
2. **An order's row** is locked only inside definer functions, since the API role holds no UPDATE on `legal_orders`: `FOR SHARE` by `decisions_record` for an `order` decision and by `order_notices_release`; `FOR UPDATE` by `order_event_record` and `order_correct`; by its own update in a command of the command line. It comes after profiles and cases and before decisions. So a `closed_without_effect`, a correction, a hold's end or extension and the order's time stamp either commit before an effect and are read by it, or wait for it; and an extension either moves a release or is refused `already_released` (6.3). **A correction of an order that is given effect** begins as the effect does: the route takes the profiles of the authors of the order's posts `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, in id order, before it calls `order_correct`, which then takes the order's row, the order's decisions in id order and their posts in id order. So it runs before or after an effect, a decision or a purge about one of those authors, never across one. Without the profiles it could hold the order's row and wait for a post that an effect's snapshot holds, while that effect waits for the order's row.
3. **`order_preservation_end`** takes the case of the item's decision `FOR UPDATE`, then the item's row `FOR UPDATE`, the post `FOR UPDATE` and the post's image rows in id order `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, and reads what is due in a statement after them: the order of `redact_r4_body` (3c 6.4), with the item between the case and the post. The item comes before the post because the effect transaction holds the item's row before it locks any post; when the item's last decision closed its case, the effect opens a new case, and no case lock then orders the two. A correction that writes the item holds the same case, so that pair stays ordered. 3c's contention lab, which must gain the ten-item transaction (section 3), runs it against a retention round on one of its items.
4. **The purge and a disabled post.** `purgeProfile` anonymises its own profile row (which sets `deleted_at`) before it writes the tombstones, where today it does so after them; it holds that row `FOR UPDATE` from its first statement, so no lock moves. `posts_guard` then lets the tombstone of a `disabled` post through (6.3). The author's own `deletePost` filters `state <> 'disabled'` in its update and answers `not_found`; it holds the author's profile `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, as the effect transaction does, so the two run one after the other in either order.
5. **`finishPurge`** is two statements on the auth pool (6.5): the first clears the credentials without condition, the second overwrites the address with `and not auth.address_held(id)` in its WHERE. The function reads `auth.report_holds` and the clock, and no row of `public`.
6. **The audit head.** Every command of the command line and every confirmed action writes its audit row as its last statement, as 3a requires; `order_event_record`, `order_correct` and `gap_close` write before it, and the deferred checks of 6.2 and 6.6 only read after it.
7. **A decision that corrects an `order` decision** takes that decision's row `FOR SHARE` in `decisions_record` (6.3), after its case and before the order's row is read, and `order_notices_release` takes the untold decisions `FOR UPDATE` in id order: the two wait for each other on that row and on nothing else.
8. **The report's hold.** `report_open` takes the subject account's row of `auth.report_holds` `FOR UPDATE` inside the decision's triggers, after the image rows; `notification-sent` and `notification-declined` take that row first and the record after it (6.5).

## 6. Data model (migrations 0024 to 0029)

Six migrations, none changing a file that exists, each run by drizzle's migrator in one transaction with the others pending.

**The numbers.** The six are numbered from the first number free after 3e: 0024 to 0029 unless 3c's review task or 3e adds a migration, in which case each moves up by one. This section's headings and shorthand use the first numbering; no other section writes a migration's number, and a file is named by its name. Before Task 1, 3e is complete, and `--upgrade-from` names 3e's last migration.

| Migration | Name | Task | Holds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0024 | `messages_alerts`, generated and completed by hand | 1 (the watcher and the alert channel) | the role's check, `order_rules()`, messages, the outbox, `alert_steps()` (6.1) |
| 0025 | `orders`, generated and completed by hand | 2 (order records and the clock) | the enum values, the three order tables, the new columns, the hold and clock functions, `order_event_record`, `order_correct`, the deferred check of a console event (6.2) |
| 0026 | `orders_ledger` | 3 (giving effect) | the ledger's functions replaced, `post_preserved`, `order_notices_release` (6.3) |
| 0027 | `orders_retention` | 4 (preservation and retention) | four functions (6.4) |
| 0028 | `authority_records`, generated and completed by hand | 5 (records to authorities) | three tables without any API grant, `auth.report_holds`, their functions (6.5) |
| 0029 | `recovery`, generated and completed by hand | 6 (recovery) | `restore_gaps`, `copy_marks`, `gap_close` (6.6) |

0026 and 0027 are hand-written (`pnpm --filter @korbat/db generate:custom --name=...`) and change no table, so the generate gate prints "No schema changes" after Tasks 3 and 4.

**Conventions**, as 3c 6 and 3e 6: every function is owned by `korbat_migrate`, has its `search_path` pinned (`public, pg_temp` unless said), names every object schema-qualified and has EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC. Times written by the database are `now()`, the transaction's start, unless `clock_timestamp()` is named. A "forced" column is set by a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger whatever the insert supplied. An error carries its code as the message under SQLSTATE P0001 unless a SQLSTATE is named. A read that must see what committed while a lock waited is a statement of its own after that lock.

- **A new closed list is `text` with a CHECK**, never an enum: only the eight values of 6.2 extend an enum.
- **"For every role but the migrator"** in a plain trigger means `current_user <> 'korbat_migrate'`. Inside a definer function the current role is the migrator, so the ledger's triggers and the definer functions below pass such a trigger, and so does the command line.
- **Every new table** has row level security on, with an allow-all policy for each role that is granted anything on it. 0001's default privileges give `korbat_api` every write on a new table of `public` and `korbat_auth` every write on a new table of `auth`: each table's migration first revokes all of them and then grants what this section lists.

**Replacing a function.** `CREATE OR REPLACE` keeps owner and grants; the migration states each replaced function whole, as the built text with the changes this section lists and no other. Step numbers are those of the comments in `0016_decisions_and_ledger.sql`. At the clone's HEAD 0017 to 0023 are not built, so "as 3e leaves it" means the function as 3e 6 (and, where 3e does not replace it, 3c 6.3 or 6.4) designs it. `0017_appeals.sql` is in the clone's working tree, uncommitted (3c's Task 8), and already replaces `decisions_record`, `decisions_apply` and `decision_actions_apply`: for those three the base is 0017's text and whatever 3e's migrations make of it, not 0016's. The first step of the task that replaces a function is to compare its built text with the lab's patch (`make_proto.py`, an exact-text replacement written against 0016); Task 1 copies `lab-3f/` with its `RUN.txt` into the repository as `apps/api/test/lab/orders/`, where 3c keeps its labs, so that an implementer inside the repository can run it. 3c's and 3e's own tests of each replaced function run unchanged and pass (6.7), which is how a change outside the lists is caught.

**The rule for the new enum values** (3e 6; LAB A). A value added by `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` cannot be used as an enum literal before the migrating transaction commits (`55P04`). So in 0025 to 0029:

1. The eight new values are named as enum literals **only inside PL/pgSQL bodies**.
2. Views, SQL-language functions and CHECKs compare `kind::text`.
3. No index predicate and no default names one.
4. No migration statement executes a function that touches one.

A source test refuses a new value as a literal outside a PL/pgSQL body in these files (section 12).

### 6.1 Migration 0024: messages and alerts (Task 1)

**The role `korbat_notify`.** The role and its USAGE on the schema `public` are the superuser's to give, not the migration's: `korbat_migrate` does not own `public` and holds its privileges there without the grant option, so a `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public` inside a migration grants nothing and only warns (measured on PostgreSQL 18.1 by the build review: the tables' grants succeed and the role's first statement then answers `permission denied for schema public`). So, in three places:

- `infra/postgres/init/01-roles.sh` creates the role with `KORBAT_NOTIFY_DB_PASSWORD` (the form of the three passwords it joins) and runs `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA public TO korbat_notify`;
- `scripts/test-db.mjs`, in the block it runs as the superuser, creates the role when it is missing (a `DO` block on `pg_roles`, with the password of `infra/.env`) and gives the same grant on the test database;
- on a data directory that exists the operator runs two statements once, against korbat's database: the role, then the grant (README, Upgrading).

The migration grants only tables and functions. Its first statement raises `create the role korbat_notify first` unless the role exists **and** `has_schema_privilege('korbat_notify', 'public', 'USAGE')`, so a half-done upgrade stops at the migration and not at the watcher. The role gets nothing in `auth`.

**`public.order_rules()`**: an IMMUTABLE SQL function that returns one row of named values, EXECUTE to `korbat_api` and `korbat_notify`. Every function and view of this section reads its numbers from it. (The model places it in 0025; it is created here because this migration's trigger and `alert_steps()` read its alert numbers.)

**One constant mirrors one function**, as 3e's `STANDING_RULES` mirrors `standing_rules()`: the contracts' `ORDER_RULES` holds every column of `order_rules()`, in camel case under the same word (`preserve_margin_days` is `preserveMarginDays`), and the constants test compares the two name by name (6.7). Task 1 builds both. A number the database reads lives here and nowhere else: the alert numbers and the retention periods among them, so the contracts' `ALERT_RULES` keeps only what the database never reads (the watcher's poll, the tick's period, the mail's retry), and `RETENTION` reads 3f's periods from `ORDER_RULES`.

| Name | Value | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| `hour_minutes`, `target_minutes` | 60, 55 | a removal order's deadline and the console's target (row 10) |
| `items_per_action`, `decisions_per_day`, `records_per_day` | 10, 50, 20 | row 15; row 7 |
| `hold_days`, `hold_extension_days` | 43, 42 | `order_hold_end`, for a `tco_removal` (row 24) |
| `hold_warning_days` | 7 | every staff account is alerted this long before a held notice is released (row 46) |
| `unconfirmed_hold_hours` | null | row 25: no cap on a hold that nobody has confirmed |
| `preserve_months`, `preserve_margin_days` | 6, 1 | `order_preserve_until` (row 28) |
| `report_hold_days`, `report_alert_hours` | 7, 24 | rows 35, 33 |
| `stale_hours` | 2 | a message read again after an outage raises no push |
| `quiet_minutes` | 15 | a further message raises no first push of its own while an unhandled message's first push is this young (row 20); 0 switches the bound off |
| `escalate_minutes`, `remind_minutes` | 15, 45 | row 20 |
| `channel_down_minutes`, `push_owed_minutes` | 3, 60 | row 22: when the channel reads as down, and for how long a row is still owed its push |
| `copy_overdue_hours`, `handover_alert_days` | 48, 7 | rows 36, 34 |
| `message_keep_days`, `record_keep_years`, `gap_keep_months` | 30, 3, 12 | row 42 |

**`contact_messages`**: one row a message at the contact point. It holds no sender, no subject and no content.

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | `uuid` primary key | default `gen_random_uuid()` |
| `folder` | `text not null` | `inbox` or `junk` |
| `uidvalidity`, `uid` | `bigint not null` | unique with `folder` (`contact_messages_uid_key`) |
| `received_at` | `timestamptz not null` | the IMAP internal date |
| `seen_at` | `timestamptz not null` | forced, `clock_timestamp()` |
| `stale` | `boolean not null` | forced: `received_at < seen_at - stale_hours` |
| `disposition` | `text` | null, or one of `order`, `referral`, `first_order_notice`, `preservation_request`, `scrutiny_decision`, `clarification`, `not_an_order` |
| `handled_by` | `uuid` | references `profiles` |
| `handled_at` | `timestamptz` | forced when the disposition is set |

- Grants. `korbat_notify`: INSERT (`folder`, `uidvalidity`, `uid`, `received_at`), SELECT. `korbat_api`: SELECT, UPDATE (`disposition`, `handled_by`).
- `contact_messages_insert`, plain, BEFORE INSERT, for every role but the migrator: forces `seen_at` and `stale`, `received_at = least(supplied, clock_timestamp())`, and `disposition`, `handled_by` and `handled_at` null. A mail host whose clock runs a few seconds ahead would otherwise meet `legal_orders_received` as a raw CHECK failure when a person opens the order at once; the lesser only moves a deadline earlier.
- `contact_messages_guard`, plain, BEFORE UPDATE, for every role but the migrator: a disposition that is set never changes (`message_handled`); setting it needs `handled_by` and forces `handled_at = now()`. A definer function that sets a disposition as its owner sets `handled_at` itself (6.2, `order_event_record` step 5).
- `contact_messages_alert`, AFTER INSERT, SECURITY DEFINER: unless the message is stale, it inserts the outbox row of kind `contact_mail`, step 0, priority 5, scope `on_call`, with the message's id and `facts` `{ "receivedAt", "dueAt" }` (`dueAt` is `received_at + hour_minutes`). So the first push needs neither the API nor a job.
- **The bound on a flood.** The trigger writes no step-0 row for a message while another message that is not stale has no disposition and its own step-0 row is younger than `quiet_minutes`: the person who is already awake reads the whole mailbox, and the first message's deadline is the earlier one. The message's row is written all the same, and its escalation and its `order_due` run as for any message (the table below), so nothing is dropped. It bounds what anyone who knows the public address can do to the on-call phone to one first push in `quiet_minutes`; with 0 every message rings (row 20).

**`alert_outbox`**: one row an alert, with its two legs.

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | `bigint` identity primary key | |
| `kind` | `text not null` | the closed list below |
| `priority` | `smallint not null` | 3 or 5 |
| `scope` | `text not null` | `on_call` or `all`: the topic |
| `step` | `smallint` | null for an alert an event raised; a running number, from 0, for a time-driven one |
| `facts` | `jsonb not null` | CHECK `alert_outbox_facts`: an object of at most 6 members, each a number or a string of the form of a UTC timestamp. Counts and times only |
| `message_id`, `order_id` | `uuid` | the subject of a stepped alert; no foreign keys, so a record's erasure never waits for an alert's row |
| `created_at` | `timestamptz not null` | forced, `clock_timestamp()` |
| `pushed_at`, `mailed_at` | `timestamptz` | forced null at insert |
| `push_attempts` | `integer not null` | forced 0 at insert; a counter for the log, and no cap (below) |

- The kinds: `contact_mail`, `contact_mail_escalated`, `order_due`, `order_opened`, `order_corrected`, `order_effected`, `order_reinstated`, `order_unverified`, `hold_ending`, `report_owed`, `handover_undestroyed`, `copy_overdue`, `copy_taken`, `alert_channel_down`, `mailbox_unreachable`, `alert_test`, and 3c's and 3e's `moderation_urgent`, `moderation_waiting`, `moderation_burst`, `moderation_reads`, `moderation_reveal`, `moderation_csam_lifted`.
- **Which leg a kind has** is a member of its entry in the contracts' `ALERT_KINDS` (section 10), with its priority and scope. `alert_channel_down` is mailed only and `alert_test` is pushed only: `notify` and the alert tick each skip the kind that is not theirs. The tick mails only the kinds whose mail is their push sentence (`mail: "sentence"`: every kind 3f adds); the six kinds of 3c and 3e, whose mail says more than the push (`mail: "own"`), are mailed by `staffAlert` itself, and their row is there for the push alone (section 5).
- **Scope and priority** are those of `ALERT_KINDS`. The database's own writers (the trigger above and `alert_steps()`) write `on_call` for `contact_mail` and `all` for every other kind they write; the priorities are section 10's.
- **A row is owed its push** while `pushed_at` is null and it is younger than `push_owed_minutes`. `notify` tries such a row every round, oldest first; an alert about the hour is worth sending while the hour runs, and ntfy's cache then replays it to a phone that reconnects. A row past that age is left to its mail and counts for nothing any more, so one failed push never reads as an outage for good.
- Unique index `alert_outbox_step_key (kind, message_id, order_id, step) NULLS NOT DISTINCT where step is not null`.
- `alert_outbox_insert`, plain, BEFORE INSERT, forces `created_at`, `pushed_at`, `mailed_at` and `push_attempts` for every role.
- Grants. `korbat_api`: INSERT, SELECT, UPDATE (`mailed_at`). `korbat_notify`: SELECT, UPDATE (`pushed_at`, `push_attempts`). A command of the command line whose console twin alerts (`order-open` first) inserts the row itself, as the migrator, in its transaction before its audit row: `notify` pushes it and the tick mails it, so an order opened from the shell is heard like any other.

**`notify_state`** (one row, `id = 1`: `beat_at`, `mailbox_ok_at`, `push_ok_at timestamptz`; `error text`, null or one of `imap_connect`, `imap_timeout`, `imap_tls`, `imap_login`, `imap_folder`, `imap_protocol`, `push_connect`, `push_refused`: the CHECK's eight words, which every section uses) and **`notify_folders`** (`folder text` primary key, `uidvalidity bigint`, `last_uid bigint`). `korbat_notify`: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE on both. `korbat_api`: SELECT on both.

**`public.alert_steps() returns integer`**, PL/pgSQL, SECURITY DEFINER (`search_path = public, auth, pg_temp`), EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: every time-driven alert, as inserts into `alert_outbox`. It answers how many rows it wrote. One rule for all of them: **a row is written when its condition holds and no row of the same kind and subject was created at or after its threshold**; its `step` is the subject's highest step of that kind plus one, numbered for each kind by itself; `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on the unique key drops what a concurrent call wrote. 0025, 0026, 0028 and 0029 replace the function, each adding the rows of its task. A daily kind has the rolling threshold "24 hours ago": the function fixes no hour of the day, which would be a number outside `order_rules()`.

| Kind | Subject | Condition | Threshold | Since |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `contact_mail_escalated` | the message | not stale, no disposition, and no staff session was created or used since `received_at` (`auth.staff_sessions.created_at`, `last_used_at`: a person's own action in the console, so someone already signed in who opens the message stops it too) | `received_at + escalate_minutes`, once | 0024 |
| `order_due` | the message | not stale, no disposition | the later of `received_at + remind_minutes` and `received_at + hour_minutes` that has passed | 0024 |
| `alert_test` | none | always | 24 hours ago | 0024 |
| `contact_mail` | the order | a `tco_removal` that is not closed, whose clock was started again by a `grounds_ceased` or a `clarification_received` and runs, with `items_open > 0` | that restart's `clock_start`, once a restart: the step is the order's highest `contact_mail` step plus one, so a second restart alerts again. Scope `on_call`, priority 5, as a message's first push | 0025 |
| `order_due` | the order | a `tco_removal` that is not closed, whose clock runs (`legal_order_clock`: not stopped) with `items_open > 0`: an order given effect in part is still due | the later that has passed of `clock_start + remind_minutes` and `deadline_at` | 0025 |
| `order_unverified` | none | at least one of: an order has an item with `disabled_at` and (`verified_at` is null or that item's `added_at` is later than `verified_at`); an order that is given effect has no `reasons` recorded; a DSA order holds a notice without an end (`order_hold_until` is `'infinity'` and a decision of it is untold) | 24 hours ago | 0025 |
| `hold_ending` | none | an order has an untold decision and its `order_hold_until` is less than `hold_warning_days` ahead | 24 hours ago | 0026 |
| `report_owed` | none | at least one notification record is owed (6.5) and was opened more than `report_alert_hours` ago | 24 hours ago | 0028 |
| `handover_undestroyed` | none | a production holds `files`, was delivered more than `handover_alert_days` ago and has `destroyed_at` null | 24 hours ago | 0028 |
| `copy_overdue` | none | the newest `copy_marks.at` is older than `copy_overdue_hours` (or there is none and an audit row is that old) | 24 hours ago | 0029 |

The facts of each row are counts and times read in the same statement (how many, since when, the deadline). `order_unverified` carries one count for each of its three conditions (`count`, `textsMissing`, `holdsOpen`, with `oldestAt`; the names are section 10's, as every fact's are), and `hold_ending` the count of held notices and the first end (`count`, `at`), so that whoever has the server checks the mailbox for an extension before the database releases. A message from which an order was opened has the disposition `order`, so its own reminders end there and the order's begin. `copy_overdue` has this one writer: no round of the API raises it through `staffAlert` as well.

**The schedule of `order_due`** is two alerts a subject: at `remind_minutes` (45) and at the deadline (60). An order's reminders end when it is given effect in full, closed, or stopped by an Annex III, never at its first item. A row of `order_due` or `contact_mail` whose subject is still open is not deleted by the retention job (6.4), so an old threshold is never met a second time.

**The heartbeat's check** is the alert tick's, not this function's, so that `NOTIFY=off` can skip it (section 5). It is the tick's own insert into `alert_outbox`, with no limit-store key: `alert_channel_down` when `beat_at` is older than `channel_down_minutes`, or a row that is still owed its push (above) is older than that; and `mailbox_unreachable` when `beat_at` is fresh and `mailbox_ok_at` is older than that. Each at most once in 24 hours, by the rule above. Because a row past `push_owed_minutes` is owed nothing, the line and the alarm clear by themselves, at the latest that long after the push server is back.

### 6.2 Migration 0025: orders (Task 2)

**Eight enum values**, each with `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE`: `action_kind` gains `disable_by_order` (a measure, on a post case, in an `order` decision) and `reinstate_by_order` (its reversal, in a correction); `decision_kind` gains `order`; `decision_source` gains `order`; `intake_kind` gains `order`; `intake_channel` gains `authority`; `moderation_level` and `post_state` gain `disabled`. `media_moderation_state` gains nothing: an image follows its post's state in every reader.

**`legal_orders`**: one row an order. "Console" columns are supplied by the API's insert; "command line" columns are written only by the migrator; "forced" as the conventions say.

| Column | Type | Set by | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | `uuid` primary key | console | supplied, so the audit row can name it |
| `kind` | `text not null` | console | `tco_removal`, `dsa_art9` or `dsa_art10` |
| `member_state` | `text not null` | console | `^[A-Z]{2}$`: the issuing state |
| `authority`, `reference` | `text not null` | console | 1 to 200 characters each; unique together with `member_state` (`legal_orders_reference_key`) |
| `channel` | `text not null` | console; forced `mailbox` with a message | `mailbox`, `portal`, `post` or `other`: how the order arrived. An order opened from a message is `mailbox`, by force; one opened without a message stores the channel the person picks in the form ("How it arrived", section 8), and the command line supplies its own. `order_correct` corrects it (below) |
| `message_id` | `uuid` | console | references `contact_messages`; null for an order opened without a message (below) |
| `received_at` | `timestamptz` | forced | the named message's `received_at`; null for an order without a message, until the command line records it |
| `sent_at`, `issued_at`, `due_at` | `timestamptz` | command line | the sending time the channel states; the order's own time stamp; a DSA order's deadline as its text states it |
| `section_c` | `boolean not null` | console | "the author must not be told": Section C of a removal order, or the same statement in a DSA order's text. No default: an insert that leaves it out is refused. The form's third choice, "I cannot tell", is stored as true, so an untouched or unclear box never tells |
| `emergency` | `boolean not null` | console | as the order shows it |
| `cross_border` | `boolean not null` | console | the API sets it: the issuing state is not `HOME_STATE` |
| `withhold_from_author` | `boolean not null` | console | forced true for a `tco_removal` and for any order with `section_c`: a notice can be held only on a post its author cannot see |
| `preserve` | `boolean not null` | forced | true for a `tco_removal`, false otherwise |
| `authority_hold_until` | `timestamptz` | command line | the end the authority states. For a `tco_removal` it counts when earlier than the computed end; for a DSA order it is the end, of any length |
| `hold_basis` | `text` | command line | at most 500 characters: the provision or the order's sentence that the end rests on (for a DSA order) |
| `hold_extended` | `boolean not null default false` | definer, command line | the one extension of a `tco_removal`: by `order_event_record` (an admin, from a picked message) or by the command line |
| `hold_ended_at`, `hold_end_reason` | `timestamptz`, `text` | definer, command line | the reason is `not_authentic`, `annulled`, `released_by_authority` or `not_ordered` (korbat's own mistake: the order held no such statement) |
| `tell_person`, `tell_person_at` | `text`, `timestamptz` | command line | for a `dsa_art10` only: when the person the order concerns is told (`at_effect`, `at_instant` with the instant, or `never`); its basis is `hold_basis`. Null until recorded |
| `opened_by` | `uuid` | console | references `profiles` |
| `opened_via`, `opened_at` | `text`, `timestamptz not null` | forced `console`, `clock_timestamp()` | `console` or `cli` |
| `verified_at`, `verified_how` | `timestamptz`, `text` | command line | at most 500 characters: which register entry, how checked |
| `reasons`, `reasons_salt`, `reasons_digest`, `redress`, `redress_salt`, `redress_digest` | `text` | command line | the authority's reasons and its Section G (for a DSA order, its statement of redress), at most 4000 characters each, held as `staff_texts` holds a text |
| `scope`, `scope_salt`, `scope_digest` | `text` | command line | for a DSA order: the order's territorial scope in the authority's words (DSA Art. 9(5)), at most 500 characters, held the same way |
| `document_sha256`, `document_bytes` | `text`, `bigint` | command line | the order's document is not stored (row 9) |
| `closed_at`, `imported_at` | `timestamptz` | definer, command line | `closed_at` is set and cleared by `order_event_record` (`closed_without_effect`, `reopened`) and cleared by the command line's `order-reopen`; `imported_at` by the import |

- CHECK `legal_orders_received`: `received_at` and `sent_at` are not after `opened_at`. CHECK `legal_orders_issued`: `issued_at` is not after `coalesce(received_at, opened_at)`, so a mistyped time stamp cannot lengthen a hold. CHECK `legal_orders_tco`: a `tco_removal`, and any order with `section_c`, has `withhold_from_author`; a `tco_removal` has `preserve`. CHECK `legal_orders_tell`: `tell_person` is null or one of its three values, is null unless the kind is `dsa_art10`, and `tell_person_at` is set exactly when it is `at_instant`.
- Grants. `korbat_api`: SELECT; INSERT on the console's columns; no UPDATE.
- `legal_orders_insert`, plain, BEFORE INSERT. For every role but the migrator, in this order: an advisory lock of the opening account (`korbat.order_records:<id>`); `rate_limited` when that account opened `records_per_day` orders in the last 24 hours; the message's test (below); then it forces `opened_at`, `opened_via` and `received_at`, forces `channel` to `mailbox` when a message is named and keeps the supplied channel when none is (one of the four, by the column's CHECK), and nulls every command-line column. For every role it forces `withhold_from_author` true for a `tco_removal` and for an order with `section_c`, and `preserve`.
- **The message's test.** A named message must exist (`order_needs_message`). An insert that names none is accepted at any time, whatever the watcher's state: the trigger reads no row of `notify_state`. So when the watcher is down, the mailbox cannot be read, or the watcher looks healthy and never wrote a row for the order (the mail host filed it in a third folder, or it came another way), the person on call, who reads the order in the mail host's app, opens it from the console: `received_at` is null, `channel` is what the person picked under "How it arrived", the console says "deadline unknown: act now", and the command line records the instant afterwards (row 4). The hour never depends on a shell.

**`legal_order_items`**: one row a link the order names.

- `id uuid` primary key; `order_id uuid not null` references `legal_orders`; `position smallint not null`, 1 to 50, unique in its order; `link text not null`, at most 500 characters, as named; `post_id uuid`, null for a link that named nothing, with no foreign key (a record outlives its post), unique in its order where not null (`legal_order_items_post_key`).
- `added_at timestamptz not null`: forced `clock_timestamp()` (the import supplies it). It says which items a verification saw: an item added after `verified_at` is unverified until `order-verify` runs again.
- `intake_item_id uuid` references `intake_items`: set by the API inside the effect transaction.
- Written only by the ledger's triggers and the migrator: `decision_id`, `action_id` (the newest decision and measure that serve the item), `disabled_at` (the first disabling), `preserve_until`, `preserve_ended_at`, `reinstated_at`.
- Written only by `order_correct` (below) and the migrator: `closed_at timestamptz` and `closed_reason text`, `no_post` (the link names nothing korbat can disable for an order: an account, another site, a post that does not exist), `not_named` (the order does not name this link: korbat's own mistake in entering it) or `withdrawn` (the authority withdrew this item before korbat acted on it, while other items of the order stand). CHECK `legal_order_items_closed`: both are null or both are set, the reason is one of these three, and a closed item has no `disabled_at`.
- Index `legal_order_items_preserved_idx (post_id) where preserve_until is not null`: the freeze's fourth arm.
- Grants. `korbat_api`: SELECT; INSERT (`id`, `order_id`, `position`, `link`, `post_id`); UPDATE (`intake_item_id`). `legal_order_items_insert`, plain, BEFORE INSERT, forces `added_at`, and the other nine columns null, for every role but the migrator.

Three terms, used from here on:

- An item is **open** while it has neither `disabled_at` nor `closed_at`. An item whose link named no post is open too, until a person closes it with `no_post`: the order named something, and somebody must have looked at it.
- An order is **given effect** when one of its items has `disabled_at`, and **given effect in full** when it is given effect and none of its items is open. The reminders, the alerts and the clock's judgement of the hour follow every item: they end only when the order is given effect in full (6.1, the view below).
- An item's measure **stands** while its `action_id` is in `measures_in_force`.

**`legal_order_events`**, insert-only (no UPDATE, DELETE or TRUNCATE for the API role): `id uuid` primary key; `order_id uuid not null`; `item_id uuid`; `kind text not null`; `at timestamptz not null`; `effective_at timestamptz`, not after `at`; `ground text`; `by_staff uuid`; `via text not null` (`console`, `cli` or `system`). Index `(order_id, at)`.

| Kind | Written by | Ground |
|---|---|---|
| `opened` | the API's insert, with the order | |
| `annex3_sent` | `order_event_record` | `force_majeure`, `manifest_error` or `insufficient_information` |
| `grounds_ceased`, `clarification_received`, `annex2_sent`, `receipt_confirmed` | `order_event_record` | |
| `closed_without_effect` | `order_event_record` | `withdrawn`, `annulled`, `scrutiny`, `not_authentic`, `wrong_item` or `not_addressed` |
| `reopened` | `order_event_record` (an admin) or the command line (`order-reopen`) | |
| `hold_extended` | `order_event_record` (an admin, from a picked message) or the command line | |
| `corrected` | `order_correct` | which rule allowed it: `entry` (before any effect), `caution` (after effect, "must not be told" set) or `admin` (after effect, anything else) |
| `effect_given`, `reinstated` | `decision_actions_apply` (6.3) | for `reinstated` the measure's `order_ground` |
| `notice_released` | `order_notices_release` (6.3) | |
| `preservation_ended` | `order_preservation_end` (6.4), `order_correct` | |
| `verified`, `received_set`, `sent_at_set`, `issued_at_set`, `due_set`, `hold_until_set`, `texts_recorded`, `document_recorded`, `preservation_extended`, `scrutiny_requested`, `tell_person_set`, `person_told`, `imported` | the command line | |
| `hold_ended` | the command line; `order_correct` (with `not_ordered`) | the hold's end reason |
| `scrutiny_decided` | the command line | `upheld` or `annulled` |

- CHECK `legal_order_events_ground`: the ground is one of its kind's list, and null for a kind without one. Each list is a constant of the contracts with the same spelling, and the table test compares the two (section 10).
- Unique index `legal_order_events_opened_key (order_id) where kind = 'opened'`: an order is opened once.
- Grants. `korbat_api`: SELECT, INSERT.
- `legal_order_events_insert`, plain, BEFORE INSERT. For every role but the migrator: only `opened` is accepted (`order_event_cli_only` for a command-line kind, `order_event_refused` for any other), and `at` is forced `clock_timestamp()` and `via` `console`. The migrator may supply `at` (the import); a null `at` becomes `clock_timestamp()`. So the console's events have two writers, `order_event_record` and `order_correct`, which run as their owner. (The model let the API role insert them itself; a state check that the API could step around would not be one.)
- **`legal_order_events_confirmed`**, a deferred constraint trigger, AFTER INSERT, for every event with `via = 'console'` whose kind is not `opened`: a console's event stands only with its audit row, as a decision does (`decision_holds`). At commit, the transaction's head audit row must be the matching action on this order (`target_type` `order`), must name this event (`metadata.eventId`) and must have been written by the event's `by_staff`. The action is `order_event_recorded` for the events of `order_event_record`, and it carries a step-up; it is `order_corrected` for a `corrected` event, and carries a step-up when the event's ground is `admin`. Otherwise the commit is refused `order_event_refused`. The trigger only reads, so the audit row stays the transaction's last write. With it, a statement run as the API's role cannot stop a clock, close or reopen an order, extend a hold or correct a record without a row that `audit-verify` checks.

**`decisions`** gains `order_item_id uuid` references `legal_order_items`. `informed_at` loses NOT NULL, with the CHECK `decisions_informed`: `informed_at is not null or author_mail::text = 'none'`. An untold decision has no mail pending, whatever its kind: under a hold, korbat's own decisions about the same post are untold too (6.3, step 10). Which decisions may be untold is `decisions_record`'s rule, and the function test pins who writes the column (6.7). `decisions_restricted` and `decisions_ground_law` are dropped and added again with `or kind::text = 'order'`: an order decision restricts on the law with no text of korbat's. **`decision_actions`** gains `withhold_author boolean` (forced, 6.3) and `order_ground text` (`withdrawn`, `annulled`, `scrutiny`, `not_authentic` or `wrong_item`; supplied and signed). Until 0026 no function accepts an `order` decision, so nothing stores a new value before Task 3.

**Plain functions**, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`.

- **`order_hold_end(from_instant timestamptz, extended boolean) returns timestamptz`**, IMMUTABLE SQL: `(((from_instant AT TIME ZONE 'Etc/GMT-3')::date + hold_days + (hold_extension_days when extended))::timestamp) AT TIME ZONE 'Etc/GMT+1'`. The POSIX signs are inverted: the calendar date of the instant in UTC+3, then midnight in UTC-1. It names no session time zone and gives the same value in any (LAB E).
- **`order_hold_until(order_id uuid) returns timestamptz`**, STABLE SQL: null when the order has no `section_c`; `hold_ended_at` when set; else by the kind of order, compared as text:
  - a `tco_removal`: the lesser of `order_hold_end(coalesce(issued_at, received_at, opened_at), hold_extended)` and `authority_hold_until`. The Regulation's period and its one extension; never early;
  - a `dsa_art9` or a `dsa_art10`: `authority_hold_until` when the command line has recorded it, of any length, earlier or later than six weeks; and `'infinity'` while it has recorded none. DSA Art. 9(5) and 10(5) tell the person "at the time provided by the issuing authority", and the Regulation's weeks do not cap that. `order_hold_end` and `hold_extended` are not read for these kinds.

  A notice is **held** while this is later than the clock. A hold without an end is ended only from the command line (`hold_ended_at`, with its reason) or by a recorded end, and is alerted daily while it lasts (6.1, `order_unverified`).
- **`order_preserve_until(disabled timestamptz) returns timestamptz`**, IMMUTABLE SQL: `((disabled AT TIME ZONE 'UTC') + preserve_months + preserve_margin_days) AT TIME ZONE 'UTC'`, the form of `appeal_deadline`.

**`legal_order_clock`**, a `security_invoker` view over the `tco_removal` orders, SELECT to `korbat_api`:

| Column | Is |
|---|---|
| `order_id` | |
| `clock_start` | the greatest of `least(received_at, sent_at)` and the newest `coalesce(effective_at, at)` of a `grounds_ceased` or `clarification_received`; null while neither time is recorded |
| `stopped` | an `annex3_sent` whose `at` is later than that newest restart exists |
| `deadline_at`, `target_at` | `clock_start + hour_minutes`, `+ target_minutes`; null while stopped |
| `items_open` | how many of the order's items are open (6.2: neither `disabled_at` nor `closed_at`) |
| `first_effect_at`, `last_effect_at` | the earliest and the latest `disabled_at` of the order's items. Annex II states the first |
| `within_hour` | null while `items_open > 0` or nothing was disabled; afterwards `last_effect_at <= clock_start + hour_minutes`, and true when the last effect was given while the clock was stopped (an order stopped at minute 30 and given effect at minute 90 with no restart is not outside its hour) |

The database records times; it does not judge an Annex III. When the clock is stopped, since when and on which ground (the contracts' `stoppedAt` and `stopGround`) are the `at` and the `ground` of the newest `annex3_sent` event, which the API reads from the events beside the view. One item given effect ends no reminder and proves no hour: `order_due` runs while `items_open > 0` (6.1), and the hour reads as met only when the last open item was disabled or closed inside it.

**`public.order_event_record(order_id uuid, kind text, ground text, message_id uuid, by_staff uuid) returns uuid`**, PL/pgSQL, SECURITY DEFINER (`search_path = public, auth, pg_temp`), EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: the one writer of the six clock events (`annex3_sent`, `grounds_ceased`, `clarification_received`, `annex2_sent`, `receipt_confirmed`, `closed_without_effect`) and of `reopened` and the console's `hold_extended`. (The model's signature has four parameters; the fifth is who acted.) Each refusal is `order_event_refused` with a detail unless a code is named. In this order:

1. The order's row `FOR UPDATE`. `order_closed` when `closed_at` is set, for every kind but `reopened`; `reopened` is refused unless it is set.
2. In a statement of its own, the acting account's `auth.users` row `FOR SHARE`: it is live staff (`staff_confirmation_ended` otherwise), and an admin for `closed_without_effect`, `reopened` and `hold_extended` (`admin_required`).
3. The kind fits the order:
   - `annex3_sent`, `grounds_ceased`, `clarification_received`, `annex2_sent` and `hold_extended` only on a `tco_removal`; `receipt_confirmed` only on a DSA order.
   - `annex3_sent` only while the clock is not stopped, with a ground of its list.
   - A restart only while the clock is stopped, and by the ground of the newest `annex3_sent`: `grounds_ceased` after `force_majeure`, `clarification_received` after `manifest_error` or `insufficient_information`. So every Annex III has exactly one restart, and the database holds the rule the console shows. `clarification_received` names a message (`order_needs_message`).
   - `annex2_sent` only when the order is given effect.
   - `closed_without_effect` only while no item's measure stands, with a ground of its list.
   - `reopened` only on a closed order: an order closed by mistake, or one a court restored, is open again with its items, events and clock as they were.
   - `hold_extended` only with `section_c`, while `hold_extended` is false, and names a message (`order_needs_message`): the authority's reasoned extension, as the person read it. It is refused `already_released` when an event `notice_released` of the order exists or `hold_ended_at` is set.
4. The effective time: the named message's `received_at`, or null (the event's own time). For a restart it is the later of that and the `at` of the newest `annex3_sent`, and nothing is refused: an Annex III sent at 10:00 and recorded at 10:30, with the clarification of 10:20, restarts the clock at 10:30, which is the later and the cautious reading. (In LAB B a clarification dated before the Annex III left the clock stopped; the task's lab runs this case.)
5. A named message that has no disposition gets `clarification`, `handled_by` the acting account and `handled_at = now()`. The function sets the time itself: it runs as its owner, for whom `contact_messages_guard` forces nothing.
6. `closed_without_effect` sets the order's `closed_at`; `reopened` clears it; `hold_extended` sets `hold_extended`, the same single extension the command line records, which the command line confirms (`order-hold-extend`) or undoes (`order-hold-end`) afterwards.
7. It inserts the event with `via = 'console'` and answers its id.

The route then writes the audit row `order_event_recorded` through `append_confirmed_audit`, with the kind, the ground and the message as its params and the event's id as `metadata.eventId`; `legal_order_events_confirmed` refuses the commit without it. So `by_staff` is not taken on trust: what the key confirmed is what is stored.

**`public.order_correct(order_id uuid, facts jsonb, by_staff uuid) returns uuid`**, PL/pgSQL, SECURITY DEFINER (`search_path = public, auth, pg_temp`), EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: the one writer of what a person entered, after the insert. A record typed wrongly at night must never stand in the way of the hour, and a missed "must not be told" must never end in telling. `facts` holds only the members to change, under the contracts' names: `kind`, `memberState`, `authority`, `reference`, `channel`, `sectionC`, `emergency`, `withholdFromAuthor`, `crossBorder` (which the API sets with `memberState`), and `closeItems`, a list of `{ itemId, reason }`. Any other member is `invalid_request`. `channel` is one of the column's four values and is taken for an order without a message alone: an order opened from a message stays `mailbox`, and a `channel` for it is `invalid_request`. It falls under the rules of step 2 like every member, `entry` before the first effect and `admin` after it, so a wrong channel never stands. In this order:

1. The order's row `FOR UPDATE`; `order_closed` when it is closed (an admin reopens it first). In a statement of its own, the acting account's `auth.users` row `FOR SHARE`: live staff.
2. Which rule allows it, stored as the event's ground:
   - `entry`: no item of the order has `disabled_at`. Any staff member, any member of `facts`, no step-up.
   - `caution`: the order is given effect, and `facts` holds `sectionC: true` and nothing else. Any staff member, no step-up: whoever is on call sets a missed "must not be told" at once.
   - `admin`: the order is given effect, and `facts` holds anything else (unsetting `sectionC` among it). An admin (`admin_required`), under a step-up.
3. Items: each named item must be of this order and open; it gets `closed_at = now()` and its reason. An item that was given effect is never closed: a wrong one is reinstated with `wrong_item` (row 18).
4. The row's columns are written, and the forced ones follow as at the insert: `withhold_from_author` for a `tco_removal` and for `section_c`, `preserve` for a `tco_removal`. `legal_orders_reference_key` answers `order_exists` when the corrected state, authority and reference are another order's.
5. What follows for an order that is given effect, each in a statement of its own (before 0026 no order is given effect, so this step has nothing to do until Task 3):
   - `section_c` became true: every `order` decision of the order, every correction of one, and every other told decision about the same posts that was made after them (the ones step 10 of `decisions_record` would have held, 6.3) is untold again (`informed_at` null, `author_mail` `none`). A mail not yet sent is not sent, the notice leaves the author's screens, and `order_notices_release` tells it again at the hold's end. What was already read cannot be taken back; the event's time records when korbat stopped saying it.
   - `section_c` became false while a decision of the order is untold: `hold_ended_at = now()`, `hold_end_reason = 'not_ordered'` and the event `hold_ended` with that ground; the next dispatch round tells the author, and the notice says that the silence was korbat's mistake (section 18).
   - `preserve` became true (the kind became `tco_removal`): every item whose measure stands and whose `preserve_until` is null gets `order_preserve_until` of its decision's `decided_at`. It became false: every running preservation gets `preserve_ended_at = now()` and the event `preservation_ended`; nothing is erased.
   - `withhold_from_author` changed: the `withhold_author` of the order's measures in force follows it, and `moderation_apply` derives the level of their posts again (`disabled` or `removed`).
6. It inserts the event `corrected` with the ground of step 2 and `via = 'console'`, and answers its id. The events of step 5 are written with `via = 'system'`.

The route then writes the audit row `order_corrected`, whose metadata holds `rule` (the event's ground), `changed` (the names of the changed members with their old and new values, never a text; a closed item as its id and reason) and `eventId`: through `append_confirmed_audit` for the ground `admin`, and as a plain audit row otherwise. `legal_order_events_confirmed` refuses the commit without it. After the commit every staff account is alerted (`order_corrected`, 6.1, through `staffAlert`): a correction of an order's record, above all one made without a key, is never silent. The decisions already made keep what they recorded (their category and keyword are not rewritten: the ledger is append-only), and `text-verify` compares an order's decisions with its `order_effected` row and the `order_corrected` rows since (section 8).

**`append_confirmed_audit`** (as 3e leaves it, which is as 3c built it), replaced: its action list gains `order_event_recorded`, `order_corrected`, `order_effected`, `gap_closed` and `audit_exported`. Nothing else changes. (The model lists this under 0026; Task 2's events and corrections need the first two names, and one replacement serves all five. The third is written only by Task 3's route and the last two only by Task 6's.)

**`alert_steps()`**, replaced: 6.1's rows marked 0025.

### 6.3 Migration 0026: the ledger (Task 3)

Hand-written; it changes no table. Each function below is replaced as 3e leaves it; only what changes is written.

**`measures_in_force`**, `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW`, same columns: the kind list gains `or a.kind::text = 'disable_by_order'`. Its decision is never interim, so the measure is in force until a row reverses it, whatever its case does.

**`moderation_apply`.** A post's level becomes the first of these that applies:

| Level | When |
|---|---|
| `disabled` | the post is not erased, and a `disable_by_order` in force whose `withhold_author` is true lists it |
| `removed` | the post is erased; or a `remove` is in force; or a `disable_by_order` is in force (one that does not withhold from the author, or one on an erased post) |
| `withheld` | a `withhold` is in force |
| `visible` | otherwise |

- `posts.state` mirrors the level through text (`v.level::text::public.post_state`), as built, and never moves to or from `deleted_by_author`.
- Image states do not change: no reader shows an image of a `disabled` post, whatever the image's own state.
- 3e's three Explore columns follow the level: a move between two levels that are not `visible` writes none of them (3e 6.4).
- So the result does not depend on the order in which a `remove`, a `disable_by_order` and their reversals arrive (LAB C, both orders), and a reinstated post that korbat also removed under a rule stays `removed`.

**`decisions_record`**: a branch for the kind `order`, and edits to six steps.

- **Step 2**, the new branch. `order_item_id` names an item; in this order:
  1. the item's order, its row `FOR SHARE` (section 5, the lock order, item 2), then read in a statement of its own;
  2. `invalid_decision` unless: the order is not closed and is not a `dsa_art10`; the case is an open post case whose target is the item's `post_id`; the outcome is `restricted`; the ground is `illegal`; and all three text digests are null;
  3. `invalid_decision` unless the item's `intake_item_id` names an intake item of this case, about this post, of an intake of the kind `order` (compared as text), that is unanswered. The API's role may write that column, so the trigger does not trust it: pointed at a reporter's unanswered item, the decision would otherwise answer a notice korbat never weighed;
  4. `invalid_decision` (detail "already given effect") when the item's measure stands.

  Only an `order` decision names an item (`invalid_decision` otherwise). A correction may reverse an `order` decision as it may an interim or a final one; its outcome is then always `reversed`, since an order decision holds one measure (`decision_holds` (b)). **The correction of an `order` decision takes that decision's row `FOR SHARE`** and reads it again in a statement of its own (the built text reads the corrected decision without a lock, `0016:261`): it either waits for a release that is telling that decision and is then told at once, or the release's second step waits for it and its third step sees it (below).
- **Step 5.** An `order` decision considers its own intake item alone, which must be unanswered. A final or an interim decision never considers an item of an `order` intake. A correction of an `order` decision considers the item that decision answered. Recusal (`staff_party_conflict`) is not asked for an `order` decision or for the correction of one (row 13): a reinstatement leaves no more discretion than the disabling did, and "shall immediately reinstate" must not wait for a second admin.
- **Step 6.** `needs_admin` is false for an `order` decision and true for the correction of one. `suspect` and `unseen` are false for an `order` decision. **A `dsa_art9` order is verified before effect** (row 12): no clock forces the night for this kind, and no register lists who may issue it. So an `order` decision on a `dsa_art9` order whose `verified_at` is null has `needs_admin` true, and is refused `order_not_verified`, in place of `admin_required`, when the decider is not an admin. An admin may give effect to an urgent one before the check is recorded, which `decision_holds` ties to the signed member `urgent` (below).
- **Step 7.** The two counts of the decision caps leave `order` decisions out, and an `order` decision is not refused by them. It is refused `order_cap` when the deciding account made `decisions_per_day` order decisions in the last 24 hours, counted under the same advisory lock.
- **Step 8** needs no change: the order's intake alleges illegality (below).
- **Step 9.** `source` is `order`.
- **Step 10.** Four changes.
  1. "A final decision that closes its case" is one that leaves no unanswered item above its `through_seq`, where the built text tests `through_seq = intake_seq`: an answered order item may lie above the range. (The model names this for `decisions_apply` only.)
  2. For an `order` decision: `through_seq` 0; for a `tco_removal` the category `STATEMENT_CATEGORY_RISK_FOR_PUBLIC_SECURITY` with `KEYWORD_TERRORIST_CONTENT`, for a `dsa_art9` the category `STATEMENT_CATEGORY_NOT_SPECIFIED_NOTICE` and no keyword, both forced (korbat does not assess what an order names and classifies nothing an authority ordered, and the decision is never exported); `informed_at` null and `author_mail` `none` while the notice is held (`order_hold_until(order) > now()`), else `informed_at = decided_at` and `author_mail` `pending`.
  3. The correction of an `order` decision that is untold (`informed_at` null) is untold too: `informed_at` null, `author_mail` `none`.
  4. **Nothing reaches an author while a notice is held, on any path.** Any other decision of any kind (interim, final, correction, review) that the built text would tell its author (`author_mail` `pending`), on a post case whose target has an untold `order` decision whose measure is in force, is untold too: `informed_at` null, `author_mail` `none`. The test is a statement of its own after the case's lock. A decision the built text tells nobody of keeps `informed_at = decided_at` and `author_mail` `none`, so an untold decision is always one that is owed a telling, and the release can tell it. Why: korbat's own statement about such a post cannot be both true and silent. A `remove` under a rule on a disabled post has `no_effect` `already`, and the built statement then says "Another decision already keeps this post from being shown" (`packages/rules/src/statement.ts:50`); a reversal on appeal says that if the post is still restricted "another decision listed in Settings, Cases applies", where none is listed. The text is therefore held with the notice. The reporter's own outcome line moves as today: it names no order. The cost is row 47's: an author's appeal that is answered during a hold is answered to them only at the release. Its time to appeal counts from the release (`appeals_check`, below).

**The order's intake.** The effect transaction inserts one intake for the action: kind `order`, channel `authority`, `alleges_illegal` true, no reporter, no contact, no rule, not `csam`, the category the decisions will carry, and one item a post at lane 0. `intakes_insert` gives it the receipt `none`, and the attach trigger makes no item of it suspect and gives each the outcome mail `none`. So nothing of 3c's notifier paths has anyone to tell: the daily release marks such an item released and no mail, status page or list shows it.

**`decisions_apply`.**

- For an `order` decision, in this order: it answers its own item as `restricted_legal` (`invalid_decision` unless exactly one row was written); it recomputes the lane, the oldest open time and the closing from the items left; it moves `state_version` and `last_decided_at`; it sets `answered_seq = intake_seq` only when no item is left, which closes the case; for a case it closed it calls `moderation_apply`.
- For a `final` decision, `answered_seq` becomes `intake_seq` when the decision closes the case and `through_seq` otherwise (`cases_closed_answered` asks for equality).

**`decision_actions_check`.**

- **Step 2**: `withhold_author` is cleared, and `order_ground` on every kind but `reinstate_by_order`. Both new kinds are post kinds (`post_id` is the case's target). `is_measure` gains `disable_by_order`.
- **Step 3**: an `order` decision holds `disable_by_order` and nothing else; no other decision kind holds it. `reinstate_by_order` only in a correction, with an `order_ground` (`invalid_decision` otherwise).
- **Step 6**: `reinstate_by_order` lifts `disable_by_order`.
- **Step 8**: `staff_target` does not refuse an `order` decision (row 13).
- **Step 9**: `reinstate_by_order` marks the decision `needs_admin` and refuses a decider who is not an admin (`admin_required`), as `unseal_media` does.
- **New, for `disable_by_order`**: `withhold_author` is forced to the order's `withhold_from_author`.
- **Step 10**, `no_effect`: for `disable_by_order`, `deleted` for a deleted post, and `already` when the level is `disabled`, or is `removed` and the measure does not withhold from the author. A `remove` on a `disabled` post is `already` too.

**`decision_actions_apply`.** The built first step derives the post's level for both new kinds, since they name a post. Added:

- **`disable_by_order`**, one update of the decision's item and one event. The trigger reads the item's row first, before it clears anything, because the row tells two cases apart:
  - **An earlier preservation of the item has ended** (`preserve_ended_at` is not null: by a reinstatement, or by 6.4 step 3). The post may have been public in between, so this is a new disabling: when the order preserves, `preserve_until = order_preserve_until(now())`, whatever the column held. Six months run from the new disabling, never from the first (TCO Art. 6(2) counts from "the removal or disabling"). What an earlier end erased stays erased.
  - **No preservation has ended** (`preserve_ended_at` is null: a first effect, or one taken again after a restore on an item whose preservation never ended). `preserve_until` is set only when it is null, so a measure taken again after a restore starts no new period (row 38); a kept end that has already passed is not renewed, and the next retention round ends the preservation (6.4).
  - In both cases: `decision_id` and `action_id` become this decision and this row; `reinstated_at` and `preserve_ended_at` become null; `disabled_at` is set only when it is null, since it is the first disabling and Annex II and the clock's `first_effect_at` and `last_effect_at` read it; `closed_at` and `closed_reason` become null (an item given effect is no longer a closed one).
  - the event `effect_given`, by the deciding account.
- **`reinstate_by_order`**: the item whose `action_id` is the lifted measure gets `reinstated_at` and `preserve_ended_at` (`now()`); the event `reinstated` with the measure's ground.
- The built last step marks no item for a correction of an `order` decision: it reads final decisions only, and an order's item has no sender.

**`decision_holds`.**

- **For an `order` decision** the check of (a) is this, and (b) is "exactly one `disable_by_order` and no other row":
  1. the transaction's head audit row is `order_effected` on the decision's order (`target_type` `order`), carries a step-up, and was written by the deciding account;
  2. `metadata.decisionIds`, an object from item id to decision id, names this decision under its item; and every pair in it names an `order` decision of this transaction on that item;
  3. `params` without `items` and `urgent` equals the order's row under the contracts' names: `kind`, `memberState`, `authority`, `reference`, `sectionC`, `emergency`, `withholdFromAuthor`. Since the row's `withhold_from_author` is forced true with `section_c`, signed params that say "must not be told" and "not withheld" equal no row and are refused;
  4. `params.items` lists `{ itemId, postId }` for this item with the case's target, has as many entries as `decisionIds`, and at most `items_per_action`;
  5. for a `dsa_art9` order whose `verified_at` is null (read under the order's row lock, which `decisions_record` took): `params.urgent` is `true`, else `order_not_verified`. For every other order (a `tco_removal`, a verified `dsa_art9`) the member is absent or false, else `invalid_decision`: the word is never signed where it means nothing.

  So one audit row covers up to ten decisions of one transaction, nothing it does not name, and nothing it names is missing (LAB D); and an admin who gives effect to an unverified `dsa_art9` order has signed that it was urgent, which the alert `order_effected` then says.
- **For every other decision** the comparison stands. An action's signed form gains `orderGround` when the row has one. When the audit row's `metadata` holds a member `target`, it must equal `{ type, id }` of the decision's case's target (`invalid_decision` otherwise); the decide and review routes write it from Task 3 on, so that after a restore a lost decision's audit row says what it was about even when its case is lost too (6.6). The model assumed that a decision's audit row already held this.

**`posts_guard`.** One clause is added: for every role but the migrator, a post whose level is `disabled` moves to `deleted_by_author` only when its author's profile is deleted (`profiles.deleted_at` is set); otherwise `moderation_column`.
