So the author's own deletion of a disabled post is refused in the database as the route refuses it (LAB C: without the clause the API role's update went through), and the purge, which writes its profile row before its tombstones (section 5), still tombstones every post. A tombstone is terminal as before: a later reinstatement changes the level and leaves the state `deleted_by_author` (OL E20.3: nothing asks korbat to republish what its author deleted).

**`appeals_check`** (as 3e leaves it), replaced: before its other tests, an appeal against a decision of the kind `order` is refused `not_appealable`. As 3c 6.3 designs it, the function would refuse it `appeal_not_open` (the decision is not `final`), which the app words as a window that has closed. **`decision_final_at`** needs no change: as 3c 6.4 designs it, every decision that is not a `final` restriction is final at its `decided_at`, an `order` decision among them. Task 3 checks both in the built text and adds the tests either way. **A held decision of korbat's own (step 10 of `decisions_record`) gives its time to appeal from its `informed_at`, so from the release:** 3c's `appeals_check` and `decision_final_at` both read `appeal_deadline(informed_at)`, which is null while the decision is untold, so until then no appeal is open, the decision is not final and nothing is redacted on it; Task 3 checks this in the built text too, and tests it. 3c's due rule for a case's evidence then treats an order's case like any closed case, and gate E keeps the snapshot of a preserved post.

**`public.order_notices_release() returns integer`**, PL/pgSQL, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: the writer of a decision's `informed_at` after its insert (the only other one is `order_correct`, which can only untell). It answers how many decisions it told, in both of its steps. In this order:

1. The rows of the orders that have an untold `order` decision, `FOR SHARE`, in id order.
2. In a statement of its own: the untold `order` decisions whose order holds nothing (`order_hold_until` is null) or whose `order_hold_until(order) <= clock_timestamp()`, `FOR UPDATE` in id order. Each gets `informed_at = clock_timestamp()`, read after the test, so the stored time is never before the end (`now()`, the transaction's start, could be), and `author_mail = 'pending'`; its item gets the event `notice_released`, `via` `system`.
3. In a statement of its own, **which runs in every call, also when step 2 told nothing**: every untold decision that is not an `order` decision (a correction of one, or a decision of korbat's own that step 10 of `decisions_record` held), on a post case whose target has no untold `order` decision any more, gets the same two values. So a reinstatement that committed beside a release is told by the next round at the latest, ten minutes on, and never stays untold for good. A held decision of korbat's own waits for every `order` decision about its post, also one whose measure was lifted meanwhile, because its recorded statement may name "another decision"; until then its restriction, where it now shows, shows its author no chip (a known limit, as long as the hold).

A decision released to an account that no longer exists is set to `pending` like any other and given up by the mailer, since `mailAccount` then has no address (the release's test covers it).

**The commands that move a hold** (`order-issued`, `order-hold-until`, `order-hold-extend`, `order-hold-end`) take the order's row `FOR UPDATE` first and read the rest after it.

- They are refused `already_released` only when the order has `section_c` and an event `notice_released` of the order exists, or `hold_ended_at` is set. An order that holds nothing takes `order-issued` at any time: its decisions were told at once, and its time stamp is still owed.
- `order-issued` refuses, unless `--release` is given, an instant whose computed end is not in the future while a notice of the order is held, and prints the end it would have set: a time stamp typed a year early must not tell an author within ten minutes. The CHECK `legal_orders_issued` refuses one after the receipt (6.2).
- `order-hold-extend` is a `tco_removal`'s, once. `order-hold-until` on a DSA order records the end with its basis (`hold_basis`), earlier or later than any earlier record, and is that kind's only way to give a hold an end.
- An extension before a release is seen by step 2; one after it is refused.

**The authority's reasons reach the author with a mail and a recorded time.** A time limit of the author's runs from receiving them (the 48 hours of TCO Art. 4(4); a court's deadline in the issuing state). So the command that records the texts (`order-texts`), as the migrator and in its own transaction, sets `author_mail` to `pending` again on every told `order` decision of the order, with the event `texts_recorded`; the dispatch round then mails the author that korbat has added to a notice (the mail `order_notice_added`: a decision with `author_mail_at` set gets that mail, not the first notice again). The recorded time is the newest such event's `at`, or the decision's `informed_at` when that is later (a notice released after the texts were recorded), and the notice prints it (the contracts' `orderTexts.textsAt`). No column stores it a second time: the event is the record. Texts that are still missing are counted by the daily `order_unverified` (6.1), so "they follow" has an end.

**`public.post_preserved(post uuid) returns boolean`**, a new STABLE SQL function, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: `exists (select 1 from public.legal_order_items oi where oi.post_id = post_preserved.post and oi.preserve_until > now() and oi.preserve_ended_at is null)`, and nothing else. It is the one place the test is written. **`frozen_for`** gains a fourth arm: `or public.post_preserved(frozen_for.post)`. Both are SQL functions and name no enum value. The name `frozen_for` stays, and from here on "frozen" means frozen or preserved:

- gate K keeps a preserved post's image rows against every delete, the purge's included;
- gate E keeps its snapshots against `erase_case_evidence` and makes `redact_r4_body` wait (`erase_snapshots` takes the freeze from `post_frozen`);
- gate V opens none of its images for staff;
- a final decision on a case about a preserved post is `unseen`, as on any frozen post (3c 6.2 step 6).

**`snapshot_withheld` is not replaced.** It keeps its one meaning, "this copy's content was erased", because the author's side reads it too: `authorDecision` selects it for the decision the author opens (`moderation/author-repo.ts:135`) and `contentOf` words "withheld and not erased" as an R4 redaction (`moderation/author.ts:79`). With a preservation arm, every earlier decision about a preserved post would tell its author "Content erased when the R4 decision became final", at the moment of the effect and under a hold. So preservation has a predicate of its own, for the one reader that needs it: the staff case page's snapshot read (`moderation/repo.ts:696`) adds `or public.post_preserved(s.target_id)` for a post snapshot, and shows no snapshot text of a preserved post. It does not use `post_frozen`, which would also take the text of every post under a suspect item from the moderators who must read it. An earlier decision's screen on the author's side shows what it showed before the order, which under a hold is the only answer that tells nothing (row 47, with its flag for counsel).

**`alert_steps()`**, replaced: 6.1's row marked 0026 (`hold_ending`).

**Not replaced.** `snapshot_withheld` (above). `item_current_outcome`, `release_outcomes` and `case_history` read an order's rows as they read any; `strike_due` and 3e's bases read `final` decisions only, so an order is never a strike and never the basis of a suspension: it is no finding of korbat's.

**What every reader does with the level** (the code is section 7's; the architecture test pins each).

| Reader | Rule |
|---|---|
| `postAccess` (`social/visibility.ts`) | `StoredPostState` gains `disabled`, whose case answers `hidden` for every viewer; the file does not compile until it does |
| `serve` (`media/flows/serve.ts`) | no change: `isShown` refuses a post state it was not taught, for the author too |
| `reach`'s `served` (`media/repo.ts`) | gains `p.state <> 'disabled'` |
| the author's side (`moderation/author-repo.ts`), the chip's `chipFacts` included | reads only decisions with `author_mail <> 'none' and informed_at is not null` (`toldToAuthor`): both halves, since every decision of 3c has `informed_at` set, also those whose author is told nothing |
| the export | leaves out a `disabled` post, its files and every untold decision |
| the access draft (`access-request`, read by the operator alone) | leaves the same out of what is sent, and marks the case for the operator: a held notice, its end, and that the reason is noted in the operator's records, not in the answer |
| `deletePost` | answers `not_found` |
| a profile's counters | do not move when a post is disabled or reinstated |
| `text-verify` | recomputes the level with the table above |
| the console's case page | shows the measure, and no image or snapshot text while the post is preserved (`post_preserved` in its snapshot read; gate V for the images) |
| the author's screen of an earlier decision about the post | unchanged: `snapshot_withheld` has no preservation arm, so the content line reads as it did before the order. The post's images on that screen are served to nobody, the author included (`serve`, above), and drawn as an image that could not be loaded, with no word of why (section 7's table) |

### 6.4 Migration 0027: preservation and retention (Task 4)

Hand-written; it changes no table. Every function is SECURITY DEFINER with EXECUTE to `korbat_api` alone; the retention job calls them (section 7).

**`public.order_preservation_end(item_id uuid) returns boolean`.** The end of a preservation, and the only erasure 3f makes. In this order:

1. The locks of section 5 (the lock order, item 3): the case of the item's decision, the item, the post, its image rows. The item comes before the post because the effect transaction holds an item's row (it writes `intake_item_id`) before it locks any post. An imported item whose measure was never taken again has no decision; then the item, the post and its images.
2. In statements of their own: `read_committed_only` unless the isolation is READ COMMITTED; `not_due` unless the item's `preserve_until <= now()` and its `preserve_ended_at` is null.
3. When the item's measure does not stand: `preserve_ended_at = now()` and the event `preservation_ended`. Nothing is erased. It answers true.
4. When `post_frozen(post)` is still true (an unanswered suspect item, a seal in force, or another order's item that still preserves the post): it changes nothing and answers false. The job tries again at its next round, as it does for `redact_r4_body`.
5. Otherwise, while the measure stands:
   1. `preserve_ended_at = now()`;
   2. `posts.body = ''` and `posts.erased_at = now()`; every image row of the post gets `erased_at = now()`;
   3. every unerased snapshot whose target is the post, in this case and in any other, through `erase_snapshots`;
   4. the case's holds on those images are released (`release_case_holds`);
   5. `moderation_apply(array[post], true)`: an erased post's level is `removed`, for good;
   6. the event `preservation_ended`, `via` `system`. It answers true.

A later reinstatement lifts the measure in the ledger and restores nothing, which the notice says (section 18). A removal order's hold has always ended by then: `hold_days + hold_extension_days` is 85 days, and the shortest preservation is six months (the constants test pins the inequality). A DSA order's hold may be longer, and a DSA order preserves nothing.

**An extension** is the command line's update of `preserve_until` to a later time, under the item's row lock, on an item whose preservation has not ended, with the event `preservation_extended`; the request's five facts (who asked, their reference, the proceedings, the further end, when it was received) are the metadata of the command's audit row.

**`public.order_records_due() returns table (order_id uuid)`**, STABLE: the orders whose newest event is older than `record_keep_years`, none of whose items has a measure that stands or a preservation that has not ended, and about none of whose items' posts a decision is untold (the order's own decisions, their corrections, and korbat's own decisions held with them: 6.3).

**`public.erase_order_records(order_id uuid) returns integer`**: the order's row `FOR UPDATE`; `not_due` unless `order_records_due()` names it, read after the lock; then it deletes the order's events, its items (a decision's `order_item_id` becomes null: the foreign key is `on delete set null`), the order's row with its texts, and its message when no other order names it. The ledger's rows and the audit log's stay (3c 6.6): a decision of the kind `order` then says only that an order was given effect, and when.

**`public.delete_contact_messages(older_than timestamptz) returns integer`**: raises unless `older_than <= now() - message_keep_days`; deletes the messages handled before `older_than` that no order names, and the outbox rows created before it, except the rows whose subject is still open: a message without a disposition, or an order that is neither closed nor given effect in full. (Deleting those would let `alert_steps()` meet an old threshold again and write a new `order_due` at priority 5 every 30 days.) An unhandled message is never deleted, a stale one included: the row is the record that a message arrived and nobody looked at it. The reviews differ here (one asks for this sentence, one for deleting a stale row 30 days after it was seen); this is the side that destroys nothing, and what it costs is that the stale rows a changed UIDVALIDITY makes stay on the Contact point screen until a person closes them, which the console lets a person do for all of them together (section 8).

### 6.5 Migration 0028: records to authorities (Task 5)

**No grant to the API role.** `authority_notifications`, `authority_notification_events` and `productions` get `REVOKE ALL ... FROM korbat_api` and no policy for it: only the command line, as the migrator, reads and writes them, and the definer functions below answer the two questions the console and 3d may ask (row 31). No column holds an address, a handle or a value that was sent; an account is named by its id.

**`authority_notifications`**: one row a notification that is owed, sent or declined.

| Columns | Notes |
|---|---|
| `id uuid` primary key; `state text not null` | `owed`, `sent` or `declined` |
| `duties text[] not null` | of `dsa18_1`, `dsa18_2`, `tco14_5`, `voluntary`; empty while a seal's record is only owed |
| `case_id uuid`, `seal_action_id uuid`, `account_id uuid` | the case; for sealed images the seal's ledger row; the account concerned. No foreign keys: the record outlives a purge |
| `opened_at timestamptz not null`, `opened_via text not null` | forced `now()`, except that a row with `imported_at` keeps a supplied time that is not after the clock; `system` (a seal) or `cli` |
| `first_report_at`, `admin_answer_at timestamptz` | awareness: the first report's time and the admin's answer's |
| `checked_by text`, `checked_at`, `check_result boolean`, `check_reason text` | the check: who, when, yes or no, why (at most 500 characters) |
| `state_concerned text`, `state_basis text` | an ISO code and why that state; or null and the reason it is not identifiable |
| `authority text`, `channel text`, `sent_at`, `receipt text`, `sent_by text` | where it went, when, the channel's receipt, who sent it |
| `own_reference text`, `their_reference text` | korbat's reference and the authority's |
| `sent_kinds text[]` | what was sent, by kind, never by value: of `handle`, `address`, `created_at`, `post_ids`, `post_times`, `report_time`, `files`. One list, a constant of the contracts with this CHECK (section 10) |
| `not_kept_version smallint` | which fixed sentence of what korbat does not keep went with it (section 18) |
| `answer text`, `answered_at` | the authority's answer, one of `not_illegal`, `illegal`, `no_assessment` or `other`, and its date. A closed list, not a text: the record holds no sentence about a person |
| `declined_reason text`, `closed_at`, `imported_at` | `unsealed` when the database closed it; else the operator's reason |

**`authority_notification_events`**: later requests and answers. `id`, `notification_id` (references the record, `on delete cascade`), `kind text` (`request`, `answer` or `note`), `at` (forced, except that an event of an imported record keeps a supplied time that is not after the clock), `occurred_at`, `reference text`, `not_to_person boolean not null`, `not_to_person_until timestamptz` (null with the mark set: an open end).

**The forced times and the import.** Every writer of these three tables is the migrator, so their forcing is unconditional but for that one case: the import after a restore inserts a record again with its original times (6.6), and a record forced to "opened today" would make `report_owed` wait another 24 hours and start the three years again.

**`productions`**: one row a production (row 34).

| Columns | Notes |
|---|---|
| `id uuid` primary key; `instrument text not null` | `notification`, `demand`, `court_order`, `seizure`, `dsa_art10` or `preserved_access` |
| `notification_id`, `order_id`, `order_item_id`, `seal_action_id`, `account_id uuid` | what it belongs to; no foreign keys |
| `purpose text` | for `preserved_access` only: `review`, `complaint` or `authority` (TCO Art. 6(1)) |
| `authority text`, `own_reference text`, `their_reference text` | a recipient is an authority |
| `asked text[]`, `produced text[] not null`, `not_held text[]` | what the order or demand asked for, what was produced, and what korbat does not hold, each by kind. `asked` is a list of kinds and never a sentence: its values are the closed list of a notification's `sent_kinds` above (the seven, the same constant of the contracts, with its CHECK), since the table holds no free text about a person. What korbat holds and did not produce here (the times and device types of the account's sign-ins) is the fixed sentence's second half (section 18) |
| `files jsonb`, `archive_sha256 text` | a hand-over: each file's name, size and SHA-256, and the archive's |
| `channel text`, `receipt text`, `delivered_at` | |
| `destroyed_at`, `destroyed_how text` | the destruction of every copy; `taken by the authority` for a `seizure` |
| `not_to_person_until timestamptz`, `opened_at` (forced, as a notification's), `recorded_by text`, `imported_at` | |

A production for a `dsa_art10` order is refused by its command (`tell_rule_missing`) until the order's `tell_person` is recorded (6.2, by `order-tell-rule`): what the person is told, and when, is a property of the order, and its cautious default is to ask before producing.

**`auth.report_holds`**: `user_id uuid` primary key, `set_at timestamptz not null`, `seal_final boolean not null` (false while only a seal taken while deciding holds the record, true once a seal for good does: the deletion mail's sentence differs, section 18), `released_at`, `release_reason text` (`sent`, `declined` or `expired`). `REVOKE ALL ... FROM korbat_auth`: the trigger below and the command line write it. It holds no address: the address stays where it is, in `auth.users`.

**Functions**, SECURITY DEFINER.

- **`public.report_open()`**, the function of the trigger `decision_actions_report`, AFTER INSERT ON `decision_actions` (it fires after `decision_actions_apply`, by name), `search_path = public, auth, pg_temp`. **First, for every row it handles, it takes the subject account's row of `auth.report_holds` `FOR UPDATE`**, inserting it as released when there is none, and reads the records in a statement of its own after that lock. The commands `notification-sent` and `notification-declined` take the same row first. So a second seal about the account either sees the record still `owed`, or sees it `sent` and opens a new one: it never reads `owed` while a command is committing `sent`, which would leave the new seal with no record and no hold.
  - **For a `seal_media` row**, taken while deciding or for good, the first of these that applies:
    1. the subject account has a record in the state `owed`: nothing but the hold's `seal_final`, which becomes true when this seal is for good;
    2. `report_close_unsealed` closed a record of the account in this transaction (`declined_reason` `unsealed`, `closed_at = now()`): that record is `owed` again and its hold unreleased, with their first times. This is the seal for good that the admin's decision takes after its answer ended the seal taken while deciding;
    3. otherwise a new record (`owed`, the case, the seal's row, the account, `first_report_at` from the case's earliest suspect item) and the account's hold (`set_at = now()`, `seal_final` by the seal; a released row is set again).
  - **For an `unseal_media` row** (a correction or a review that lifts a seal): after `decision_actions_apply` has derived the states, it calls `report_close_unsealed` for the case. A seal that korbat itself found mistaken thus leaves no record owed, no daily alert and no address held.
- **`public.report_close_unsealed(case_id uuid) returns integer`**, EXECUTE to nobody. For the case's subject account: when no `seal_media` of that account is in force, every `owed` record of the account that a seal opened becomes `declined` with the reason `unsealed`, and the account's hold is released (`declined`). It has two callers: `decisions_apply`, for a final decision that rests on a suspect item, after it has answered the items; and `report_open`, for an `unseal_media` row. **`decisions_apply`** is replaced once more for that call and nothing else.
- **`public.reports_owed() returns table (case_id uuid, kind text, since timestamptz)`**, STABLE, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: the `owed` records; `kind` is `sealed_images` for a seal's record and its first duty otherwise; `since` is `opened_at`. It is everything the console learns.
- **`public.seal_report(action uuid) returns table (reported boolean, their_reference text, answered_at timestamptz)`**, STABLE, EXECUTE to nobody yet: for 3d's release.
- **`auth.address_held_until(account uuid) returns timestamptz`**, STABLE, EXECUTE to `korbat_auth`: null when nothing is held; otherwise the instant the hold ends at the latest, `set_at + report_hold_days`, while the account's hold is not released and that instant is ahead by `clock_timestamp()`. The deletion mail prints its day: the auth module, which writes the mail, cannot read `set_at` itself. **`auth.address_held(account uuid) returns boolean`**, the same grant: "that is not null". **`auth.address_hold_final(account uuid) returns boolean`**, the same grant: the hold's `seal_final`, by which the mail picks its sentence. The command line's `notification-sent` and `notification-declined` release the hold; after seven days it releases nothing by itself and simply reads as not held.
- **`finishPurge` is two statements** while `REPORT_HOLD` is on. The first clears every credential and personal fact but the address, without condition (the password hash, the TOTP secret, the recovery codes, the phone hash, the age and terms marks). The second overwrites the address and sets `purged_at` only `where not auth.address_held(id)`. One update with the condition on all of it would keep the credentials for seven days; the hold is for the address alone.
- **`public.erase_authority_records() returns integer`**, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: deletes each notification record whose newest time (opened, sent, answered, closed, or an event's) is older than `record_keep_years` and whose seal is not in force; each production that old whose files, if it has any, are recorded as destroyed; and each hold that is released or expired and whose account has no record left. **It keeps what something still reads:** a notification record while one of its events has `not_to_person` set and `not_to_person_until` null or ahead, and a production while its `not_to_person_until` is ahead. An authority's "not to the person" outlives the three years: `access-request` reads the mark from the record (section 7), and without the record the next answer to that person would tell.
- **`alert_steps()`**, replaced: 6.1's two rows marked 0028. Both count rows and name none.

### 6.6 Migration 0029: recovery (Task 6)

**What an audit row must hold.** The off-server copy is the audit log's tail (row 36), so the import can re-create only what the rows say. Every write of 3f therefore puts into its audit row's metadata every column that the console or the command line supplied, with the ids of the rows it made, and never a text: an order's reasons, redress and scope texts, a verification's wording and a decision's texts are named by digest and stay lost with a lost row. A decision's row names its case's target (`metadata.target`, 6.3) and, when it took a lost measure again, the gap it closed (`metadata.gapId`): the first classes a lost row, the second says which gap a decision closed, and section 10's table lists both. A console event's row names its event (`metadata.eventId`, 6.2).

**A link is not copied into the chain.** A link "as named" may hold a handle (`/u/<handle>`) or any text an outsider put into an address, and audit rows stay for good and leave the server daily in the keeper's file. So the metadata of an order's rows names an item by its `postId` when the link resolved, and by the SHA-256 and the length of the link when it did not (`linkSha256`, `linkLength`); the item's row holds the text for its three years, and the chain proves it. After a restore the import writes a re-created item's `link` as the post's address, or as `sha256:<digest>` for a link that named no post. A reference is copied as typed, and may name a person (a court's file): the runbook says so of the keeper's file.

**`restore_gaps`**: one row for each lost decision and each lost record that an import found.

| Column | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `id` | `uuid` primary key | |
| `import_id` | `uuid not null` | one value an import |
| `place`, `hash` | `bigint not null`, `text not null` | the lost audit row's place in the keeper's file and its hash |
| `action` | `text not null` | the lost row's action (`case_decided`, `order_effected`, ...) |
| `target_type`, `target_id` | `text` | the lost row's target. An `order_effected` row gives one gap an item, with `order_item` and the item's id |
| `metadata` | `jsonb` | the lost row's metadata, whole |
| `acted_at` | `timestamptz not null` | when the lost row was written |
| `class` | `text not null` | `order_effect`, `seal_final`, `seal_interim`, `standing`, `restriction`, `reversal`, `no_measure` or `record` |
| `closed_at`, `closed_by`, `disposition` | `timestamptz`, `uuid`, `text` | `closed_by` is the decision that took the measure again; the disposition is `reapplied`, `not_needed` or `imported` |

- Unique `(import_id, place, target_id)`. Index `(class) where closed_at is null`.
- The class is the import's reading of the lost row: `order_effected` is `order_effect`; a decision's row is `seal_final` or `seal_interim` when its signed actions hold a `seal_media` (by the decision's kind), else `standing` for a `suspend` or `terminate`, else `restriction` for any other measure, else `reversal` when it holds reversals only, else `no_measure`. A row that describes a plain record is `record`; the import inserts that record again and writes its gap closed, with the disposition `imported`. A lost record that the import could only note (a later step of a notification record, a production) is `record` too and stays an open gap: the operator enters the record again with the notification or production command that wrote it and `--gap <gap>` (section 8), and that command closes the gap in its own transaction, as the migrator, with the disposition `imported`; it refuses a gap of another class and one that is not open.
- The order of row 38 is the order of the classes as listed here.
- Grants. `korbat_api`: SELECT. The import writes it as the migrator, and `gap_close` as its owner; the third writer is the command line's notification or production command given `--gap`, as the migrator, which closes an open gap of the class `record` as `imported` and writes nothing else of the row.

**`copy_marks`**: `id bigint` identity primary key, `staff_id uuid not null`, `place bigint not null` (the newest place the download held), `at timestamptz not null` (forced `clock_timestamp()`). `korbat_api`: SELECT, INSERT.

- **The download is a confirmed action** (`audit_exported`, in `append_confirmed_audit`'s list, 6.2): it holds every row's metadata, which the audit page never shows, and so needs a key, not only a session. The export route itself writes that one row, the confirmed audit row `audit_exported`, whose signed params name the places it releases, and no mark: a key's confirmation is used once, so the route that takes the step-up writes the confirmed row, and that row records a download also when its file never arrived. Every staff account is alerted (`copy_taken`) when anyone but the last downloader takes one.
- **The mark is the keeper's confirmation, not the stream's end.** When the console holds the whole body and has checked that its lines run without a break, it posts the last line's place and hash; the mark route inserts the mark, with the plain audit row `copy_marked`, only when that place holds that hash and the caller's own newest `audit_exported` row signed that place (`copy_mismatch` otherwise; section 7). It needs no step-up. A download that broke off on the way to the keeper leaves no mark, so `copy_overdue` stays true and the next download starts where the last whole one ended.
- `staff.js audit-export` writes its mark itself, with `copy_marked`, since its output goes to a file on the server, under the system actor's id (`SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID`, `apps/api/src/cli/staff.ts:32`); "the caller's newest mark" never matches that id.

**`public.gap_close(gap uuid, decision uuid, by_staff uuid) returns void`**, PL/pgSQL, SECURITY DEFINER (`search_path = public, auth, pg_temp`), EXECUTE to `korbat_api`. (The model's signature has two parameters; the third is needed where no decision closes the gap. Every caller passes the session's account.) It raises one code of its own, `gap_mismatch`, for a gap that is not open and for a decision that does not fit it. In this order:

1. The gap's row `FOR UPDATE`. `gap_mismatch` when it is closed.
2. **With a decision** (the measure was taken again): the decision was inserted by this transaction (the `xact` and `decided_at` pair of `0016:513`) and fits the gap, else `gap_mismatch`:
   - `order_effect`: an `order` decision whose `order_item_id` is the gap's item;
   - `seal_final`, `seal_interim`: a final, or an interim, decision that holds a `seal_media`;
   - `standing`: one that holds a `suspend` or a `terminate`; `restriction`: one that holds another measure; `reversal`: one that holds a reversal; `no_measure`: any decision;
   - for every class but `order_effect`: the decision's case has the target that the gap's `metadata.target` names, when it names one.

   The gap gets `closed_at`, `closed_by` and the disposition `reapplied`. The decide, review and effect routes call it after their ledger rows and before their audit row, so the decision's own check at commit covers the transaction.
3. **Without a decision**: the acting account's `auth.users` row `FOR SHARE` shows an admin (`admin_required`); the gap gets `closed_at` and the disposition `not_needed`. The route then writes the audit row `gap_closed` through `append_confirmed_audit`.

**`restore_gaps_confirmed`**, a deferred constraint trigger, AFTER UPDATE ON `restore_gaps`, for a row whose disposition became `not_needed`: at commit the transaction's head audit row must be `gap_closed` on this gap, carry a step-up, and have been written by an account that is an admin (the gap stores no account for this disposition, so the check takes the audit row's actor). Otherwise the commit is refused `gap_mismatch`. A gap closed with a decision needs no check of its own: that decision's `decision_holds` covers the transaction. So no statement run as the API's role can empty the list of gaps, which is what opens the app's site after a restore, without a key.

Nothing here writes the ledger: a gap is a list entry, and closing one restricts nothing.

**`public.delete_restore_gaps(older_than timestamptz) returns integer`**, SECURITY DEFINER, EXECUTE to `korbat_api`: raises unless `older_than <= now() - gap_keep_months`; deletes the gaps closed before `older_than` as `not_needed` or `imported`, and the copy marks older than it, except the newest mark. **A gap that a decision closed (`reapplied`) is never deleted:** the statement of a decision taken again reads the lost decision's day from it, at every opening, and it is one small row for as long as the ledger's row, which 3c never deletes.

**`alert_steps()`**, replaced: 6.1's row marked 0029.

### 6.7 What the schema, grants and function tests pin

- **The schema test**: thirteen more tables; the eight enum values in their types' order; `decisions.order_item_id` and its foreign key's `on delete set null`; `informed_at` nullable with `decisions_informed`; the two re-added CHECKs by name; the two columns of `decision_actions`; the two deferred constraint triggers (`legal_order_events_confirmed`, `restore_gaps_confirmed`); every column, key, CHECK and index of 6.1 to 6.6.
- **The grants test.**
  - As `korbat_api`: no privilege at all on `authority_notifications`, `authority_notification_events` and `productions`, and EXECUTE on `seal_report` and `report_close_unsealed` refused. No UPDATE on `legal_orders`; no INSERT of a command-line column; on `legal_order_items` UPDATE of `intake_item_id` only; on `contact_messages` UPDATE of `disposition` and `handled_by` only; on `alert_outbox` UPDATE of `mailed_at` only; no write on `restore_gaps`, `notify_state` and `notify_folders`; an event other than `opened` refused by the trigger; an update of `decisions.informed_at` refused.
  - As `korbat_api`, the calls that a key must cover, each committed without its audit row and refused at commit: `order_event_record` for `closed_without_effect`, for `annex3_sent`, for `reopened` and for `hold_extended`; `order_correct` under each of its three grounds, and under `admin` with a plain audit row in place of a confirmed one; `gap_close` without a decision.
  - As `korbat_notify`: `has_schema_privilege('korbat_notify', 'public', 'USAGE')` is true (the superuser's grant, 6.1); exactly the table grants of 6.1; and nothing on any other table, view or function but `order_rules()`.
  - As `korbat_auth`: no privilege on `auth.report_holds`; EXECUTE on `auth.address_held`, `auth.address_held_until` and `auth.address_hold_final` only.
- **The function test** (3c 6.5: it reads every definition owned by `korbat_migrate`) gains these pins:
  - only `decisions_record`, `order_notices_release` and `order_correct` write a decision's `informed_at`, and `order_correct` only to null;
  - only `decision_actions_apply`, `order_preservation_end` and `order_correct` write the six ledger columns of `legal_order_items`, and only `order_correct` and `decision_actions_apply` its two closing columns;
  - only `order_event_record`, `order_correct` and `erase_order_records` write or delete a row of `legal_orders`; only `order_event_record`, `order_correct`, `decision_actions_apply`, `order_notices_release` and `order_preservation_end` insert an event;
  - only `decision_actions_check` and `order_correct` write `decision_actions.withhold_author`;
  - `posts.erased_at` and `post_media.erased_at` are written by `redact_r4_body` and `order_preservation_end`, beside `moderation_apply`'s columns as 3c and 3e pin them; `evidence_snapshots.erased_at` still only by `erase_snapshots`;
  - `frozen_for` is named only by `post_frozen` and `post_media_keep`, as 3c pins it; `post_preserved` is named only by `frozen_for`; and `snapshot_withheld`'s text is 3c's, unchanged;
  - only `report_open` and `report_close_unsealed` write `auth.report_holds` and a notification record;
  - only `gap_close` and `delete_restore_gaps` write `restore_gaps`;
  - no function or view of 0024 to 0029, and no line they add to a replaced function, holds an interval literal or a number of 6.1's table: each comes from `order_rules()`. The replaced `decisions_record` keeps its caps' two interval literals as built.
- **The early check**: a transaction that runs `SET CONSTRAINTS ALL IMMEDIATE` after an `order` decision and then inserts a second measure is refused at commit (0016's `decision_actions_recheck`).
- **3c's and 3e's own function tests** of every replaced function run unchanged and pass.
- **The upgrade test**: `scripts/test-db.mjs --upgrade-from` with 3e's last migration and `--fresh` run the six migrations in one transaction, after the role exists with its USAGE on `public` (`scripts/test-db.mjs` gives both, 6.1), and a run without the grant stops at the first migration's check; the source test of the enum-literal rule reads the six files.
- **The constants test** compares `order_rules()` with `ORDER_RULES`, name by name, and asserts that `hold_days + hold_extension_days` days are fewer than `preserve_months` months.

### 6.8 Retention

What 3f keeps, and for how long. Every period but the first is a design choice published in the terms (row 42; flagged for counsel).

| Record | Kept | Then | Anchor |
|---|---|---|---|
| A post disabled for a `tco_removal`: its body, its images, the snapshot the effect took | six calendar months and a day from the disabling, and from the new disabling when it is disabled again after a reinstatement; longer on a recorded request; through the author's deletion of the account as snapshot and images | by `order_preservation_end`: body blanked, post removed for good, images marked erased, every snapshot of the post erased. Ended without erasure by a reinstatement | TCO Art. 6; row 28; 6.3 |
| A post disabled for a `dsa_art9` | as any removed post (3c 6.6) | as there | OL E20.3 |
| A contact message's row | 30 days after it was handled; an order's message with the order; an unhandled one, stale or not, until a person has closed it | deleted | 6.4 |
| An outbox row | 30 days; a row whose message or order is still open, until that is handled, closed or given effect in full | deleted | 6.4 |
| An order's row, items, events and texts | 3 years after its last event, and never while a measure of it stands, a preservation runs or a decision about one of its posts is untold. So the texts of a removal order that is never withdrawn stay for as long as its measure does, past the content (erased at six months) and the account | deleted; the ledger's and the audit log's rows stay | 6.4; 3c 6.6; row 42 |
| A notification record with its events; a production record | 3 years after its last event; a notification's never while its seal is in force, nor while one of its events carries "not to the person" with an end that is open or ahead; a production's never while its own such end is ahead; a hand-over's never while its files are not recorded as destroyed | deleted | 6.5 |
| A hold of an address | until the notification is sent or declined, at most 7 days | the purge's last step overwrites the address at its next round; the hold's row goes with the account's last record | 6.5; row 35 |
| A restore gap closed as not needed or imported; a copy mark | 12 months after the gap closed; 12 months | deleted | 6.6 |
| A restore gap that a decision closed | as long as that decision's ledger row | never deleted: the decision's statement reads the lost decision's day from it | 6.6 |
| `notify_state`, `notify_folders` | one row, and one a folder | overwritten | 6.1 |

## 7. Flows

Short names as in the model: D is the 3c design, E3 the 3e design, each with its section. Every time is the database's, stored as an instant; a screen shows it in UTC with the viewer's local time beside it. Every route below stands behind the staff gate and `requireStaff` (`apps/api/src/staff/routes.ts:22`, `:66`); "step-up" means `requireStaffStepUp` in front of the handler, with the path's id as the action's target (`staff/cases.ts:88`). A sentence a person reads is named by its key; section 18 holds the words. Where a function of sections 5 and 6 is called, this section says what it is given and what follows from its answer, and section 6 holds its body.

Four rules hold in every flow, and each is stated once here:

1. **No measure without a key.** `disable_by_order` and `reinstate_by_order` enter the ledger only inside a transaction whose last statement is `append_confirmed_audit` for a step-up of the deciding account. The command line, the jobs, `notify` and the import write no row of `decisions` or `decision_actions`.
2. **The audit row is last.** Every write of 3f ends with one audit row in the same transaction, as its last statement (`staff/audit.ts:43`). Its metadata holds the record's facts as ids, enums, numbers, times, an authority's name and a reference, never a text about a person. Section 10.5 lists the actions. An event of the clock, an order's closing or reopening, a correction made after the first effect and a gap's closing are each held to their audit row at commit, as a decision is (7.5, 7.17): what the key confirmed is what is stored.
3. **After the commit, nothing can undo.** A mail, a push or an alert that follows a commit runs in a try of its own, unwaited for (`ModerationDeps.background`), and a failure leaves a row that a job picks up.
4. **Nothing reaches an author while a notice is held, on any path.** While the notice of an order is held, the post reads to its author on every screen, in every statement, mail and export exactly as a post that no order touched would read in that state; where a text cannot be that and stay true, the text is held with the notice. An order's record that says the author must not be told always hides the post from its author too, for every kind of order (7.4). 7.10 lists every reader and every text this rule changes, and section 12's route walk runs each as the author.

### 7.1 The watcher's loop (`notify`)

`notify` runs one loop, alone, with no lock in the limit store: one container, one connection to the mail host, one to PostgreSQL as `korbat_notify`. A round starts 20 seconds after the last one ended (`ALERT_RULES.pollSeconds`).

1. **Connect.** TLS to `NOTIFY_IMAP_HOST` on port 993 with the roots compiled into Node and the certificate's name checked; `LOGIN`, the user and the password sent as quoted strings (a value that holds a line break is refused at start). The port and one more root can be set for the development mailbox alone (`NOTIFY_IMAP_PORT`, `NOTIFY_IMAP_CA`, section 5), and `notify` refuses both when `NODE_ENV` is `production`. A failure sets `notify_state.error`, one of the eight words of section 6 (`imap_connect`; `imap_timeout`, when the host does not answer in time; `imap_tls`; `imap_login`; `imap_folder`; `imap_protocol`; `push_connect`; `push_refused`: `NOTIFY_ERRORS`, 10.1), and leaves `mailbox_ok_at` as it was; the round goes on to step 5. A wrong password is never retried faster than the round: a mail host locks an account that is tried in a loop.
2. **Each folder** (`INBOX`, then the folder named by `NOTIFY_IMAP_JUNK`, sent as a quoted string): `EXAMINE <folder>`, which opens it read-only, so no flag can change and the person's own mail app still shows the message unread. A folder that cannot be opened is `imap_folder`. The folder's validity number is read from the untagged line `* OK [UIDVALIDITY <n>]` of that answer and compared with `notify_folders`:
   - equal: `UID FETCH <last_uid + 1>:* (UID INTERNALDATE)`;
   - different, or no row yet: the mark is reset to 0 and the folder is read whole.
3. **Each answer line.** The mailbox is shared with a person's mail app, and a server may send a line nobody asked for at any time (RFC 9051, 2.2.2 and 5.2), so the client names the lines it knows, each by one anchored expression, and what it does with the rest:
   - read: the tagged completion (`OK`, `NO`, `BAD`) of the command in flight; `* OK [UIDVALIDITY <n>]` after `EXAMINE`; `* <n> FETCH (...)` that holds both `UID <n>` and `INTERNALDATE "<date-time>"`, in either order; `* BYE`;
   - ignored, without an error: every other untagged line that holds no literal (`EXISTS`, `RECENT`, `EXPUNGE`, `FLAGS`, `LIST`, `CAPABILITY`, an `OK`, `NO` or `BAD` with another code, a `FETCH` without both members);
   - an error (`imap_protocol`; the connection is closed and the folder does not count as read): a line that announces a literal (it ends in `{<n>}`), since the client asks for none and will not read its bytes; a line longer than a fixed bound; a completion that is not for the command in flight.

   The client asks for no header, no body, no flag and no envelope, so no line it reads holds a byte an outsider wrote. A UID at or below the mark is dropped: `n:*` always answers the newest message, also when `n` is above it (RFC 9051, the UID command's note on ranges).
4. **Insert.** For each new message, in UID order, one statement: `insert into contact_messages (folder, uidvalidity, uid, received_at) ... on conflict do nothing`, then the folder's mark in `notify_folders`. Each message is its own transaction, so the first push never waits for the rest of a long folder. The triggers force `seen_at` and `stale`, store a `received_at` that lies ahead of the database's clock as the clock's time (a mail host a few seconds ahead only moves a deadline earlier), and for a message that is not stale write the step-0 row of `alert_outbox` (kind `contact_mail`, scope `on_call`, priority 5, facts `receivedAt` and `dueAt` = `received_at` + 60 minutes). One bound keeps a flood from ringing the phone for every mail: while another message that is not stale has no disposition and its own step-0 row is less than `ORDER_RULES.quietMinutes` (15) old, the new message gets its row and no step-0 row. The person who is awake reads the whole mailbox, the first message's deadline is the earlier one, and the escalation and `order_due` (7.2) run for every message all the same. With `quietMinutes` 0 every message rings.
5. **Publish.** Every round: `select ... from alert_outbox where pushed_at is null and created_at > now() - <pushOwedMinutes>` for the kinds that have a push leg (10.1), oldest first; `ORDER_RULES.pushOwedMinutes` is 60, since an alert about the hour is worth sending while the hour runs. For each: `POST http://ntfy:2586/<topic>` with the publisher's token, the priority, one tag and the title and sentence of `ALERT_TEXTS[kind]` filled from `facts`; the topic is `korbat-oncall` for the scope `on_call` and `korbat-all` for `all`. A 200 sets `pushed_at`; anything else adds one to `push_attempts`, which is a counter for the log and no cap, and sets `push_connect` or `push_refused`. A row is tried again every round until it is published or older than the bound; then it is left to its mail (7.2) and is no longer owed, so the channel reads healthy again by itself.
6. **Beat.** One update of `notify_state`: `beat_at` = now; `mailbox_ok_at` = now when both folders were read; `push_ok_at` = now when the last publish succeeded or nothing was owed; `error` as set or null.
7. `NOOP` keeps the connection for the next round (an unasked line in its answer is step 3's); a dropped connection is found by the next command's failure and step 1 runs again. When PostgreSQL has been unreachable for longer than a round, `notify` exits 1, and a watchdog timer exits 1 when no round has finished for two minutes (section 5): Docker starts it again, and the beat stops meanwhile, which is what the channel's check reads. On `SIGTERM`: `LOGOUT`, the pool closed, exit 0.

What the loop never does: mark a message, move it, fetch a part, write an order, send a mail, or read a table beyond its grants (section 6).

| Case | What happens |
|---|---|
| The first start on a mailbox with old mail | every message is inserted; each received more than 2 hours before it was seen is `stale` and raises nothing; the rest alert |
| The process stopped between an insert and the mark | the folder is read from the old mark; the unique key drops the repeats |
| `UIDVALIDITY` changed | the folder is read whole and every message becomes a new row, since its UIDs are new; the old ones are stale, and one under 2 hours old alerts a second time, which is the cautious side. The earlier rows keep their dispositions; the new stale rows stay, as every message nobody handled does, until a person closes them, which the Contact point screen lets a person do for all of them together (7.3, 7.18) |
| A message deleted in the mailbox | its row stays; korbat never learns of it |
| The person's mail app marks a message read, or a new one arrives, while a command is in flight | the server's unasked lines (`* 3 EXISTS`, `* 2 FETCH (FLAGS (\Seen))`) are ignored; no error |
| ntfy is away for four minutes and then answers | the owed rows are published when it is back; the channel reads `ok` again without anyone touching a row |
| ntfy is away for longer than `pushOwedMinutes` | the rows of that time are left to their mails; the channel reads `ok` once the newer rows are published |
| The watcher was down for more than 2 hours | messages that arrived meanwhile are stale and raise nothing when it returns; `alert_channel_down` was mailed while it was down (7.2), and the console's Contact point screen lists them once it is back. Section 11.3 names this |
| A message the watcher never wrote a row for (it is down, the mailbox cannot be read, or the mail host filed the message in a folder that is not watched) | a person who reads it in the mail host's app opens the order in the console without a message (7.4); the hour never waits for the watcher or for a shell |

### 7.2 The alert tick, the steps and the two legs

**The tick.** The API runs a job `alert-tick` under the jobs' shared lock (`lib/jobs.ts:81`), a round 30 seconds after the last one ended, on one instance at a time. The job always runs: `NOTIFY=off` skips step 3 alone, so the reminders of an order, a report and a copy, which are not about the mailbox, are written and mailed in development, in the tests and on a server that has no mailbox yet. The lock is the limit store's (`index.ts:53`), so while that store is down no round runs: the first push of a message still goes, since `notify` uses no lock, and there is no mail of it, no escalation, no reminder and no channel alarm until the store is back. A round, each step in a try of its own:

1. `select alert_steps()`: the database writes every time-driven row that is due (below). The unique key makes a second call write nothing.
2. **The mail leg.** `select ... from alert_outbox where mailed_at is null` for the kinds whose mail is their push's sentence (`ALERT_KINDS[kind].mail` is `sentence`, 10.1: every kind of 3f that has a mail), whoever wrote the row: the trigger, `alert_steps()`, the tick itself, `staffAlert` or a command. For each: `mailStaff({ template: <kind>, ...facts })`; when at least one address accepted it, `mailed_at` = now. A row nobody accepted is tried again next round, for 24 hours from `created_at` (`ALERT_RULES.mailRetryHours`), then logged and left. The tick never mails a row of 3c's or 3e's kinds: their mails say more than `facts` holds, and `staffAlert` sends them itself (below).
3. **The channel's health**, skipped with `NOTIFY=off`. The channel is down when `notify_state.beat_at` is older than 3 minutes (`channelDownMinutes`), or a row that is still owed a push (a kind with a push leg, `pushed_at` null, younger than `pushOwedMinutes`: the predicate of 7.1 step 5) is older than 3 minutes. While it is down the tick inserts one row of the kind `alert_channel_down` into `alert_outbox` (mail only: `notify` publishes no row of that kind); when the beat is fresh and `mailbox_ok_at` is older than 3 minutes, one row of the kind `mailbox_unreachable` (push and mail). Each is written at most once in 24 hours, by the outbox's own rule (no row of that kind was created in the last 24 hours) and with no key in the limit store, and step 2 mails it. A row past its push bound no longer counts, so the line on the home screen and the alarm clear by themselves, at the latest an hour after the push server is back. `GET /api/staff/overview` reads the same facts fresh for the home banner.

**`alert_steps()`** writes every time-driven row by one rule (section 6): a row is written when its condition holds and no row of the same kind and subject was created at or after its threshold; its `step` is the subject's highest step of that kind plus one, so steps are counted a kind, and the unique key drops what a concurrent call wrote. Every number is a column of `order_rules()` (10.1).

| Kind | Written when | Scope, priority | Ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| `contact_mail_escalated` | a message that is not stale has `received_at` at least 15 minutes back (`escalateMinutes`), no disposition, and no staff session was created or used since `received_at` (`auth.staff_sessions.created_at`, `last_used_at`: a person's own action in the console, never a request the page sent by itself) | `all`, 5 | written once a message |
| `order_due` for a message | the message is not stale and has no disposition: one row at `received_at` + 45 minutes (`remindMinutes`) and one at `received_at` + 60 (`hourMinutes`), the deadline it would have as an order. Facts: `dueAt`, `minutesLeft` | `all`, 5 | when the message is handled |
| `order_due` for an order | a `tco_removal` that is not closed, whose clock runs (`legal_order_clock`: not stopped, a deadline) and whose `items_open` is above 0: one row at `clock_start` + 45 minutes and one at `deadline_at`. Facts: `dueAt`, `minutesLeft`, `itemsOpen` | `all`, 5 | when every item is disabled or closed with its reason (7.5), so one item given effect ends nothing; when the clock is stopped; when the order is closed |
| `contact_mail` for an order | a `grounds_ceased` or `clarification_received` event restarted the clock of a `tco_removal` that is not closed, and the clock runs with `items_open` above 0: one row for the order at its new `clock_start`, with the new `dueAt` (7.5). Its step is the order's highest step of that kind plus one, so a second restart of one order alerts again | `on_call`, 5 | written once a restart |
| `order_unverified` | daily, while (a) an order has an item with `disabled_at` set and its `verified_at` is null or earlier than that item's `added_at`, or (b) an order that is given effect has no `reasons` recorded (7.7), or (c) a DSA order holds a notice and no end of the hold is recorded (7.10). Facts: `count` (the orders of (a)), `textsMissing` (those of (b)), `holdsOpen` (those of (c)), `oldestAt` | `all`, 5 | `order-verify`, run again after items were added; `order-texts`; `order-hold-until`, `order-hold-end` |
| `hold_ending` | daily, while an order has an untold decision and its `order_hold_until` is less than 7 days ahead (`holdWarningDays`). Facts: `count`, `at` (the first end) | `all`, 3 | the release; `order-hold-extend`, `order-hold-until` |
| `report_owed` | daily, while a notification record is `owed` and was opened more than 24 hours ago (`reportAlertHours`, 7.13). Facts: `count`, `oldestAt` | `all`, 5 | the record is sent or declined |
| `handover_undestroyed` | daily, while a production with an archive was delivered more than 7 days ago (`handoverAlertDays`) and has no `destroyed_at` (7.14). Facts: `count` | `all`, 3 | `handover-destroyed` |
| `copy_overdue` | daily, while the newest `copy_marks.at` is older than 48 hours (`copyOverdueHours`), or there is none and the audit log's oldest row is that old (7.16). Facts: `hours` | `all`, 3 | a confirmed copy |
| `alert_test` | daily | `all`, 3, push only | |

"Daily" is the same rule with the threshold "24 hours ago": a daily kind is written when no row of its kind was created in the last 24 hours, so on the first day at once. Section 6 fixes no hour of the day. A row of `order_due` or `contact_mail` whose subject is still open (a message nobody handled, an order past its deadline that is neither given effect in full nor closed) is kept by the retention job (7.18), so an old threshold is never met a second time and no reminder is written again every 30 days.

**`staffAlert`'s second leg.** 3c's and 3e's staff alerts and 3f's own alerts after a commit (`order_opened`, `order_corrected`, `order_effected`, `order_reinstated`, `copy_taken`) go through `staffAlert` (`moderation/alerts.ts:21`). After the throttle let the alert pass:

1. it inserts one row of `alert_outbox` (the kind, its scope and priority from 10.1, the facts as counts and times only), which `notify` publishes;
2. the mail, by the kind's `mail` member (10.1):
   - `sentence` (3f's own kinds): it mails every staff account at once with the kind's sentence and sets the row's `mailed_at` when an address accepted the mail; a row left without it is mailed by the tick's step 2;
   - `own` (the six kinds of 3c and 3e, whose mail says more than the push): it mails as today, with the caller's full `StaffMessage`, and then sets `mailed_at` whether or not an address took it. The row is for the push alone, and the tick never renders such a mail from `facts`;
3. it answers `sent` when either leg took the alert, and gives the throttle's hit back (D 7.12) only when neither did, so that the next occasion sends it again (`alerts.ts:31`).

A failed insert is logged and the mail still goes: an alert fails open. D 7.12's keys, windows and the three cases of "still calls for someone" are unchanged. `moderation_reveal` and `moderation_csam_lifted`, which 3c sends through `mailStaff` without a throttle (`decide.ts:75`), go the same way without a key. A kind without a throttle key (`order_opened`, `order_corrected`, `order_effected`, `order_reinstated`, `copy_taken`) is bounded by the caps of 7.4 and 7.6 and by the key each needs or the day's limit of records.

**A command alerts as its console twin does.** A command whose console twin raises an alert (`order-open`: `order_opened`) inserts that kind's row of `alert_outbox` itself, as the migrator, in its transaction before the audit row; `notify` pushes it and the tick mails it. So no order is opened, from the console or from the shell, without every staff account hearing of it.

**Which mails are their push's sentence.** Every kind of 3f has one entry of `ALERT_TEXTS` (10.1): a title and one sentence, filled from `facts`, which holds numbers and times and nothing else; its push and its mail both print it. 3c's and 3e's kinds keep their mails word for word, the staff handle of `moderation_burst`, `moderation_reads`, `moderation_reveal` and `moderation_csam_lifted` and the lanes of `moderation_waiting` included (`auth/flows/notifiers.ts:70`); only their push is new, one fixed sentence with a count and a time. D 7.15 is not amended.

**What a person does with an alert** is the runbook's (section 18): read the mailbox in the mail host's own app, never in korbat; sign in to the console, or act in it when already signed in, either of which stops the 15-minute step.

### 7.3 A message at the contact point

1. `GET /api/staff/contact` answers `ContactPage` (10.2): the messages newest first by `received_at`, 50 a page, each with its folder, `receivedAt`, `seenAt`, `stale`, its disposition and the orders opened from it. No sender and no subject exist to show. `?open=1` lists only the messages without a disposition.
2. A message that is an order: 7.4. Its disposition becomes `order` there.
3. A message that is not: `POST /api/staff/contact/:message/close` with `{ disposition }`, one of `referral`, `first_order_notice`, `preservation_request`, `scrutiny_decision`, `clarification`, `not_an_order`. No step-up: it restricts nothing and tells nobody. One transaction: the update of `disposition` and `handled_by` (the trigger forces `handled_at`), then the audit row `contact_closed` (target `contact_message`; metadata `{ disposition, receivedAt, folder }`).
   - A message that already has a disposition: the route's update, which asks for `disposition is null`, changes no row, and the answer is 409 `message_handled`. A disposition never changes; a mistake is corrected by what follows it (an order opened from the message all the same, 7.4 step 4).
   - An unknown id: 404 `not_found`.
   - A message nobody handled is never deleted, a stale one included (7.18), so the screen lets a person close the stale ones it lists together, as `not_an_order` or another word (section 8). That is the console's own loop over this route, one request and one audit row a message: there is no route for several.
4. Closing ends the message's steps: `alert_steps()` reads the disposition.
5. What a disposition leads to is the runbook's, with one line each on the screen (section 8): a `referral` staff act on is a moderator's own finding and an ordinary decision of D 7.5; a `first_order_notice` (TCO Art. 3(2)) starts nothing in the software; a `preservation_request` is recorded with `order-preserve-extend` (7.11); a `scrutiny_decision` with `order-scrutiny` and, where it annuls, a reinstatement (7.9); a message about an order korbat holds that extends a non-disclosure period is recorded at once by an admin as the event `hold_extended` (7.5, 7.10), so that the release never overtakes it while the operator is away.

### 7.4 Opening an order record

**From the console**, by any staff member: `POST /api/staff/orders` with `OrderOpenRequest` (10.2). No step-up: the record restricts nothing, copies nothing and tells nobody, and Annex III must be recordable before any effect.

1. **The day's limit.** The API counts the caller's rows of `legal_orders` opened in the last 24 hours, by the database's clock; at `ORDER_RULES.recordsPerDay` (20) it answers 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`, before anything is written, as a finding's limit is counted (`moderation/cases.ts:655`). The insert's trigger counts again under the account's advisory lock and raises the same code (section 6), so two requests at once cannot pass the limit together.
2. **The links.** Each of the up to 10 links is parsed by `parseLink` (`moderation/links.ts:23`) against `APP_ORIGIN`. Nothing is ever fetched. What a link names decides its item, and the answer says which (`linkKind`, 10.2):
   - `post`: a post's page. The item's `post_id` is that id when a row of `posts` has it, in any state;
   - `image`: the address of an image korbat serves. The item is the post the image is attached to (one read-only function of the media module's index, `imagePost(id)`), and the link is kept as named. An order that names a picture's address is not short of information, and the measure is the post's (section 2, rows 16 and 43);
   - `account`: a profile's page. `post_id` is null: an order against an account as such is not built in 3f (section 2, row 43), and the console says so in words of its own, never as missing information (section 8);
   - `other`: another site's address, or a post or an image that does not exist. `post_id` is null and the link is stored as named.

   Two links that name one post are refused (422 `invalid_request`). A `dsa_art10` order takes no link; the other two kinds take at least one.
3. **The message.** `messageId` names the message the order came in, and its `received_at` becomes the order's. The form's choice "The message is not listed" sends `messageId` null: the watcher was down, the mailbox could not be read, the mail host filed the message in a folder that is not watched, or the order reached a person another way. The order is then opened with `received_at` null: its state is `unknown_deadline`, every screen says `orders.deadlineUnknown` (act now), "Disable for an order" needs no deadline, and `order-received` records the instant afterwards (7.7). With that choice the form asks "How it arrived", and `channel` carries the person's pick from `ORDER_CHANNELS`, stored as sent (`mailbox` for a mail that is in the mailbox and in no row; `portal`, `post` or `other` for an order that came another way); with a message named the channel is `mailbox`, and the trigger forces it. The console still takes no typed time, and the API asks for no reason and does not test the channel's state: a test that is wrong on the one night it matters would put the hour behind a shell. The day's limit and the alert `order_opened` bound this path as they bound every record.
4. **One transaction** on the API's pool, through `insertOrder(tx, facts)`, the one function of the moderation module that writes these rows for the route, for `order-open` and for the import (7.17):
   1. the insert of `legal_orders` with a new id, the kind, state, authority, reference, message or none, the channel, Section C, the emergency flag, `cross_border` (`memberState` differs from `HOME_STATE`) and `withhold_from_author`. The trigger forces `opened_at`, `opened_via` `console` and `received_at` from the message, forces `channel` `mailbox` when a message is named and keeps the request's channel when none is, and forces `withhold_from_author` true for a `tco_removal` and for every order whose Section C is set, whatever the request says: a notice can be held only on a post its author cannot see (rule 4). `sectionC` is a required member with no default; the form's third choice, "I cannot tell", is sent as true, so an untouched or unclear box never tells;
   2. one row of `legal_order_items` a link, in the order given, positions from 1; the trigger forces `added_at`;
   3. the event `opened`;
   4. the message's disposition `order`, when one is named and has none (a message closed as something else keeps that word, and the order is opened all the same: at night a wrong tap must not send the person to the shell);
   5. the audit row `order_opened` (target `order`; metadata: every member of the request but the links' texts, each item as 10.5 words it, `receivedAt` or null, `openedAt`, `crossBorder`, `via: "console"`).
5. **Refusals.** A named message that does not exist: 404 `not_found` from the route's read, and 422 `order_needs_message` from the trigger when it went between the two. The same state, authority and reference as an existing order: 409 `order_exists`. It is the one refusal of 3f that carries a member: `orderId`, the order that stands, which the route reads after the refusal, so that the console links to it. That record never stands in the way: whatever a person entered wrongly in it is corrected on its screen (below), and a closed one is reopened by an admin (7.8).
6. **After the commit:** `staffAlert` with `order_opened` (no throttle; facts: the order's count of items and `dueAt` or null). The answer is 201 with `OrderView`.

**More items.** `POST /api/staff/orders/:order/items` with `{ links }` adds up to 10 links to an order that is not closed, up to position `ORDER_ITEMS_MAX` (50), by the same steps 2 and 4.2, with the audit row `order_items_added`. An order that names more than ten addresses is opened with the first ten, so that the first effect never waits for the typing of the rest. An item added after the order's verification is unverified until `order-verify` runs again: the item carries `addedAt`, `OrderView.owed.verification` is true again, and the daily `order_unverified` alert covers it once it is given effect (7.2, 7.7). A verification is a fact about the items a person compared with the order, never about items added afterwards.

**Correcting what a person entered.** `POST /api/staff/orders/:order/correct` with `OrderCorrectRequest` (10.2): `facts`, which holds only the members to change (`kind`, `memberState`, `authority`, `reference`, `channel`, `sectionC`, `emergency`, `withholdFromAuthor`, and `closeItems`, a list of `{ itemId, reason }`), and a step-up where the rule below asks for one. `channel` is corrected like every member, under `entry` before the first effect and under `admin` after it, for an order opened without a message: one opened from a message stays `mailbox`. A record typed wrongly at night must never stand in the way of the hour, and a missed "must not be told" must never end in telling.

1. One transaction: `boundLockWaits` with 4 waits (the order, the acting account, the key, the audit head); for an order that is given effect the route first takes the profiles of the authors of the order's posts (`lockAccounts`, "no key update", in id order), as the effect transaction begins, and the bound counts one more wait for each of them and for each decision and post that the function then takes (section 5, the lock order); `order_correct(order, facts, account)`, to whose `facts` the API adds `crossBorder` when `memberState` changes; then the audit row `order_corrected` (target `order`; metadata `{ rule, changed, eventId }`, 10.5). The function decides under the order's row `FOR UPDATE` which rule allows the change, and stores it as the event's ground:

| Rule | When | Who, and how |
|---|---|---|
| `entry` | no item of the order was given effect | any staff member, any member of `facts`, no step-up; a plain audit row |
| `caution` | the order is given effect, and `facts` is `{ sectionC: true }` and nothing else | any staff member, no step-up: whoever is on call sets a missed "must not be told" at once |
| `admin` | the order is given effect, and `facts` holds anything else (unsetting `sectionC`, the kind, a name, the channel, an item's closing) | an admin, under the step-up `order_corrected`, whose params are `facts`; the audit row goes through `append_confirmed_audit` |

2. **An item's closing.** `closeItems` closes an open item with a recorded reason, one of `ORDER_ITEM_CLOSE_REASONS`: `no_post` (the link names nothing korbat can disable for an order: an account, another site, a post that does not exist), `not_named` (the order does not name this link: korbat's own mistake in entering it) or `withdrawn` (the authority withdrew this item before korbat acted on it, while other items of the order stand; an order withdrawn whole is closed, 7.8). An item that was given effect is never closed: a wrong one is reinstated with `wrong_item`, and one the authority withdraws after its effect with `withdrawn` (7.9). An order is given effect in full only when each of its items is disabled or closed this way, and until then its reminders run (7.2, 7.5).
3. **What follows**, each written by the function (section 6): the forced columns follow the corrected ones as at the insert (`withhold_from_author` for a `tco_removal` and for Section C). When Section C is set on an order that was given effect, every decision of the order, and every decision held with them (7.10), is untold again: a mail not yet sent is not sent, the notice leaves the author's screens, and the release tells it at the hold's end; what was already read cannot be taken back. When Section C is unset while a notice is held, the hold ends with the reason `not_ordered`, and the notice says that the silence was korbat's mistake. A changed kind starts or ends the preservation of the items in force, and nothing is erased by a correction. A changed withholding moves the `withhold_author` of the order's measures in force, and the level of their posts is derived again (`disabled` or `removed`).
4. **At commit** `legal_order_events_confirmed` holds the `corrected` event to its audit row: `order_corrected` on this order, naming this event, written by the acting account, and carrying a step-up when the rule is `admin` (7.5).
5. **Refusals:** 409 `order_closed` (an admin reopens it first, 7.8); 403 `admin_required`; 409 `order_exists` when the corrected state, authority and reference are another order's; 422 `invalid_request` for a member outside the list, for a `channel` on an order opened from a message, or for an item that is not an open item of this order; 404 `not_found`.
6. **After the commit:** `staffAlert` with `order_corrected` (facts: `count`, how many members changed, and `at`), so no record changes without every staff account hearing of it. The answer is 201 with `OrderView`; a confirmation of "Disable for an order" that was signed before the correction answers `order_changed` (7.6).

**From the command line**, for an order that arrived another way (a portal, post, a courier) when its time of receipt is to be recorded with it, which the console cannot take: `staff.js order-open`, as the migrator. It supplies what the console may not, `received_at` (or none) and `sent_at`, and the `channel` as the console's form does, with a message's id (`--message`) only where a row lists the mail. It reads `HOME_STATE` and `APP_ORIGIN`, which the migrate service is given for it, and refuses with one sentence when either is missing; it parses the links with `parseLink` and writes through `insertOrder`, both exported by the moderation module's index as plain functions that take the caller's transaction. The same rows and the same audit action with `via: "cli"` and the system actor, and before the audit row the command inserts the `order_opened` row of `alert_outbox` itself (7.2): an order opened at the shell is heard of like any other. An order without `received_at` has no deadline, and every screen says `orders.deadlineUnknown` until `order-received` records the instant.

### 7.5 The order's screen, the clock and its events

**Reading.** `GET /api/staff/orders` answers the orders that are not closed and those closed in the last 30 days (`OrdersPage`). `GET /api/staff/orders/:order` answers `OrderView`:

- the row's facts, with who opened it as a staff handle, and `verified` (whether `verified_at` is set);
- the clock, one row of `legal_order_clock`: `clockStart`, `stopped` with `stoppedAt` and `stopGround` (the `at` and the ground of the newest `annex3_sent` event, which the API reads beside the view: the view has no column for them), `deadlineAt`, `targetAt` (start + 55 minutes, the target until 3d purges edge copies), `itemsOpen`, `firstEffectAt`, `lastEffectAt`, `withinHour`; all null for a DSA order, which has `dueAt` as its text states it, or null. An item is open while it is neither disabled nor closed with a reason (7.4); the order is given effect in full only when `itemsOpen` is 0. `withinHour` is null while an item is open or nothing was disabled, then compares `lastEffectAt` with the deadline, and is true when the last effect was given while the clock was stopped. `firstEffectAt` stays for Annex II. So one item given effect ends no reminder and proves no hour;
- the items, each with its link as named, its `linkKind` (7.4), `addedAt` and `unverified` (the order is not verified, or the item was added after its verification), and, for one with a post, read live: the author's handle, the post's state, its creation time, the day its author deleted it or null, the count of its images and, only while the post is `visible`, the first `ORDER_ITEM_PREVIEW` (80) characters of its body. A post that is not public shows its state's word and no text: the order's screen is no second way to read what a case's access log guards (D 7.3). An item also carries `decisionId`, `caseId`, `disabledAt`, `noEffect` (the measure's, `deleted` or `already`), `preserveUntil`, `preserveEndedAt`, `reinstatedAt` with `reinstatedGround`, and `closedAt` with `closedReason` once set;
- the events, oldest first, each with its kind, ground, `at`, `effectiveAt`, a staff handle or `system`, and `via`;
- `owed`: which of the command line's records are missing (`received`, `verification`, `issuedAt`, `texts`, `document`; `holdUntil` for a DSA order that holds a notice with no recorded end; `tellPerson` for a `dsa_art10` whose rule for telling the person is not recorded, 7.14), and `scrutinyUntil` (`received_at` + 48 hours, TCO Art. 4(4)) for a cross-border `tco_removal` without a `scrutiny_requested` event while that time is ahead. `verification` is owed while the order is not verified or one of its items is `unverified`;
- the hold: `until` (`order_hold_until`), `basis` (`regulation` for a removal order, whose end the database computes; `authority` for a DSA order, whose end is what the authority states), `open` (true while a DSA order's hold has no recorded end), `extended`, `endedAt` with `endReason`, `releasedAt`, and `noticesHeld`, the count of its decisions with `informed_at` null;
- `mayCorrect`: the rule under which the caller could correct the record now (`entry`, `caution`, `admin`, or null), with `mayClose` and `mayEvents` an aid for the console. The database decides.

The read writes no access row: it shows nothing that a signed-out visitor could not open, plus the order's own facts. The register entry to check against and the Annex II and III fields are the console's, from `STATE_FACTS` and the view (section 8).

**Recording an event.** `POST /api/staff/orders/:order/events`, step-up `order_event_recorded` with the params `{ kind, ground, messageId }`.

1. One transaction: `boundLockWaits` with 5 waits (the order, the acting account, the message, the key, the audit head); `order_event_record(order, kind, ground, message, account)`, whose fifth argument is the session's account; then `append_confirmed_audit` (action `order_event_recorded`, target `order`, metadata `{ params, eventId, effectiveAt }`).
2. The function decides everything under the order's row `FOR UPDATE`; the route only maps its refusals:

| Kind | Allowed on | Ground | Effective time | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `annex3_sent` | a `tco_removal` that is not closed, while its clock is not stopped | one of `ANNEX3_GROUNDS` | now | the clock stops: no deadline; `order_due` ends |
| `grounds_ceased` | the same, while stopped by an Annex III with the ground `force_majeure` | none | the later of the picked message's `received_at` (or now) and the Annex III's `at` | a fresh 60 minutes from the effective time (TCO Art. 3(7)); a new `contact_mail` alert for the order |
| `clarification_received` | the same, while stopped by an Annex III with another ground | none | the later of the picked message's `received_at` and the Annex III's `at`; a message is required | a fresh 60 minutes (Art. 3(8)); a new `contact_mail` alert for the order; the message's disposition becomes `clarification` when it has none, with `handled_at`, which the function sets itself |
| `annex2_sent` | a `tco_removal` with at least one item given effect | none | now | recorded; `owed` loses nothing: Annex II is the person's mail, and this row is its record |
| `receipt_confirmed` | a `dsa_art9` or `dsa_art10` order | none | now | recorded: the information DSA Art. 9(1) and 10(1) owe the authority was sent |
| `closed_without_effect` | any order with no measure in force, by an admin | one of `ORDER_CLOSE_GROUNDS` | now | `closed_at` is set; the order's alerts end (7.8) |
| `reopened` | a closed order, by an admin | none | now | `closed_at` is cleared: the order is open again with its items, events and clock as they were (7.8) |
| `hold_extended` | a `tco_removal` with Section C whose hold was not extended and whose notice is not released, by an admin; a message is required (the authority's reasoned extension, as the person read it) | none | now | the one extension of 42 days (7.10); the command line confirms it (`order-hold-extend`) or undoes it (`order-hold-end`) afterwards |

   So every Annex III has exactly one restart, and the function holds the rule the console shows.
