A restart is never refused for its time: an Annex III sent at 10:00 and recorded at 10:30, with the clarification of 10:20, restarts the clock at 10:30, the later and the cautious reading (in LAB B a clarification dated before the Annex III left the clock stopped for good).
3. **At commit** the deferred trigger `legal_order_events_confirmed` holds every console event but `opened` to its audit row, as `decision_holds` holds a decision: the transaction's head audit row is `order_event_recorded` on this order, names this event (`metadata.eventId`), carries a step-up and was written by the event's account. So the acting account is not taken on trust, and a statement run as the API's role cannot stop a clock, close or reopen an order or extend a hold without a row that `audit-verify` checks.
4. **Refusals:** 422 `order_event_refused` for a kind out of place (an Annex II before any effect; an Annex III while the clock is stopped; a cease or a clarification while the clock runs, or after an Annex III of the other ground; a ground outside its list; a reopening of an order that is not closed; an extension of a hold that was extended once, or of an order without Section C; an event without its audit row at commit); 422 `order_needs_message` for a clarification or an extension without a message; 409 `already_released` for an extension after the notice was released or the hold ended; 403 `admin_required`; 409 `order_closed` for every kind but `reopened`; 404 `not_found`. The ended and the late confirmation answer as a decision's do (`staff/cases.ts:91`).
5. The restart's alert is the database's: the next tick's `alert_steps()` writes the `contact_mail` row for the order (7.2), so it goes also when the API stops between the commit and the answer.
6. Annex II and Annex III themselves leave from the mailbox, by the person's hand, to the address in the order's Section F and to the home state's copy address (`STATE_FACTS[HOME_STATE].annexCopy`). korbat sends no mail to an authority.

### 7.6 "Disable for an order"

`POST /api/staff/orders/:order/effect`, step-up `order_effected` with `OrderEffectedParams` (10.3): the order's facts as the console showed them, `urgent`, and `items`, 1 to 10 entries `{ itemId, postId }` sorted by `postId`. One key touch gives effect to every item selected; an order with more than ten is given effect in several actions, and it is given effect in full only when none of its items is open (7.5): until then its reminders run and its hour is not judged.

**Verified first, where no hour runs.** A `tco_removal` is given effect by any staff member before anyone has verified it: its hour runs, the measure is reversible and preserves everything, and the verification follows by the next working day (7.7). A `dsa_art9` order has no hour of the Regulation and no register that lists who may issue it, so nothing is done in doubt: it is given effect only once `verified_at` is set. The one exception is an admin's, for an order that is urgent by its own text: the signed member `urgent: true`, which the alert `order_effected` then says to every staff account. `decisions_record` and `decision_holds` hold the rule (422 `order_not_verified`: for an account that is not an admin, whatever it signed, in the place of `admin_required`, and for an admin without the signed `urgent`), and `urgent` is refused as `invalid_decision` on a `tco_removal` and on a verified order, so that the word is never signed where it means nothing.

**Before the transaction** (reads; the database decides again):

1. The confirmation is for this action, this order and the session's key, or the route throws: a wiring fault, as in `decide.ts:111`.
2. The order exists (404 `not_found`), is not closed (409 `order_closed`) and is not a `dsa_art10` (422 `invalid_decision`); a `dsa_art9` is verified or the action is an admin's urgent one (above).
3. The signed facts equal the order's row, member by member, and every item is the order's and names its post (409 `order_changed`: the console reads the order again and the person confirms what is there now). A correction made since the console read the order (7.4) is found here. An item that was closed with a reason, or reinstated, may be given effect: the measure's trigger clears the closing, and a re-disabling is a new disabling (step 5.3).
4. Each post's author is read (`SocialForModeration.postAuthor`); a post's author never changes.
5. After a restore (7.17): the open gaps of class `order_effect` that name one of these items are read; the request carries nothing for them.

**The transaction**, on the API's pool, in section 5's lock order:

1. `boundLockWaits(tx, expiresAt, ORDER_EFFECT_WAITS(n))`, with `5 + 9 * n` waits for `n` items (once: the order, the deciding account's row, the cap's advisory lock, the key, the audit head; an item: the author's profile, the case, the item's row, the account's row, the post, up to four images). The item's row comes after its case and before its post, in this transaction and in `order_preservation_end` (7.11), so the two never wait for each other. With under a millisecond of the challenge left nothing is written (`late`).
2. `lockAccounts(tx, authors, "no key update")`: the authors' profiles in id order, in one statement. A decision or a seal about one of them waits here and meets nothing else.
3. One row of `intakes`: kind `order`, channel `authority`, a fresh reference, `alleges_illegal` true, the category the kind fixes (10.1), no sender, no text, no receipt.
4. For each item in `postId` order: the post's open case, opened when there is none (`openCase`, `moderation/repo.ts:91`), then the intake's item (`insertItem`), whose attach trigger takes the case. So the cases are taken in their targets' order before any post; the API's role cannot lock a case itself. Then the item's `intake_item_id`.
5. For each item in `postId` order:
   1. the snapshot of the post as it stands, taken the intake's way: `capturePost` under its share lock, `MediaForCases.hold` for the image rows, `storeSnapshot` (`moderation/intake.ts`). An order's item always gets a snapshot of its own, also when the case's newest one is equal: the preservation keeps exactly this row;
   2. the `decisions` row: kind `order`, `order_item_id`, the case's `state_version` as read after step 4, `through_seq` 0, outcome `restricted`, ground `illegal`, no rule, the three digests null, `RULES_VERSION` and `REDRESS_VERSION` as the API serves them, the snapshot and its digest. The triggers force the source `order`, the category and keyword, `needs_admin` false (true for a `dsa_art9` order that is not verified, which only an admin's urgent effect reaches), and `informed_at` and `author_mail` by the hold (7.10); they refuse a `dsa_art9` order that is not verified unless the confirmed params hold an admin's `urgent` (`order_not_verified`), and an item whose intake item is not this action's own (`invalid_decision`);
   3. one `decision_actions` row, `disable_by_order`. Its triggers force `withhold_author` from the order, derive the level (`disabled`, or `removed` for a `dsa_art9` that does not withhold, which by 7.4 is never an order that holds its notice), write the item's `decision_id`, `action_id`, `disabled_at` (the first disabling, kept) and `preserve_until`, clear a closing, and insert the event `effect_given`. A disabling after an earlier preservation of the item ended (by a reinstatement or by time) is a new disabling: `preserve_until` is six months and a day from now, never from the first date, and what the earlier end erased stays erased. A measure taken again after a restore on an item whose preservation never ended starts no new period (7.17).
6. For each gap read before the transaction: `gap_close(gap, decision, account)` with its item's decision and the deciding account (7.17).
7. Last: `append_confirmed_audit` with the action `order_effected`, the target `order`, and the metadata `{ params, decisionIds }`, where `decisionIds` maps each item's id to its decision's id.
8. At commit `decision_holds` runs for each decision (section 6): the head audit row is a confirmed `order_effected` on this order by the deciding account, its params equal the order's row (`urgent` and `items` apart), it names this item among at most ten and as many decisions as items, and the decision's ledger is exactly one `disable_by_order`.

**Retry and refusals.** A deadlock (`40P01`) or a lock timeout the bound cut short (`55P03`) runs the transaction once more with the same confirmation while more than a minute of the challenge is left (`decide.ts:159`); otherwise `late`. Every code a trigger raises is answered as its `ModerationError`:

| Code | Status | When |
|---|---|---|
| `order_cap` | 429 | the account's order decisions of the last 24 hours, with this action's, would pass `ORDER_RULES.decisionsPerDay` (50) |
| `invalid_decision` | 422 | an item already given effect by a measure in force; an item whose intake item is missing, of another case or post, not of an `order` intake, or already answered; `urgent` on a `tco_removal` or on a verified order; any difference `decision_holds` finds |
| `order_not_verified` | 422 | a `dsa_art9` order that is not verified, without an admin's `urgent`: from an account that is not an admin always (section 6 answers this code in the place of `admin_required`), from an admin without the signed `urgent` |
| `case_changed`, `case_closed` | 409 | the case moved under the transaction, as D 7.5 |
| `staff_session_required` | 401 | the confirming account or key ended (`staff_confirmation_ended`) |
| `challenge_expired` | 400 | `late` |

What does not refuse, and why (section 2): `staff_recused`, `staff_target`, `decision_cap` and `admin_required` (an unverified `dsa_art9` order is refused `order_not_verified` in its place). A post that its author deleted is given a decision whose measure has `no_effect` `deleted`: the record shows that the order was carried out on what was left.

**After the commit**, each in a try of its own, unwaited for:

1. for each decision, `sendStatement`: an `order` decision that is told (`author_mail` `pending`) is mailed `order_notice`; a held one has `author_mail` `none` and nothing is sent;
2. `staffAlert` with `order_effected` (facts: `items`, `at`, `itemsOpen`, and `urgent`, 1 for an admin's urgent effect on an unverified order and 0 otherwise, which the kind's sentence says);
3. 3c's count of the account's decisions in 15 minutes, order decisions included, and `moderation_burst` at 20.

**The answer** is 201 with `OrderView` as it now stands. The screen shows the time of effect against the deadline and the target, how many items are still open, and `orders.checkOutside` (open the post's address in a private window: 404; an image may be served from an edge copy for up to five minutes).

### 7.7 After the effect

1. **Annex II.** The person sends it from the mailbox; the console shows its fields as text, filled from the order (the reference, `received_at`) and one line an item: its link, what korbat did or found (disabled; already deleted by its author; already disabled or removed by another decision; closed with its reason) and its time. An order given effect in two actions, or with an item whose measure had no effect, is reported as what happened to each address. Then the event `annex2_sent` (7.5).
2. **By the next working day, on the server.** Each command is one transaction as the migrator with the 10-second `lock_timeout`; it updates the order's row, inserts its event with `via` `cli`, and ends with the audit row `order_recorded` (system actor; target `order`; metadata `{ event, ...facts }`, never a text):

| Command | Writes | Event |
|---|---|---|
| `order-verify` | `verified_at`, `verified_how` (which register entry or, for a DSA order, which coordinator or looked-up number; how checked; that every item is a link the order names; at most 500 characters). It may be run again and then moves `verified_at`: items added since the last check are unverified until it is | `verified` |
| `order-issued` | `issued_at`, the order's own time stamp, from which a removal order's hold is counted (7.10). Taken at any time for an order that holds nothing. Refused for an instant after the receipt (the CHECK `legal_orders_issued`: a mistyped stamp cannot lengthen a hold) | `issued_at_set` |
| `order-texts` | one of `reasons`, `redress` and `scope` (the order's territorial scope in the authority's words, DSA Art. 9(5), at most 500 characters), read from standard input, with salt and digest; the audit row holds the digest. For an order with decisions whose author was told, it tells each author of the addition (step 3) | `texts_recorded` |
| `order-document` | `document_sha256`, `document_bytes`; the file itself is read, hashed and left where it was | `document_recorded` |
| `order-received`, `order-sent-at`, `order-due` | `received_at` of an order opened without a message; `sent_at`; a DSA order's `due_at` | `received_set`, `sent_at_set`, `due_set` |
| `order-scrutiny` | nothing on the row | `scrutiny_requested`; `scrutiny_decided` with the ground `upheld` or `annulled` |
| `order-reopen` | clears `closed_at`, as the admin's `reopened` event does in the console (7.8) | `reopened` |
| `order-tell-rule`, `order-told` | for a `dsa_art10`: when the person the order concerns may be told and on which basis; that the operator told them (7.14) | `tell_person_set`, `person_told` |

3. **The authority's words reach the author with a mail and a recorded time.** `order-texts` changes what the author's notice shows from then on (7.10). The author's own time limits run from receiving the reasons (the 48 hours of TCO Art. 4(4); the issuing state's court deadline), so a text that only appeared on a screen already read would cost them a remedy. For each decision of the order whose author was told, the command's transaction therefore sets `author_mail` to `pending` again, beside its event `texts_recorded`, whose time is the recorded instant: no column of its own holds it (section 6.3). The dispatch round mails `order_notice_added`, the mail of an `order` decision whose `author_mail_at` is already set, and the notice prints since when it holds the text (`orderTexts.textsAt`, 10.4: the event's time, or the decision's `informed_at` when that is later). A held notice needs no such mail: its release tells everything at once. For a `dsa_art9` order, whose notice owes the reasons, the redress and the territorial scope at the time of effect (DSA Art. 9(5)), the runbook puts `order-texts` before "Disable for an order" unless the order is urgent, so `ORDER_NOTICE.textsFollow` stands only for a removal order and for the urgent case. Reasons that are still missing once an order is given effect are counted in the daily `order_unverified` alert (7.2), so that "they follow" has an end.
4. `order-verify` ends the daily `order_unverified` alert for what it checked. An order found to be forged: `order-hold-end` first (7.10), then an admin's reinstatement with `not_authentic` (7.9).
5. **Scrutiny** (TCO Art. 4). For a cross-border `tco_removal` the order's screen shows `orders.scrutinyUntil` (`OrderView.owed.scrutinyUntil`; the home screen has no line for it, 10.2) until `scrutiny_requested` is recorded or 48 hours from receipt have passed. A decision that annuls is followed by 7.9 with the ground `scrutiny`; korbat reinstates "immediately" (Art. 4(7)), so that step is the console's and waits for no shell.

### 7.8 Declining

An order that is not addressed to korbat, not authentic, withdrawn or annulled before any effect is closed by an admin: the event `closed_without_effect` with its ground (7.5). The order's alerts end; its items stay as the record of what was named. Closing is refused while a measure of the order is in force (`order_event_refused`): that order ends by 7.9, item by item, and is then closed by the same event. An order whose items were all reinstated may be closed with the ground of the reinstatement.

**Reopening.** A closing can be undone: an order closed by mistake, or one a court restored after an appeal, is opened again by an admin with the event `reopened` (7.5), under the same step-up, or by the operator with `staff.js order-reopen`. `closed_at` is cleared, and the order has its items, events and clock as they were. So a closed record never blocks a true order: opening the order a second time answers `order_exists` with the closed record's id, and that record is reopened and, where it was typed wrongly, corrected (7.4).

Nobody but an admin declines: declining a true order is as grave as obeying a forged one. A moderator on call who doubts a removal order gives effect (the measure is reversible and preserves everything) or records Annex III; section 18's runbook says which. A `dsa_art9` order in doubt is not given effect at all until it is verified (7.6).

### 7.9 Reinstatement

1. The withdrawal, the annulment or the scrutiny decision arrives at the contact point and is alerted as any message; the admin verifies it as an order is verified, and closes the message with its disposition.
2. On the case of the item's decision the admin makes a correction: `POST /api/staff/cases/:case/decisions` (step-up `case_decided`) with kind `correction`, `reviewsDecisionId` the order decision, the outcome `reversed`, one action `{ kind: "reinstate_by_order", mediaId: null, orderGround }` and a saved reason, which the composer fills from `ORDER_DRAFTS.reinstate[orderGround]`. The decide route's list of kinds (E3 7.1) gains `reinstate_by_order`; it never holds `disable_by_order`, which only 7.6 writes.
3. The transaction is D 7.5's. `decision_actions_check` asks for a correction, an admin (`admin_required`) and a ground (`invalid_decision`); recusal is not asked of the correction of an order decision, as it is not asked of the order decision (section 6.3). `decision_actions_apply` lifts the measure, sets the item's `reinstated_at` and `preserve_ended_at`, inserts `reinstated` with the ground, and derives the level again. A post also removed under a korbat rule stays `removed`, shown to its author with that decision's chip once that decision is told (7.10).
4. korbat never lifts an order's measure on its own view of the content: the five grounds are facts about the order (`withdrawn`, `annulled`, `scrutiny`, `not_authentic`) or about korbat's own mistake (`wrong_item`).
5. **Told.** `decisions_record` makes the correction of an untold decision untold too. It takes the corrected order decision's row `FOR SHARE` and reads it again in a statement of its own, so a correction that commits beside a release either waits for the release and is told at once, or the release waits for it and its third step tells it (7.10): no reinstatement stays untold behind a notice that was told. So:
   - the author was told of the disabling: the correction's statement is mailed at once (`order_reinstated_notice`) and says why the disabling ended, in the fixed sentence of its ground;
   - the notice is still held: nothing is told until the release tells both (7.10);
   - `not_authentic`: the operator first runs `order-hold-end` with the reason `not_authentic`, so the author learns at once what happened and for how long.
6. After the commit: `staffAlert` with `order_reinstated`.
7. What a reinstatement cannot bring back: content erased at the preservation's end (7.11). The notice says so beforehand.
8. A reinstated item can be given effect again, when a court restores the order after an appeal: the order is open or reopened (7.8), and "Disable for an order" writes a new decision on the item (7.6). That is a new disabling, with six months and a day of preservation from its own date.
9. A reinstatement while the notice is still held leaves the hold as it is: whether an order that was withdrawn may now be told is the authority's to say, and the operator ends the hold with `order-hold-end` when it says so. Until then a decision of korbat's own about the same post that is held with the notice stays held (7.10), and section 11.3 names what the author sees in that time.

### 7.10 The author's notice: told at once, held, released

**Which of the two** is decided once, in `decisions_record`, from the order's row at the decision's time:

| The order | `informed_at` | `author_mail` | The author |
|---|---|---|---|
| no Section C, or its hold already ended | `decided_at` | `pending` | is mailed `order_notice` after the commit and finds the notice on the Cases screen |
| Section C, and `order_hold_until(order) > now()` | null | `none` | is told nothing, by any path (below) |

A tick in the console holds at once, before the command line has recorded the order's time stamp or the verification (section 2, row 25), and the form's "I cannot tell" holds like a tick (7.4). A hold always comes with a post its author cannot see: the database forces `withhold_from_author` for every order with Section C, so the level under a hold is `disabled` for every kind of order, and the signed params cannot say otherwise (10.3).

**The end of a hold, by the kind of order** (`order_hold_until`, section 6; `OrderView.hold.basis` says which, and the console prints it):

| Kind | The end | Lengthened by |
|---|---|---|
| `tco_removal` (`basis` `regulation`) | the end the database computes: `order_hold_end` from the order's own day, else the receipt, else the opening, 43 days counted so that it is never early in any zone of the EU's European territory (section 3, LAB E); an end the authority wrote counts when it is earlier | the Regulation's one extension, 42 days (TCO Art. 11(3)), and nothing else |
| `dsa_art9` (`basis` `authority`); a `dsa_art10` has no notice of the software, and the same rule is the operator's record of when its person may be told (7.14) | what the issuing authority states, "at the time provided by the issuing authority" (DSA Art. 9(5), 10(5)): the instant `order-hold-until` records with its basis, of any length, earlier or later than six weeks. While the authority has named none the hold has no end (`hold.open`): the Regulation's weeks do not cap it | `order-hold-until`, again |

A hold without an end is ended only by a recorded end or by `order-hold-end`; `OrderView.owed.holdUntil` lists it and the daily `order_unverified` alert counts it while it lasts (7.2), since a forgotten release is a silent removal.

**Somebody is warned before any release.** From 7 days before a hold's end (`ORDER_RULES.holdWarningDays`) `alert_steps()` writes `hold_ending` daily to every staff account, with the count of held notices and the first end: whoever has the server checks the mailbox for an extension. One that arrived is recorded before the release can overtake it: for a removal order by an admin in the console at once (the event `hold_extended`, from the picked message, 7.5) or by `order-hold-extend`; for a DSA order by `order-hold-until`. The design accepts being a day late so as never to be early (section 2, rows 24 and 46), and this is what keeps a weekend from making it early.

**Other decisions about the same post are held with the notice.** A decision of korbat's own on a post that an order disabled would tell its author of the order in 3c's built words: its `remove` has `no_effect` `already`, and the statement prints "Another decision already keeps this post from being shown" (`packages/rules/src/statement.ts:50`); a review that lifts an earlier removal prints "If your post or image is still restricted, another decision listed in Settings, Cases applies" (`:62`), which is false while none is listed. So `decisions_record` holds every decision, of any kind, whose case's target post has an `order` decision that is untold and whose measure is in force: `informed_at` null, `author_mail` `none`, as for the correction of an untold order decision. The reporter's or notifier's own line moves as today, since it names no order. The release tells these decisions with the notice (below), and each one's time to appeal counts from the day it is told, as `appeal_deadline(informed_at)` counts for any decision. The cost is section 2's (row 47): an appeal that is answered during a hold is answered to its author only at the release.

**The notice** is no statement of reasons (DSA Art. 17(5)). `statementParagraphs` renders an `order` decision from `ORDER_NOTICE` (10.7), by the kind of order, from the decision's data and the order's row as it stands:

- the act, the authority the order names, its state, the day of the order and its reference. Until `order-verify` has run, korbat knows only that a message named that authority, and the lead says exactly that; once it has, the notice adds `ORDER_NOTICE.lead.confirmed` (`DecisionStatement.order.verified`, 10.4). A person is told nothing korbat does not know;
- that korbat did not assess the post under its own rules (`ORDER_NOTICE.notAssessed`, by the kind of order: only a removal order's says that the law leaves korbat no choice); that korbat's appeal does not apply;
- the authority's reasons and remedies and, for a `dsa_art9` order, the order's territorial scope, each in the authority's own words under a label of its own, once `order-texts` recorded them, with the time since when the notice holds them when they were added after the author was told (`ORDER_NOTICE.textsSince`, 7.7); until then `ORDER_NOTICE.textsFollow` (they follow, and a copy of the order can be asked for at the address under Contact in korbat's Terms);
- for a `dsa_art9` order, after the authority's own scope, `ORDER_NOTICE.everywhere` (korbat has no geography: whatever territory the order names, the post is disabled for everyone);
- for a notice that was held, `ORDER_NOTICE.heldUntilNow`, by the kind of order, with the two times that are facts, since when the post has been disabled and when korbat tells (`disabledAt`, `informedAt`); when the hold was ended as `not_ordered`, `ORDER_NOTICE.heldUntilNow.notOrdered` in its place, which says that the silence was korbat's own mistake; and a `dsa_art9` order's paragraph (`heldUntilNow.dsa_art9`) ends with the sentence that the post is kept from its author too for as long as the measure stands, because the order came with that statement;
- for a `tco_removal`, the preservation and its end (`ORDER_NOTICE.preserved`, and after the erasure `ORDER_NOTICE.erased` with the day).

No text of the notice or of a mail prints an address: it names "the address under Contact in korbat's Terms", so `statementParagraphs` needs no setting and the API none for it.

The post itself is never shown while it is disabled, on the Cases screen as elsewhere: `DecisionView.content` is null for an `order` decision whose measure is in force (10.4).

**What every reader does with a disabled post.** Each row is a change of Task 3, and the route walk of section 12 runs as the author:

| Reader (today's code) | Change |
|---|---|
| `postAccess` (`social/visibility.ts:20`) | `StoredPostState` gains `disabled`; its case answers `hidden` for every viewer, the author included. The switch does not compile until it does |
| the lists' SQL fragment (`social/repo.ts:358`) | none: it shows the author only `withheld` and `removed`, so a `disabled` post is in no list of anyone's. A test pins the fragment's two states |
| `serve` (`media/flows/serve.ts:35`) | none: `isShown` refuses a post state it was not taught, for the author too. Every image of the post answers 404 |
| `reach`'s `served` (`media/repo.ts:363`) | gains `p.state <> 'disabled'` |
| `chipFacts` (`moderation/author-repo.ts:175`) | it names the levels `withheld` and `removed`, so a disabled post has no chip; and it takes the fragment `toldToAuthor(d)` below, which it lacks today (`:175` joins `measures_in_force` to `decisions` with no test of whether the decision was told): a chip names a measure only of a decision its author was told of. A `dsa_art9` order that does not withhold gives the level `removed`, and the chip's measures gain `disable_by_order` so that it names the order's decision; by 7.4 such an order never holds its notice |
| `authorDecisions`, `authorDecision` (`author-repo.ts:71`, `:131`), `chipFacts`, the export's cases, `access-request` | one fragment, `toldToAuthor(d)`: `d.author_mail <> 'none' and d.informed_at is not null`, in this form in sections 5 and 6 too. Today's reads test the first half only (`:86`, `:170`); the second is added so that no later change of the mail's column can show a held decision. The first half must stay: 3c sets `informed_at` on every decision, also on those whose author is told nothing (a report answered with "no breach"), and the second half alone would list them. The architecture test pins the fragment, with both halves, as the only read of `decisions` on the author's side |
| `access-request` (D 7.13), the operator's own draft at the shell | leaves an untold decision and its post out of what is sent, and marks the case for the operator in the place of its lane-0 mark: `access.noticeHeld`, with the hold's end or "open". The operator leaves the order out of the answer and notes the reason in their own records, never in the answer (in Germany BDSG s. 34(2) asks that the reasons are documented). The mark tells the author nothing: only the operator reads the draft |
| the author's screen of a decision told before the order (`authorDecision`'s `snapshot_withheld`, `contentOf` at `moderation/author.ts:79`) | none, and that is the point: `snapshot_withheld` gains no preservation arm, so the content line of an earlier decision about the post reads as it did before the order, and never as `r4Redacted` ("Content erased when the R4 decision became final") for a post that is preserved |
| the images of the post on that screen (an earlier decision about the same post, whose view names them as before) | none in the API, and no branch in the app: `serve` answers 404 for every image of a post that an order disabled, to anyone, the author included (the row of `serve`, above). The app draws each exactly as it draws an image that could not be loaded, in the words it already has for that case and no other: no placeholder of 3f's and no reason, so nothing on the screen says why while the notice is held (section 9.2; section 2, row 47, flagged for counsel) |
| a decision of korbat's own about the post, taken while the notice is held | held with the notice (`decisions_record`, above): no mail, no Cases row, no chip, until the release |
| an appeal of an earlier decision about the post, sent during the hold | accepted as always, and its answer names nothing of the order; the review that answers it is held with the notice |
| the export's posts and files (`social/flows/export.ts:77`) | leave out a post whose state is `disabled`, and its images |
| `deletePost` (`social/repo.ts:517`) | answers `not_found` for a `disabled` post, as for a post that does not exist; `posts_guard` refuses the update for every role but the migrator in any case |
| the profile's counters | do not move at a disabling or a reinstatement, as at a removal |
| a reporter's or notifier's line (`item_current_outcome`) | an order's intake item is no user's item; an `order` decision answers its own item alone, so no other item's line moves with it |
| the mail `account_deleted` (`mail/templates.ts:163`) | one sentence for every account, since a variant for this account alone would tell (7.19) |
| `text-verify` (D 7.13) | recomputes the level `disabled`, and checks every `order` decision against its `order_effected` row and the `order_corrected` rows since, as it checks the others against `case_decided`; it reports every console event and every gap closed as `not_needed` that has no audit row |
| the case page (D 7.3) | shows the measure with its order; no image and no snapshot text while the post is preserved: gate V for the images, and for the text the new predicate `post_preserved(post)` in the staff snapshot read (`moderation/repo.ts:696`), which the author's side never reads. `snapshot_withheld` keeps its one meaning, "this copy's content was erased" |

So under a hold the author's own view is the public's: the post is absent from the lists, its page and images answer as missing, and there is no chip, case, mail or export line, no changed word on an earlier decision's screen (whose images of the post are drawn as images that could not be loaded, like every image of the post anywhere), no statement of another decision about the post, and nothing in what an access request is answered with (section 2, row 26). The route walk of section 12 runs each row as the author under a held `tco_removal` and under a held `dsa_art9`, on a post that has a decision told before the order and one taken during the hold, and with a 3c decision that told its author nothing.

**The release.** The dispatch round (`moderation/dispatch.ts:380`) gains a step `order-notices`, in a try of its own, before step 2 (the statements):

1. one transaction: `select order_notices_release()`, then, when it answered more than zero, the audit row `order_notices_released` (system actor; metadata `{ count }`);
2. the function, in three statements: (a) the rows of the orders that have an untold `order` decision, `FOR SHARE` in id order; (b) the untold order decisions whose order holds nothing or whose `order_hold_until(order) <= clock_timestamp()`, `FOR UPDATE` in id order: each gets `informed_at = clock_timestamp()`, read after the test, so the stored time is never before the end, and `author_mail = 'pending'`, and its item the event `notice_released`; (c) in every call, also when (b) told nothing: every untold decision that is not an `order` decision (a correction of one, or a decision of korbat's own that was held with it), about a post that has no untold `order` decision any more, gets the same two values; a decision of korbat's own thus waits for every `order` decision about its post, also one whose measure was lifted meanwhile (7.9, 11.3). The answer counts (b) and (c). Because (c) runs in every call and the correction reads its order decision under a share lock (7.9), a reinstatement that commits beside a release is told by that round or the next, never left behind;
3. step 2 of the same round then mails them (`order_notice`; for a correction `order_reinstated_notice`; for a decision of korbat's own the mail it would have had), and gives up after `RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays` as for any statement. A notice released to an account that was deleted during the hold is set to `pending` like any other and given up the same way, since `mailAccount` has no address for it; the release's test covers that case.

A round runs every ten minutes, so a notice leaves at most about ten minutes after its hold's end and never before it: the end is compared with the database's clock inside the function, and `order_hold_end` is never early in any zone of the EU's European territory (section 3, LAB E).

**The hold's commands** (as the migrator; each inserts its event and ends with `order_recorded`):

| Command | Does | Refused |
|---|---|---|
| `order-issued` | sets `issued_at`; a removal order's computed end moves to the order's own day, which is never later than the end computed from the receipt (the CHECK refuses an `issued_at` after the receipt). While a notice of the order is held, an instant whose computed end is not in the future would release the notice within minutes, which cannot be taken back: the command prints that end and changes nothing unless `--release` is given | `already_released`, only for an order with Section C whose notice was released (an event `notice_released` exists) or whose hold was ended. An order that holds nothing takes the command at any time |
| `order-hold-until` | sets `authority_hold_until`, the end the authority stated, with `hold_basis`, the provision or the order's sentence it rests on. For a removal order it counts when earlier than the computed one; for a DSA order it is the hold's end, of any length, and `none` in the instant's place records that the authority named no time. An instant that is not in the future changes nothing unless `--release` is given, as for `order-issued` | `already_released`, by the same rule |
| `order-hold-extend` | a removal order's one extension: sets `hold_extended`, 42 more days, once (TCO Art. 11(3)), on the authority's reasoned decision; it also confirms an extension that an admin recorded in the console (7.5) | a second time: `already_extended`; after the release: `already_released`; for a DSA order, whose later time is `order-hold-until`'s |
| `order-hold-end` | sets `hold_ended_at` = now with `hold_end_reason` (`not_authentic`, `annulled`, `released_by_authority`, or `not_ordered`: korbat's own mistake, the order held no such statement and the record said it had); the next round releases. It also undoes a console extension that turns out not to be the authority's, though never before the day the first period ended, since that would tell early | when no notice is held: `nothing_held` |

`already_released` asks whether a notice was released, not whether any decision is told: an order without Section C tells each decision at once, and its time stamp must still be recordable the next day. Nothing lengthens a removal order's hold but the one extension; a DSA order's hold is as long as its authority states; and nothing but the database's clock ends a hold silently, after seven days of warning.

**An author writes about a notice.** The notice asks its reader to write from the email address of their korbat account. The operator looks an order up only when the mail names a notice the app has shown (it quotes the reference or the notice's day, which a held notice never shows); for everything else the runbook's fixed answer stays (section 18). Before a copy of the order or the preserved text of `order-access --purpose complaint` (7.11) goes out, `staff.js address-check --handle <handle> < address` answers whether the address read from standard input is that account's: it prints yes or no and no address, and ends with the audit row `address_checked` (system actor; target the account; metadata `{ match }`). The copy goes to that address only. "The order does not name this post" is an admin's to check against the order in the mailbox, and ends in a reinstatement with `wrong_item` (7.9) or an answer why not, within the time the runbook states.

### 7.11 Preservation: extended, ended, opened

1. **Its start** is the disabling (7.6): `preserve_until` = `order_preserve_until` of the disabling's time, six calendar months and one day in UTC, for a `tco_removal` only. A disabling after an earlier preservation of the item ended, by a reinstatement or by time, starts the six months anew from its own date, never from the first (TCO Art. 6(2) counts from "the removal or disabling"); what the earlier end erased stays erased. A correction that makes the order a `tco_removal` starts the preservation of its items in force from their decisions' times, and one that makes it another kind ends it without erasing anything (7.4). From the start `post_preserved(post)` is true and with it `frozen_for`, whose fourth arm it is, so gates K, E and V (D 6) keep the post's image rows, its snapshots and its text against the author's deletion, the account's purge, the R4 redaction and every retention step, and open no image of it for staff; the staff case page shows no snapshot text of it, by the same predicate in its own read (7.10). `snapshot_withheld` is not changed: it is the author's side's predicate too.
2. **Extended.** `staff.js order-preserve-extend` records a request of an authority or a court (who asked, their reference, the proceedings, when it was received, the further end): it moves `preserve_until` forward, never back, inserts `preservation_extended` and ends with `order_recorded`. It is refused once the preservation ended (`not_preserved`).
3. **Ended by a reinstatement:** 7.9 sets `preserve_ended_at`; the post is the author's again and nothing is erased.
4. **Ended by time.** The retention job (D 7.9) gains the step `order_preservation`: for each item with `preserve_until <= now()` and no end, one transaction that calls `order_preservation_end(item)` and ends with a `moderation_retention` audit row (metadata: the step, the item, whether content was erased). Under its locks, taken in section 5's order (the case of the item's decision, the item's row, the post, the post's image rows), the function sets `preserve_ended_at` and, while the item's measure stands, blanks the post's body, erases its snapshots, marks its images erased, derives the state again and inserts `preservation_ended`. The image files then go the way every erased image's do (D 7.9). `not_due` (false) writes nothing.
5. From then the notice reads `ORDER_NOTICE.erased` with the day, and a later reinstatement restores an empty post. `MODERATION_RETENTION=off` (a restore, 7.17) holds this step with the others.
6. **Opened.** `staff.js order-access <item> --purpose <review|complaint|authority>` prints the preserved snapshot's text, alt texts and the images' ids, pixel sizes and creation times (`post_media` holds no digest), never an image, and ends with the audit row `preserved_accessed` (metadata: the item, the purpose, the counts) and, for `authority`, a production record (7.14). It reads the image rows through `preservedImages(tx, postId)`, a read-only function the media module's index gains for it: no file of the command line names `post_media`, which the architecture test forbids outside that module. For `complaint` the text goes to the author's own address only, after `address-check` (7.10). No console screen and no API route opens preserved content (TCO Art. 6(3)).
7. A `dsa_art9` order preserves nothing: its item has no `preserve_until`, and the post follows 3c's rules for a removed post.

### 7.12 The status of a post's page

`GET /api/posts/:id/status` is a small router of its own in the social module, `social.statusRoutes`, built over the repository's read and mounted in `app.ts` after the global limit and before `auth.resolveSession`, as `media.fileRoutes` is mounted ahead of both (`app.ts:67`). The module's other routes are mounted after the session's resolution (`app.ts:69`, `:81`), and Caddy's question carries the visitor's cookies, so a route among them would read a session for every page view; this one reads none. It parses the id itself, since `validate` would answer a malformed id with a JSON body. It answers 204 when a post with this id has the state `visible`, and 404 with an empty body in every other case (no such post, a malformed id, `deleted_by_author`, `withheld`, `removed`, `disabled`). It answers the same for everyone, so it tells nothing that a signed-out read of the post does not, and never 451. No interface gains a member for it: nothing outside the social module calls it.

Caddy asks it for every request of `/post/*` on the app's site and serves the app's shell under the status it got (200 for 204, 404 for 404); when the API does not answer, or answers anything else (a 429 of the global limit included), the shell goes out with 200, as today. The question carries the visitor's address as every proxied request does, so the global limit counts it against the visitor. The shell is the same bytes in every case, with the page's security headers in each: only the status line differs, which is what an authority's checker reads. Nobody has tried this on the pinned Caddy: Task 4's first step does (section 13), and if the image's own directives cannot do it, the page status is not built and section 2's row 17 goes back to the owner.

### 7.13 A notification to an authority and its record

The API's role holds no grant on `authority_notifications`, its events or `productions`. Every step below but the first is a command as the migrator, one transaction, ending with the audit row `notification_recorded` (10.5).

1. **Owed.** A `seal_media` row, taken while deciding or for good, fires `report_open()`. It first takes the account's row of `auth.report_holds` `FOR UPDATE`, inserting it as released when there is none, and reads the records in a statement of its own; `notification-sent` and `notification-declined` take the same row first. So a second seal that arrives while the first record is being recorded as sent waits, finds no record owed and opens its own: no seal is left without an owed record and a hold. When the subject account has no record in state `owed`, the function inserts one (the case, the seal's action, the account, awareness = the first suspect item's time; no duty yet: `duties` is empty while a seal's record is only owed, `reports_owed()` answers `sealed_images` for it, and `notification-sent` records the duties met) and sets the account's hold, with whether the seal is for good (which the deletion mail's sentence reads, 7.15). Nothing is mailed to anyone.
2. **Seen.** `reports_owed()` gives the console a case, a kind and a time, nothing else: `StaffOverview.reportsOwed` (10.2), the home banner and the line on the case page. After 24 hours `alert_steps()` writes `report_owed` daily.
3. **The admin answers the suspect items** (D 7.5).
   - Without a seal in force for the account: `decisions_apply` calls `report_close_unsealed(case)`; the record becomes `declined` with the reason `unsealed`, and the hold is released. The check that ended in "no" stays as a record.
   - With a seal for good: the record stays `owed` until the operator has reported. Where the admin's answer first ended a seal taken while deciding and `report_close_unsealed` closed the record in the same transaction, the seal for good sets that record `owed` again, and its hold unreleased, with their first times (section 6.5).
   - A seal lifted later, by an admin's correction or by a review on the author's appeal (`unseal_media`), ends with no final decision: the same trigger that opens a record calls `report_close_unsealed` for that row too, after the states are derived. A seal that korbat itself found mistaken leaves no record owed, no daily alert, no banner and no address held.
4. **Reporting.** On the server:
   1. `staff.js notification-facts <record>` prints the account's handle, address and creation time and the sealed posts' ids and times, and opens a production record of the instrument `notification` for what it printed (7.14). It replaces 3c's `account-facts`, and refuses a record that is not `owed`;
   2. the operator reports through the state's intake (`STATE_FACTS[HOME_STATE].notificationIntake`), by hand;
   3. `staff.js notification-sent <record>` records the duties met (one notification may meet several), the state concerned and its basis or "not identifiable" with the reason, the authority, the channel, when it was sent and the channel's receipt, korbat's reference, what was sent by kind (of `NOTIFICATION_SENT_KINDS`, 10.1; never a value), the fixed sentence of what korbat does not keep, and who sent it. The record becomes `sent`; the hold is released;
   4. `staff.js notification-answer <record>` records the authority's reference, its answer (one word of `NOTIFICATION_ANSWERS`, 10.1: a closed list, never a sentence about a person) and the day. 3d's release of sealed images reads these two through `seal_report(action)`.
5. **A check on other content.** `staff.js notification-open --case <id> --duty <dsa18_1|dsa18_2|tco14_5|voluntary>` opens a record by hand; `notification-declined <record> --reason <text>` closes one whose check ended in "no". Neither sets a hold: only a seal does.
6. **Later.** A request or an answer of the authority about the same matter is `staff.js notification-event <record>`, with its mark `not_to_person` and that mark's end, which may be open. While any event of a record carries the mark and its end is ahead or open, `staff.js access-request` (D 7.13) prints for the record's case, in place of its lane-0 mark, `access.authorityHold`: an authority asked that the person is not told, with the day it ends or "open". The record is kept for as long as such a mark runs, whatever its age (7.18): the mark is read from the record, and without it the next answer to that person would tell.
7. `staff.js records` lists the owed and the open records and the productions not yet destroyed; it reads only and writes no audit row.

Whether the sealed files go with the notification is not a rule of the software: it is a field of the hand-over (7.14), and the runbook says which reading the operator's counsel confirmed (section 2).

### 7.14 Productions and the hand-over of sealed files

One row of `productions` for every time something leaves korbat for an authority. Each command is as the migrator and ends with the audit row `production_recorded` (10.5). A recipient is an authority; the commands refuse nothing on that, the record names who.

**Account data** (an order to provide information, or a notification's facts). `staff.js production-account <order> --handle <handle> --asked <kind,...>` prints the three facts korbat gives of the account (handle, address, creation time; section 18 fixes the list) and the fixed sentences of what it does not hold and of what it holds and has not produced here (the times and device types of the account's sign-ins, which korbat gives only when an order names them and counsel says the law requires it), and records the production with the instrument `dsa_art10`, the authority, both references, what the order asked for by kind (`--asked`, a list of the seven kinds of `NOTIFICATION_SENT_KINDS`, 10.1, and never a text: `productions.asked` holds no sentence about a person) and the kinds produced. The operator sends the answer by the channel the order names and records `receipt_confirmed` (7.5).

**Telling the person an order to provide information names** (DSA Art. 10(5)). The EU-level rule is that the person is told, with the authority's reasons and the redress that exists, at the latest when effect is given or at the time the authority states; national law may delay or forbid it. So the order's record says which applies before anything is produced: `staff.js order-tell-rule <order> --when <at_effect|never|instant> --basis <text>` records when the person may be told (`tell_person`: `at_effect`, `at_instant` with its instant, or `never`) and the provision or the order's sentence it rests on (`hold_basis`), with the event `tell_person_set`; `STATE_FACTS[HOME_STATE].tellPersonDefault` carries the home state's default, which the command prints. `production-account` refuses (`tell_rule_missing`) until it is recorded, and `OrderView.owed.tellPerson` lists it. For `at_effect` and `at_instant` the operator tells the person by hand, from the contact address to the account's address, with the authority's reasons and remedies, and records it with `staff.js order-told <order>` (the event `person_told`, refused while the rule is `never` or its instant is ahead). korbat's software sends no such mail: an order to provide information has no decision and no notice. For a `dsa_art9` order the same fact is its Section C and its hold (7.10).

**Sealed files**, in two parts, so that the database's most privileged role never mounts the image store and the program that reads files has no network:

1. `staff.js handover-list <seal action> --instrument <notification|demand|court_order|seizure> --authority <name> --reference <text>` opens the production and prints one line a file: the store's name of every stored size of every image row of the posts in `seal_posts` for that seal. It reads them through `sealFiles(tx, sealActionId)`, a read-only function the media module's index gains for it (a post's image rows' stored names and their count), and refuses (`handover_mismatch`, exit 1, nothing written) when a post's image rows are not as many as its `seal_posts.images`.
2. `docker compose --profile handover run --rm -T -v <directory>:/handover handover < list > digests`. The service has no network and the store mounted read-only; the directory for the archive is an argument of the command, not a setting, so that an installation that never made a hand-over needs none, and it is owned by uid 1000 on an encrypted volume (section 5). The store's naming rule is one pure function exported by the media module; the program imports no module and keeps its own copy, and a unit test feeds both the same names and fails when they differ. It refuses a name outside the store's layout, copies each listed file byte for byte into one zip of stored entries (`handover-<production>.zip`), and prints each name, size and SHA-256 and the archive's. It decodes nothing and shows nothing. A missing file ends it with exit 1 and no archive.
3. The operator delivers the archive by the channel the authority names.
4. `staff.js handover-record <production> --channel <text> --receipt <text> < digests` stores the files' names, sizes and digests, the archive's digest, the channel and the receipt. It refuses digests whose names are not exactly the list of step 1.
5. After deleting every copy: `staff.js handover-destroyed <production> --how <text>`. A production with an archive and no `destroyed_at` after 7 days raises `handover_undestroyed` daily.
6. With `seizure` there is no archive: the record says that the authority took the files, and which.

No seal is lifted and no state changes: the images stay sealed for everyone. When 3d moves sealed files to a quarantine volume, step 2 mounts that volume.

### 7.15 A deletion while a report is owed

The hold is on by default and can be switched off with `REPORT_HOLD=off` (section 2, row 35); the setting is read in the auth module alone, at the two places below.

1. The person confirms the deletion as always (`auth/flows/deletion.ts:66`). `takeDown` runs: the account is gone for everyone, its sessions end.
2. The mail `account_deleted` gains one sentence when the address is held. The auth module, which writes the mail, cannot read `auth.report_holds`; it asks two functions of section 6: `auth.address_held_until(account)`, the instant the hold ends at the latest (`set_at` + 7 days) or null, whose day the sentence prints, and `auth.address_hold_final(account)`, whether the seal that holds the record is for good. The sentence has two variants, so that it says no more than the statement the person already received said:
   - `account_deleted.held.final`, after a seal for good, whose statement told the person that the images are reported to the police: that korbat has sealed images of the account and keeps the address because the law may oblige it to report them;
   - `account_deleted.held.deciding`, after a seal taken while deciding, whose statement said only that korbat hides the images until an admin has answered a report: that korbat keeps the address, and nothing else of the account, until that is settled, because the law may oblige it to keep it for that time. No word of a report to an authority: nobody has judged anything yet.

   Both say what GDPR Art. 12(4) asks of an answer to a request that is not carried out in full: the reason, until when at the latest, and the three routes (to write to korbat, to complain to a data protection authority, to go to court). Neither offers a right to "object", which the ground the hold rests on (Art. 6(1)(c), Art. 17(3)(b)) may not give (section 2, row 35, for counsel). And whatever is held, the mail's first sentence never says that everything is gone (7.19).
3. The purge runs at once and purges the profile, the posts and the images as today (`account/flows/deletion.ts:21`); what a freeze keeps, it keeps (D 7.10).
4. `finishPurge` (`auth/repo.ts:511`) becomes two statements for a row that is deleted and not purged:
   1. every credential and personal fact but the address is cleared, without condition (the password hash, the TOTP secret, the recovery codes, the phone hash, the age and terms marks);
   2. `email = 'deleted:<id>'` and `purged_at = now()` only `where not auth.address_held(id)`.
   One UPDATE with the condition on all of it, as the model's 3.4 words it, would keep the credentials for seven days; the hold is for the address alone. Sections 5 and 6 say the same two statements.
5. While the address is held the account stays in `pendingPurges()`, which orders held rows last so that they never crowd out the round's 100. Each round runs steps 3 and 4 again, which change nothing; `finishPurge` answers `held` for it, and `purgePending` counts it under `held` (`PurgeReport.held`), not `purged`.
6. The hold ends when the record is sent or declined (7.13), when the seal is lifted and nothing else is owed (7.13 step 3), or 7 days after `set_at` by the clock inside `auth.address_held_until`, of which `auth.address_held` is "that is not null". The next purge round, within five minutes, overwrites the address.
7. Nothing reads the held address but `notification-facts` and `production-account`, as the migrator.
8. A termination's take-down (E3 7.8) reaches `finishPurge` the same way and is held the same way.

An order to provide information needs no hold: its production is made when it is recorded.

### 7.16 The daily copy

1. On the daily visit the keeper opens Audit, notes the head as before (3a, README "The kept head") and presses `audit.download`. The console knows where to start from `AuditPage.copy.lastPlace`, the caller's own newest copied place (null before the first copy, which starts after place 0), and where to end from the head the page shows. When nothing is newer it says so and asks for no key.
2. **The download is a confirmed action**: the step-up `audit_exported` with `AuditExportedParams` `{ after, through }` (10.3), `after` the last copied place and `through` the head's place as the page showed it, at most `after` + `AUDIT_EXPORT_MAX` (50,000), so a long log is taken in several downloads. A line carries its row's metadata, which the audit page never shows (`staff/audit.ts:66`): each order's authority and reference, held ones included, and every decision's signed params. The import of 7.17 needs it, and the chain's hash cannot be checked without it. So the download is more than the page, and a session without its key (an unlocked phone, which is exactly the on-call device this design adds) cannot take it.
3. `POST /api/staff/audit/export` with the params and `stepUp`. One transaction holds `append_confirmed_audit` alone (action `audit_exported`, target `audit`, metadata `{ params }`): a key's confirmation is used once (its counter moves, `auth/staff/step-up.ts:135`), so the action that used it has its row, as every confirmed action has, and that row says who took which places also when the file never arrives. Then the API streams the rows with places above `after` up to `through`, oldest first, one JSON object a line (`AuditExportLine`, 10.5), as `application/x-ndjson` with `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and the file name `korbat-audit-<from>-<to>.jsonl`. It writes no mark. After the commit, when anyone but the last downloader takes a copy (the route's test: the caller is not the staff member who holds the newest mark), every staff account is alerted, `staffAlert` with `copy_taken`, as section 6.6 says: the keeper's stand-in is expected, anybody else is a key in the wrong hands.
4. **The mark is the keeper's confirmation, not the stream's end.** Between the API's last write and the keeper stand Caddy, the tunnel and a browser that must hold the whole body before it can save it. When the console holds the body it checks that the lines run from `after` + 1 to `through` without a break, saves the file, and only then posts `POST /api/staff/audit/export/mark` with `{ through, hash }`, the last line's place and hash. One transaction: the row at that place holds that hash, and the caller's own newest `audit_exported` row signed that `through` (409 `copy_mismatch` otherwise); then the caller's `copy_marks` row and the audit row `copy_marked` (metadata `{ after, through, rows }`). It needs no step-up: it shows nothing, and it can only say "I hold it" of a file a key released, so a session without a key can neither take the file nor quiet `copy_overdue`. A download that broke off on the way leaves no mark: `copy_overdue` stays as it was, the next download starts at the same place, and the keeper's files have no silent hole.
5. `staff.js audit-export [--after <place>]` prints the same lines for the same rows and writes its mark itself, with `copy_marked`, since its output goes to a file on the server and no browser has to confirm it. The mark carries the system actor's id (`SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID`; `copy_marks.staff_id` is never null), which no staff member's "newest mark" matches.
6. The copy's age is `alert_steps()`'s alone: `copy_overdue`, daily while the newest mark is older than 48 hours (7.2). The dispatch round raises nothing for it.

What a line's metadata may hold is bound in 10.5, and 11.3 says what the keeper's file tells whoever finds it.

### 7.17 A restore, and what is applied again

1. **As the README's Restoring:** the database and `media-store` from the same moment; `MODERATION_RETENTION=off`, `MEDIA_RECONCILE=off`, `APP_SITE=closed` (the app's site answers 503 to every path; the console's site is unchanged); the API stopped; `audit-head`; `revoke`; `grant-role`.
2. **`staff.js restore-import [<place>:<hash> ...] < <the keeper's files, oldest first>`**, as the migrator, in one transaction. The files reach the command concatenated on standard input, through `docker compose -f infra/compose.yml run --rm -T migrate node dist/staff.js restore-import ...` (`-T`: without it Compose allocates a terminal and the redirect fails): every file from the one that holds the restored head's place onward. A keeper's file covers the places between two visits and a backup is taken at its own hour, so the restored head lies inside a file and never at its edge.
   1. It reads the restored head and every line of the input. **Lines at or below the head** are not imported, and each is checked: its hash recomputes from its own members (`staff/chain.ts:45`, `:69`), it names the line before it, and the line at the head's place holds the head's hash, which ties the files to this database more strongly than a `prevHash` alone. **The first line above the head** must be the head's plus one with `prevHash` equal to the head's hash; input that begins above that place is refused (`tail_not_continuous`, exit 1, nothing written), since the lost rows cannot be downloaded again. **A changed file refuses everything** (`tail_not_continuous`): a line whose own hash does not recompute, a line at the head's place with another hash, or a kept place given as an argument that does not hold the hash the keeper noted, as `audit-verify` checks kept places. **A hole is reported, not fatal:** the import takes the unbroken run that starts at the head and stops at the first line that does not name its predecessor, prints "the files hold nothing between place <a> and place <b>: what happened there is not imported", exits 0, and records the two places in the `restore_imported` row's metadata. What lies behind a hole is the mailbox's and the operator's notes' to restore, as everything after the last download is;
   2. for each lost row that describes a plain record, it inserts the record again with its original times and `imported_at`, and writes its gap closed with the disposition `imported`: an order (`order_opened`, through `insertOrder`), its items (`order_items_added`), its corrections (`order_corrected`, applied in their order), its console events (`order_event_recorded`, the closing, the reopening and the console's extension among them), its command-line facts (`order_recorded`), and the skeleton of a notification record (`notification_recorded` of the steps `opened`, `sent`, `declined`: 10.5). An imported order keeps its `received_at`, `sent_at`, `issued_at`, hold and `preserve_until`; its `message_id` only when the restored database holds the message. An audit row holds no link's text (10.5): an imported item's `link` is its post's address when the lost row named a post, and `sha256:<digest>` when it named none, and the operator reads the address from the order in the mailbox;
   3. an item's imported `preserve_until` freezes its post at once, before any measure is back (LAB F);
   4. for every lost decision (each decision of a `case_decided`, an `appeals_reviewed` and an `order_effected` row) it writes one row of `restore_gaps` with the lost row's place, hash, action, target, metadata and time, and the class of the table below. It writes no row of `decisions` or `decision_actions`;
   5. for every lost row it could only note (a later step of a notification record, a production, 10.5) it writes a gap of class `record`, which stays open until the operator has entered the record again (step 3);
   6. last, the audit row `restore_imported` (metadata: the first and the last place, the counts by class, the places around each hole, the SHA-256 of each file) and, on standard output, the gaps in the table's order and each hole's warning.
3. The operator compares the mailbox since the last copy with the imported orders and opens what is missing with `order-open --received <instant>`. Before any order's disabling is taken again, the operator compares each imported hold with the mailbox and their notes since the keeper's last download: a lost `order-hold-extend`, `order-hold-until` or console extension would make the decision taken again tell at once, so it is recorded first. The operator also enters again, from the authority's letters and their own notes, the records of the gaps of class `record`, with the same notification and production commands as 7.13 and 7.14, each naming its gap (`--gap <gap>`, section 8), which the command then closes in its own transaction, as the migrator, with the disposition `imported`; it refuses a gap of another class and one that is not open. Until then the gap is listed as open on the console's `/restore`. A seal taken again (step 5) opens a new owed record; where an imported record shows that the report was made, the operator declines the new one and names the earlier record as the reason.
4. The API starts; staff enrol new keys; `notify` reads the mailbox from its restored mark: what arrived since is stale and raises nothing.
5. **The console's `/restore`** (`GET /api/staff/restore`, `RestoreView`) lists the open gaps by class, in this order, each with what to do:

| Order | Class | The lost row | Taken again by |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `order_effect` | a decision of an `order_effected` row | "Disable for an order" on the imported order (7.6), which closes the gaps of its items |
| 2 | `seal_final` | a final decision with `seal_media` | a finding citing R2 and the seal, with the fixed drafts (D 7.5) |
| 3 | `seal_interim` | an interim decision with `seal_media` | the same, while deciding |
| 4 | `standing` | a decision with `suspend` or `terminate` | a finding and the decision, written again (E3 7.6 to 7.8) |
| 5 | `restriction` | a decision with any other measure | a finding and the decision, written again |
| 6 | `reversal` | a correction or a review that lifted something | a correction, or nothing when the measure it lifted is itself lost and not taken again |
| 7 | `no_measure` | a decision without a measure | shown for reference; an admin closes it as `not_needed` |
| 8 | `record` | a record the import could only note | the operator's notification or production command with `--gap` (step 3), which closes the gap as the migrator with the disposition `imported` |

6. **Closing a gap.** A decision taken for a gap is an ordinary confirmed decision under a newly enrolled key. The decide and the review routes take the gap as the query member `gap`; inside their transaction, after their ledger rows and before the audit row, they call `gap_close(gap, decision, account)` with the session's account. It refuses with one code, `gap_mismatch` (409), a gap that is closed and a decision whose measures or target do not fit the gap's class. The decision's own check at commit covers that transaction, and its audit row names the gap (`metadata.gapId`). A gap that needs nothing is closed by an admin: `POST /api/staff/restore/gaps/:gap/close`, step-up `gap_closed` with `{ disposition: "not_needed" }`; the route calls `gap_close(gap, null, account)` and then writes the audit row `gap_closed` through `append_confirmed_audit`. At commit the deferred trigger `restore_gaps_confirmed` holds that closing to its audit row (`gap_closed` on this gap, with a step-up, written by an admin), or the commit is refused `gap_mismatch`. So no statement run as the API's role can empty the list of gaps, which is what opens the app's site, without a key.
7. **Told.** The author of a decision taken again is told as of a new decision, with `STATEMENT_WORDS.reapplied` and the lost decision's day, which the statement reads from the gap. Under a running hold nothing is told. A notice that the lost database had released is released again by the first dispatch round, with a second `order_notice`: the mail names no content, and the Cases screen shows one notice.
8. **Opening.** When no gap of class `order_effect`, `seal_final` or `seal_interim` is open, `RestoreView.mayOpen` is true and the screen says so; the operator sets `APP_SITE=open`. The two retention switches go back on when no gap of the classes 1 to 5 is open: a preservation that ended in the lost time ends again then, by its stored date.

What stays lost: the texts of lost decisions; what a record of class `record` held beyond the operator's notes; and everything after the keeper's last download, for which the mailbox and the operator's notes are the record.

### 7.18 What each job gains, and the yearly counts

| Job | Round | Gains |
|---|---|---|
| `notify` (its own service) | 20 s | the whole of 7.1 |
| `alert-tick` (new, API) | 30 s | 7.2: `alert_steps()`, the mail leg, the channel's health. It runs also with `NOTIFY=off`, which skips the third alone |
| `moderation-dispatch` (`dispatch.ts:460`) | 10 min | the step `order-notices` before the statements (7.10), which also mail `order_notice_added` (7.7). It raises no alert of 3f's: the copy's age is `alert_steps()`'s |
| `account-purge` (`account/purge.ts:35`) | 5 min | the held address (7.15) |
| the retention job (D 7.9) | as D 7.9 | the steps below |

The retention job's new steps, each a transaction of its own that ends with a `moderation_retention` audit row when it changed anything (metadata: the step and the count), all held by `MODERATION_RETENTION=off`:

| Step | Call | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `order_preservation` | `order_preservation_end(item)` for each due item | 7.11 |
| `order_records` | `order_records_due()`, then `erase_order_records(order)` | deletes an order's row, items, events and texts 3 years (`recordKeepYears`) after its last event, never while a measure of it is in force, a preservation of it runs or a decision about one of its posts is untold (the order's own, their corrections and korbat's own decisions held with them) |
| `contact_messages` | `delete_contact_messages(older_than)` | deletes a message's row 30 days (`messageKeepDays`) after it was handled, when no order names it, and the outbox rows that old, but for a row whose subject is still open: a message without a disposition, or an order that is neither closed nor given effect in full (7.2). A message nobody handled is never deleted, a stale one included: the row is the record that a message arrived and nobody looked at it. The duplicates a changed UIDVALIDITY makes therefore stay, and count on the home screen, until a person closes them, which the Contact point screen does for all of them together (7.3) |
| `authority_records` | `erase_authority_records()` | deletes a notification record with its events, and a production record, 3 years after the matter's last event, never while the record's seal is in force or an archive is not recorded as destroyed, and never while something still reads it: a notification record is kept while one of its events carries "not to the person" with an end that is open or ahead, and a production while its own such end is ahead (7.13). The function answers a count and nothing else: the API's role still reads none of these rows |
| `restore_gaps` | `delete_restore_gaps(older_than)` | deletes a gap closed as `not_needed` or `imported` 12 months (`gapKeepMonths`) after it closed, and the copy marks that old but the newest. A gap that a decision closed (`reapplied`) is never deleted: the statement of a decision taken again reads the lost decision's day from it at every opening (7.17), and it is one small row for as long as the ledger's row |

The ledger's and the audit log's rows stay, as D 6.6 keeps them.

**The yearly counts.** In January the operator runs `staff.js tco-counts <year>` (as the migrator, read-only, no audit row): from rows, for the calendar year in UTC, the figures of section 2, row 40. A figure it cannot compute from rows prints "not recorded", never 0. The report's text and its publication before 1 March are the runbook's until 3d (section 18).

### 7.19 Mails and pushes

- **To staff.** Every alert kind of 3f that has a mail leg (10.1: `mail` is `sentence`) is a template of the same name in the mailer's closed union and a member of `StaffMessage` (`auth/index.ts:208`); both legs render the kind's title and one sentence of `ALERT_TEXTS` (7.2). It carries counts and UTC times: no content, no link, no handle, no address, no authority's name, no reference. 3c's and 3e's six kinds keep the mails they have, word for word, the staff handle included; what they gain is a push, which holds a count and a time and no handle.
- **To a person.** `mailTemplateOf` (E3 7.15) gains three rows in front of its list: an `order` decision is mailed `order_notice`; a correction that holds `reinstate_by_order` is mailed `order_reinstated_notice`; an `order` decision whose notice gained the authority's words after its author was told (its `author_mail_at` is already set) is mailed `order_notice_added` (7.7). Each says only that a notice is on the Cases screen, or that it has grown: no content, no link, no authority. `AuthForModeration.mailAccount` gains the three. A decision of korbat's own that was held with a notice is mailed at the release with the template it would have had (7.10).
- **The deletion mail.** `account_deleted` (`mail/templates.ts:163`) says today "Your posts, images and profile are gone". After 3f korbat may keep a post preserved under an order for six months, and an address for seven days, and under a hold a truthful variant for one account alone would be the telling Section C forbids. So the sentence changes for every account, as a rule and not a finding about this one: the posts, images and profile are gone, except what the law obliges korbat to keep for a time, which the terms' "Your data" names (section 18). It never says that everything is gone while something is kept. The sentence of 7.15 follows it when the address is held, in the variant of its seal.
- 3c's sentence "korbat staff never ask for your password or a code, and korbat does not read replies to this email." closes the three new mails, as it closes every statement mail.

## 8. The console and the command line

3c 8 and 3e 8 stay whole: text-only rendering, one CSS file, every answer parsed with the contracts' schemas, the key asked only after the console checked what it signs, no link into the app, people shown as handles. 3f adds four routes, five answers and words. Every sentence below is a member of `ORDER_WORDS` in `apps/admin/src/messages.ts`, under the key in brackets. Every number in one is printed from the answer or from `ORDER_RULES` (section 10), never typed, so a table test can hold them (section 12).

### 8.1 What the console reads, and the phone

- **Router.** `contact` (`/contact`), `orders` (`/orders`), `order` (`/orders/<id>`, the id a lower-case uuid, anything else `unknown`) and `restore` (`/restore`). `ConsolePath` gains `"/contact" | "/orders" | "/restore"`. `nav.tsx` gains `OrderLink`, whose `href` is `orderHref(id)` over an `OrderId`, a branded string that only `parseOrderId` makes, as `CaseLink` is built (`apps/admin/src/router.ts:17-26`). The source test's rule "`href={` only in `src/nav.tsx`" now pins three forms.
- **Navigation.** "Contact point" and "Orders" stand directly after "Home", before "Queue": at night they are what the person needs. "After a restore" is shown once an import ran (`RestoreView.importedAt` is not null).
- **Answers** (section 10 defines them). `StaffOverview`'s members `contact`, `alerts`, `reportsOwed`, `ordersUnverified`, `copy` and `restore` for the home screen; `ContactPage` from `GET /api/staff/contact`; `OrdersPage` from `GET /api/staff/orders`; `OrderView` from `GET /api/staff/orders/:id`; `RestoreView` from `GET /api/staff/restore`. On a case: `CaseDecisionAction.orderId`, `orderGround` and `withholdAuthor`, and `CaseView.preserved`, `reportOwed` and `noticeHeld`. No answer of 3f holds a sender, a subject, a mail address, an authority's document or a notification record.
- **What the screens print and section 10 must carry** (each a member a sentence below reads; the table test of section 12 holds every sentence to them). `OrderSummary` carries `clock` (as `OrderView`'s, with `stoppedAt`, `stopGround`, `lastEffectAt` and `itemsOpen`), `closedAt` and `closeGround`, so the list of 8.4 prints its clock lines without reading each order. An item's `post` carries `createdAt` and `deletedOn`; an item carries `linkKind` (what its link names: `post`, `image`, `account` or `other`), `addedAt`, `unverified`, `reinstatedGround`, `noEffect` and, when it was closed without effect, `closedAt` and `closedReason` (the column `closed_reason`: `no_post`, `not_named` or `withdrawn`). `OrderView.hold` is null for an order without Section C; otherwise it carries `until` (null while `open`), `releasedAt`, `basis` (`regulation` for a removal order, `authority` for a DSA order) and `open` (true while a DSA order's hold has no recorded end). `OrderView` also carries `mayCorrect` (the rule under which the caller could correct the record now: `entry`, `caution`, `admin`, or null), `tellPerson` (`{ rule, at }` for an order to provide information, or null) and, in `owed`, `holdUntil` and `tellPerson`. The refusal `order_exists` carries `orderId`, from which the form builds its `OrderLink`.
- **The same, beyond an order.** `StaffMe` carries `homeState` (a key of `STATE_FACTS`, or null) and `contactPoint` (the address, or null): the console's page takes no build argument, so it reads the two from that answer and `STATE_FACTS` from the contracts by that key. `AuditPage` carries `copy` (`{ lastPlace }`, the caller's own newest copied place, or null).
- **Times.** Every time is the database's instant, printed by one function `bothTimes(at)` in `ui.tsx`: UTC first, then the browser's local time in brackets ("02:00 UTC (04:00 your time)"). A deadline also shows the minutes left, counted from the answer's own time, and the screen reads again every 20 seconds without the activity mark.
- **Text from an authority.** The authority's name, the reference and each link are typed by staff from a document an outsider wrote. They are drawn through `UserText` inside a frame labelled "As written in the order", never in the console's own label positions, with 3c 8's "Show hidden characters".
- **The phone.** Every screen of 3f, and Home, the case page and the composer's confirmation, are usable at a width of 360 px: one column, no table (every list is a list of blocks, label above value), no hover, every control at least 44 px high and wide, no control that needs a second hand (no drag, no long press).
