A control is an `a`, `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea` or `summary` element; a tick box or a radio button is measured together with its label, and a link inside a sentence (a `CaseLink` or `OrderLink` of a Home line) is laid out as a block of its own at widths under 600 px, so that it can be measured and touched. `app.css` gains one media query for widths under 600 px and the rules for these blocks; the console keeps its one CSS file and no `style` prop. The browser check runs every run of 8.10 at 360 by 740 px.
- **A refusal the page did not foresee** means the facts moved between the read and the key. The console words `invalid_decision` on an order as [changed]: "The database refused this: the order, its items or their posts are no longer what this page showed. Read the order again."

### 8.2 Home

The home screen gains a block "Authorities" above the lanes, from those six members of `StaffOverview`. Each line is shown only while it applies, in this order.

| Key | Shown while | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| [home.contact] | `contact.open` is above zero | "Mail at the authorities' contact point since {oldestAt}: {open} messages nobody has handled. Read it in the mail host's app, then open Contact point. A removal order is due one hour after it arrived." |
| [home.clock] | `contact.dueAt` is ahead: the nearest running deadline of a message or an order (a link to `/orders`) | "The nearest deadline is {dueAt}: {minutes} minutes left." |
| [home.overdue] | `contact.dueAt` has passed while a message is not handled or an order still has an item that is neither disabled nor closed | "A deadline passed at {dueAt} and not everything is done: a message is unread, or an order has an item that is neither disabled nor closed. Open Orders and act now, then tell the operator." |
| [home.channel] | `alerts.channel` is `down`: `notify`'s beat, or an alert that is still owed a push, is older than {channelDownMinutes} minutes | "Phone alerts are down since {since}. Alerts arrive by mail only: keep the mail host's app open on the contact-point mailbox until this line is gone. An order you find there that Contact point does not list is opened under Orders, with "The message is not listed"." |
| [home.mailbox] | `alerts.channel` is `mailbox` | "korbat has not been able to read the contact-point mailbox since {since}. Check it in the mail host's app until this line is gone. An order you find there is opened under Orders, with "The message is not listed"." |
| [home.off] | `alerts.channel` is `off` | "Phone alerts and the contact point's watcher are switched off on this server. Nothing watches the contact-point mailbox: the operator switches them on." The sentence names no setting: the page cannot know how the server spells it. |
| [home.owed] | a notification is owed (one line a case, each a `CaseLink`) | "A report to the authorities is owed since {since}." After 24 hours it adds "More than 24 hours: every staff account is alerted daily until whoever has the server records it as sent or declined." |
| [home.unverified] | an order has a disabled item and is not verified, or has a disabled item that was added after its last check (each an `OrderLink`) | "Orders given effect and not verified, or with a post added after the check: {n}. Whoever has the server compares each with the order in the mailbox and records the check with staff.js order-verify; every staff account is alerted daily until then." |
| [home.copy] | the newest download of the audit log's tail is older than {copyOverdueHours} hours | "The copy is {days} days old. The keeper downloads the audit log's tail on the daily visit: Audit log, "Download since my last copy"." |
| [home.restore] | `restore.open` is above zero (a link to `/restore`) | "After a restore: {open} gaps are open, {measures} of them measures to take again. The app's site stays closed until every order disabling and seal among them is back." |

The console sees of a notification only that one is owed, for which case and since when ([home.owed]); who was reported to whom is the command line's (8.11). The 48 hours in which korbat may ask for the scrutiny of an order from another Member State are shown on that order's own screen ([orders.scrutinyUntil], 8.5 block 7) and not here: `StaffOverview` carries no member for them, and the runbook's step 8 (W.9) is what sends the operator there.

### 8.3 Contact point (`/contact`)

The messages `notify` recorded, newest first, 50 a page with "Load older". The screen opens with [contact.what] "korbat records that a message arrived and when. It never reads who sent it or what it says: read the message in the mail host's app. Every message here may be a removal order until a person has read it. The junk folder is watched too: an order filed as junk has still arrived. While one message's alarm is less than {quietMinutes} minutes old, further mail is listed here and raises no new alarm of its own: read every message listed, not only the first. A message that is in the mailbox and not listed here (the watcher was down, or the mail host filed it in another folder) is opened as an order all the same: Orders, "Open an order", "The message is not listed"."

A message's block:

- "Received {receivedAt}", from the mail host's delivery time; "Inbox" or "Junk folder".
- [contact.stale], when `stale`: "korbat first saw this message more than 2 hours after it arrived (after an outage or a restore). It raised no phone alert."
- "Not handled", or "Handled as {disposition} by @{handle} on {handledAt}".
- While not handled: "Open an order from this message" (opens the form of 8.4 with the message picked) and "Close as", a list of six choices with one "Close" button. No step-up: closing restricts nothing.

| Disposition | Label | Hint under the choice |
|---|---|---|
| `referral` | "Referral" | "An authority points to content and orders nothing. Open a case of your own on it (Queue, Open a case) and decide it under korbat's rules or the law." |
| `first_order_notice` | "Notice before a first order" | "An authority tells korbat about its procedures and deadlines at least 12 hours before its first removal order (Article 3(2)). Keep the mail and tell the operator: an order from that authority may follow." |
| `preservation_request` | "Request to preserve longer" | "An authority or a court asks korbat to keep disabled content beyond six months. Whoever has the server records it with staff.js order-preserve-extend." |
| `scrutiny_decision` | "Scrutiny decision" | "The home authority's decision on a removal order from another Member State (Article 4). An admin acts on it on the order: reinstates, or leaves the order in force." |
| `clarification` | "About an order korbat holds" | "A clarification after an Annex III, a withdrawal, an annulment, or an extension or end of a non-disclosure period. A clarification that restarts the clock is recorded on the order itself ("Clarification received"), and an extension of a removal order's non-disclosure period by an admin on the order itself ("Extension received"), at once: the database releases the author's notice by itself at the old end. Either closes this message. Close it here only for the others, and tell the operator the same day; for a later end that an authority gives for a DSA order's silence, only whoever has the server can record it (staff.js order-hold-until), so call them." |
| `not_an_order` | "Not an order" | "Anything else: a question, an advertisement, a user's mail sent to the wrong address." |

- [contact.close] "Closing ends this message's alerts and cannot be changed. Close it only after you have read it in the mail host's app."
- [contact.reply] "Never answer an authority from korbat, and never open an attachment on a device that holds a staff key before you have checked the sender against the register."

A message opened as an order reads "Handled as an order" with an `OrderLink`. The screen holds no search and no filter: at one or two messages a month it is a list.

**Stale messages, together.** A message nobody handled is never deleted, however old (section 6): its row is the record that a message arrived and nobody looked at it. So the rows that an outage, a restore or a changed validity of the mailbox leaves behind stay listed, and counted in [home.contact], until a person closes them. While the page lists more than one message that is `stale` and not handled, it offers above the list "Close the {n} stale messages as", the six choices and one button "Close them". The console closes each through the contact point's close route (section 10), with the chosen word and an audit row each, a page of 50 at a time, and reads the page again. [contact.staleAll] "These {n} messages reached korbat's record more than {staleHours} hours after they arrived and raised no alert. Look at each in the mail host's app before you close them: one of them may be an order, and it is then opened, not closed. Closing cannot be changed."

### 8.4 Orders (`/orders`) and the form "Open an order"

**The list.** The orders that are not closed, the one with the nearest deadline first, then the closed ones of the last 30 days. A block holds the kind (`ORDER_KIND_LABELS`: "Removal order (terrorist content)", "Order to act against content (DSA Article 9)", "Order to provide information (DSA Article 10)"), the issuing state, the authority and the reference in their frame, one clock line and an `OrderLink`.

| Key | Clock line, for | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| [clock.running] | a removal order whose clock runs | "Due {deadline}: {minutes} minutes left. Target {target}: an image may stay in an edge cache for up to 5 minutes after the disabling." |
| [clock.overdue] | past its deadline, with an item that is neither disabled nor closed | "Overdue since {deadline}: {itemsOpen} of {items} items are neither disabled nor closed. Act now." |
| [clock.stopped] | an Annex III is newer than the clock's last start | "Clock stopped at {stoppedAt}: Annex III sent ({ground}). No deadline runs. A fresh {hourMinutes} minutes start when the grounds cease or the clarification arrives, never the rest of the old hour." |
| [orders.deadlineUnknown] | opened without a message, no receipt time | "Deadline unknown: act now. Whoever has the server records the time of receipt with staff.js order-received." |
| [clock.partial] | some items disabled, `itemsOpen` above zero: the deadline still runs and the reminders go on | "{done} of {items} items disabled, the first at {firstEffectAt}. {itemsOpen} are still open: the deadline {deadline} still runs for them." |
| [clock.effect] | given effect in full: every item is disabled or closed with a reason (`itemsOpen` is zero) | "Given effect: the last post was disabled at {lastEffectAt}, {elapsed} minutes after the clock started: inside the hour." (or "outside the hour."; an effect given while the clock was stopped reads "while the clock was stopped: inside the hour.") The line judges the hour only once no item is open. |
| [clock.dsa] | a DSA order | "Due {dueAt}, as the order states." or "No deadline recorded: act without undue delay." |
| [clock.closed] | closed | "Closed on {closedAt} without effect: {ground}." or "Closed on {closedAt}." |

**The form**, opened from a message or with "Open an order" above the list. It restricts nothing, copies nothing and tells no author; it needs no step-up, so that Annex III can be recorded before any effect.

| Field | Control | Words |
|---|---|---|
| Message | a list of the messages not handled, then those handled in the last 24 hours with what they were closed as (received time, folder), and last, always, the choice "The message is not listed". Nothing is preselected | [open.message] "Pick the message the order came in. Its delivery time at the mail host becomes the time of receipt: the console takes no typed time. If the order is in the mailbox and not in this list (the watcher was down, or the mail host filed it in another folder), or it reached korbat another way, choose "The message is not listed". Never pick another message in its place: its time would become the order's." With that choice, [open.noMessage] "The order is opened without a time of receipt, so korbat cannot show its deadline. Act now, as if the hour were almost over. Whoever has the server records the time afterwards (staff.js order-received)." |
| How it arrived | shown only with "The message is not listed": a required choice of the four `ORDER_CHANNELS`, none preselected, under `ORDER_CHANNEL_LABELS` (W.1): "By mail to the contact point: it is in the mailbox and not in this list" (`mailbox`), "Through a portal" (`portal`), "By post or in person" (`post`), "Another way" (`other`). With a picked message the field is not shown and the request carries `mailbox`, which the database forces (section 6) | [open.channel] "Choose how the order reached korbat. It is recorded with the order. A wrong choice can be corrected on the order's screen." |
| Kind | three choices, the labels above | [open.kind] "A message that orders nothing is closed on Contact point, not opened here." |
| Issuing state | the 27 Member States by name, the home state first | |
| Authority | the names `STATE_FACTS` holds for that state, and "Another authority" with a text field (1 to 200 characters) | [open.authority] "Choose the authority as your copy of the Commission's register lists it. If it is not listed, type its name as the order writes it and check it before you act." |
| Reference | text, 1 to 200 characters | "The order's reference, exactly as written." |
| Links | a text area, one link a line; none for an order to provide information | [open.links] "The korbat addresses the order names, one a line, at most {itemsPerAction} now; more are added on the order's screen. korbat resolves each to a post and fetches nothing. The address of an image is taken as the address of the post it belongs to, and kept as the order names it. A link that names an account, another site or no post is recorded as named, and the order's screen says what to do about it." |
| Must the author not be told? | a required choice of three, none preselected, so that an untouched control never decides. For a removal order: "Section C is ticked: the author must not be told", "Section C is not ticked", "I cannot tell". For a DSA Article 9 order: "The order says the author must not be told", "It does not say so", "I cannot tell". "I cannot tell" is sent as the first (`sectionC` true): it holds like a tick | [open.sectionC], for a removal order: "Choose what the order shows. If you cannot tell, the notice is held as if Section C were ticked, and whoever has the server settles it. A held notice is released by the database alone, {holdDays} days after the day of the order, counted so that it is never early, and {holdExtensionDays} days later when the authority extends the period once." [open.sectionC.dsa], for a DSA Article 9 order: "Choose what the order says. If you cannot tell, the notice is held. For this kind of order no law fixes the period: the notice is held until the time the authority states, which whoever has the server records (staff.js order-hold-until), and while none is recorded it is released at no time." Under either, [open.sectionC.fix] "A wrong choice can be corrected on the order's screen: towards silence by anyone at once, away from it only by an admin." |
| Emergency | a tick box, for a removal order only | "The order says it is an emergency, so no notice came 12 hours before it." |
| Withheld from the author | a tick box, for a DSA Article 9 order only; ticked and locked whenever the choice above is not "It does not say so" | [open.withhold] "Tick it only if the order's text says the content must be kept from its author too. Without it the post is removed as under a korbat rule: its author still sees it and is told at once." While locked, [open.withholdLocked] "An author who must not be told cannot be shown their post marked as removed. So the post is kept from its author too, and it stays so for as long as the order's measure stands." A removal order always withholds. The database forces the same (section 6): no request can open an order that holds its notice and does not withhold. |

- The button "Open the order". Afterwards the order's screen, with [open.done] "Order opened. Nothing is restricted yet and nobody was told. Every staff account is alerted that an order was opened. Read what you entered against the order once more: a mistake is corrected here, under "Correct what the order shows", before you disable anything."
- After "I cannot tell", the order's screen opens with [open.unsure] "You could not tell whether the author may be told. The notice is held. Tell the operator now: the order must be read again." The choice is sent as `sectionC` true and nothing more is stored: the sentence is shown once, on the screen that follows the opening.
- [open.limit] (the record cap): "You have opened {recordsPerDay} orders in 24 hours, the most one staff account may. Another staff member opens the next; tell the operator."
- [open.duplicate] (an order of the same state, authority and reference exists): "An order of this authority with this reference is already recorded. If that record is wrong, correct it on its screen; if it is closed, an admin reopens it there. Do not open the order a second time under a changed reference." with its `OrderLink`. A wrong record never stands in the way: every member a person entered can be corrected (8.5, block 10), and a closed order can be reopened.
- A message closed as something else by a wrong tap can still be picked: the order is opened all the same, and the message keeps the word it was closed with.

### 8.5 An order (`/orders/:id`)

Blocks in this order; at 360 px the first screenful holds the clock and the button.

**1. The clock**, as 8.4 words it, refreshed every 20 seconds.

**2. The check**, worded by the order's kind.

- **A removal order.** [order.register] "Before you act, check the order against your offline copy of the Commission's register: is {authority} listed for {state}, and does the address in the order's Section F belong to that entry? A signature is neither needed nor proof." [order.doubt] "If you are in doubt and the hour is running, give effect: the measure is reversible and keeps everything. Whoever has the server records the check by the next working day." [order.doubt] is shown for a removal order only: it is the hour that justifies acting in doubt, and no other kind has one. For the home state (`StaffMe.homeState`) the block lists each authority's name and `registerEntry` from `STATE_FACTS`; a member that is not filled in prints [state.unknown] "not filled in: see the README, Before going public".
- **A DSA order.** [order.register.dsa] "No register lists who may issue such an order. Ask korbat's Digital Services Coordinator whether it holds a copy of it (Article 9(4) of the Digital Services Act), or call the authority on a number you looked up yourself, never one the message gives." [order.verifyFirst] "No hour runs for this kind of order, so nothing is done in doubt: it is given effect only after the check is recorded. Whoever has the server records it (staff.js order-verify), and counsel reads the order first." While `verified` is false, block 4 shows this sentence in the place of its button (the code `order_not_verified`, 8.9).

**3. The items**, each a block:

- "Item {n}", the link in its frame, and what it resolved to, read live: "@{handle}, post of {createdAt}, {images} images" and, only while the post is public, its first `ORDER_ITEM_PREVIEW` characters in an author frame; a post that is not public shows its state's word and no text, and no image is ever shown here.
- [item.image] (the link is the address of an image): "The order names an image. korbat takes it as the post the image belongs to: disabling the post stops every image of it." The item is the post; the link stays as the order names it.
- [item.account] (the link names a profile): "This link names an account, not a post. korbat cannot disable an account for an order from this screen. Add the address of each post the order means. For the display name or the bio, and when the order means the whole account, call the operator now. Do not send Annex III for it: the order is not unclear. Then close this item without effect, as a link that names nothing korbat can disable for an order: until a person has, it counts as open and the order's reminders go on."
- [item.missing] (another site's address, or a post id that never existed): "This link names nothing that korbat serves. Compare it with the order, letter by letter: a mistyped link is closed here as korbat's mistake, and the right one added under "Add links". Only if the order itself gives this address, and korbat cannot find the content from it, does the order lack information (Annex III). Either way this item counts as open until a person closes it without effect."
- [item.deleted] "Its author deleted this post on {deletedOn}. Giving effect records the order and changes nothing anyone sees."
- Its state: "Not disabled"; "Disabled at {disabledAt}" with "Preserved until {preserveUntil}" or [item.erased] "Preservation ended on {day}: the content was erased."; "Reinstated at {reinstatedAt}: {reinstatedGround}"; "Closed without effect at {closedAt}: {closedReason}" (the reason's label, W.1). A disabled item links to its case. An item added after the order's last check shows [item.unchecked] "Added at {addedAt}, after the order was checked: not compared with the order by whoever has the server yet."
- A tick box, set for every item that can be disabled.
- **"Close this item without effect"**, on an item that is open (neither disabled nor closed). It is a correction of the record and calls the correction's route (block 10) with `closeItems`: the item's id and one of the three reasons of `ORDER_ITEM_CLOSE_REASONS`, "The link names nothing korbat can disable for an order" (`no_post`: an account, another site, a post that does not exist), "korbat's mistake: the order does not name this link" (`not_named`) or "The authority withdrew this item" (`withdrawn`: before korbat acted on it, while other items of the order stand). With the third, [item.withdrawn] "The authority withdrew this item before korbat acted on it, and other items of the order stand. Check the withdrawal as you check an order before you close the item. If the authority withdrew the whole order, an admin closes the order instead ("Close without effect")." Before the order's first effect whoever is on call closes an item, without the key; once an item of the order is disabled it is an admin's, with the key (the step-up `order_corrected`). The database writes no event of its own for it: the closing is in the `corrected` event and its audit row. [item.close] "Use this for a link that names nothing korbat can disable for an order, once you have done what its line above says, or for a link that korbat entered by mistake and the order does not name, or for an item the authority withdrew before korbat acted on it. Until every item is disabled or closed, the order is not given effect in full: its deadline and its reminders go on." A link that names no post is an open item too, until a person closes it with the first reason: the order named something, and somebody must have looked at it. An item that was disabled is never closed: a wrong one is reinstated on its case with the ground `wrong_item` (8.6).
- Under the items, "Add links" (the form's Links field): an order may name up to {itemsPerOrder} addresses (`ORDER_ITEMS_MAX`), added ten at a time, so that the first effect never waits for the typing of the rest. [item.added] "A link added after the order was checked makes the order unverified again: every staff account is alerted daily until whoever has the server has compared the new link with the order and recorded the check again."

**4. "Disable for an order".** One button for all ticked items, with these lines above it:

| Key | Shown | Sentence |
|---|---|---|
| [effect.what] | a removal order, or a DSA order that withholds | "This disables the ticked posts for everyone, their authors included: each then answers like a post that does not exist, and its images are not served. korbat does not assess them. Nothing is deleted." |
| [effect.preserve] | a removal order | "Each post, its images and a copy of its text are preserved for six months from now, and erased then unless an authority or a court asks for longer." For an item disabled before and reinstated since, the six months count anew from this disabling; only a disabling taken again after a restore keeps the end it had (8.8), and the line then reads "preserved until {preserveUntil}, as before the restore". |
| [effect.removed] | a DSA order that does not withhold (so one whose author may be told) | "This removes the ticked posts for everyone but their authors, who still see them, as after a removal under a korbat rule. korbat does not assess them." |
| [effect.told] | the author may be told (no Section C) | "Each author is told at once: a notice in the app, and an email that says only that there is one." |
| [effect.held] | a removal order with Section C | "Section C is ticked: the authors are told nothing, and see what everyone sees, until {holdUntil}. The database releases the notice then by itself; every staff account is alerted {holdWarningDays} days before." |
| [effect.held.dsa] | a DSA order whose author must not be told | "The order says the author must not be told: the authors are told nothing, and see what everyone sees, until the time the authority stated ({holdUntil})." or, while `hold.open`: "... and no end is recorded: nothing is released until whoever has the server records the authority's time (staff.js order-hold-until). Every staff account is reminded daily that it is missing." |
| [effect.urgent] | a DSA order that is not verified, for an admin only | a tick box "Urgent: give effect before the check", with "Tick this only when the order itself sets a time that cannot wait for the check. It is signed with your key and every staff account is told that an unverified order was given effect as urgent." A moderator sees [order.verifyFirst] and no button. |
| [effect.anyone] | always | "Any staff member on duty does this, also for a post of someone they know or of a staff member: an order leaves no choice. Every staff account is alerted." |
| [effect.max] | more than {itemsPerAction} items | "At most {itemsPerAction} items at a time: confirm these, then the rest." |

- The button reads "Disable {n} posts: confirm with your staff key". The console builds the params of `order_effected` from the order as shown and each ticked item's id and post (and `urgent: true` when [effect.urgent] is ticked), checks the options against them, and asks the key once.
- [orders.checkOutside] "Disabled at {at}: {n} posts. Now check from outside: open each address below in a private window. It must say that the post does not exist (HTTP 404). An image may still load from an edge cache for up to 5 minutes. Then send Annex II from the mailbox and record it here." The addresses are text, never links (3c 8).
- [effect.rest], while `itemsOpen` is above zero after an effect: "{itemsOpen} items of this order are still open. The order is not given effect in full yet: its deadline {deadline} runs for them, and the reminders go on. Tick them and confirm again, or have an admin close an item that names nothing korbat can disable, that the order does not name or that the authority withdrew."
- A measure that changed nothing: "already disabled or removed by another decision: recorded, changed nothing now" (`no_effect` `already`); "deleted by its author: recorded, changed nothing" (`deleted`).
- [effect.cap] (`order_cap`): "You have given effect to {decisionsPerDay} order items in 24 hours, the most one staff account may. Another staff member goes on; tell the operator at once."
- [effect.gone] (an item's post was purged between the read and the key): "A post of this order no longer exists. Read the order again."

**5. Recording what was sent and received.** Each is one confirmation with the key (`order_event_recorded`), offered only while the database would accept it. What the key confirmed is what is stored: the database refuses an event at commit unless the transaction's audit row is this action, on this order, for this event and by this account (section 6), as it does for a decision. korbat sends nothing to an authority: the person sends each form from the contact point's mailbox and records it here.

| Button | Offered | Words |
|---|---|---|
| "Annex III sent" | a removal order, not closed | a choice of ground: "Force majeure or impossibility not korbat's fault (Article 3(7))", "The order has manifest errors (Article 3(8))", "The order lacks information (Article 3(8))". [annex3.what] "Record this after you sent Annex III. The clock stops. korbat records the time and does not judge the form." |
| "The grounds have ceased" | while stopped after a force-majeure Annex III | [ceased.what] "Record this the moment korbat can carry out the order again. A fresh {hourMinutes} minutes start now." |
| "Clarification received" | while stopped after an Annex III on errors or missing information | a list of the messages that arrived since the order was opened, newest first: the Annex III may have been recorded some minutes after it was sent, so a clarification can be older than that record. [clarified.what] "Pick the message the clarification came in. A fresh {hourMinutes} minutes start at its delivery time, or at the time Annex III was recorded when that is later, so the deadline may already be near." Nothing is refused for its time. |
| "Annex II sent" | a removal order with an item disabled | [annex2.what] "Record this after you sent Annex II to the address in the order's Section F and the copy below." |
| "Authority informed" | a DSA order | [informed.what] "Record this after you told the authority, from the mailbox, that korbat received the order and what effect it gave and when (Articles 9(1) and 10(1) of the Digital Services Act)." It records `receipt_confirmed`. |
| "Close without effect" | an admin; no measure of the order in force | a choice of ground, the six of `ORDER_CLOSE_GROUNDS`: "The authority withdrew it", "A court annulled it", "Scrutiny found that it infringes (Article 4)", "It is not authentic", "It is not addressed to korbat", and, for an order whose items were reinstated on that ground, "korbat's mistake: the order does not name this post" (`wrong_item`). [close.what] "Closing ends the order's alerts and its clock. An order with a disabled item is not closed: reinstate the item on its case first. A record that was only entered wrongly is not closed either: correct it below." |
| "Reopen the order" | an admin; a closed order | [reopen.what] "Use this when the order was closed by mistake, or when a court has restored an order that was closed as withdrawn or annulled. The order is then as it was before it was closed and can be given effect. Its times are unchanged, so a deadline that passed meanwhile shows as overdue." It records `reopened`. |
| "Extension received" | an admin; a removal order with a held notice that was not extended yet | a list of the messages that arrived since the order was opened. [extended.what] "Record this the moment the authority's decision to extend the non-disclosure period arrives: without it the database releases the author's notice by itself on {holdUntil}. The notice is then held {holdExtensionDays} days longer, once. Whoever has the server checks the decision afterwards and either confirms it or ends the hold (staff.js order-hold-end)." It records `hold_extended`; a second extension is refused (`order_event_refused`), and one after the notice was released or the hold was ended too (`already_released`). |

**6. The forms' values**, as plain text to copy into the form by hand; korbat does not hold the forms' own wording, which the operator keeps with the register (README).

- [annex2.text], once an item is disabled: "Reference of the removal order: {reference}"; "Issuing authority: {authority}, {state}"; "Time and date of receipt of removal order: {receivedAt}"; then one line for each item, since an order may be carried out in several actions and not every address is met in the same state: "{link}: access disabled in all Member States at {disabledAt}", or "{link}: already not accessible when the order arrived (restricted by an earlier decision of korbat's); recorded at {disabledAt}", or "{link}: deleted by its author before the order arrived; nothing was accessible; recorded at {disabledAt}", or "{link}: not disabled yet", or "{link}: names no content that korbat serves" (an item closed with `no_post`, or still open without a post; an item closed with `not_named` has no line, since the order does not name it), or "{link}: not disabled: the authority withdrew this item" (an item closed with `withdrawn`); "Contact point: {contactPoint}"; "Send to: the address in the order's Section F. Copy to: {annexCopy}". `{contactPoint}` is `StaffMe.contactPoint` and `{annexCopy}` the home state's member of `STATE_FACTS`. When a staff member wrote a post the order names, the runbook has the person say so to the authority with Annex II (W.9): that staff member reads the contact-point mailbox like everyone on call, whatever Section C says.
- [annex3.text], for a removal order with no item disabled: the first three lines; "Reason: {ground}"; "Describe in the form what is impossible, wrong or missing: korbat does not write that part."; the last two lines.
- Each time is printed in UTC with its offset written out ("2026-10-10 01:00:12 +00:00"), since the form asks for a time and date and the authority reads it in its own zone.

**7. What the command line still owes**, one line each while it is missing:

- [owes.verify] "The check is not recorded (staff.js order-verify)." or, when an item was added after the last check, "{n} links were added after the order was checked: they are not compared with the order yet (staff.js order-verify, again)."
- [owes.issued] (a removal order with Section C) "The order's own date and time are not recorded (staff.js order-issued). Until they are, the non-disclosure period is counted from the receipt, which is never earlier."
- [owes.holdUntil] (a DSA order whose author must not be told, `hold.open`) "The time until which the authority wants the author not told is not recorded (staff.js order-hold-until). Until it is, the notice is released at no time, and every staff account is reminded daily."
- [owes.texts] "The authority's reasons and remedies are not recorded (staff.js order-texts). The author's notice says that they follow; when they are recorded, the author is sent an email and the notice shows since when it holds them." For a DSA Article 9 order that is not given effect yet: "Record the authority's reasons, its remedies and the order's territorial scope before you give effect (staff.js order-texts, three times): the author is owed them with the notice (Article 9(5) of the Digital Services Act). Only an order marked urgent goes first."
- [owes.tellRule] (an order to provide information) "Whether and when the person may be told of this order is not recorded (staff.js order-tell-rule). Nothing is produced before it is."
- [owes.document] "The document's digest is not recorded (staff.js order-document)."
- For an order from another Member State, within 48 hours of receipt and with no scrutiny recorded: [orders.scrutinyUntil] "An order from another Member State: korbat may ask for its scrutiny until {until} (Article 4(4) of Regulation (EU) 2021/784). Tell the operator today."

**8. The author's notice.** The block says which of three things holds, and for a hold whose end it is.

- An order whose author may be told: "Told at {disabledAt}." for each disabled item, since the notice goes out with the effect (the item's `disabledAt`; `hold` is null).
- A removal order's hold (`hold.basis` `regulation`): [hold.until] "Held until {holdUntil}: the end the database computes from the Regulation's six weeks." with "counted from the receipt" or "counted from the order's own date", and "extended once" as they apply.
- A DSA order's hold (`hold.basis` `authority`): [hold.authority] "Held until {holdUntil}: the time the issuing authority stated. No law caps it; whoever has the server records a later time when the authority gives one." or [hold.open] "Held with no end: the authority has named no time, or none is recorded. Nothing is released until whoever has the server records one (staff.js order-hold-until)."
- After it, [hold.released] "Released at {releasedAt}." or [hold.ended] "Hold ended at {endedAt}: {reason}."
- [hold.warn], in the last {holdWarningDays} days before a hold's end: "The author's notice is released on {holdUntil}. If the authority has extended or prolonged the silence, record it before then: every staff account is alerted daily until that day."

The console does three things to a hold and no more (section 2, row 25): whoever is on call sets a missed "the author must not be told" (block 10); an admin records a removal order's one extension ("Extension received", block 5); and an admin, with the key, unsets a tick the order never had (block 10), which ends the hold as `not_ordered`. Ending a hold for any other reason, and every other change of its end, is the command line's (8.11).

**9. The events**, newest first: the time, the words of `ORDER_EVENT_WORDS` (W.1) and "@{handle}, console", "command line" or "korbat".

**10. "Correct what the order shows".** A record that a person entered wrongly is corrected, never worked around: no second record under a changed reference, no closing of a true order, no effect given on facts known to be wrong. Every member the form took can be corrected: the kind, the issuing state, the authority, the reference, how it arrived (the form's four choices, with the recorded one selected; for an order opened without a message alone, since one opened from a message arrived in the mailbox and shows no such control), whether the author must not be told, the emergency flag, "withheld from the author", and each link: a wrong one, or one that names nothing korbat can disable, is closed without effect (block 3; the request's `closeItems`) and the right one added with "Add links". The console sends `OrderCorrectRequest` to `POST /api/staff/orders/:order/correct` with only the members that change, and shows the controls that `OrderView.mayCorrect` allows. The database writes the event `corrected`, whose ground is the rule that allowed it (`entry`, `caution` or `admin`, the three rows below), and the audit row `order_corrected` in the same transaction, with the names of the changed members; every staff account is alerted (`order_corrected`, W.4), so no record changes unheard.

| Who, and when | What | Key |
|---|---|---|
| Any staff member, while no item of the order is disabled (the rule `entry`) | every member, and the closing of an item | none: nothing is restricted and nobody was told; the audit row says who changed what |
| Any staff member, once an item is disabled (the rule `caution`) | "the author must not be told" from no to yes and nothing else in the same request; "withheld from the author" follows by force | none: it is the cautious direction, and whoever is on call must be able to set it at once |
| An admin, once an item is disabled (the rule `admin`) | every other change, among them "the author must not be told" from yes to no, how the order arrived and the closing of an item | the step-up `order_corrected`, whose params are the request's `facts`: the changed members and their new values |

- [correct.what] "Change only what was entered wrongly, so that the record says what the order says. Every change is recorded under your name."
- [correct.silence] "This holds every notice of the order and keeps its posts from their authors. A notice that has already gone out is withdrawn until the hold ends: it leaves the author's screens, an email not yet sent is not sent, and korbat tells the author again at the release. What an author has already read cannot be taken back: if a notice had gone out, tell the operator at once." The database does the withdrawing (section 6): every decision of the order, every correction of one, and every decision about the same posts that was told after them is untold again until the release.
- [correct.unsilence], for the admin: "Only when the order has no non-disclosure decision and korbat's record said so by mistake. The hold ends with the reason "not ordered", each author is told with the next dispatch round, and the notice says that the delay was korbat's mistake."
- A changed kind, or a changed "withheld from the author", on an order that was given effect is followed by the database (section 6): the level of the posts whose measure stands is derived again (`disabled` or `removed`), their preservation starts when the record became a removal order's, and a preservation that is no longer owed ends with nothing erased.
- A moderator who tries a change that is the admin's reads `admin_required`'s words. A change that would make the record equal to another order's is answered with [open.duplicate]. A closed order takes no correction (`order_closed`): an admin reopens it first.
- After a correction the screen is read again from the answer. The author's notice is rendered from the order's row as it stands (W.2), so a corrected authority, state or reference is what a notice shows from then on.

### 8.6 A case that holds an order's measure

- **The item.** An order's intake item is listed with the source "An authority's order" and an `OrderLink`, never with a reporter's summary, a reveal control or a mark. It is answered when it is shown: no order item waits in the queue.
- **The decision** is listed as "Order" (`DECISION_KINDS` gains it), its measure as "Disabled for an order" (`MEASURES`, W.1) with "in force" or what lifted it, and "withheld from its author" or "shown to its author". The composer offers no order measure: an order is given effect on its own screen.
- **Preserved.** While `CaseView.preserved` is true the evidence shows no snapshot text and no image, under [case.preserved] "This post is preserved under an order. Its text and images are shown to no staff member here; they are opened only from the command line, for a recorded purpose (staff.js order-access)." Other open items of the case are decided on what the reports say, or wait, as 3c 8 has it for images that cannot be opened. `preserved` is the staff case page's own predicate, `public.post_preserved(post)` (section 6): `snapshot_withheld` is not widened and still means only that a copy's content was erased, so no screen, export or draft of the author's reads the preservation, and an earlier decision about the same post shows its author what it showed before.
- **korbat's own rules.** [case.own] "The order is not korbat's judgement. Reports about this post under korbat's rules are decided on their own: a removal under a rule is a second decision with its own statement and appeal, and it stays if the order's measure is lifted."
- **While the order's notice is held**, every decision about the post is held with it, also a decision under korbat's own rules and the answer to an appeal the author made before the order: the database records each without telling its author and tells all of them at the release (section 6). The case page and the composer's confirmation say so, with no word of the order's own facts: [case.heldToo] "The author of this post must not be told of an authority's order yet. So this decision is recorded now and told to the author only when that notice is released: on {holdUntil}, as things stand (or: at a time that is not recorded yet). Whoever reported the post is answered now, as always. Do not tell the author anything yourself." The page reads this from the case's answer (`CaseView.noticeHeld`: `{ until }`, with `until` null while the hold has no recorded end, and the member null when no notice about the case's post is held). The reporter's line moves as it always does: it names no order.
- **"Reinstate"**, beside the measure, for an admin: a correction that holds `reinstate_by_order` and the signed `orderGround`. The reason is the fixed draft of that ground (`ORDER_DRAFTS`, W.2), saved as a staff text like any other and not editable: a free text beside an order could say what a non-disclosure decision forbids.

| Ground | Label |
|---|---|
| `withdrawn` | "The authority withdrew the order" |
| `annulled` | "A court annulled the order" |
| `scrutiny` | "The home authority's scrutiny found that the order infringes (Article 4)" |
| `not_authentic` | "The order was not authentic" |
| `wrong_item` | "korbat's mistake: the order does not name this post" |

- [reinstate.what] "Reinstating ends the order's measure on this post and its preservation. korbat never lifts an order's measure on its own view of the content: choose what happened, after you checked the message it came in as you check an order. If another decision still restricts the post, it stays restricted. Should the order be restored later and the post be disabled again, its six months count anew from that disabling."
- [reinstate.told] "If the author was told of the disabling, they are told at once why it ended. If the notice is still held, nothing is told until the hold ends, and then both are told together."
- [reinstate.fake] (the ground `not_authentic`, the notice held) "Tell whoever has the server to end the hold first (staff.js order-hold-end), so that the author learns at once what happened and for how long."
- Like the effect, the reinstatement asks no recusal (the function's rule, section 6): it rests on a fact about the order, not on a view of the post, and it is an admin's.
- [reinstate.erased] (the preservation ended) "The content was erased on {day} when its preservation ended. Reinstating lifts the measure; it restores nothing."
- A moderator sees the control greyed with `admin_required`'s words. The preview is the notice's reinstatement paragraph (W.2).

### 8.7 Audit: the copy, and one key's actions

- **"Download since my last copy"**, above the table. [audit.download] "Downloads the rows after place {lastPlace}, the newest you downloaded, as one file of JSON lines, at most {max} rows at a time. Keep the file off the server with the head you note. After a restore it is what names the lost orders and decisions." (`{lastPlace}` is `AuditPage.copy.lastPlace`; `{max}` is `AUDIT_EXPORT_MAX`.) The first time: "You have downloaded nothing yet: this downloads the first {max} rows. Press again until nothing is newer."
- **It asks for the staff key.** A line of the file carries its row's metadata, which this page never shows (section 7, The daily copy): each order's authority and reference, held ones included, and every decision's signed params. So the download is a confirmed action (`POST /api/staff/audit/export` under the step-up `audit_exported`, whose params are the place it starts after and the head's place, `after` and `through`), and a session without its key cannot take it. [audit.key] "The file holds more than this page: confirm with your staff key." The keeper has a key in hand on the daily visit anyway.
- **The copy is recorded only when the keeper holds it.** The download writes one row, its own confirmed audit row `audit_exported`, which says who took which places also when the file never arrives; it writes no mark. When the console holds the whole body it checks that the lines run from the first place without a break, saves the file as `korbat-audit-<from>-<to>.jsonl`, and only then reports the last line's place and hash to the API (`POST /api/staff/audit/export/mark`, no key), which records the copy, with the plain audit row `copy_marked`, when that place holds that hash and the caller's own newest `audit_exported` row signed that place, and refuses otherwise (`copy_mismatch`; section 7). Afterwards [copy.done] "Downloaded places {from} to {to}: {rows} rows. The copy is recorded." or, when nothing is newer, [copy.none] "Nothing is newer than place {lastPlace}.", for which no key is asked. A download that broke off: [copy.broken] "The download broke off, or its lines do not continue each other. Nothing was recorded as copied: press again." So a file nobody received never moves the mark, and the keeper's files have no silent hole.
- **Somebody else's download is told.** When a staff account that did not take the last copy takes one, every staff account is alerted (`copy_taken`, W.4): the keeper's stand-in is expected, anybody else is a session in the wrong hands.
- **"What one key confirmed"**: a staff key named by its id alone, typed, or taken from a row by the control "This key" beside the row's `keyId`, and a period (two UTC times). The console knows no key's name: `staff.js keys` prints which id is whose. The table then lists the rows that carry a step-up of that key in that period, with a `CaseLink` or `OrderLink` where the target is one. [key.what] "Use this when a key may have been in someone else's hands. Each decision listed is undone by an admin's correction on its case; there is no batch. An order's disabling is reinstated only on one of its grounds: a stolen key is not one, so check the order itself."

### 8.8 After a restore (`/restore`)

From `RestoreView`: what the import found lost (section 7, A restore), in the order in which it is taken again.

- [restore.what] "The database was restored from a backup, and the keeper's copy names what the backup lacks. Nothing below is in force until a person takes it again under a key: korbat imports no measure. Work from the top."
- [restore.site] "The app's site is closed until no order disabling and no seal is open here." Then, when `RestoreView.mayOpen` is true, [restore.siteOpen] "No order disabling and no seal is open. The operator may open the app's site (APP_SITE=open)." and, once no gap of the first five classes is open, [restore.retention] "No measure is open. The operator may switch retention and the image sweep back on."
- [restore.lost] "What no import brings back: the texts of the lost decisions, everything after the keeper's last download, and anything the keeper's files do not hold (the import printed every place it found missing in them). For those, the contact-point mailbox and the operator's notes are the record."
- [restore.holds] "Before you disable anything again for an order: whoever has the server compares each held notice below with the contact-point mailbox since the keeper's last download. An extension or a later time that was recorded after that download is lost, and a disabling taken again under the old end would tell the author early." The orders with a held notice are listed under it with their imported `holdUntil`, each an `OrderLink`.

| Class | Heading | A gap's line and its control |
|---|---|---|
| `order_effect` | "Orders: disable again" | "Lost: the disabling of {n} posts for an order, first done at {actedAt}." with the order's `OrderLink`. "Open the order", then "Disable for an order": the preservation's end, the hold and the clock keep their imported values, since the item's preservation never ended (an item reinstated before the restore and disabled again counts six months anew, as always). Read [restore.holds] first |
| `seal_final` | "Seals for good" | "Lost: a seal for good, decided at {actedAt}." "Open the case": a finding citing R2 on the target, the seal with the fixed drafts, by an admin |
| `seal_interim` | "Seals taken while deciding" | the same, by whoever is on duty |
| `standing` | "Suspensions and terminations" | "Lost: {measure}, decided at {actedAt}." "Open the case" on the account |
| `restriction` | "Other restrictions" | "Lost: {measure}, decided at {actedAt}." "Open the case" |
| `reversal` | "Reversals" | "Lost: a reversal, decided at {actedAt}." "Open the case" and correct that decision again |
| `no_measure` | "Decisions without a measure" | "Lost: a decision without a measure, of {actedAt}. Listed for reference." |
| `record` | "Records to enter again" | "Lost: a record of {actedAt} that the copy names and cannot write again ({what})." Whoever has the server enters it again from the authority's letters and their own notes, with the command that wrote it, naming this gap (`--gap`, 8.11) |

The lines print a gap's members and nothing else (section 10's `RestoreView`): `{n}` counts the open gaps that share an `orderId` (one gap an item), `{measure}` is the words of the gap's `kinds` (the labels of `MEASURES`), `{what}` the words of its `action`, and `{actedAt}` its time.

- "Open the case" and "Open the order" carry the gap: the decision then confirmed names it, and the gap closes in that transaction as "taken again". The composer says [restore.again] "This takes again a decision of {actedAt} that was lost with a restore. Its statement says so. Write the facts and the reason again: the lost texts are gone." Under a running hold the author is told nothing, as before.
- "Not needed", for an admin, on any open gap: one confirmation with the key (`gap_closed`). [restore.notNeeded] "Use this when the measure had ended, or was reversed, before the restore, or when the target no longer exists. It is recorded with your key and cannot be undone here." As for an order's events, the database refuses the closing at commit unless the transaction's audit row is `gap_closed` on this gap, with a step-up, by an admin (section 6).

### 8.9 Messages

`CONSOLE_MODERATION_CODES` and `MODERATION_MESSAGES` gain the codes section 10 adds that a staff route can answer; the table test fails for a code without words.

| Code | Words |
|---|---|
| `order_needs_message` | "Pick the message this came in and try again. For a new order whose message is not in the list, choose "The message is not listed"." (answered when the message a new order names is no longer there, and for a clarification or an extension recorded without its message; an order without a message is accepted at any time, so the form's last choice is never refused with it) |
| `order_exists` | [open.duplicate] |
| `rate_limited`, on opening an order | [open.limit] |
| `message_handled` | "That message was already handled. Read Contact point again." |
| `order_changed` | [changed] (8.1) |
| `order_closed` | "This order is closed. Nothing more is recorded on it from here; an admin can reopen it." |
| `order_event_refused` | "That cannot be recorded now: the order is not in the state this page showed (for example, a second extension, or the grounds ceasing while no Annex III stands). Read the order again." |
| `already_released` | "The author's notice of this order was already released, or its hold was ended: an extension can no longer be recorded. Tell the operator at once." (the events route, for "Extension received") |
| `order_not_verified` | "This kind of order is given effect only after it is verified: whoever has the server records the check (staff.js order-verify). Only an admin can go first, for an order the admin marks as urgent." |
| `order_cap` | [effect.cap] |
| `gap_mismatch` | "That gap is closed already, or this decision does not take again what it lost. Read After a restore again." (one code for both of the database's refusals, a gap that is not open and a decision that does not fit it) |
| `copy_mismatch` | [copy.broken] (8.7) |
| `admin_required` | 3c's words |

The codes are section 10's (`order_event_refused`, `gap_mismatch`, `rate_limited` for the day's records, `message_handled`): a code the API does not know would be answered as a fault, so the database raises these names and no others. Section 10 adds twelve codes, and the eleven a staff route answers have their words above. `already_released` is both a route's answer and a command's refusal. The codes of the command line alone (`already_extended`, `nothing_held`, `not_preserved`, `tell_rule_missing`, `order_ambiguous`, `handover_mismatch`, `tail_not_continuous`), the database's `order_event_cli_only` and the app's `not_appealable` never reach the console and get no words here; the command line prints each of its own with its sentence (8.11, W.8).

### 8.10 The browser check

`apps/admin/test/browser/` gains fixtures for the five answers, with hostile strings in the authority, the reference and the links (bidirectional overrides, a look-alike console label, `javascript:`), and runs every run below at 360 by 740 px with the virtual authenticator, added by the task that builds its screen (section 13):

| Task | Runs |
|---|---|
| 1 | Home with [home.contact] and [home.channel]; Contact point with a stale message; a message closed as "Not an order"; three stale messages closed together |
| 2 | an order opened from a message, with [home.clock] on Home; an order opened with "The message is not listed" (deadline unknown); the form refusing to send while "Must the author not be told?" is untouched; a DSA Article 9 order with the second box locked; a correction before any effect, and a link that names an account closed without effect, without a key prompt; "Annex III sent" confirmed (the options bind the kind and ground) and the clock shown stopped; "Clarification received" with a message picked |
| 3 | "Disable for an order" on three items with one key prompt, the options binding the order's facts and the three items; the same with options that bind a fourth item, refused before the key is asked; two of three items disabled, with [clock.partial] and [effect.rest]; a DSA Article 9 order that is not verified, without a button for a moderator; the case with "Disabled for an order" and [case.heldToo]; "Reinstate" greyed for a moderator and confirmed by an admin; "Extension received" confirmed by an admin; a correction after effect refused for a moderator |
| 5 | Home with [home.owed] |
| 6 | "Download since my last copy" with its key prompt, the copy recorded only after the whole file arrived, and a download cut short that records nothing; the key filter with an id taken from a row; After a restore with one gap of each class, "Not needed" confirmed |

Every run fails on a policy or Trusted Types report, a page error, a dialog, a horizontal scroll bar or a control smaller than 44 px. The last two rules do not exist in `apps/admin/test/browser/watch.ts` today, which watches reports, errors, dialogs, failed requests and the page's elements: Task 1 builds them, with 8.1's definition of a control, and its links' rule gains the new paths as each task adds them.

### 8.11 The command line

`staff.js` gains the commands below; `STAFF_USAGE` lists each with its first line here. The rules of 3c 7.13 hold for all: the 10-second `lock_timeout` in every transaction, the audit row as the transaction's last statement, exit 1 with "<command>: <why>; nothing changed" when nothing was changed. Further:

- **Where they run.** All as the migrator, in the migrate service, as `enroll` runs: `docker compose -f infra/compose.yml run --rm migrate node dist/staff.js <command>`, and with `-T` after `--rm` for every command that reads standard input (`order-texts`, `order-document`, `address-check`, `handover-record`, `restore-import`): without it Compose attaches a terminal and a redirected file fails or arrives changed. So only a person with the server writes what the console cannot, and the API holds no path to a notification or a production.
- **What a command reads of the configuration.** Each command reads only the settings it uses, as `enroll` does, and never the API's whole configuration. The migrate service is given two more, neither required by Compose (`enroll` and the migrations run without them): `HOME_STATE` (from `API_HOME_STATE`) and `APP_ORIGIN` (from `API_APP_ORIGIN`). `order-open` needs both, to set `cross_border` and to resolve a link as the console's route does, and exits 1 with "order-open: HOME_STATE and APP_ORIGIN must be set for the migrate service; nothing changed" when one is missing.
- **Shared code.** A command enters a module only through its index. The moderation module's index exports for the command line, as plain functions that take the caller's transaction and no dependency of the module: `parseLink`, `insertOrder(tx, facts)` (the rows the console's route, `order-open` and the import all write) and the statement's data for `access-request`; the media module's index exports `imagePost(id)` (which post an image's address belongs to: opening an order, in the console's route and in `order-open`, resolves an image's link to its post through it), `preservedImages(tx, postId)` and `sealFiles(tx, sealActionId)` (section 5), since no file outside the media module may name `post_media`.
- **An alert is not skipped by using the shell.** A command whose console twin alerts every staff account inserts the same row of `alert_outbox` itself, as the migrator, in its transaction before the audit row: `order-open` writes `order_opened`, so that an order recorded from the shell, with or without Section C, is heard on every phone as one opened in the console is. `notify` pushes the row and the tick mails it (it is a kind whose mail is its sentence, W.4).
- **An order** is named by its id, or as `<state>:<reference>` (`DE:2026/123`) while exactly one order has that state and reference. Two authorities of one state may use the same reference (the record's key is the state, the authority and the reference): the command then exits 1 with "`order_ambiguous`: <n> orders of <CC> have this reference; name one by its id:" and their ids with each authority's name; nothing changed. **An instant** is written with its offset (`2026-10-10T01:00:12Z`, `2026-10-10T03:00:12+02:00`); a time without one is refused with "not an instant with an offset", since on the night the clocks go back a local time names two instants.
- **A free text** (`--how`, `--reason`, `--proceedings`, `--for`, `--basis`) is at most 500 characters, about the act and never about the person; the three texts of `order-texts` are read from standard input. `--asked` is no text: it takes kinds of `NOTIFICATION_SENT_KINDS`, as `--kinds` does.
- **A record entered again after a restore** names its gap (8.8, the class `record`): each command of the tables "Notifications to authorities" and "Productions and the hand-over" below takes `--gap <gap>`, and then closes that gap in its own transaction, as the migrator, with the disposition `imported`; a gap of another class, or one that is not open, is refused with "<command>: gap <id> is not an open gap of the class record; nothing changed".
- No command prints, takes or opens an image. None sends anything to an authority: the operator sends, the command records.
- Every line a command prints starts with its name. `{until}` is the notice's hold, printed as "held until <instant>" or "not held".

**Orders.**

| Command | Does | Prints |
|---|---|---|
| `order-open --kind <kind> --state <CC> --authority <text> --reference <text> --channel <mailbox\|portal\|post\|other> [--message <id>] [--received <instant>] [--sent <instant>] [--section-c] [--emergency] [--withhold] --link <url> ...` | opens an order whose time of receipt the console cannot take (one that came by a portal, by post or by courier; a mail that no row lists, with its delivery time read in the mail host's app), or after a restore; restricts nothing. `--channel` takes the four values of `ORDER_CHANNELS`, as the console's "How it arrived" does. `--section-c` forces `--withhold`, as the console and the database do. It writes the alert `order_opened` | "order-open: order <id> opened: <kind>, <CC>, reference <reference>, <n> items (<m> name no post). Receipt: <instant>; deadline <instant>." or "... Receipt not recorded: the console says deadline unknown." |
| `order-received <order> <instant>` | the receipt of an order opened without a message, in the console or here; once | "order-received: receipt <instant>; deadline <instant>." (or "deadline passed <n> minutes ago.") |
| `order-reopen <order> --how <text>` | clears the closing of an order that was closed by mistake or restored by a court, as the admin's "Reopen the order" does in the console; the event `reopened` | "order-reopen: order <id> is open again. Its times are unchanged." |
| `order-sent-at <order> <instant>` | the sending time the channel states; the clock runs from the earlier of the two | "order-sent-at: sent <instant>; the clock starts <instant>." |
| `order-due <order> <instant\|none>` | a DSA order's deadline as its text states it | "order-due: due <instant>." or "order-due: no deadline." |
| `order-verify <order> --how <text>` | the check: which register entry or, for a DSA order, which coordinator or which looked-up number; how checked; that every item is a link the order names. It may be run again and then moves the time of the check: items added after the last check are unverified until it is | "order-verify: order <id> verified at <instant>: <n> items compared with the order." |
| `order-issued <order> <instant> [--release]` | the order's own date and time, from which a removal order's hold is counted. Taken at any time for an order that holds nothing. Refused when the instant is after the receipt ("order-issued: an order is not issued after it was received; nothing changed"). While a notice of the order is held, an instant whose computed end is not in the future would release the notice within minutes, which cannot be taken back: the command then prints that end and changes nothing unless `--release` is given | "order-issued: issued <instant>; the notice is {until}." or "order-issued: with this date the hold would have ended on <instant>, and the author would be told at the next dispatch round. Nothing changed; give --release if that is what the order says." Refused with `already_released` only for an order with Section C whose notice was released or whose hold was ended |
| `order-texts <order> --reasons`, `order-texts <order> --redress` and `order-texts <order> --scope` | reads one text from standard input, under 3c 7.4's text rules, in the authority's words: its reasons, or its remedies as Section G states them (at most 4000 characters each), or the order's territorial scope (at most 500 characters; a DSA Article 9 order, Art. 9(5)). When the order has decisions whose author was told, each author is told of the addition: the command sets `author_mail` to `pending` again on every told `order` decision of the order, so that the dispatch round sends the mail `order_notice_added` (W.5), and the time of its event `texts_recorded` is what the notice prints as the time since when it holds the text (`orderTexts.textsAt`; no column of its own) | "order-texts: reasons recorded (<n> characters). <m> authors are sent an email that the notice has grown; the notice shows the text and this time from now." (or "... The notice is held: it will show the text once it is released, <instant>.") |
| `order-tell-rule <order> --when <at_effect\|never\|instant> --basis <text>` | for an order to provide information (DSA Art. 10(5)): whether and when the person named may be told of it, and the provision or the order's sentence that says so. It stores `tell_person` (`at_effect`, `never`, or `at_instant` for an instant, which goes into `tell_person_at`) and the basis in `hold_basis`, with the event `tell_person_set`. `STATE_FACTS` carries the home state's default (`tellPersonDefault`), which the command prints when `--when` is left out and nothing is changed. For a DSA Article 9 order the same fact is its hold (`order-hold-until`) | "order-tell-rule: the person is told <at effect\|at <instant>\|never>, on the basis recorded." |
| `order-told <order> --at <instant> --how <text>` | records that the operator told the person named in an order to provide information, from the contact address to the account's address, with the authority's reasons and remedies (the event `person_told`); refused while the rule is `never` or its instant is ahead | "order-told: recorded at <instant>." |
| `order-document <order> < file` | reads the order's document from standard input, records its SHA-256 and length, and keeps no byte of it: the document stays in the mailbox | "order-document: <sha256>, <bytes> bytes." |
| `order-scrutiny <order> requested --by <korbat\|author> --at <instant>` and `... decided --outcome <upheld\|annulled> --at <instant>` | the scrutiny of an order from another Member State (TCO Art. 4) | "order-scrutiny: request recorded." or "order-scrutiny: decided: annulled. An admin reinstates each item on its case, ground scrutiny, at once." (or "decided: upheld. The order stands.") |

**The hold, the preservation and access.**

| Command | Does | Prints |
|---|---|---|
| `order-hold-until <order> <instant\|none> --basis <text> --how <text>` | the end the issuing authority stated, with the provision or the order's sentence it rests on. **For a removal order** it replaces the computed end only when earlier: the Regulation's period caps it. **For a DSA order** it is the hold's end, of any length, earlier or later than one recorded before, and `none` records that the authority named no time; while none is recorded nothing is released. An instant that is not in the future would release the notice within minutes: the command then changes nothing unless `--release` is given, as `order-issued` | "order-hold-until: the notice is held until <instant>." or "... the computed end <instant> is earlier and stands." or "order-hold-until: the notice is held with no end. Every staff account is reminded daily until an end is recorded." |
| `order-hold-extend <order> --received <instant> --how <text>` | a removal order's one extension, {holdExtensionDays} days (TCO Art. 11(3)); also confirms one that an admin recorded in the console ("Extension received"), and is then recorded as its check. Refused when already extended from here, when released, and for a DSA order (whose later time is `order-hold-until`'s) | "order-hold-extend: the notice is held until <instant>. It cannot be extended again." or "order-hold-extend: the console's extension of <instant> is confirmed; the notice is held until <instant>." |
| `order-hold-end <order> --reason <not_authentic\|annulled\|released_by_authority\|not_ordered> --how <text>` | ends a hold at once. `not_ordered` is korbat's own mistake: the order had no non-disclosure decision and the record said it had (the notice then says so, W.2). An extension that an admin recorded in the console and that turns out not to be the authority's is undone with it too, but only on or after the day the first period ended: before that day ending the hold would tell early, so the command refuses `not_ordered` for an extended hold whose first end is ahead | "order-hold-end: the hold ended at <instant>. The next dispatch round tells the author, within about 10 minutes." |
| `order-preserve-extend <item> --until <instant> --by <text> --reference <text> --proceedings <text> --received <instant>` | moves `preserve_until` forward on an authority's or a court's request; never back | "order-preserve-extend: item <id> is preserved until <instant> (was <instant>)." |
| `order-access <item> --purpose <review\|complaint\|authority> --for <text>` | prints the preserved post's text, its alt texts and its images' ids, sizes and creation times (read through the media module's `preservedImages`; `post_media` holds no digest), and records the access: an audit row each time and, for `authority`, a production. For `complaint`, the author's own challenge, the operator sends that text by hand to the address of the author's account and to no other, after `address-check` answered yes for the mail that asked (W.9) | first "order-access: recorded, purpose <purpose>." (with "production <id>" for an authority), then the text between two marker lines |

**Notifications to authorities.**

| Command | Does | Prints |
|---|---|---|
| `records [--verify]` | lists what is owed and open; read-only, no audit row. With `--verify` it recomputes, from each record's row, the digest that the audit row of a later notification step or of a production holds, and exits 1 naming the first that differs | "records: <n> notifications owed, <n> archives not recorded as destroyed.", then a line each: the record's id, its case, duties, "owed since <instant>", "address held until <instant>" |
| `notification-open --case <id> --duty <dsa18_1\|dsa18_2\|tco14_5\|voluntary>` | opens a record for a check on content that no seal opened one for | "notification-open: record <id>, owed since <instant>." |
| `notification-facts <record>` (replaces `account-facts`) | prints the account's handle, address and creation time, the ids and times of the case's posts, and W.8's sentence of what korbat does not hold; records the reading as a production | the facts as "name: value" lines, then "notification-facts: recorded as production <id>." |
| `notification-sent <record> --authority <text> --channel <text> --duty <duty> ... --state <CC\|none> --basis <text> --own-reference <text> --kinds <kind,...> --at <instant> [--receipt <text>]` | records the notification: to whom, how, for which duties, which state is concerned and why (or `none` with the reason), what was sent by kind (the seven of section 6's `sent_kinds`, one constant of the contracts, `NOTIFICATION_SENT_KINDS`, with its CHECK: `handle`, `address`, `created_at`, `post_ids`, `post_times`, `report_time`, `files`), never by value; releases the address hold | "notification-sent: record <id> sent to <authority> at <instant>. The address hold is released." |
| `notification-declined <record> --reason <text>` | a check that ended in "no"; releases the hold | "notification-declined: record <id> closed. The address hold is released." |
| `notification-answer <record> --their-reference <text> --at <instant> [--answer <not_illegal\|illegal\|no_assessment\|other>]` | the authority's reference and the day of its answer, which 3d's release reads, and the answer as one word of section 6's closed list (`NOTIFICATION_ANSWERS` in the contracts): the record holds no sentence about a person | "notification-answer: recorded." |
| `notification-event <record> --kind <request\|answer\|note> --at <instant> --how <text> [--not-to-person [--until <instant>]]` | a later request or answer, with the authority's demand that the person not be told, whose end may be open | "notification-event: recorded." and, with the mark, "Until <instant\|further notice>, nothing about this may be told to the person: korbat's access-request draft marks the case." |

**Productions and the hand-over.**

| Command | Does | Prints |
|---|---|---|
| `production-account <order> --handle <handle> --asked <kind,...>` | for an order to provide information: prints the three facts korbat gives of the account (handle, address, creation time) and W.8's sentences, and records the production with what the order asked for, by kind and never as a text (`--asked`, a list of the seven kinds of `NOTIFICATION_SENT_KINDS`, as `notification-sent --kinds`; section 6's `productions.asked`, under the same CHECK). Refused (`tell_rule_missing`) until `order-tell-rule` has recorded whether the person may be told | the facts, then "production-account: recorded as production <id>, instrument dsa_art10." |
| `address-check --handle <handle> < address` | answers whether the address read from standard input is the address of that account, and prints no address: for a person who writes to the contact address about an order's notice (W.9). The address is read from standard input so that it is in no shell history; one audit row (`address_checked`, the account as its target, no address in it) | "address-check: yes" or "address-check: no" |
| `handover-list <seal action> --instrument <notification\|demand\|court_order\|seizure> --authority <text> --reference <text>` | opens a production and prints the stored names of the image files of that one seal's posts, one a line, read through the media module's `sealFiles`; refuses (`handover_mismatch`) when the rows differ from the seal's own counts | on standard error "handover-list: production <id>: <n> files of <m> posts."; on standard output the list, whose first line names the production |
| `docker compose --profile handover run --rm -T -v <directory>:/handover handover < list > digests` | the `handover` service (section 5): copies exactly the listed files, byte for byte, into `/handover/handover-<production>.zip`, uncompressed. The directory is given on the command line, since the service has no bind mount in `compose.yml` (an empty one would stop every Compose command of the stack); it is on an encrypted volume and owned by uid 1000, the user the service runs as | a line a file "<name> <bytes> <sha256>", then "archive <bytes> <sha256>"; "handover: refused: <name> is outside the store" and no archive otherwise |
| `handover-record <production> --channel <text> --receipt <text> < digests` | records each file's name, size and digest, the archive's, the channel and the authority's receipt. With `seizure`: `--taken`, and no digests | "handover-record: production <id>: <n> files, archive <sha256>. Delete every copy once the authority confirms, then run handover-destroyed." |
| `handover-destroyed <production> --how <text>` | records that every copy outside the store is destroyed | "handover-destroyed: recorded at <instant>." |

**Recovery and the counts.**

| Command | Does | Prints |
|---|---|---|
| `audit-export [--after <place>]` | the keeper's download, from the server: the rows after that place as JSON lines on standard output. It writes its copy mark itself, with the audit row `copy_marked`, since its output goes to a file on the server and no browser has to confirm it: the mark carries the system actor's id (`copy_marks.staff_id` is never null), which is never "the caller's newest mark" of a staff member in the console | on standard error "audit-export: places <from> to <to>, <n> rows." |
| `restore-import [<place>:<hash> ...] < files` | after a restore (section 7, A restore), with the keeper's files concatenated on standard input: checks the lines against the restored head, writes the plain records again and the gaps; never a decision or a measure. What it takes of the files is said under this table | "restore-import: places <from> to <to> continue the head <place>:<hash> (<n> earlier lines checked, not imported)."; "written again: <n> orders, <n> items, <n> events, <n> notification records, <n> productions."; "gaps, in the order to take them again:" and a numbered line each (class, target, "first done <instant>"); "The app's site stays closed until gaps 1 to <k> are closed on the console's After a restore screen."; "Not in these files: anything after <instant>, the keeper's last download. Compare the contact-point mailbox since then with the orders above; open what is missing with order-open."; for a hole, "restore-import: the files hold nothing between place <a> and place <b>: what happened there is not imported", exit 0, the warning in the `restore_imported` row's metadata. Refused whole (`tail_not_continuous`, exit 1): input that begins above the head's plus one, a line whose own hash does not recompute, a line at the head's place or at a kept place with another hash: "restore-import: refused: line <n> does not continue the restored head <place>:<hash>; nothing was written" (a changed file, not a missing one) |
