| `tco-counts <year>` | the figures of TCO Art. 7(3) and 21(1) for that calendar year in UTC, from rows; read-only, no audit row | a line a figure: "removal orders received: <n> (<CC>: <n>, ...; from another Member State: <n>)"; "items disabled following a removal order: <n>"; "minutes from the clock's start to the disabling of an order's last item: <list>; outside the hour: <n>; with an item still open: <n>" (an effect given while the clock was stopped counts as inside); "Annex III sent, by ground: ..." (the three of `ANNEX3_GROUNDS`); "scrutiny: requested <n>; annulled <n>; upheld <n>"; "reinstated, by ground: ..."; "access to preserved content by an authority: <n>"; "restricted by korbat as terrorist content without an order: <n>"; "referrals closed: <n>". A figure the rows cannot give prints "not recorded", never 0 |

**What `restore-import` takes of the keeper's files.** Every file from the one that holds the restored head's place onward: a keeper's file covers the places between two visits, so the head lies inside one and never at its edge. Lines at or below the head are not imported but checked: each hash recomputes, each names the line before it, and the line at the head's place has the head's hash, which ties the files to this database. The first line above the head must be the head's plus one and name the head's hash. A hole further on is reported, not fatal: the import takes the unbroken run from the head and stops at the first line that does not name its predecessor, so that one missing download does not make every file useless at the moment it is needed.

`text-verify` (3c 7.13) recomputes the level `disabled` with the others and reports an order item whose `disabled_at` has no measure, and a measure in force whose item has none. It compares every `order` decision with its `order_effected` row and the `order_corrected` rows since, as it compares the others with `case_decided`, and reports every console event and every gap closed as not needed that has no audit row (sections 6 and 7).

## 9. The app (`apps/app`)

3c 9 and 3e 9 stay. 3f adds no screen: one kind of row on Cases, one way the decision's screen renders, three sentences elsewhere. They live in `ORDER_TEXTS` in `apps/app/src/lib/orders.ts` (keys in brackets); the notice's own sentences are `ORDER_NOTICE` of `@korbat/rules` (W.2), which `statementParagraphs` renders and the app never words itself. Times print with 3e 9's `standingTime`.

### 9.1 A disabled post, for everyone

A post at the level `disabled` is, to every reader of the app, a post that is not there. Its author is one of those readers: the app holds no branch for them, because the API answers them as it answers a stranger (section 6, The readers).

| Where | What is shown, to everyone, its author included |
|---|---|
| The post's own page (`/post/<id>`) | the app's words for `not_found`, "That no longer exists.", under HTTP status 404 (section 5), exactly as for a post id that never existed |
| A slot that names it: the post a reply answers, a quoted post | the tombstone "This post is unavailable." (`PostCard.tsx:33`), as for a withheld or removed post of someone else |
| Every list: the profile, Home, Latest, Explore, bookmarks | absent |
| Its images | not served (404); a grid or viewer that held one shows its ordinary failed-image state |
| Counters (a profile's posts, a parent's replies) | unchanged: a count that moved would tell |
| Like, repost, bookmark, reply, quote, report, delete on it | `not_found` |

No chip, no placeholder with a reason, no banner and no status 451 exists for it: each would say that something was done to this item, which during a non-disclosure period is the one thing korbat may not say, and the app cannot know whether one runs. A person who reported the post reads nothing new: their item keeps the line it had, since an order answers only its own item (section 6).

A DSA Article 9 order that does not withhold gives the level `removed` instead. Its author then sees the post as after any removal, with the chip [chip.order] "Removed: an authority's order" linking to the notice, and everyone else sees what the table says. Such an order never holds its notice: an order whose author must not be told always withholds, for every kind of order, by the form and by the database (section 6), so a post at the level `removed` for an order is always one whose author was told in the same transaction. And the chip's read, `chipFacts`, takes the fragment `toldToAuthor` like every other read on the author's side, so no later path can make a chip name a decision its author was not told of.

### 9.2 While the notice is held

Nothing, anywhere, on any path. This is the first invariant of 3f, and it is wider than the order's own texts: **while a notice is held, the post reads to its author on every screen, in every statement, mail and export exactly as a post that no order touched would read in that state, and where that cannot be done truthfully the text is held with the notice.**

| Path | What the author meets while the notice is held |
|---|---|
| Cases | no row for the order's decision, and none for a decision taken about the same post during the hold |
| The post, its lists, its images | 9.1's table: absent, like a post that is not there; no chip, since the chip's read is filtered by `toldToAuthor` |
| A decision of korbat's own about the post, taken during the hold (a report answered under a rule, the answer to an appeal the author made earlier) | held with the notice: recorded, and told only at the release, with its own statement. So no statement says "another decision already keeps this post from being shown", and none says that a restriction was lifted while the post stays gone |
| A decision about the post that the author was told of before the order | its screen shows what it showed before: `snapshot_withheld` is not widened for the author's side, so the copied text stays and no line says that content was erased. The post's images on that screen are served to nobody while an order disables the post, the author included: each is drawn exactly as the app draws an image that could not be loaded, in the words the app already has for that case and no other, and nothing on the screen says why while the notice is held (the row of section 7's table of readers; section 2's row on what else is held with a notice flags it for counsel) |
| A notice that went out before a missed "must not be told" was set on the order (8.5, block 10) | withdrawn until the release: its row leaves Cases, its mail is not sent if it had not gone yet, and the post is kept from its author like any held one. What the author already read cannot be taken back, which is a known limit (section 2) |
| Mails | none; and the mail that confirms an account's deletion has one wording for every account (W.5) |
| The export | neither the post nor a line about it, nor any decision held with the notice |
| A data request | the operator's draft leaves the order, the post and every held decision out of what is sent, and marks the case for the operator alone (`access.noticeHeld`, W.8) |
| Every route that names the post | `not_found`, as for a post that does not exist; no code of 3f can reach the author |

What the author can notice is the gap: a post of theirs is missing from their profile and its address says that it no longer exists. And one delay: an appeal the author made before the order, about an earlier decision on the same post, is answered to them only at the release, which for a removal order can be twelve weeks and a day later and for another order as late as the authority's time. korbat's terms say beforehand that both can happen and that korbat tells them when the law lets it (W.6); nothing in the app says so at the post, because that would be telling.

A person who writes to the contact address about a missing post is answered by a person, from the runbook (W.9), with a sentence that is true for every missing post and discloses nothing.

### 9.3 Once the author is told

At the release (at once, when no hold runs), the decision joins the author's reads like any other and the mail `order_notice` goes out (W.5). Every decision about the post that was held with the notice is told in the same step, each with its own row and statement; a time to appeal that such a decision gives counts from that day, not from the day it was taken (section 6).

- **Cases** (`/settings/cases`, "About you"). A row from `CASE_ROW_WORDS`, which gains: "a post disabled on an authority's order", "a post removed on an authority's order" (not withheld), "an authority's order lifted". The row shows the day the author was told and the post's creation time; where the two days differ from the disabling's, the notice says why.
- **The notice** (`/settings/cases/[id]`). For a decision of the kind `order` the screen is titled "Notice: an authority's order", not "Decision", and `statementParagraphs` renders the notice from `ORDER_NOTICE` (W.2) in place of a statement of reasons. Unlike a statement, the notice is read fresh at every opening, since it grows: the authority's reasons and remedies appear when korbat has recorded them, and later the preservation's end. The author is not left to find an addition by reloading: when korbat records the authority's reasons, remedies or scope for a notice that was already told, it sends the mail `order_notice_added` (W.5), and the notice prints the time of that record (`orderTexts.textsAt`) above the authority's block, because a time limit of the author's runs from receiving them (W.2 `textsSince`, `scrutiny`). Under the notice [notice.fresh] "korbat adds to this notice what it learns: the authority's reasons and the ways to challenge the order as soon as it has recorded them, and what happens to the content. korbat sends you an email each time it adds the authority's words, and the notice says since when it has held them."
- **No content.** The screen shows the post's creation time and its address as text, never its text or images (`DecisionView.content` is null while the measure is in force): [notice.noContent] "korbat does not show the post here. While it is disabled korbat keeps it only for the purposes the law names, and shows it only to the authorities and courts that law names."
- **No appeal.** The screen has no "Appeal" control. In its place [notice.noAppeal] "This is not korbat's decision, so korbat's appeal does not apply. The notice says where the order can be challenged." A request to the appeal route answers `not_appealable`, worded the same.
- **korbat's own mistake.** [notice.wrongItem] "If the order does not name this post, korbat made a mistake that it can correct itself. Write to {contact} from the email address of your korbat account, with the address of the post and the reference this notice shows." (`EXPO_PUBLIC_CONTACT_EMAIL`, as 3c 9's Contact section; this is the app's own sentence, so it can print the address, where the notice's paragraphs of `@korbat/rules` name "the address under Contact in korbat's Terms" and print none, W.2.) The sender's address is how korbat knows that the writer is the author, and the reference is how it knows that the writer has read a notice (W.9).
- **Afterwards.** When an admin reinstates, the post is again what its other decisions leave it (visible, when there is none), returns to every list by itself, and the notice gains its reinstatement paragraph. When the preservation ended first, the notice says that the content was erased and cannot return. A post that a DSA Article 9 order kept from its author because they were not to be told stays kept from them after the release, for as long as the order's measure stands; the notice says so (W.2 `heldUntilNow`).

### 9.4 The deletion screen, errors and accessibility

- **Settings, Account, "Delete account"** gains, under its existing warning, [delete.hold] "Two things the law can oblige korbat to keep for a time (Terms, Your data). If korbat has sealed images of this account, it keeps your email address, and nothing else, for up to {reportHoldDays} days, until it has reported them to the authorities or has decided that it need not. And a post that an authority's order obliges korbat to preserve is kept until that time ends. Everything else is deleted at once, as described above." It is shown to every account, since it is a rule and not a finding about this one; it promises no report, and it names no post.
- **Deleting a disabled post.** The author cannot: the post is not shown to them, and the route answers `not_found` as for any post that is not there. Deleting the account works as always; what is preserved outlives it (W.6), and the mail that confirms the deletion says so in the one wording every account gets (W.5).
- **Errors.** The app's message table gains `not_appealable`: [notice.noAppeal]'s first sentence.
- **Accessibility.** The notice is a `role="article"` whose title is a level-1 header; each paragraph is text, and the authority's words stand in a block labelled "The authority's words" so a screen reader announces where korbat's sentences end. The row on Cases says what it is in words, not by colour.

## 10. Contracts

`packages/contracts/src/orders.ts` (new), `moderation.ts`, `staff.ts`, `social.ts`, `account.ts`, and `packages/rules`. As in D 10 and E3 10, each table is the one source of its words and numbers, and a test pins each. A member that ends in `At` is an instant (ISO, UTC); one that ends in `On` is a UTC day. No request has a transform. People are handles; no staff answer has a member named like `email`, `address`, `userId`, `accountId` or `actorId` (the route-table test's `PERSONAL` runs over the new answers too).

### 10.1 Constants

**`ORDER_RULES`** mirrors the SQL function `order_rules()` (section 6): one constant mirrors one function, as 3e's `STANDING_RULES` mirrors `standing_rules()`. It holds every column of the function, in camel case under the same word, and a database test compares the two name by name. A number the database reads lives here and nowhere else: the alert numbers and the retention periods among them, which no function holds as a literal. Each is a cautious default, flagged in section 2, and one line to change in each place. Task 1 builds the constant with the function.

| Member | Value | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| `hourMinutes` | 60 | the clock of a `tco_removal` (TCO Art. 3(3)); a restart is a fresh 60; the deadline a message would have as an order |
| `targetMinutes` | 55 | the console's target until 3d purges edge copies |
| `itemsPerAction` | 10 | the most items one "Disable for an order" confirms, and the most links one request names |
| `decisionsPerDay` | 50 | `order_cap`: order decisions of one staff account in 24 hours |
| `recordsPerDay` | 20 | order records one staff account opens in 24 hours |
| `holdDays`, `holdExtensionDays` | 43, 42 | `order_hold_end`, for a `tco_removal` alone (7.10) |
| `holdWarningDays` | 7 | `hold_ending`: every staff account is alerted this long before a held notice is released |
| `unconfirmedHoldHours` | null | no shorter hold for a tick the command line has not confirmed (section 2, row 25); a number here would cap it |
| `preserveMonths`, `preserveMarginDays` | 6, 1 | `order_preserve_until` |
| `reportHoldDays`, `reportAlertHours` | 7, 24 | `auth.address_held_until`; `report_owed` |
| `staleHours` | 2 | a message read again after an outage raises no push |
| `quietMinutes` | 15 | a further message raises no first push of its own while an unhandled message's first push is this young (7.1); 0 switches the bound off |
| `escalateMinutes`, `remindMinutes` | 15, 45 | `contact_mail_escalated`; the first `order_due` (the second is at the deadline) |
| `channelDownMinutes`, `pushOwedMinutes` | 3, 60 | when the channel reads as down, and for how long a row is still owed its push (7.1, 7.2) |
| `copyOverdueHours`, `handoverAlertDays` | 48, 7 | `copy_overdue`; `handover_undestroyed` |
| `messageKeepDays`, `recordKeepYears`, `gapKeepMonths` | 30, 3, 12 | the retention steps of 7.18 |

**`ALERT_RULES`** holds only what the database never reads, and no number is in both tables: `pollSeconds` 20 (`notify`'s round), `tickSeconds` 30 (the tick's), `mailRetryHours` 24 (how long the tick tries a row's mail). The draft's `pushAttempts` is gone with the cap it named (7.1), and `dueEveryMinutes` with the schedule it named (7.2).

**`ALERT_KINDS`**: the closed list of alerts, the CHECK of `alert_outbox.kind` written from it. Each entry holds `priority` (3 or 5), `scope`, `legs`, `mail` and `facts` (the names of its numbers and times, at most 6). `mail` says who renders the kind's mail: `sentence`, the kind's own entry of `ALERT_TEXTS`, which the tick can mail again from the row's `facts`; `own`, a mail of 3c or 3e that says more than `facts` holds and that `staffAlert` alone sends (7.2); or none, for a kind that is pushed only. **`ALERT_TEXTS`**, beside it, holds a title and one sentence a kind, and for a `sentence` kind the mail's line (section 18); `notify` imports both tables.

| Kind | Priority | Scope | Legs | Mail | Written by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `contact_mail` | 5 | `on_call` | push, mail | `sentence` | the `contact_messages` trigger; `alert_steps()` for an order whose clock restarted |
| `contact_mail_escalated`, `order_due` | 5 | `all` | push, mail | `sentence` | `alert_steps()` |
| `order_unverified`, `report_owed` | 5 | `all` | push, mail | `sentence` | `alert_steps()`, daily |
| `hold_ending`, `handover_undestroyed`, `copy_overdue` | 3 | `all` | push, mail | `sentence` | `alert_steps()`, daily |
| `alert_test` | 3 | `all` | push | none | `alert_steps()`, daily |
| `order_opened`, `order_corrected`, `order_effected`, `order_reinstated`, `copy_taken` | 3 | `all` | push, mail | `sentence` | `staffAlert`, after the commit; `order-open` for its own `order_opened` |
| `mailbox_unreachable` | 5 | `all` | push, mail | `sentence` | the tick's own insert |
| `alert_channel_down` | 3 | `all` | mail | `sentence` | the tick's own insert |
| `moderation_urgent` | 5 | `on_call` | push, mail | `own` | `staffAlert` (D 7.12) |
| `moderation_waiting`, `moderation_burst`, `moderation_reads`, `moderation_reveal`, `moderation_csam_lifted` | 3 | `all` | push, mail | `own` | `staffAlert` (D 7.12, E3 7.14) |

The mail of every kind goes to every staff account whatever the scope: the scope chooses the push topic alone. `moderation_urgent` wakes the phone on call and repeats as D 7.12 says; the others of 3c are read in the morning. The facts of each kind, under these names in the row and in the braces of section 18's sentences: `contact_mail` and `contact_mail_escalated`: `receivedAt`, `dueAt` (a restart's row for an order has `dueAt` alone); `order_due`: `dueAt`, `minutesLeft`, and `itemsOpen` for an order; `order_opened`: `items`, `dueAt` (left out without a deadline); `order_corrected`: `count` (how many members changed), `at`; `order_effected`: `items`, `at`, `itemsOpen`, `urgent` (0 or 1); `order_reinstated`: `at`; `order_unverified`: `count`, `textsMissing`, `holdsOpen`, `oldestAt`; `hold_ending`: `count`, `at`; `report_owed`: `count`, `oldestAt`; `handover_undestroyed`: `count`; `copy_overdue`: `hours`; `copy_taken`: `at`, `rows`; `mailbox_unreachable` and `alert_channel_down`: `since`; `alert_test`: `at`; of 3c's and 3e's kinds, for the push alone: `moderation_urgent`: `count`, `oldestAt`; `moderation_waiting` and `moderation_burst`: `count`; `moderation_reads`: `limit`; `moderation_reveal` and `moderation_csam_lifted`: `at`. A sentence may also print a number of `ORDER_RULES`, which is no fact of the row.

**The ledger's kinds.**

- `ACTION_KINDS` gains `disable_by_order` and `reinstate_by_order`, in the order of the database's enum. `MEASURES.disable_by_order`: `interim` false, `final` false (only an `order` decision holds it), `lifts` null, `target` `post`. `MEASURES.reinstate_by_order`: `lifts` `disable_by_order`, `target` `post`. `MEASURE_KINDS` then holds 14 and `REVERSAL_KINDS` 10. The labels are section 18's.
- The decision kinds gain `order`, the sources `order`, the intake kinds `order`, the intake channels `authority`; `ModerationLevel` and the post states gain `disabled`. `PostView` never carries a `disabled` post, so the app's types gain nothing.
- `ORDER_DECISION`: what an `order` decision is by its order's kind, which `decisions_record` forces and `OrderEffectedParams` therefore does not carry.

| Order kind | Category | Keyword | Preserves | `withholdFromAuthor` | Effect before it is verified | The hold's end |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tco_removal` | `STATEMENT_CATEGORY_RISK_FOR_PUBLIC_SECURITY` | `KEYWORD_TERRORIST_CONTENT` | yes | forced true | yes: the hour runs | computed (`regulation`) |
| `dsa_art9` | `STATEMENT_CATEGORY_NOT_SPECIFIED_NOTICE` | none | no | forced true whenever Section C is set; otherwise as the order's text says, false when the form leaves it (the author then sees the post as removed and is told at once) | no, but for an admin's signed `urgent` | what the authority states (`authority`), none while it has stated none |

An `order` decision is never exported to the Transparency Database (DSA Art. 17(5)). The category of a `tco_removal` is the Regulation's own subject. That of a `dsa_art9` is a filler for a column that may not be null: korbat did not assess the content, no order form states a category, and nothing reads the column for such a decision (the counts read the order's kind).

**Closed lists**, each a `text` CHECK of section 6 written from the list and tested against it:

| List | Values |
|---|---|
| `ORDER_KINDS` | `tco_removal`, `dsa_art9`, `dsa_art10` |
| `ORDER_CHANNELS` | `mailbox`, `portal`, `post`, `other`: how an order arrived. The one list of the form's "How it arrived", of `OrderOpenRequest.channel`, of the correction's `channel` and of `order-open --channel` (7.4) |
| `CONTACT_DISPOSITIONS` | `order`, `referral`, `first_order_notice`, `preservation_request`, `scrutiny_decision`, `clarification`, `not_an_order`; the close route takes all but `order` |
| `ORDER_CONSOLE_EVENTS` | `annex3_sent`, `grounds_ceased`, `clarification_received`, `annex2_sent`, `receipt_confirmed`, `closed_without_effect`, `reopened`, `hold_extended`: the kinds `order_event_record` writes and the events route takes |
| `ORDER_CLI_EVENTS` | `verified`, `issued_at_set`, `texts_recorded`, `document_recorded`, `received_set`, `sent_at_set`, `due_set`, `hold_extended`, `hold_ended`, `hold_until_set`, `preservation_extended`, `scrutiny_requested`, `scrutiny_decided`, `reopened`, `tell_person_set`, `person_told`, `imported` |
| `ORDER_CORRECTION_RULES` | `entry`, `caution`, `admin`: the ground of a `corrected` event (7.4) |
| `ORDER_ITEM_CLOSE_REASONS` | `no_post`, `not_named`, `withdrawn` (7.4) |
| `ORDER_LINK_KINDS` | `post`, `image`, `account`, `other` (7.4) |
| `ANNEX3_GROUNDS` | `force_majeure` (TCO Art. 3(7)), `manifest_error`, `insufficient_information` (Art. 3(8)) |
| `ORDER_GROUNDS` | `withdrawn`, `annulled`, `scrutiny`, `not_authentic`, `wrong_item`: the `orderGround` of a reinstatement |
| `ORDER_CLOSE_GROUNDS` | `ORDER_GROUNDS` and `not_addressed` |
| `HOLD_END_REASONS` | `not_authentic`, `annulled`, `released_by_authority`, `not_ordered` (korbat's own mistake: the order held no such statement) |
| `HOLD_BASES` | `regulation` (a removal order: the computed end), `authority` (a DSA order: the end its authority states) |
| `TELL_PERSON_RULES` | `at_effect`, `at_instant`, `never`: when the person a `dsa_art10` order names may be told (7.14) |
| `SCRUTINY_OUTCOMES` | `upheld`, `annulled` |
| `NOTIFICATION_DUTIES` | `dsa18_1`, `dsa18_2`, `tco14_5`, `voluntary` |
| `NOTIFICATION_SENT_KINDS` | `handle`, `address`, `created_at`, `post_ids`, `post_times`, `report_time`, `files`: what a notification sent, by kind and never by value; the one list of `notification-sent --kinds` and of the CHECK on `sent_kinds`, and of `production-account --asked` and the CHECK on `productions.asked` (what an order asked for, by kind and never as a text) |
| `NOTIFICATION_ANSWERS` | `not_illegal`, `illegal`, `no_assessment`, `other`: an authority's answer to a notification; the one list of `notification-answer --answer` and of the CHECK on `answer`, a word and never a text |
| `NOTIFY_ERRORS` | `imap_connect`, `imap_timeout`, `imap_tls`, `imap_login`, `imap_folder`, `imap_protocol`, `push_connect`, `push_refused`: the eight words of `notify_state.error` (7.1) |
| `PRODUCTION_INSTRUMENTS` | `notification`, `demand`, `court_order`, `seizure`, `dsa_art10`, `preserved_access` |
| `PRESERVED_PURPOSES` | `review`, `complaint`, `authority` (TCO Art. 6(1)) |
| `GAP_CLASSES` | `order_effect`, `seal_final`, `seal_interim`, `standing`, `restriction`, `reversal`, `no_measure`, `record`, in the order of 7.17 |
| `GAP_DISPOSITIONS` | `reapplied`, `not_needed`, `imported` |

The model's list of events names `received_set`, `sent_at_set`, `due_set` and `hold_until_set` nowhere: its commands `order-received`, `order-sent-at`, `order-due` and `order-hold-until` need an event each, and these four are it; section 6's table of kinds and its CHECK hold them, with every other value of these lists in the same spelling. `reopened` and `hold_extended` are in both lists: an admin writes them in the console and the operator at the shell. The events the database alone writes are `opened`, `corrected` (by `order_correct`), `effect_given`, `reinstated`, `notice_released` and `preservation_ended`, and a `hold_ended` with `not_ordered` when a correction unsets Section C. A scrutiny decision's ground is one of `SCRUTINY_OUTCOMES`, and an Annex III's one of the three `ANNEX3_GROUNDS`: the console offers exactly these, and each has exactly one restart (7.5).

**`STATE_FACTS[code]`**, by ISO code, and `HOME_STATE` picks one: `name`; `authorities` (each `id`, `name` and `registerEntry`, the words of the Commission's register to check an order against); `annexCopy` (the address that gets a copy of Annex II and III); `notificationIntake` (`name`, `how`); `languages` (the contact point's); `yearlyFiling` (a runbook line or null); `tellPersonDefault` (one of `TELL_PERSON_RULES` or null: the state's default for an order to provide information, which `order-tell-rule` prints, 7.14). The console's authority list for an order from another state offers that state's entry when the table has one, and a typed name otherwise. Germany's entry is filled by Task 2 from the sources section 3 cites, as the worked example; a member without a verified value is null, the console prints `state.unknown` for it, and the going-public gate lists it. No rule of the database reads the table; `cross_border` is computed by the API from `HOME_STATE` when an order is opened.

**Others.**

- `ORDER_ITEMS_MAX` 50 (an order's positions); `ORDER_ITEM_PREVIEW` 80; `AUDIT_EXPORT_MAX` 50,000; `CONTACT_PAGE_SIZE` 50; `ORDER_SCRUTINY_HOURS` 48 (TCO Art. 4(4): the API computes `scrutinyUntil` from it, and nothing in the database reads it).
- Bounds of an order's strings: `authority` and `reference` 1 to 200 characters, one line, under `text.ts`'s rules (no control character, none of the invisible direction and spacing characters); a link at most 500 characters, stored as named; `verified_how`, `hold_basis` and `scope` at most 500 each (a production's `asked` is no string: a list of `NOTIFICATION_SENT_KINDS`); `reasons` and `redress` at most 4,000 each.
- `RETENTION` gains `contactMessageDays`, `orderPreserveMonths`, `orderRecordYears` and `restoreGapMonths`, each read from `ORDER_RULES` (`messageKeepDays`, `preserveMonths`, `recordKeepYears`, `gapKeepMonths`) and typed nowhere else (section 2, row 42); the terms' table test holds section 18's sentences to them. The draft's `authorityRecordYears` is gone: `order_rules()` has one `record_keep_years`, for an order's records and for an authority's.

### 10.2 Staff requests and answers

Every id in a path is a lower-case uuid (`LowerCaseUuid`); every object is strict.

| Route | Request | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/staff/contact` | query `cursor?`, `open?` (`1`) | `ContactPage` |
| `POST /api/staff/contact/:message/close` | `ContactCloseRequest` `{ disposition }`, one of `CONTACT_DISPOSITIONS` but `order` | 204 |
| `GET /api/staff/orders` | none | `OrdersPage` |
| `POST /api/staff/orders` | `OrderOpenRequest` | 201 `OrderView`; 409 `order_exists` with `orderId` |
| `POST /api/staff/orders/:order/items` | `OrderItemsRequest` `{ links }`, 1 to 10 | 201 `OrderView` |
| `POST /api/staff/orders/:order/correct` | `OrderCorrectRequest` `{ facts }`, and `stepUp` where the rule is `admin` (7.4) | 201 `OrderView` |
| `GET /api/staff/orders/:order` | none | `OrderView` |
| `POST /api/staff/orders/:order/events` | `OrderEventRecordedParams` and `stepUp` | 201 `OrderView` |
| `POST /api/staff/orders/:order/effect` | `OrderEffectedParams` and `stepUp` | 201 `OrderView` |
| `GET /api/staff/restore` | none | `RestoreView` |
| `POST /api/staff/restore/gaps/:gap/close` | `GapClosedParams` and `stepUp` | 201 `RestoreView` |
| `POST /api/staff/audit/export` | `AuditExportedParams` `{ after, through }` and `stepUp` | JSON lines of `AuditExportLine` (10.5), with the file name `korbat-audit-<from>-<to>.jsonl` |
| `POST /api/staff/audit/export/mark` | `AuditMarkRequest` `{ through, hash }`: a place and the 64 hex characters of its hash | 204; 409 `copy_mismatch` |
| `GET /api/staff/audit` | `AuditPageQuery` gains `key?` (a key's id), `from?` and `to?` (instants) | `AuditPage`, which gains `copy` (`{ lastPlace }`, the caller's own newest copied place, or null); an `AuditEntry` gains `keyId`, null without a step-up |
| `GET /api/staff/me` | none | `StaffMe` gains `homeState` (a key of `STATE_FACTS`, or null) and `contactPoint` (the address, or null) |
| `POST /api/staff/cases/:case/decisions`, `POST /api/staff/decisions/:decision/review` | gain the query member `gap?` (7.17) | unchanged |
| `GET /api/posts/:id/status` (the app's side, no session) | none | 204 or 404, no body |

The console's page takes no build argument (`infra/caddy/Dockerfile:29`), so it learns the home state and the contact point from `StaffMe` and reads `STATE_FACTS` from the contracts by that key: the form's "the home state first", the register entries of an order's screen and Annex II's "Contact point" and "Copy to" lines have no other source. A key is named by its id alone, in the query and in an entry: the console knows no key's name, and `staff.js keys` prints which id is whose.

**`OrderOpenRequest`**:

| Member | Shape |
|---|---|
| `messageId` | a uuid, or null for "The message is not listed" (7.4) |
| `channel` | one of `ORDER_CHANNELS`, required: the form's "How it arrived". `mailbox` whenever `messageId` is set, which the schema refines and the database forces; with `messageId` null, what the person picked, stored as sent |
| `kind` | one of `ORDER_KINDS` |
| `memberState` | two upper-case letters; refused unless it is an EU Member State's code (a list of the contracts) |
| `authority`, `reference` | strings under 10.1's bounds |
| `links` | 0 to 10 strings of at most 500 characters, without repeats; none for a `dsa_art10`, at least one otherwise |
| `sectionC` | a boolean, required, with no default: "the author must not be told", Section C of a removal order or the same statement in a DSA order's text. The form's third choice, "I cannot tell", is sent as true |
| `emergency` | a boolean, as the order shows it |
| `withholdFromAuthor` | a boolean; must be true for a `tco_removal` and whenever `sectionC` is true, and false for a `dsa_art10`. The database forces the same |

**`OrderCorrectRequest`**: `{ facts, stepUp? }`. `facts` is a strict object with at least one member, of `kind`, `memberState`, `authority`, `reference`, `channel`, `sectionC`, `emergency`, `withholdFromAuthor` (each as in `OrderOpenRequest`) and `closeItems` (1 to 10 entries `{ itemId, reason }`, `reason` one of `ORDER_ITEM_CLOSE_REASONS`, no item twice). It holds only what changes; `crossBorder` is the API's, set with `memberState`. `channel` is one of `ORDER_CHANNELS`, correctable under `entry` before the first effect and under `admin` after it (7.4), and taken only for an order opened without a message (`invalid_request` otherwise). The same refinement holds: `sectionC` true never comes with `withholdFromAuthor` false.

**`ContactPage`**: `{ items, nextCursor }`, each item `id`, `folder` (`inbox`, `junk`), `receivedAt`, `seenAt`, `stale`, `disposition` (or null), `handledAt`, `handledBy` (a staff handle or null) and `orders` (the ids of orders opened from it).

**`OrdersPage`**: `{ open, closed }`, each a list of `OrderSummary`: `id`, `kind`, `memberState`, `authority`, `reference`, `openedAt`, `state`, `clock`, `dueAt`, `closedAt`, `closeGround`, `items`, `itemsInForce`, `verified`, `noticesHeld`.

- `state`: `running`, `stopped`, `overdue`, `unknown_deadline`, `given_effect`, `closed`, or `no_clock` for a DSA order without effect. A removal order is `given_effect` only when it is given effect in full: while an item is open it stays `running`, `stopped`, `overdue` or `unknown_deadline`.
- `clock`: null for a DSA order, else `{ clockStart, stopped, stoppedAt, stopGround, deadlineAt, targetAt, itemsOpen, firstEffectAt, lastEffectAt, withinHour }`, each null where the view's column is; `stoppedAt` and `stopGround` are the newest Annex III's while the clock is stopped, read from the order's events (7.5). The list prints its clock lines from it, without reading each order.
- `closedAt`, `closeGround`: the closing and its ground (one of `ORDER_CLOSE_GROUNDS`), null while the order is open, and null again after a reopening.

**`OrderView`**: the summary and

| Member | Holds |
|---|---|
| `channel`, `messageId`, `receivedAt`, `sentAt`, `issuedAt`, `sectionC`, `emergency`, `crossBorder`, `withholdFromAuthor`, `preserve` | the row |
| `openedBy`, `openedVia`, `importedAt` | a staff handle or null; `console` or `cli` |
| `tellPerson` | for a `dsa_art10`: `{ rule, at }` (`rule` one of `TELL_PERSON_RULES`, `at` the instant of `at_instant`), or null while it is not recorded; null for every other kind |
| `items` | each `id`, `position`, `link`, `linkKind` (one of `ORDER_LINK_KINDS`), `addedAt`, `unverified`, `postId`, `post` (null when the link named none, else `{ authorHandle, state, images, preview, createdAt, deletedOn }`, `preview` null unless the post is `visible`), `decisionId`, `caseId`, `inForce`, `disabledAt`, `noEffect` (`deleted`, `already` or null), `preserveUntil`, `preserveEndedAt`, `reinstatedAt`, `reinstatedGround`, `closedAt`, `closedReason` (one of `ORDER_ITEM_CLOSE_REASONS`, the column `closed_reason`; the summary's `closeGround` is another thing, the ground of the order's own closing), `gap` (true when an open gap names it) |
| `events` | each `id`, `kind`, `ground`, `at`, `effectiveAt`, `itemId` (or null), `by` (a staff handle or null), `via` |
| `owed` | `{ received, verification, issuedAt, texts, document, holdUntil, tellPerson }` booleans, and `scrutinyUntil` or null |
| `hold` | null without Section C, else `{ until, basis, open, extended, endedAt, endReason, releasedAt, noticesHeld }`: `basis` one of `HOLD_BASES`; `open` true while a DSA order's hold has no recorded end, and `until` then null; `releasedAt` the time of the newest `notice_released` |
| `mayClose`, `mayEvents`, `mayCorrect` | aids for the console: whether the caller could close it, which of `ORDER_CONSOLE_EVENTS` the function would take now, and under which of `ORDER_CORRECTION_RULES` the caller could correct it (or null). The database decides |

The view carries no text of `reasons`, `redress`, `scope`, `verified_how` or `hold_basis`: those are the command line's, and the author's notice is where the first three are read. An order without Section C has no hold to print; the console prints "Told at" from each item's `disabledAt` (section 8).

**`RestoreView`**: `{ importedAt, mayOpen, gaps }`; `importedAt` null when no import ever ran, and the console then hides the screen's entry. A gap: `id`, `class`, `actedAt`, `action`, `caseId` or `orderId` and `itemId` (when the restored database holds them), `kinds` (the lost decision's action kinds), `closedAt`, `disposition`, `closedByDecisionId`. No metadata beyond these.

**`StaffOverview`** gains, each read fresh:

| Member | Holds |
|---|---|
| `contact` | `{ open, oldestAt, dueAt }`: messages without a disposition, stale ones included, and the earliest deadline of a message that is not handled or of an order that still has an open item, also once it has passed. The scrutiny's 48 hours are on the order's own screen (`OrderView.owed.scrutinyUntil`), not here |
| `alerts` | `{ channel, since }`, `channel` one of `ok`, `down`, `mailbox`, `off` (with `NOTIFY=off`) |
| `reportsOwed` | `{ total, items }`, each `caseId`, `kind`, `since`, at most `OVERVIEW_LIST_MAX`: the answer of `reports_owed()` and nothing more |
| `ordersUnverified` | `{ total, items }`, each `orderId`, `effectAt`: the orders with a disabled item that are not verified, or whose disabled item was added after the last check |
| `copy` | `{ newestAt, overdue }` |
| `restore` | `{ open, measures }`: open gaps, and those of the classes 1 to 5 |

**`CaseView`** gains: on a `CaseDecisionAction`, `orderId`, `orderGround` and `withholdAuthor` where the row has them; `preserved` (true while `post_preserved` is true for the case's post: the console then shows no image and no snapshot text, which the API withholds in any case); `reportOwed` (`{ since }` or null, from `reports_owed()`); `noticeHeld` (`{ until }` while a notice about the case's post is held, with `until` null while that hold has no recorded end, and the member null when none is held: the case page and the composer's confirmation then say that a decision taken now is told to the author only at the release, section 8). A decision of kind `order` shows as one, with its order's id.

### 10.3 The step-up's params

`STAFF_STEP_UP_ACTIONS` gains five keys, `StaffStepUpOptionsRequest` five members of its union, and `append_confirmed_audit`'s list the five actions.

| Action | Target | Params (strict) |
|---|---|---|
| `order_effected` | `order`, the order's id | `OrderEffectedParams`: `kind` (`tco_removal` or `dsa_art9`), `memberState`, `authority`, `reference`, `sectionC`, `emergency`, `withholdFromAuthor`, `urgent`, `items` |
| `order_event_recorded` | `order` | `OrderEventRecordedParams`: `kind` (one of `ORDER_CONSOLE_EVENTS`), `ground` (a member of the kind's list, or null), `messageId` (a uuid or null) |
| `order_corrected` | `order` | `OrderCorrectedParams`: `facts`, the object of `OrderCorrectRequest` (10.2). Asked only for the rule `admin` (7.4); a correction under `entry` or `caution` has no step-up and a plain audit row |
| `gap_closed` | `restore_gap`, the gap's id | `GapClosedParams`: `disposition`, the literal `not_needed` |
| `audit_exported` | `audit`, the place `through` as decimal digits | `AuditExportedParams`: `after` and `through`, two places, `after` below `through` and at most `AUDIT_EXPORT_MAX` apart (7.16) |

- **`items`**: 1 to `itemsPerAction` entries `{ itemId, postId }`, both lower-case uuids, sorted by `postId`, without a repeat of either. So one action has one hash, and its order is the lock order.
- **`urgent`**: a boolean. True only in an admin's effect on a `dsa_art9` order that is not verified (7.6); the schema refuses it for a `tco_removal`, and the database refuses it on a verified order (`invalid_decision`) and from an account that is not an admin (`order_not_verified`). `decision_holds` compares the params with the order's row without `items` and `urgent`, which are the action's and not the order's.
- **The strings.** D 10's rule (C7) that a param is never a free string gains one named exception: `authority` and `reference`, in `OrderEffectedParams` and in the `facts` of `OrderCorrectedParams`. They are an authority's name and its file number, not words about a person; they are bound as in 10.1; and `decision_holds` compares both with the order's row at commit, so the key signs exactly the order that the record names. The schema test lists the exception by name and fails for any other string.
- **Refinements** (the triggers check each again): `withholdFromAuthor` is true for a `tco_removal` and whenever `sectionC` is true, so no signed params can say that a held notice's post is shown to its author; `ground` is one of `ANNEX3_GROUNDS` for `annex3_sent`, one of `ORDER_CLOSE_GROUNDS` for `closed_without_effect`, null otherwise; `messageId` is set for `clarification_received` and `hold_extended`, may be set for `grounds_ceased`, and is null otherwise.
- **`case_decided`.** An action entry gains a third strict shape, `{ kind: "reinstate_by_order", mediaId: null, orderGround }`, `orderGround` one of `ORDER_GROUNDS`. Only a correction with the outcome `reversed` holds it, alone, and the decision it corrects is of kind `order`. `disable_by_order` is in no action entry of `case_decided`: the schema refuses it there. `compareDecisionActions` is unchanged. A decision's signed form gains `orderGround` only where it is set, so rows made before 3f verify as they are.
- **`appeals_reviewed`** is unchanged: an `order` decision has no appeal.

### 10.4 What the app's side gains

- **`DecisionStatement`** gains `order`, null for every decision but an `order` decision and the correction of one. The API builds it at every read from the decision's row, its item's and the order's row as they stand, so the notice grows without a new decision:

| Member | Holds |
|---|---|
| `kind`, `memberState`, `authority`, `reference` | the order's |
| `verified` | whether `order-verify` has run: until then the lead says only that the order names that authority (7.10) |
| `orderedOn`, `orderedFrom` | the day of `issued_at` and `issued`, else the day of `received_at` and `received`, else null |
| `crossBorder`, `homeState` | for the paragraph on scrutiny: whether the order comes from another Member State than korbat's, and korbat's own |
| `withholdAuthor` | the measure's: whether the post is kept from its author too |
| `disabledAt` | when the measure was taken |
| `held`, `informedAt`, `holdEndReason` | whether the notice was held; the instant its author was told (the release, for a held one); and, when the hold was ended by hand, its reason (one of `HOLD_END_REASONS`, else null: `not_ordered` selects `ORDER_NOTICE.heldUntilNow.notOrdered`). `informedAt` is the one name for the day the author was told; the draft's `heldUntil` is gone |
| `preserveUntil`, `preserveExtended`, `preserveEndedOn` | a `tco_removal`: the preservation's end, whether an authority or a court had it moved, and the day it ended |
| `orderGround` | a correction's |

  The measure and the reversal are in `measures` and `reversals` as for any decision.
- **`DecisionView`** carries the statement and, read fresh, `orderTexts`: `{ reasons, redress, scope, textsAt }`, each text the recorded one or null (`scope` for a `dsa_art9` order) and `textsAt` the instant the newest of them was told to the author (the time of the newest `texts_recorded` event, or the decision's `informed_at` when that is later, as for a notice released after the texts were recorded; null while no text is recorded; the notice prints `ORDER_NOTICE.textsSince` only when it is later than `informedAt`), so the authority's words appear when the command line has recorded them, also on a notice released earlier, with the time from which the author's own time limits can count; and `erasedOn`, the day the preservation's end erased the content, or null. `content` is null while the order's measure is in force and after an erasure. `appealUntil` is null and `appealable` false.
- **`DecisionContent`** gains no kind. The content line of a decision told before the order, about a post that is now preserved, reads as it did (7.10): `snapshot_withheld` has no preservation arm, so `contentOf` never answers `r4_redacted` for it. Its images are named as before and answer 404 while an order disables the post; the app draws each as an image that could not be loaded (7.10).
- **`AuthorCasesPage`** rows name the two new kinds from `MEASURES`; a row of an `order` decision carries `order: true` and no rule.
- **`PostView.moderation`** (the chip) is unchanged in shape; it names a measure only of a decision its author was told of (`toldToAuthor`), and it exists for an order only when the level is `removed` (a `dsa_art9` order that does not withhold, which never holds its notice), with `legal` true.
- **`ExportFormat`**: the posts and files leave out a `disabled` post; `moderation.cases` holds a decision only once `toldToAuthor` is true, an order's notice as the statement's data with `orderTexts`. Section 18 lists the members.
- **`NoticeStatus`**, `ReportsPage` and every request of the app are unchanged. No answer of the app names an order while its notice is held, and none differs for the held post from what it would be for a post that no order touched: not a list, not a chip, not another decision's row or content, not an error code (`DELETE /api/posts/:id` answers `not_found`, as for a post that does not exist; an appeal of an earlier decision is accepted as always).
- **`STATEMENT_WORDS.reapplied`** and `DecisionStatement.reapplied` (`{ lostOn }` or null): a decision that closed a restore gap (7.17). The day is read from the gap, which is kept for as long as the decision (7.18).

### 10.5 The audit actions and the export's line

Every action below is written as its transaction's last statement. "System" is the system actor (`SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID`), for the command line and the jobs.

| Action | Actor | Step-up | Target | Metadata |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `contact_closed` | staff | no | `contact_message` | `disposition`, `receivedAt`, `folder` |
| `order_opened` | staff; system from `order-open` | no | `order` | `kind`, `memberState`, `authority`, `reference`, `channel`, `messageId` or null, `receivedAt` or null, `sentAt`, `openedAt`, `sectionC`, `emergency`, `crossBorder`, `withholdFromAuthor`, `via`, `items` (each `itemId`, `position`, `addedAt`, and `postId` when the link named a post, else `linkSha256` and `linkLength`) |
| `order_items_added` | staff | no | `order` | `items`, in the same form |
| `order_corrected` | staff | for the rule `admin` | `order` | `rule` (one of `ORDER_CORRECTION_RULES`), `eventId`, `changed` (each changed member's name with its old and its new value; a closed item as `itemId` and `reason`); with a step-up also `params` |
| `order_event_recorded` | staff | yes | `order` | `params`, `eventId`, `effectiveAt` |
| `order_effected` | staff | yes | `order` | `params`, `decisionIds` (item id to decision id) |
| `order_recorded` | system | no | `order` | `event` and that event's facts: instants, an item's id, a ground, a rule of `TELL_PERSON_RULES`, a document's digest and length, the digests of `reasons`, `redress`, `scope`, `verified_how`, `hold_basis` and of a preservation request's free words; the requesting authority's or court's name and reference |
| `order_notices_released` | system | no | none | `count` |
| `preserved_accessed` | system | no | `order_item` | `purpose`, `productionId`, the counts |
| `address_checked` | system | no | the account | `match` |
| `notification_recorded` | system | no | `notification` | steps `opened`, `sent`, `declined`: `step`, `caseId`, `sealActionId`, `duties`, `sentKinds`, the step's instant. Steps `answered`, `event`: `step`, `digest` |
| `production_recorded` | system | no | `production` | `step` (`account`, `listed`, `recorded`, `destroyed`), `digest` |
| `audit_exported` | staff | yes | `audit` | `params` (`after`, `through`): the confirmed download (7.16) |
| `copy_marked` | staff; system from `audit-export` | no | none | `after`, `through`, `rows`: the copy the keeper confirmed |
| `restore_imported` | system | no | none | `firstPlace`, `lastPlace`, `counts` by class, `holes` (each two places, between which the files held nothing), `files` (each a SHA-256) |
| `gap_closed` | staff | yes | `restore_gap` | `params` |
| `case_decided`, `appeals_reviewed` (D 10) | | | | gain `target` (`{ type, id }` of the decision's case's target, on every row from Task 3 on: after a restore a lost decision's gap says what it was about even when its case is lost too) and `gapId` when the decision closed a gap. `decision_holds` compares `target` with the case at commit |
| `moderation_retention` (D 7.9) | system | no | none | the new steps of 7.18 with their counts |

**The bound on metadata.** Ids, enums, numbers, booleans, instants and hex digests; and two kinds of string, named here and nowhere else: an authority's or a court's name, and a reference. Never an address, a handle, a text someone wrote about a person, or a value sent to an authority. **A link is not copied into the chain.** A link "as named" may hold a handle (`/u/<handle>`) or any text an outsider put into an address, and audit rows stay for good, outlive 3e's retirement of a handle and leave the server daily in the keeper's file. So an order's rows name an item by its `postId` when the link resolved, and by the SHA-256 and the length of the link when it did not; the item's row holds the text for its three years, and the chain proves it. A reference is copied as typed and may name a person (a court's file): the runbook says so of the keeper's file. The audit-metadata test of section 12 walks every action above with the largest request and fails for any other string.

**Why two actions hold a digest in place of facts.** The audit log is read by the API's role and leaves the server in the keeper's file. A later step of a notification (the authority's answer, a demand marked "not to the person") and every production say that an authority is looking at one person; row 31 of section 2 keeps exactly that from the API. So those rows hold `digest`, the SHA-256 of the record's row at that step in the contracts' canonical JSON (`canonical.ts`), and their target is the record, never a case or an account. The chain still proves that the record existed and was not changed afterwards, and `staff.js records --verify` recomputes each digest from the row. The first steps of a notification name their case: that an account whose images were sealed for good is reported is what the terms tell everyone, and `reports_owed()` shows the console the same case.

**`AuditExportLine`**, one JSON object a line, the members of the chain's canonical text (`staff/chain.ts:18`) and the two hashes: `v`, `place`, `id`, `at`, `actor`, `action`, `targetType`, `targetId`, `requestId`, `staffSession`, `keyId`, `actionHash`, `stepUp` and `metadata` (each the stored jsonb as PostgreSQL prints it, as a string, so that a hash recomputes off the server; `stepUp` null without one), `prevHash`, `hash`. `restore-import` checks a file with `canonicalText` and `chainHash` and nothing of the database's.

### 10.6 Error codes

| Code | Status | Raised by | Answered on |
|---|---|---|---|
| `order_needs_message` | 422 | the `legal_orders` trigger, for a named message that does not exist; `order_event_record`, for a clarification or an extension without a message | `POST /api/staff/orders`; the events route |
| `order_exists` | 409 | the unique key of state, authority and reference (`legal_orders_reference_key`) | `POST /api/staff/orders` and the correct route; the answer carries `orderId`, the order that stands |
| `order_closed` | 409 | the routes' read; `order_event_record` (every kind but `reopened`); `order_correct`; `decisions_record` | the items, correct, events and effect routes |
| `order_changed` | 409 | the effect route's comparison (7.6) | the effect route |
| `order_event_refused` | 422 | `order_event_record`; `legal_order_events_confirmed` at commit, for a console event or a correction without its audit row | the events and the correct routes |
| `order_not_verified` | 422 | `decisions_record`, for a decider who is not an admin, and `decision_holds`, for an admin without the signed `urgent` (7.6) | the effect route |
| `already_released` | 409 | `order_event_record`, for an extension after the release or after the hold's end (a second extension is `order_event_refused`) | the events route |
| `order_cap` | 429 | `decisions_record` | the effect route |
| `message_handled` | 409 | the close route's own update, which asks for `disposition is null` and changes no row; `contact_messages_guard`, for any update that would change a disposition | the close route |
| `gap_mismatch` | 409 | `gap_close`, for a gap that is not open and for a decision that does not fit it; `restore_gaps_confirmed` at commit | the decide and review routes with `gap`; the effect route; the gap's close route |
| `copy_mismatch` | 409 | the mark route's comparison (7.16) | `POST /api/staff/audit/export/mark` |
| `not_appealable` | 422 | `appeals_check` | `POST /api/moderation/decisions/:id/appeal` |

- `ModerationErrorCode` gains the twelve. The console's code table (`apps/admin/src/messages.ts`) gains words for the first eleven (`MODERATION_MESSAGES` and `CONSOLE_MODERATION_CODES`, section 8), `already_released` among them, since the events route answers it; `not_appealable` is the app's, which never offers the appeal on a notice, so its words are for a hand-made request.
- Each is the code the database raises under that name and the code the API knows: section 6 raises `order_event_refused` (not `invalid_order_event`), `gap_mismatch` (not `gap_not_open`), and `rate_limited` for the day's records (not `order_record_cap`). A code the API does not know would be a 500 (`app.ts:105`).
- 3c's codes gain causes, not meanings: `invalid_decision` (an order decision that is not exactly its item's one `disable_by_order`; an item already given effect; an intake item that is not the action's own; `urgent` signed where it means nothing; a `reinstate_by_order` outside a correction or without its ground; a `disable_by_order` sent to the decide route; any difference `decision_holds` finds); `admin_required` (a reinstatement; `closed_without_effect`, `reopened` and `hold_extended`; a correction after the first effect that does more than set Section C; a gap closed as `not_needed`; never the effect of an order, where an unverified `dsa_art9` order answers `order_not_verified` in its place); `rate_limited` (the day's order records, counted by the API and again by the insert's trigger); `invalid_request` (a member outside the list of `facts`; an item that is not an open item of the order; two links that name one post); `staff_step_up_required` (a correction under the rule `admin`, or the audit download, sent without its step-up); `not_found` (an order, a message, a gap; and `DELETE /api/posts/:id` on a disabled post).
- `staff_recused` is asked neither of an `order` decision nor of the correction of one (section 6.3). `staff_target` and `decision_cap` are never answered for an `order` decision; they are answered as before for the correction of one.
- No answer carries a detail, with one exception: `order_exists` carries `orderId`. A trigger's detail is logged, and the console reads `OrderView.mayEvents`, `mayClose`, `mayCorrect` and the items' `inForce` for its aids.
- **The command line's refusals** exit 1 with one sentence and write nothing: `already_released`, `already_extended`, `nothing_held`, `not_preserved`, `tell_rule_missing`, `order_ambiguous` (an order named as `<state>:<reference>` while several orders of that state share the reference, section 8), `handover_mismatch`, `tail_not_continuous`, `order-issued`'s and `order-hold-until`'s sentence that names the end it would have set (without `--release`), a command that lacks a setting it reads (`order-open` without `HOME_STATE` or `APP_ORIGIN`), and each command's "nothing to do". A hole in the keeper's files is no refusal: `restore-import` reports it and exits 0 (7.17).
- **Database only**, answered by no route: `order_event_cli_only`; `42501` from every revoked grant; `create the role korbat_notify first` from the first migration, raised when the role is missing or has no USAGE on the schema `public`. That privilege is the superuser's to give, beside the role's creation (`01-roles.sh`, `scripts/test-db.mjs`, the README's Upgrading): a migration's own grant of it is a warning and nothing more.

### 10.7 The texts' versions and places, and the settings

`packages/rules/src/index.ts` and `statement.ts`:

- `REDRESS_VERSION` becomes 3. `REDRESS_TEXTS[3]` holds version 2's nine texts word for word and the group `order`, which is `ORDER_NOTICE`. A notice therefore reads as it was told under its own version, and a changed sentence is a new version. `RULES_VERSION` stays 3: no rule changes.
- `ORDER_NOTICE`'s keys are section 18's (W.2 and W.3 hold each key's words and condition, and its form of a key stands): `done.disabled`, `done.removed`; `lead.tco_removal`, `lead.dsa_art9` (the act, and the authority the order names, with `{authority}`, `{memberState}`, `{reference}` and `{dated}`, which prints `orderedOn` by `orderedFrom`); `lead.confirmed` (added once the order is verified); `notAssessed.tco_removal`, `notAssessed.dsa_art9`; `heldUntilNow.tco_removal`, `heldUntilNow.dsa_art9` (with `{disabledAt}` and `{informedAt}`; the second ends with the sentence on the withholding) and `heldUntilNow.notOrdered` in their place; `textsSince` (with `{textsAt}`); `reasonsLabel`, `redressLabel`, `scopeLabel`; `textsFollow`, `copy`; `scrutiny` (with `{homeState}` and `{reasonsAt}`); `preserved` (with `{preserveUntil}`), `preservedLonger`; `erased` (with `{erasedOn}`); `everywhere`; `reapplied` (with `{lostOn}`); `noAppeal.tco_removal`, `noAppeal.dsa_art9`; `wrongItem` (korbat's own mistake); and for a reinstatement `reinstated.lead`, `reinstated.<ground>` for the five `ORDER_GROUNDS`, `reinstated.now` or `reinstated.erased`, and `lifted`.
- No text of `ORDER_NOTICE`, of `REDRESS_TEXTS[3]` or of a mail has a placeholder for an address: each says "the address under Contact in korbat's Terms", and asks the author to write from the email address of their korbat account. `packages/rules` holds no address and the API has no setting for the users' contact address, so `StatementInput` stays the statement alone.
- `statementParagraphs` stays a pure function of `DecisionStatement`: for `statement.order` not null it renders `ORDER_NOTICE` and none of D's paragraphs (no rule, no facts, no reason of korbat's, no appeal text). The authority's words are not in the statement's data: the decision screen prints `DecisionView.orderTexts` under the three labels, so a text recorded later appears without a new decision.
- A table test renders a notice for each kind, with and without texts, verified and not, held and not, with a hold ended as `not_ordered`, preserved, erased and reinstated on each ground, and holds that none contains "It breaks", "appeal to korbat" in the affirmative, a staff handle, an "@", or a sentence of `REDRESS_TEXTS[2]`; that an unverified notice never says that an authority issued the order; and that only a removal order's notice says that the law leaves korbat no choice.

| Text | Read where |
|---|---|
| the notice | the app's Cases and decision screens; the export; `access-request` |
| `order_notice`, `order_reinstated_notice`, `order_notice_added` | mails: a subject and a fixed body, nothing of the notice |
| `ALERT_TEXTS[kind]` | pushes and 3f's staff mails |
| `MODERATION_MESSAGES[code]`, the console's `ORDER_WORDS` (`orders.*`, `audit.download`, `state.unknown` and the rest of section 8's keys), `access.authorityHold`, `access.noticeHeld`, `account_deleted.held.final`, `account_deleted.held.deciding`, the deletion mail's first sentence, `ORDER_DRAFTS.reinstate.<ground>` | the console, the command line and one mail; section 18 |

**Settings the API reads** (`config.ts`; section 5 holds the services' own). The names are the API's. In `infra/.env` an API setting is written `API_<NAME>` and Compose maps it (`infra/compose.yml:96`), so the README and every sentence that tells the operator what to set say `API_NOTIFY`, `API_REPORT_HOLD`, `API_MODERATION_RETENTION`, `API_HOME_STATE`, `API_CONTACT_POINT`, `API_PUSH_ORIGIN` and `API_APP_ORIGIN`, as section 5's table lists them; `MEDIA_RECONCILE`, `APP_SITE` and `COMPOSE_PROFILES` have no prefix. A sentence of the console names no variable: the page cannot know how the server spells it.

| Setting | Shape | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `HOME_STATE` | a key of `STATE_FACTS`; required once `CONTACT_POINT` is set, else the API does not start | the operator's Member State. The migrate service is given it too, for `order-open` (7.4) |
| `CONTACT_POINT` | an address, or empty | the published contact point; while empty the terms and the README print no contact-point sentence. `StaffMe.contactPoint` carries it to the console |
| `PUSH_ORIGIN` | an https origin, or empty | the push hostname, shown in the runbook's phone set-up |
| `NOTIFY` | `on` or `off`; default `off` outside production | whether the channel's health is judged (the tick's third step, 7.2). The tick itself always runs. Until the mailbox exists the operator sets it `off`, and the home screen says that phone alerts are off |
| `REPORT_HOLD` | `on` or `off`; default `on` | 7.15 |

## 11. Security and privacy

D 11 and E3 11 stand whole. This section adds what 3f makes possible to get wrong: what stays impossible even for an admin in the API and the console, with the mechanism of sections 6 and 7 and the test that proves it (section 12 lists the tests by task); then what a forged order, a stolen key and a compromised service can and cannot do; then what no control proves.

### 11.1 What stays impossible, and what proves it

| Impossible | Enforced by | Proved by |
|---|---|---|
| Disable or reinstate for an order without a fresh assertion of one's own key over exactly that order's facts and those items | `decision_holds` on the `order_effected` row (7.6 step 8); `append_confirmed_audit`'s list; the ledger's triggers, which refuse the migrator like anyone | LAB D on the real code, each refused at COMMIT with nothing left behind: no audit row, `case_decided` in its place, a forged reference in the params, an item the row does not name, a second measure, an account that is not staff; LAB F: the migrator's own copy of a decision |
| Give effect to more than 10 items with one touch or more than 50 in a day; open more than 20 order records in a day | the params' bound; `order_cap`; the day's count, by the API and again by the insert's trigger under the account's advisory lock (7.4) | a function test for each bound; the eleventh item refused by the schema; two inserts at once at the limit, of which one is refused |
| Type a receipt time in the console, or open an order there with facts only the command line may record | the `legal_orders` trigger forces `received_at` from the message, or null when none is named, with `opened_at`, `opened_via` and, when a message is named, `channel` (`mailbox`; without a message the channel is the person's pick from the closed list, a fact the console may record and correct), and nulls every command-line column; column grants; no UPDATE: a correction goes through `order_correct`, which writes the console's columns alone | as `korbat_api`: an insert with each forced column set otherwise, an order without a message (taken, with the channel it supplies, no receipt time and no deadline), an order with a message and another channel (stored as `mailbox`), and any update of an order, each refused or overwritten |
| Move the clock, close or reopen an order, extend a hold, or correct a record after its first effect, other than by an event that a key confirmed; restart the clock at a time before the Annex III; give an event a time of one's choosing | `order_event_record` and `order_correct` the only writers; insert-only events; `effective_at` only from a message's row or now, and for a restart never before the Annex III's `at`; `legal_order_events_confirmed` at commit, which asks for the event's own audit row, its step-up and its account (7.5) | LAB B on the real code, with an Annex III recorded after the clarification arrived (the clock restarts at the later time and is not left stopped); as `korbat_api`: a cli-only event (`order_event_cli_only`), a supplied `at` overwritten, and `order_event_record` called with an admin's id and no audit row, refused at COMMIT with nothing left behind |
| Hold an author's notice without Section C on the order's row; hold one on a post its author can still see; lengthen a removal order's hold beyond its one extension; end a hold silently other than by the clock | `decisions_record` forces `informed_at` and `author_mail`; the trigger and the CHECK force `withhold_from_author` with Section C, and the params' refinement says the same (10.3); `order_hold_until` is computed, by the kind of order; the API updates neither an order nor `informed_at`; one boolean for the extension, set once, by an admin's confirmed event or by the command line | function tests; as `korbat_api`: the update of `informed_at` refused, and an insert with Section C and without the withholding overwritten; `already_extended` and `already_released`; a DSA order's hold with no recorded end does not end at six weeks, and one with a recorded end of four months ends then |
| Tell the author before the hold's end, or let anything reach them while it runs, on any path | `order_notices_release()` the one writer that tells after the insert (it sets `informed_at`, against `clock_timestamp()`; `order_correct` writes the column only to null); `toldToAuthor` the one fragment, with both halves, in `chipFacts` too; `decisions_record`, which holds every decision about the post with the notice; `snapshot_withheld`, which is not changed; every reader of 7.10; the deletion mail's one sentence for every account | the release returns 0 one second before the end and all at it (the clock moved as the migrator); `order_hold_end` over every minute around four clock changes in eight EU zones: early in none; a release and a reinstatement interleaved, then one more round: the correction is told; the route walk as the author under a held `tco_removal` and under a held `dsa_art9` finds no mail row, Cases row, chip, export line or status line, the same content line on a decision told before the order, no statement of a decision taken during the hold, and an access draft that sends nothing and marks the case for the operator; a 3c decision that told its author nothing stays out of every author read; the architecture pin on `decisions` reads |
| Show a disabled post or its images to anyone, its author included; let its author delete it | `postAccess`'s switch, which does not compile without the case; `isShown`; the lists' fragment; `deletePost`; `posts_guard` | the route walk as author, another account and signed out; every image 404 to each; as `korbat_api`: the update to `deleted_by_author` refused |
| Lift an order's measure as a moderator, without a ground, or by any decision that is not a correction; close an order while its measure is in force | `decision_actions_check`; `order_event_record` | LAB C on the real code: `admin_required`, the missing ground, and the level in every order of `remove`, `disable_by_order` and their reversals |
| Give effect to a `dsa_art9` order that nobody has verified, as a moderator, or as an admin without saying so | `decisions_record` and `decision_holds` (`order_not_verified`, for an account that is not an admin whatever it signed, and for an admin without `urgent`); `urgent` in the signed params and in the alert to every staff account | a function test: refused for a moderator and for an admin without `urgent`; taken for an admin with it, and the outbox row carries it; a `tco_removal` is never refused for it |
| Let one item's effect end an order's reminders or prove its hour; disable an item that was added after the verification without an alert | `legal_order_clock`'s `items_open`, `last_effect_at` and `within_hour`; `alert_steps()`; an item's `added_at` against the order's `verified_at` | LAB B with an order of two items of which one is given effect: `order_due` is still written and `within_hour` is null; with the second disabled or closed with a reason the reminders end; an item added after `order-verify` and then disabled raises `order_unverified` until the command runs again |
| Appeal an order to korbat, or have an order's decision answer someone's report | `appeals_check` (`not_appealable`); an `order` decision answers its own intake item alone, which `decisions_record` checks is of this action's intake | a function test; a report open on the same post keeps its line before and after the order; an order decision pointed at a reporter's item is refused |
| Destroy preserved content before `preserve_until`, by the author's deletion, the account's purge, the R4 redaction or any retention step; cut a later preservation short by an earlier one's date | `frozen_for`'s fourth arm, `post_preserved`, behind gates K and E; `decision_actions_apply`, which starts `preserve_until` anew at a disabling after an ended preservation | each of the four paths on the real code leaves the snapshot and the image rows; after the end the content is erased and a reinstatement restores nothing; disable, reinstate, age the item past its first end, disable again, run the retention round: the body is still there |
| Open preserved content or a sealed file through the API or the console | gate V; `post_preserved` in the staff snapshot read; no route; `order-access` and the `handover` service alone, the second without a network | the route-table test finds no answer with a preserved snapshot's text; `caseImage` refuses; the hand-over's test copies exactly one seal's files byte for byte and refuses a list that does not match and a name outside the store; the architecture pin: `handover.ts` imports no module, and no file outside `media/` names `post_media` or the store's directory |
| Read a notification or a production record, an authority's answer or demand, or a held address, as the API's role | `REVOKE ALL`; `reports_owed()` answers a case, a kind and a time; the audit rows of 10.5 hold a digest | the grants test; the function-privilege test; the audit-metadata test |
| Keep an address past the hold, hold anything but the address, or hold without a seal | `auth.address_held_until`, whose 7 days are counted inside it; `finishPurge`'s two statements; `report_open()` fires on `seal_media` alone, under the account's hold row; `report_close_unsealed` when the seal is lifted | the purge against a hold: the credentials go at once; the address goes when the record is sent or declined, when the seal is lifted, or 7 days on; with `REPORT_HOLD=off` at once; a second seal beside `notification-sent` gets its own record and hold |
| As `korbat_notify`: read a mail's content, write anything but a message's four columns, the outbox's push columns and its own state, or raise an alert of its own | the client asks for the UID and the internal date and nothing else, and reads no literal; the grants; outbox rows come from the trigger, `alert_steps()`, the API and the commands | the fake IMAP server records every command and holds no `FETCH` of a header, a body or a flag, and sends the lines a real server sends unasked; the grants test as `korbat_notify`, statement by statement, the schema's USAGE included |
| Put content, a link, a handle, an address, an authority's name or a reference into a push, or into a staff mail of 3f | `alert_outbox.facts`' CHECK (at most 6 members, each a number or a timestamp); one fixed sentence a kind (`ALERT_TEXTS`) | the CHECK's tests; a table test renders every kind with the largest facts and finds no "@", no "/" and no letter outside the sentence |
| Take the audit log's tail without a key; record a copy that nobody holds | the step-up `audit_exported`; the mark route's two tests: the hash at the place, and the caller's own confirmed download through it (7.16) | the export without a step-up refused; a body cut at line 200 leaves no mark and the next download starts at the same place; a mark for a place the caller never downloaded refused (`copy_mismatch`); a download by another account than the last raises `copy_taken` |
| Enter the ledger by an import, take a lost measure again without a key, or empty the list of gaps without one | `restore-import` writes no row of `decisions` or `decision_actions`; the triggers force `decided_at` and `xact`; `decision_holds`; `restore_gaps_confirmed` | LAB F on the real code; a tail that begins above the head's next place is refused; one that begins 300 places before the head is taken from the head on; a line at the head's place with another hash refuses everything; a hole is reported and what lies before it imported; `gap_close` without its audit row refused at COMMIT |

**In the database.** `korbat_api`: on `legal_orders` SELECT and INSERT of the console's columns; on `legal_order_items` SELECT, INSERT of five columns and UPDATE of `intake_item_id`; on `legal_order_events` SELECT and INSERT, where the trigger takes `opened` alone from it, so every other console event has `order_event_record` or `order_correct` as its writer; on `contact_messages` SELECT and UPDATE of two columns; on `alert_outbox` INSERT, SELECT and UPDATE of `mailed_at`; SELECT on `notify_state`, `notify_folders`, `restore_gaps` and `copy_marks`, INSERT on the last; nothing on the three tables of 7.13 and 7.14; EXECUTE only on the functions section 6 grants it. `korbat_notify` holds the grants of section 6 and USAGE on the schema `public`, which is the superuser's grant beside the role's creation: a migration cannot give it, and the first migration refuses to run without it (10.6). For the migrator, the superuser and anyone with Docker on the host nothing is impossible; the ledger's rows are held to their audit rows as D 11 says, and `text-verify` and `audit-verify` find afterwards what was written around the triggers.

### 11.2 A forged order, a stolen key, a compromised service

**A forged order** (T57). The contact point's address is public, and nothing in a mail proves its sender.

| It can | It cannot |
|---|---|
| wake the person on call, as any mail to the address does, once in `quietMinutes` | enter korbat by itself: a person reads it in the mail host's app, and the software reads no byte of it |
| as a removal order: get the posts it names disabled for everyone, when the person believes it at night | destroy anything: the measure is reversible and a `tco_removal` preserves everything |
| as a DSA Article 9 order: nothing, until someone has verified it | be given effect by a moderator in doubt: no hour runs for it, and the one exception is an admin's signed `urgent`, which every staff account is told of (7.6) |
| with "must not be told", keep the author untold: for a removal order's computed hold when nobody ever verifies it | lengthen that hold (once, by an admin's confirmed event from a real message, or from the command line), or pass unseen: every staff account is told of the opening, of every correction and of the effect, `order_unverified` goes to every phone daily until `order-verify`, and `hold_ending` for seven days before the release |
| | survive verification: `order-hold-end` with `not_authentic`, then an admin's reinstatement; the author then reads what happened and for how long. Before it, the notice's lead says only that the order names that authority, never that the authority issued it |
| a forged withdrawal: ask for a reinstatement | get one from a moderator, or without the admin's own verification |
| a forged extension of a real order's non-disclosure: have an admin record `hold_extended`, 42 more days of silence | outlast the command line's check: `order-hold-extend` confirms the extension or `order-hold-end` undoes it, and never more than once an order |

**A stolen staff key with its PIN and a live session** (T50).

| It can | It cannot |
|---|---|
| open 20 order records and disable 50 posts in a day, ten a touch, each told to every staff account; `moderation_burst` at 20 decisions in 15 minutes | give its record a receipt time of its choosing (the time is a real message's, or none), or act unseen by the other staff accounts |
| tick Section C on its own record, or set it on another's (7.4, the rule `caution`), so that those authors are not told until someone verifies | record a verification, a time stamp, a hold's end or any command-line fact; touch a notification or a production |
| add a link to an order that was verified and disable that post under the order's name, with a notice that names a real authority and reference | do so without the alert: the item is unverified until `order-verify` runs again, `order_unverified` goes out daily, and every staff account was told of the effect |
| as an admin's key also: reinstate a truly ordered post, close an order without effect or reopen one, record one extension of a hold, give urgent effect to an unverified DSA order, correct an order's facts after its effect, close a gap as `not_needed`, take the audit log's tail | read preserved content, a sealed file or a held address; destroy content; do any of these without a row that names the key and an alert or an audit page that shows it |

**A staff session without its key** (an unlocked phone, which is the on-call device). It can open order records, correct them before their first effect, close a message, and set "must not be told" on an order that was given effect, which takes a told notice off its author's screens until an admin unsets it; every staff account is alerted of each (`order_opened`, `order_corrected`). It cannot disable or reinstate anything, stop a clock, close or reopen an order, take the audit log's tail or quiet `copy_overdue`.

It is undone as D 11 says: `staff.js revoke`; the audit page filtered by that key and the period lists every action; an admin corrects each order decision (`wrong_item` or `not_authentic`) and each order's record under the caps. `reverse-window` is not built (section 2, row 39).

**A compromised watcher** (the `notify` container, its database role or the mailbox's password).

| It can | It cannot |
|---|---|
| read the contact point's mail with the application password, orders included | send, move or delete mail, when the mail host's application password reads only (the owner's list checks this) |
| stay silent about a real message while its heartbeat looks healthy: no push and no mail from korbat | stop the phone's own mail app, which shows the mailbox without korbat (the runbook's second leg, which the owner's list sets up and tests with `notify` stopped), or the daily test's absence from being seen |
| insert rows for messages that do not exist, and publish pushes of its own: false alarms, and a false receipt time for an order opened from such a row | write an order, a disposition, a decision or any other table; read anything but messages, the outbox and its own state; reach `auth` |
| send requests to PostgreSQL as its own role, and to ntfy and to Caddy's push site | reach the API, the limit store, the image store or the console's API directly: it shares the network `watch` with PostgreSQL alone and the network `push` with ntfy and Caddy, and Caddy's app and console sites refuse a client from `push` (403), so it cannot use Caddy as a way in with a client address of its choosing (section 5) |

**A compromised ntfy, push hostname or phone token.** It reads the fixed sentences: when mail arrived at the contact point, when an order was opened or given effect, counts. It can drop or forge pushes.
