It reaches neither the database nor the internet (`push` is an internal network), nor the app's and the console's sites, which refuse a client from that network; and every push has its mail.

**An API under an attacker's control** (D 11) gains with 3f: order records with any facts but the forced ones, and corrections of them before their first effect; "must not be told" set on an order given effect; dispositions, which end a message's escalation; outbox rows; `mailed_at`, which suppresses a mail leg; a copy mark for a place it downloaded; an order decision, a clock event, a closing, a reopening, an extension or a gap's closing with a confirmation it made up, which `audit-verify` detects afterwards and nothing prevents. What it no longer gains by a statement alone: `order_event_record`, `order_correct` after an effect and `gap_close` without a decision are each refused at commit without their confirmed audit row, so a clock stopped, an order closed or a list of gaps emptied leaves a row that `audit-verify` checks, or nothing at all. It still cannot push in `notify`'s name, read the records of 7.13 and 7.14, read preserved or sealed content from the store's files it does not serve, or write a command-line fact.

### 11.3 What the controls cannot prove

- **One person asleep** (T58). The software can push, escalate to every phone and repeat; whether a phone wakes its owner depends on settings no server sees. There is no voice call and no iPhone (section 2). The daily test, the owner's phone checks and the night drill are the evidence, and they are the runbook's.
- **A watcher or a database that stops at night wakes nobody.** `alert_channel_down` is a mail, read in the morning; with PostgreSQL down not even that is written, and with the limit store down the tick does not run (7.2). The hour then depends on the mail host's own app on the on-call phone, signed in to the contact mailbox and allowed to ring at night, which the owner's list sets up and tests with `notify` stopped (section 18). A health check restarts a watcher that hangs (section 5). It is the owner's choice and section 2 flags it (row 22).
- **The minutes the hour does not have.** Up to 20 seconds until the poll, up to 30 more until the mail leg, and up to five minutes of an image at an edge copy after the effect; hence the 55-minute target.
- **The receipt time is the mail host's.** `received_at` is the internal date the host states, stored never later than the database's clock. korbat cannot check that clock. An order that arrives another way, and one opened in the console without a message, has no receipt time and no deadline on any screen until the operator types the instant at the shell; the console says "act now" meanwhile.
- **A message that arrived while the watcher was down for more than two hours** raises no alert when it is read: it is stale. The channel-down mail of that time is what told staff to watch the mailbox by hand, and the Contact point screen lists the message once the watcher is back. An order found in the mailbox meanwhile is opened without a message (7.4).
- **A flood of mail at the contact point.** Every message is a possible order, and dropping one may drop an order, so none is dropped: each gets its row, its escalation and its reminders. What is bounded is the first push: one in `quietMinutes` (15) while a message is unhandled. Whoever knows the public address can still ring the on-call phone four times an hour and every phone at the escalation, for as long as they like, and an alarm that rings every night is one people learn to dismiss. The mail host's own filter moves what it recognises to the junk folder, which is watched all the same. Flagged for the owner (section 2, row 20, and the owner's list).
- **A forged removal order nobody verifies** silences one post toward its author for the computed hold, about six weeks (section 2, row 25), with every staff account alerted daily. Until someone verifies, the notice and the record say only what a message claimed.
- **A removal order's hold is at most 28 hours late, and later while `issued_at` is not recorded:** it then counts from the receipt, which is never earlier than the order's day. Late is the side the law does not punish; an author in an overseas region's zone is not covered by the proof.
- **A DSA order's hold is as long as someone recorded.** Its end is the authority's, typed at the shell; while none is recorded the hold does not end by itself, and a forgotten one is a silent removal that only the daily alert and a person end. Flagged for counsel (section 2, row 46).
- **An extension that nobody records in time.** The warning goes out for seven days and an admin can record an extension from the console at once; an extension that reaches the mailbox and no person before the hold's end is still overtaken by the release.
- **The author finds a gap that nothing accounts for** until the release (row 26), and a status checker cannot tell a disabled post from a deleted one. That is the design, not a leak. During a hold a decision of korbat's own about the post, and the answer to an appeal about it, reach the author only at the release, up to twelve weeks late for a removal order. Whether the copy of the text under a decision told before the order may still be shown to its author while Art. 6 preserves the post is counsel's (row 47); it is shown, because a changed line there would tell. The post's images on that screen are served to nobody and drawn as images that could not be loaded, with no word of why (7.10).
- **A measure lifted before the notice is released.** When an order's item is reinstated while its notice is still held, and a decision of korbat's own about the post is held with it, the post is `removed` by a decision its author has not been told of: the author sees it in their own lists, without a chip, until the hold ends or the operator ends it. The operator ends the hold as soon as the authority allows (7.9). Flagged for the owner and for counsel (section 2, row 45): closing it needs the lists to know which decisions are told.
- **A correction cannot take back what was read.** "Must not be told" set after a decision was told removes the notice from the author's screens and stops a mail not yet sent; a mail that left, and what the author read, stay. The event's time records when korbat stopped saying it.
- **"Immediately reinstate" needs an admin.** With one admin who cannot be reached, a withdrawn order's post stays disabled until they can.
- **Preserved content outlives its author's wish to delete it** for six months, and is erased at the end even when the order is annulled a day later. A challenge that outlasts the six months ends with nothing to reinstate unless a court or an authority asks korbat to keep the post longer (7.11); the notice tells the author to ask.
- **An order against an account as such is not built.** A link that names a profile resolves to no post; the display name, the bio and "the whole account" are the operator's, by 3e's own measures, which tell their subject. The hour for such an order depends on the operator (section 2).
- **The person an order to provide information names** is told, where the rule says so, by the operator's hand from the contact address; the software records the rule and the telling and sends nothing.
- **A staff member who wrote a post that an order names** reads the order in the mailbox and on the Orders screen, whatever its Section C says. It cannot be prevented while every person on call reads the contact mailbox; the runbook has that person tell the authority so with Annex II.
- **The held address.** For up to seven days after a deletion korbat still holds the address of an account whose images were sealed. A new sign-up under that address in those days is answered as today's sign-up answers in the seconds between a take-down and its purge; Task 5 pins that answer, and it must never name a hold.
- **After a restore.** Everything after the keeper's last download is lost to the software, and so is what lies behind a hole in the keeper's files, which the import reports. A notice may be mailed twice. A decision taken again is a new decision with a new time. A hold's extension that was recorded after the last download is lost with it, so the operator compares each imported hold with the mailbox before a disabling is taken again (7.17). Until every gap of class `record` is entered again, `access-request` cannot print an authority's "not to the person" mark for it: the runbook answers no access request while such a gap is open.
- **The keeper's file** is as sensitive as the console: it names every order, its authority, reference and posts, held ones included, and a reference may name a person. It is taken only with a staff key, kept as the head is kept, on the keeper's encrypted device, and is no backup of content.
- **Counts.** `tco-counts` prints "not recorded" for what no row holds; a figure is as complete as the records the operator entered.

### 11.4 What each party may learn

| Party | Learns | Never learns |
|---|---|---|
| The author | at once, or when the hold ends: that the post was disabled on an authority's order, under which act, which authority of which state the order names and whether korbat has confirmed that, on which day, under which reference; that korbat did not assess it; the authority's reasons and remedies and, for a DSA order, the order's territorial scope, in its words, with a mail and a time when they are added later; that the content is preserved, until when, and when it was erased; when the notice was held, since when the post has been disabled; why a reinstatement happened; on request from the account's own address, a copy of the order | anything while a hold runs, by any screen, statement, mail, export, error code or answer to an access request; who at korbat acted; who else was told |
| Everyone else, signed in or out, and an authority's status checker | what a missing post shows: absence, 404 for the page and the images | that an order exists; never a 451 |
| Whoever reported the same post | the outcome of korbat's own decision on their item, if there is one | the order: an `order` decision answers no user's item |
| Someone who writes to the contact address in an author's name | the runbook's fixed answer | whether an order exists, and any copy of one: a copy goes to the account's own address only, after `address-check` |
| The person an order to provide information names | what the rule recorded for that order allows: by the operator's hand, the authority's reasons and the redress, at effect or at the time the authority states | anything where the rule is `never`; nothing from the software by itself |
| Staff | an order's facts, clock, events and items (the author's handle, the post's state, an image count; a preview only of a public post); that a report is owed, for which case, since when; the gaps after a restore | the texts the command line recorded, except through the author's notice; a notification's content, an authority's answers and demands, a production; preserved content, sealed files, a held address |
| The operator at the shell | everything, and each read of personal data leaves its audit row (`order-access`, `notification-facts`, `production-account`, `address-check`) | |
| The mail host | every mail at the contact point, and the staff alerts' fixed sentences | anything of korbat's database |
| ntfy, the phones, and the tunnel's operator in front of the push hostname | the alerts' fixed sentences with counts and times | content, a person, an order's facts |
| An authority | what the person sends from the mailbox (Annex II and III) and what a production record names: of an account the handle, the address and the creation time, and the fixed sentences of what korbat does not hold and of what it holds and has not produced (the times and device types of sign-ins) | korbat sends nothing by itself, and nothing beyond those three facts without counsel |

**New personal data and its end.** A contact message's row holds a folder, two numbers and times: no personal data. An order's row, items and texts may name a person through a post; they are deleted 3 years after the order's last event, never while its measure stands. For a removal order that is never withdrawn that is for good: the authority's reasons about one person's post then outlive the content, erased at six months, and the account (section 2, row 42, flagged for the owner and counsel). A notification and a production record are data about a suspicion (GDPR Art. 10; flagged for counsel): written and read only as the migrator, deleted 3 years after the matter's last event, and kept beyond that only while an authority's "not to the person" still runs. `auth.report_holds` holds an account's id, two times, why the hold was released and whether the seal was for good. A restore gap holds a lost audit row's metadata for 12 months after it closed, and for as long as the decision when a decision closed it. The chain gains ids, enums, times, digests, an authority's name and a reference, never a link and never a text; a reference may name a person. No log line of `notify` carries a mailbox's name, a UID or a password; it logs counts and the closed list of errors.

### 11.5 The threats `inputs.md` names for 3f

| Threat | 3f's control | Residual |
|---|---|---|
| T57 forged orders; a gag is what a forger wants | an order restricts only through a signed action, and a DSA Article 9 order only after it is verified; verification recorded at the shell, alerted daily until it is, also for an item added later; a removal order's hold computed by the database and lengthened once at most; a hold always hides the post from its author too, and nothing of it reaches the author by another path; until the order is verified the notice says only what a message claimed; every staff account told of every order, correction and effect, and warned for seven days before a release (C32, C33) | six weeks of silence about one post when a forged removal order is believed and never verified; a DSA order's hold that nobody ends |
| T58 the one-hour clock | the watcher; a push that is owed for an hour and not for five tries; the escalation; reminders that follow every item; one action usable from a phone; an order opened without a message when the watcher did not see it; a record that can be corrected and never stands in the way; Annex III's stop; the 55-minute target; no recusal and no cap of 3c in the way (C34) | one person asleep; a watcher or a database that stops at night; a mail flood inside its bound |
| T59 lawful elsewhere; reinstatement and preservation | effect everywhere, said in the notice after the order's own scope; reinstatement by an admin on five grounds; six months and a day of preservation from each disabling, behind `frozen_for`; scrutiny's 48 hours on the screen, and the authority's reasons mailed with a time, from which the author's own 48 hours count (C34, C36) | removal everywhere for an order meant for one state; an admin who cannot be reached |
| T30, T31 forged court orders and data requests | a production only at the shell, with a record of the instrument, the authority and what was asked; the rule for telling the person recorded before anything is produced; nothing leaves by a route; no hold on an account for a request (C38) | the operator persuaded by a forged demand; the record shows it afterwards |
| T33 threats to life (Art. 18) | the owed record opened by the database at a seal, its timer and daily alert, and closed when the seal is lifted; `notification-open` for other content; the address held against a deletion | a suspicion nobody recognised opens no record |
| T20 a restored backup | the daily copy of the audit tail, recorded only when the keeper holds it; `restore-import`, which takes a keeper's whole files and reports a hole; the gaps taken again under a key in a fixed order and closed only with a key; the app's site closed until orders and seals are back (C37) | what happened after the last copy, and behind a hole |
| T50 a stolen key | 10 items a touch, 50 a day, 20 records a day; alerts to every staff account; an item added to a verified order unverified until checked; a correction after the first effect only with an admin's key; the audit tail only with a key; the audit page by key and period; corrections one by one (C24 without its command) | 50 posts disabled for the hours until someone reads an alert |

## 12. Testing

3c 12's and 3e 12's kinds and fixtures stay (`decideForTest`, the software authenticator, `ageStandingForTest`). 3f adds seven fixtures, each built by the task that first needs it:

- `fakeImap(script)` (Task 1, `apps/api/test/notify/fake-imap.ts`): a server on `node:tls` with a certificate the test trusts, which answers from a script and records every command it was sent. Its scripts hold the lines a real server sends, not only the ones the client asks for (row 13). It has a second caller, `scripts/dev-mailbox.mjs` (Task 1), the development mailbox of the dry run: it serves an inbox and a junk folder on a local port, with a key and a certificate it makes into `.data/` on first run, and takes "drop a message" on standard input.
- `fakeNtfy()` (Task 1): an HTTP server that records each publish (topic, priority, tag, text, token) and can be told to fail, for a number of requests or for a time.
- `contactMessageForTest(receivedAt, folder)` (Task 1): inserts a message on `korbat_notify`'s role, as the watcher would, through the test environment's fourth URL, `DATABASE_URL_NOTIFY`. The harness is taught the fourth role in the same task: `assertTestTargets` refuses a `DATABASE_URL_NOTIFY` that does not name a `_test` database, so that no test of the watcher can write into the development database.
- `orderForTest({ kind, sectionC, posts })` (Task 2): opens an order through the route's own code from such a message.
- `effectForTest(order, staff)` (Task 3): "Disable for an order" through the decide code with a real step-up.
- `ageOrderForTest(order, interval)` (Task 2, grown in Tasks 3 and 4): as the migrator, moves an order's times, its items', events' and decisions' times and its message's back by the interval. It is the only way a test meets a deadline, an escalation step, a hold's end or a preservation's end: every time is forced by a trigger and no setting shortens a number of `order_rules()`.
- `snapshotForTest()` and `restoreForTest(snapshot)` (Task 6), as the migrator: the first copies every table of `public` and `auth` into a schema of its own; the second truncates them all, as the global setup does, and copies the rows back with the user triggers off for the copy (`ALTER TABLE ... DISABLE TRIGGER USER`, which a table's owner may do) and the identity columns kept. It is how a test restores "a backup": the suite cannot copy a database as LAB F did (`CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE` needs the superuser and no other session, and the suite has neither).

The fixtures' cleanup grows with the tables (Tasks 2, 3 and 5): `removeFixtures` and `removeDecided` in `moderation-fixtures.ts` and the development cleaner `scripts/clean-dev-fixtures.mjs` delete an order's events, items and row, and the outbox rows and messages a fixture made, before the intake items and the profiles they point at, or one `on delete restrict` key fails the whole cleanup.

**How they run** (the lighter loop). An implementer runs the type check, the unit suites and the integration files its task names (section 13). The whole suite, on a fresh database and on one upgraded from 3e's last migration (`--upgrade-from`, which runs 3f's six migrations as one batch), runs at the four batch ends: Tasks 1, 3, 5 and 7. A task that adds a migration and is no batch end (2, 4, 6) also runs its own integration files on an upgraded database.

**What needs what.** Three kinds of file, and section 13 names each task's by kind:

- **Unit files** need nothing: no database, no network, no Docker. The IMAP client against `fakeImap`, the publisher against `fakeNtfy` (both under `apps/api/test/notify/`; the client takes its insert as a function) and the hand-over's program (`apps/api/test/handover.test.ts`) are unit files.
- **Integration files** (under `apps/api/test/integration/`) run under the global setup, which connects to the Compose PostgreSQL and Valkey before any test: they need the data services, so Docker's engine with those two containers, and nothing else. `notify-*.test.ts` there are the role's grants, the trigger and the loop's statements, not the client.
- **Docker's engine for more than the data services** is needed at three points, and only there: Task 1 (the stack rebuilt with the profile `notify`, the smoke test, AS's labs on ntfy and Caddy), Task 4 (the stack rebuilt, the page status through Caddy) and Task 7 (the walk-through on a rebuilt stack with the profile on, and the labs in a container). `notify`, ntfy and the hand-over are behind Compose profiles that are off in development and in CI unless a task's test names them.

### 12.1 Each invariant and the test that proves it

| # | Invariant | Proved by | Task |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The six migrations run in one transaction, on an upgraded and on a fresh database | the suite after `--upgrade-from` and `--fresh`; `enum-literals.test.ts` (3e) extended to 3f's files and eight values, with a violation it catches | 2, then 3 to 6 |
| 2 | A `disabled` post is not there for anyone | `postAccess` answers `hidden` for its author, another account and signed out; its images answer 404 to each; lists, counters, the export's posts and files and the access draft hold no trace; `deletePost` answers `not_found`; a route walk as its author (`orders-route-walk.test.ts`), driven from the app's route table, in which no answer differs from a stranger's. The walk runs four posts: one under a `tco_removal`, one under a `dsa_art9` order whose author must not be told (so withheld by force), one whose author was told of an earlier decision about it before the order (that decision's view, content and export entry are byte for byte what they were before the effect, and never `r4_redacted`; each image the view names answers 404 to the author, and the screen draws it as any image that could not be loaded, with no word of 3f's), and one of an author who also has a 3c decision with `author_mail` `none` (which stays out of every read on the author's side: `toldToAuthor` is both halves, `author_mail <> 'none'` and `informed_at is not null`) | 3 |
| 3 | A held notice tells nobody, on any path | with Section C, for a `tco_removal` and for a `dsa_art9`: no mail row, no Cases row, no chip (`chipFacts` is filtered), no export line, no line in what the access draft sends (and the operator's mark `access.noticeHeld` in it), and a reporter's line on the same post unchanged; `informed_at` null and `author_mail` `none`; a request that opens a `dsa_art9` order with Section C and without the withholding is refused by the schema and by the database's CHECK. **Other decisions:** a `remove` under a rule decided during the hold, and the review of an appeal the author made before it, are recorded untold and appear in no author read; the release tells them with the notice; the reporter of that case is answered at once. **The release:** `order_notices_release()` returns 0 one microsecond before the end and releases everything at it (times moved by `ageOrderForTest`), stamping `clock_timestamp()`; a reinstatement that commits beside the release (two transactions interleaved: the correction inserted, the release run, the correction committed) is told by the next round; a notice released to an account that was deleted during the hold is set pending and given up after `RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays` without an error (`mailAccount` has no address). The API role's update of `informed_at` is refused | 3 |
| 4 | The hold never ends early | **A removal order:** `order_hold_end` over generated instants and every minute around four clock changes, in the eight zones of LAB E, against Reg. 1182/71 Art. 3: early in none; the same value in three session time zones; an extension adds exactly 42 days, from the command line and from the admin's "Extension received" alike; a second extension and one after the release refused; `order-issued` with an instant after the receipt refused, and one whose end is not in the future changes nothing without `--release`. **A DSA order:** the end is the recorded one, of any length, earlier or later than the Regulation's weeks; with none recorded `order_notices_release()` releases nothing however far the times are moved; `order-hold-extend` is refused for it. **The warning:** `hold_ending` is written daily in the last `holdWarningDays` days before any hold's end and not before; the daily reminder for a hold with no end | 2 (the function), 3 (the commands and the alerts) |
| 5 | Nothing enters the ledger without a key | each refusal of LAB D (no audit row, `case_decided` in its place, a forged reference in the params, an item the row does not name, a second measure, an account that is not staff), each leaving nothing behind; one audit row covers ten decisions and no eleventh; the import and the migrator's own copy of a lost decision write no ledger row that commits | 3, 6 |
| 6 | An order waives exactly three checks | accepted where an ordinary removal is refused: `staff_recused`, `staff_target`, the decision caps at zero; refused: the 51st item of a staff member in 24 hours (`order_cap`), a reinstatement by a moderator (`admin_required`) or without a ground. The reinstatement by an admin who follows the author is accepted: recusal is not asked for the correction of an order decision either (the function's rule) | 3 |
| 7 | The level is derived | `remove`, `disable_by_order` and their reversals in every order of arrival against the reference implementation of 3c 12: `disabled` above `removed`; a later `remove` is `already`; a reinstatement leaves `removed` while a rule removal stands; a DSA Article 9 order that does not withhold gives `removed` and freezes nothing; one whose author must not be told gives `disabled`, whatever the request said | 3 |
| 8 | An order answers only its own item | other open items of the case stay open and keep their lane; a case that closes has `answered_seq = intake_seq`; no order item is in the queue's answer; the notice status page of a notifier of the same post reads the same before and after; an item whose `intake_item_id` is pointed at a reporter's unanswered item of the same case is refused, since the item an order decision answers must be of this case, on this post, of an intake of the kind `order`, and unanswered | 3 |
| 9 | korbat's appeal does not apply | `appeals_check` refuses (`not_appealable`); `decision_final_at` is `decided_at`; the app's screen has no control | 3 |
| 10 | The clock is the events' | the view over LAB B's table: the start is the earlier of receipt and sending; an Annex III stops it; a fresh 60 minutes from a cease or a clarification, never the rest; `grounds_ceased` only after an Annex III with `force_majeure` and `clarification_received` only after one on another ground (the function holds it, not the screen); an Annex III recorded later than the clarification arrived: the restart takes the later of the two times and nothing is refused; `within_hour` is null while an item is open, compares the last effect afterwards, and is true for an effect given while the clock was stopped; a second `opened` refused; a message whose time at the mail host is a few seconds ahead of the database's clock is stored at the clock's time, so an order opened from it at once meets no CHECK; an order opened from the console without a message is accepted while the watcher's beat is old and while it is fresh, with the channel the form sent (each of the four; with a message named the row holds `mailbox` whatever was sent) and with no time of receipt and no deadline until `order-received` records one; `order_correct` changes the channel of an order without a message under `entry` before the first effect and under `admin` after it, and refuses one for an order opened from a message; no clock for a DSA order | 2 |
| 11 | What is preserved stays, and then goes | the author's deletion (refused by `posts_guard` for the API role), the account's purge, the R4 redaction and the retention job leave the snapshot and the image rows until `preserve_until`; gate V and the staff case page's `post_preserved` refuse staff, while `snapshot_withheld` answers for a preserved post what it answered before; an extension moves it only forward; at its end the content is erased, and a reinstatement after that restores nothing; a reinstatement before it ends the preservation. **A re-disabling counts anew:** disable, reinstate, age the item past its first end, disable again, run the retention round: the body, the images and the snapshot are still there, and `preserve_until` is six months and a day from the second disabling; a second disabling 100 days after the first ends six months after itself, not after the first | 3 (keeping), 4 (the end) |
| 12 | The roles hold what section 6 grants | as `korbat_api`: no read of a notification, an event of one or a production; no write of an order's command-line columns, a command-line event, an item's ledger columns or `informed_at`. As `korbat_notify`: `has_schema_privilege('korbat_notify', 'public', 'USAGE')` (the superuser's grant, which the migration checks and cannot make), its four tables' grants and nothing in `auth`; the migration stops with "create the role korbat_notify first" when the role or the schema's privilege is missing | 1, 2, 3, 5 |
| 13 | The watcher parses nothing an outsider wrote | against `fakeImap`, as unit tests: the commands sent are the five of section 5 and no `FETCH` names a header, a body, a flag or an envelope; a script with what a real server sends, namely `EXAMINE`'s whole answer with the validity number in its untagged `OK`, an unrequested `* 3 EXISTS` and `* 2 FETCH (FLAGS (\Seen))` inside a `UID FETCH` and a `NOOP`, and the two members of a `FETCH` in both orders, all read or ignored without an error; a line that announces a literal, a line over the bound and a completion for another command each end the round with `imap_protocol`; a dropped connection (`imap_connect`) and a timeout (`imap_timeout`), a changed UIDVALIDITY, a wrong password (`imap_login`, tried once a round); the client's two outgoing strings, the password and the junk folder's name, each with a quote, a backslash, a space and a brace, sent as quoted strings, and a value with a line break refused at start; 10,000 old messages (all stale, no alert). The error words are section 6's eight, the CHECK of `notify_state.error` | 1 |
| 14 | Every alert has two legs and fails open | each kind of W.4's first table through the tick with `fakeNtfy`: the push, and the mail that is its sentence; for 3c's and 3e's six kinds the push from the row and the mail as today from the caller's full message, with `mailed_at` set either way, so the tick never tries to render one from `facts`. With ntfy failing, the mail and `alert_channel_down`; ntfy failing for four minutes and then answering: the push goes and the channel reads `ok` again without anyone touching a row; failing for longer than `push_owed_minutes`: the row is left to its mail and no longer counts, and the channel reads `ok` once the newer rows are published. With `NOTIFY=off` the tick still runs and step 3 alone is skipped: every mail, every row of `alert_steps()`. The channel's two alarms are the tick's own rows, once in 24 hours, raised with the limit store down as far as the tick runs. The steps at 15 minutes and at 45 minutes and the deadline, ending when the message is handled or the order's last item is disabled or closed; a person who was already signed in and works in the console is not reported as absent; a first and a second restart of one order's clock each alert again (`contact_mail`, the next free step); a second message inside the quiet period writes its row and no second step-0 push; `copy_overdue` once a day, from `alert_steps()` alone | 1, 2 |
| 15 | An alert carries counts and times only | `alert_outbox`'s CHECK refuses a string in `facts`; a unit test renders every `ALERT_TEXTS` entry with two sets of facts and finds no difference outside digits; no entry holds "@", "http" or a placeholder that is not a count or a time; the lists of kinds in SQL and in the contracts are equal, and every `ALERT_KINDS` entry says whether its mail is its sentence or its own | 1 |
| 16 | A hand-over holds one seal's files and nothing else | the service against a store of harmless files that are not images: the archive's entries equal the list byte for byte and are stored, not compressed; a name outside the store's layout and a list that differs from `seal_posts`' counts are refused with no archive; the recorded digests equal the printed ones. The store's naming rule exists twice, exported by the media module and copied in `handover.ts`, which imports no module: a unit test feeds both the same names and fails when they differ. The architecture test: `handover.ts` imports nothing under `./`, and no file outside `media/` but `config.ts` and `index.ts` names the store's directory setting | 5 |
| 17 | The report hold keeps an address and nothing else | a seal opens an `owed` record once an account; an admin's answer without a seal closes it, and so does a seal lifted by a correction or by a review (the record `declined` with `unsealed`, the hold released, the home line gone); a second seal that commits beside `notification-sent` still gets its record and its hold. With a record owed, a deletion takes everything down at once: the password hash, the TOTP secret and the phone hash go in the purge's first statement, and the address and `purged_at` alone wait, then go when the record is sent, declined or {reportHoldDays} days old. The mail: its one wording for every account, and the hold's paragraph in the variant of the seal that holds the record (`deciding` or `final`, by `auth.address_hold_final`), with the day from `auth.address_held_until`; nothing but `notification-facts` and `production-account`, as the migrator, reads the held address | 5 |
| 18 | A restore brings back records, never measures | a restore by `restoreForTest`, with the keeper's files as a keeper holds them: a tail that starts 300 places before the restored head (the earlier lines checked, not imported); a tail whose line at the head's place has another hash, a line whose hash does not recompute, and a tail that begins above the head's plus one, each refused whole; a hole in the files (the run up to it imported, the hole printed and in the audit row, exit 0). The orders written again with their original times, and an imported owed notification record with its original `opened_at`; an imported `preserve_until` freezes its post before any measure is back; a disabling taken again keeps `disabled_at`, `preserve_until`, the hold and the clock, and closes its gap in the same transaction; "Not needed" only by an admin, with a key, and refused at commit without its audit row | 6 |
| 19 | A status check sees what a person sees | through Caddy: `/post/<id>` answers 404 with the shell for a missing, deleted, withheld, removed and disabled post alike and 200 for a public one; 200 when the API cannot be asked or answers 429; the security headers equal in each; never 451; with `APP_SITE=closed` every path answers 503. The status route is mounted before the session's resolution: a request with a signed-in visitor's cookie reads no session | 4 |
| 20 | The counts come from rows | `tco-counts` over a fixture year equals a count by hand; a figure whose rows were erased prints "not recorded" | 7 |
| 21 | Records are kept as the terms say | `order_records_due()` empty while a measure of the order stands, while a decision or a correction of the order is untold, and before 3 years after its last event; a handled message deleted after 30 days and its `handled_at` set also when an order's event handled it, and a message nobody handled, stale or not, never deleted, however far its times are moved; a gap closed as not needed or imported deleted after 12 months, and a gap that a decision taken again closed kept, so that the decision's statement still prints its `reapplied` sentence a year later; a notification record kept while an event of it carries "not to the person" with an end that is open or ahead, and a production while its own is ahead; an `order_due` for a subject past its deadline is not written again when its old outbox rows are deleted; each step's audit row | 4 (orders, messages), 5 (the authority's records), 6 (gaps) |
| 22 | The notice says what korbat knows and nothing an order forbids | `statementParagraphs` for an `order` decision of each kind of order, verified and not, with and without recorded texts and scope, held and not (and held by korbat's mistake, `not_ordered`), reinstated on each ground, erased: none holds "korbat found", "It breaks" or a rule; none says that an authority issued the order before `verified` is true; none says "leaves korbat no choice" or "obliges" of a DSA order; none says "shows them to nobody"; the authority's words appear only in their block; no paragraph prints a mail address; the API answers no notice for a decision whose `informed_at` is null | 3 |
| 23 | The lock order holds | 3c's contention lab with the ten-item transaction against intakes on the same posts, a seal on one author and the purge of another, and against a retention round that ends the preservation of one of its items (the round takes the case, the item, the post, its image rows, in that order): no deadlock beyond the one retry, and the latency recorded | 3, 4 |
| 24 | A clock event, an order's closing and a gap's closing are what a key confirmed | as `korbat_api`, a call of `order_event_record` for `closed_without_effect`, for `annex3_sent`, for `reopened` or for `hold_extended`, a call of `order_correct` under each of its three rules (and under `admin` with a plain audit row in place of a confirmed one), and a `gap_close` to `not_needed`, each without the matching audit row as the transaction's head, with another order's row, without a step-up, or under another account: refused at commit, nothing left behind; with the row: stored, and `by_staff` is the audit row's actor | 2 (events; a correction before any effect), 3 (a correction after effect), 6 (gaps) |
| 25 | A wrong record can be corrected, and never stands in the way | before any effect, any staff member corrects each member the form took, with the event `corrected` (its ground the rule, `entry`) and the plain audit row `order_corrected` (the changed members by name); after an effect a moderator may only set "the author must not be told" (`caution`), which forces the withholding and makes every told decision of the order, and every decision about its posts told after them, untold again until the release (no mail pending, no row on Cases, no chip), and every other change needs an admin and the step-up `order_corrected` (`admin`); unsetting it ends the hold as `not_ordered` and the notice says so; a changed kind or withholding derives the level and the preservation of the measures in force again; a `corrected` event without its audit row is refused at commit (`order_event_refused`); every correction raises the alert `order_corrected`; a correction onto another order's state, authority and reference is `order_exists`; a closed order reopened by an admin can be given effect, with its times unchanged | 2 (before effect), 3 (after) |
| 26 | The reminders and the unverified alert follow every item | an order of two items with one disabled: `items_open` 1, `order_due` still written at 45 minutes and at the deadline, [home.overdue] shown, `within_hour` null; with the second disabled, or closed with a reason through `order_correct` (`closeItems`, once with each of the three reasons, `no_post`, `not_named` and `withdrawn`, and refused with any other word; by an admin with the key, since an item is disabled), the reminders end; an item whose link names no post counts as open until it is closed with `no_post`, and before any effect any staff member closes it without a key; an item added after `order-verify` and then disabled makes the order unverified again (the daily alert, `owed.verification`, Home's line) until `order-verify` is run again | 2 (the view), 3 |
| 27 | An order without an hour is verified before effect | a `dsa_art9` order with `verified_at` null: "Disable for an order" refused (`order_not_verified`) for a moderator and for an admin without `urgent`; accepted for an admin whose signed params hold `urgent: true`, and the alert `order_effected` then carries it; a `tco_removal` is never refused for it | 3 |
| 28 | The authority's reasons reach the author, with a time | `order-texts` on an order with a told decision sets `author_mail` pending again, the dispatch round mails `order_notice_added` (a decision whose `author_mail_at` is set gets that mail, not the first notice again), and the notice prints `orderTexts.textsAt`, the time of the event `texts_recorded`; on a held notice nothing is mailed, the release covers it, and `textsAt` is then the decision's `informed_at`, the later of the two; missing texts are counted in the daily alert | 3 |
| 29 | The copy is the keeper's, and a broken download moves no mark | `POST /api/staff/audit/export` without a step-up refused, and with one it writes its confirmed audit row `audit_exported` and no mark; the mark route writes the mark and the plain audit row `copy_marked` only when the named place holds the named hash and the caller's own newest `audit_exported` row signed that place (409 `copy_mismatch` otherwise, also for a place the caller did not download with a key); a body cut at line 200 leaves the `audit_exported` row and no mark, and the next download starts at the same place; a download by an account that did not take the last copy raises `copy_taken`; `audit-export` marks under the system actor's id, which no staff member's "newest mark" matches | 6 |

### 12.2 By kind

- **Unit.** `ORDER_RULES` equals `order_rules()`, member for column, each member the column's name in camel case (a database test reads the function; one constant mirrors one function, as 3e's `STANDING_RULES` does); `ALERT_RULES` holds only what the database never reads (`pollSeconds`, `tickSeconds`, `mailRetryHours`), and no number is in both. The step-up params of `order_effected`, `order_event_recorded`, `order_corrected`, `audit_exported` and `gap_closed` (strict, no transform, 3c's walk C7); `OrderEffectedParams` and `OrderOpenRequest` refuse `sectionC` true with `withholdFromAuthor` false, for every kind. `STATE_FACTS` has the home state's entry whenever `CONTACT_POINT` is set. The IMAP client's anchored expressions, one for each line it knows, over well-formed, unrequested and hostile lines (row 13). The zip writer against a reader, and against `node:zlib`'s `crc32`. The store's naming rule in its two copies (row 16).
- **The tables (P9).** Every number in `ORDER_WORDS`, `ORDER_TEXTS`, `ORDER_NOTICE`, `ALERT_TEXTS`, the terms' sections of W.6 and the README is held to `ORDER_RULES`; the console's message table has an entry for each new code a staff route can answer; each closed list of section 6 (dispositions, event kinds and grounds, the correction's rules, an item's closing reasons, hold end reasons, the rules for telling a person, scrutiny outcomes, duties, instruments, what was sent by kind, an authority's answers, the watcher's error words, gap classes) equals its constant, read with `pg_get_constraintdef`, so that one thing has one spelling in the database, the contracts and the words.
- **Mail.** The staff templates of W.4's first table and the three of W.5 pinned: no link in any; no handle, address, authority or reference in a 3f staff mail (3c's and 3e's six keep their own words, with the handle where they name one); `staffMail`'s and `userMail`'s closing sentences; `account_deleted` in its one wording for every account, and with the hold's paragraph in each of its two variants; "sub-project 3f" gone from `moderation_urgent` and `moderation_waiting`.
- **HTTP.** Each new staff route behind the staff gate, with its row in the staff route-table test and `PERSONAL`'s check over its answer; `GET /api/posts/:id/status` (204 or 404, no body, no session read, the same for every viewer; a malformed id is 404 with no body, not `validate`'s JSON); the audit export's stream (a `POST`, whose one write is its confirmed audit row) and the mark's route (row 29).
- **Console and app.** Server-rendered checks of every sentence of sections 8 and 9; the browser check of 8.10.
- **Smoke.** The stack with the profile off and `API_NOTIFY=off`: `docker compose config -q` passes with none of `notify`'s, ntfy's or the hand-over's settings set; a post's page answers 404 after its author deleted it and 200 before; `/api/staff/orders` answers 401 without a staff session. With the profile `notify` on: the push site answers 404 to `/v1/config`, and from inside the ntfy container the app's and the console's sites answer 403 (`--push`, a new option of `scripts/smoke-auth-stack.mjs`). In the dry run, with `notify` started on the host against the development mailbox (`scripts/dev-mailbox.mjs`, `pnpm --filter @korbat/api notify:dev`) and ntfy in Compose: a message dropped into that mailbox raises a push at a subscribed test client.

| Lab | On the real code | Passes when | Task |
|---|---|---|---|
| `lab-3f/` A to F as they are | Task 1 copies the folder into the repository as `apps/api/test/lab/orders/`, with its `RUN.txt`, as 3c keeps its labs under `apps/api/test/lab/`: an implementer in the repository cannot run a file outside it. Run again on the PostgreSQL 18.6 image (the model's ran on a native 18.1, since Docker's engine was down), on a database built from the migrations up to 0016 alone, as `RUN.txt` lists them, not from every migration the repository holds by then | the outputs equal the folder's `out-*.txt` but for times and ids | 3, as its first step |
| AS L2, L3 and L5 (minutes) | ntfy at the pin Task 1 takes, behind a Caddy site built like korbat's, on ports of their own | every path but the two topics' answers 404; a phone user's token cannot mint a token through Caddy; a message published during a restart is replayed. They are Task 1's gate | 1 |
| AS L1 and L4 (an hour idle; a WebSocket held open through Caddy for 12 hours) | started beside Task 1, not before it: they would hold the build for half a day | memory and disk recorded, the connection still open; their numbers are due in the task's record before Task 3 begins | 1, due before 3 |
| A, B, C, D | the built migrations and the routes, as tests (rows 1, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11, 24 and 26 above). LAB B gains an order with two items of which one is given effect, and an Annex III recorded later than the clarification arrived; LAB C and D gain the re-disabling after a reinstatement with the first end passed; the release's lab gains the correction that commits beside it | as the rows say | 2, 3 |
| D's timing | the ten-item transaction, quiet machine | its p95 recorded beside LAB D's 279 ms | 3 |
| E | `order_hold_end` as built | row 4 | 2 |
| F | a restore by `restoreForTest` with the keeper's files, which start before the restored head (LAB F's tail was cut exactly at the head, the one case a keeper never holds) | row 18 | 6 |
| all | once more in a container on 18.6, with the report in the task's record | no difference from the earlier runs | 7 |

## 13. Tasks

Seven tasks in build order. Each is one commit, written tests first by one implementer, and each leaves the stack working and the whole suite able to pass. Whatever a task's tests need is built in that task, or the test belongs to the task that builds it: no task reads a constant, a route, a screen or a fixture that a later task makes.

**What each task names.** "First" (a trial or a check that decides how the task is built, with what is built when it fails); "Migration"; "Builds"; "Files" (every file the task changes, among them the tests that pin what it changes: the unit suites are a gate of every task, so a pinned file that a task does not list stops it); "Unit files" and "Integration files" (12, What needs what); "Tests" (the invariants of 12.1); "Docker"; "Leaves".

**The gates** are light for every task: install gates when the lockfile changed, the type check, the unit suites, `generate` printing "No schema changes", the task's migrations applied, the console's build and browser check or the web export when that side changed, the integration files the task names, the bundles. A task that adds a migration and is no batch end (2, 4, 6) also runs its own integration files on a database upgraded from 3e's last migration. **The whole suite**, on a fresh and on an upgraded database, runs at the four batch ends (Tasks 1, 3, 5, 7) and nowhere else.

**Docker.** Every integration file needs the data services (the Compose PostgreSQL and Valkey), so every task needs those two containers. Docker's engine is needed for more at three points, which the tasks name: Task 1 (the stack rebuilt with the profile `notify` and smoke-tested; ntfy's labs), Task 4 (the stack rebuilt and smoke-tested; the page status through Caddy) and Task 7 (the walk-through on a rebuilt stack with the profile on; the labs in a container). `notify`, ntfy and `handover` are behind Compose profiles that are off in development and in CI: no test outside those three points starts them, and with the profiles off and none of their settings set every Compose command of the stack still works (Task 1's first smoke step).

**Before Task 1.** 3c and 3e are complete. The six migrations are numbered from the first number free after 3e's; section 6 alone writes the numbers. The tasks name them: the two hand-written ones as section 6 does (`orders_ledger`, `orders_retention`), and the four generated ones by the name passed to the generator (`messages_alerts`, `orders`, `authority_records`, `recovery`). Where a task replaces a function, it first reads the built text of that function "as 3e leaves it" (or "as 3c leaves it") and writes its replacement from that text, not from this design; a difference from section 6 is recorded as an amendment. For `decisions_record`, `decisions_apply` and `decision_actions_apply` that base is the text 3c's appeals migration (0017) and then 3e's leave, never 0016's, whose comments this design's step numbers follow. `alert_steps()` is created by `messages_alerts` and replaced by each later migration that adds a kind it writes.

### Task 1. The watcher and the alert channel (batch end)

- **First.** (1) The supply-chain check of section 5's table on the build's day: the newest ntfy release that is at least 7 days old on that day, pinned by tag and digest (the design's v2.28.0 was the newest on the day it was read, and its maintainer supports only the newest); its release notes read from v2.28.0 to it for the settings section 5 uses (`auth-default-access`, `auth-users`, `auth-access`, `auth-tokens`, `web-root`, `behind-proxy`, `cache-duration`, `listen-http`) and for the paths Caddy lets through; the image's licence label still `Apache-2.0, GPL-2.0`; no new package in korbat's programs. (2) AS's labs L2, L3 and L5 on that image, on ports of their own: minutes, and this task's gate. L1 and L4 (an hour, twelve hours) are started beside the task and their numbers are due in its record before Task 3 (12.3). (3) `lab-3f/` is copied into the repository as `apps/api/test/lab/orders/` with its `RUN.txt`, for Task 3. (4) A read of the chosen mail host against section 5's requirements, when the owner has chosen one; the dry run has none, uses the development mailbox, and says so in the task's record.
- **Migration.** `messages_alerts`. Its first statement checks that the role `korbat_notify` exists and holds USAGE on the schema `public`, and stops with "create the role korbat_notify first" otherwise: the schema's privilege is the superuser's to grant, and a grant of it inside the migration would be a warning and nothing more. Then `order_rules()`, `contact_messages`, `alert_outbox`, `notify_state`, `notify_folders`, the grants on tables and functions, and `alert_steps()` with the kinds that need no order (`contact_mail_escalated`, `order_due` for a message, `alert_test`). The channel's two alarms, `alert_channel_down` and `mailbox_unreachable`, are the tick's own rows and no part of the function.
- **Builds: the role and the harness.** The role and its schema privilege in three places: `infra/postgres/init/01-roles.sh`; `scripts/test-db.mjs` (in the block it runs as the superuser: the role when it is missing, and the grant for the test database); the README's Upgrading lines (W.9). The fourth role in the harness, by name: `KORBAT_NOTIFY_DB_PASSWORD` in `infra/.env.example` and passed to the `postgres` service beside the three it gets today; `DATABASE_URL_NOTIFY` in `apps/api/.env.example`; `notify` in the role list of `scripts/gen-dev-env.mjs` and in the pattern by which it derives `apps/api/.env.test`; a fourth key in `assertTestTargets`, with its test.
- **Builds: the services.** `notify` (`apps/api/src/notify/`: `main.ts`, `imap.ts`, `push.ts`) as a bundle and a Compose service under the profile `notify`, with `NOTIFY_IMAP_PORT` and `NOTIFY_IMAP_CA`, which it refuses in production; ntfy at its pin, reading its users, access and tokens from a mounted file (`infra/ntfy/server.yml`, git-ignored, made from the committed `server.yml.example`, which holds one development publisher and one development phone user); no setting of a profiled service required by Compose (`${NAME:-}`; the program names the missing setting and exits 1); Caddy's third site, and its two other sites refusing a client from the `push` subnet; the networks `push` (a fixed subnet) and `watch` (PostgreSQL and `notify`, nothing else); `infra/compose.dev.yml` publishing the push site's port (`DEV_PUSH_PORT`); `scripts/dev-mailbox.mjs` and the script `notify:dev`; `--push` for `scripts/smoke-auth-stack.mjs`. `notify`'s memory is measured idle and after the 10,000-message first read of row 13, and section 5's limit is taken from the number.
- **Builds: the alerts.** The alert tick, which always runs (`NOTIFY=off` skips its step 3 alone); its mail leg, for the kinds whose mail is their sentence; `staffAlert`'s outbox row for every kind of 3c and 3e, whose mails go as today with the caller's full message; the channel's health and the tick's own two alarms. `ORDER_RULES` and `ALERT_RULES` with the test against `order_rules()` (the function is created here, so its mirror is too); `ALERT_KINDS` and `ALERT_TEXTS` (W.4) in the contracts; the staff templates `contact_mail`, `contact_mail_escalated`, `order_due`, `mailbox_unreachable`, `alert_channel_down`; the last sentence of `moderation_urgent` and `moderation_waiting` (W.4).
- **Builds: the console.** The router's `contact`; the Contact point screen without its button "Open an order from this message", which needs Task 2's form; Home's [home.contact] and the three channel lines ([home.channel], [home.mailbox], [home.off]), not [home.clock] or [home.overdue], which link to Task 2's `/orders`; `bothTimes`; the phone rules in `app.css` for every existing screen the night needs (Home, a case, the composer's confirmation); the browser check's two new failure rules (8.10).

### Task 2. Order records and the clock

- **Migration.** `orders`: the eight enum values; `legal_orders`, with the CHECK that an order whose author must not be told withholds, the unique key on state, authority and reference, and the command line's columns (`hold_basis`, the scope's three, and `tell_person` and `tell_person_at` for an order to provide information, among them); `legal_order_items` with `added_at` and an item's closing (`closed_at`, `closed_reason`); `legal_order_events` with every kind section 6 lists, among them the four that the commands `order-received`, `order-sent-at`, `order-due` and `order-hold-until` insert, and `corrected`, `reopened`, the console's `hold_extended`, `tell_person_set` and `person_told`, with the deferred trigger `legal_order_events_confirmed`; the columns on `decisions` and `decision_actions`, and `decisions_informed`; `append_confirmed_audit` with the five new actions; the hold's function, whose end goes by the order's kind, and the preservation's; the view `legal_order_clock` with `items_open` and `last_effect_at`; `order_event_record` and `order_correct`, each bound to its audit row at commit; `alert_steps()` with an order's steps (`order_due` while an item is open, `contact_mail` on a restart) and `order_unverified` with its three conditions and a count for each.

No row can hold the level or a null before Task 3.

## 14. Success criteria

1. A message at the authorities' contact point is recorded with the mail host's delivery time within one poll, raises a push on the on-call phone and a mail to every staff account, and escalates by the database's clock until a person has acted. korbat reads nothing of the message but that it arrived and when.
2. From a phone, with one security key, a person on call records an order and gives effect to up to ten items with one confirmation, inside the hour and without the shell, also when the watcher is down, the mailbox cannot be read or the message is not listed; a check from outside then reads "not found" for each, by the page and by its HTTP status. An order counts as given effect in full only when each of its items is disabled or closed with a recorded reason, and its reminders run until then.
3. No post is disabled or reinstated for an order except by a decision that a staff key confirmed and that names its order. No job, command, import or trigger adds a measure, after a restore as before. An order waives exactly recusal's relation test, the staff-target rule and the decision caps, under a cap of its own, with every staff account told. No clock event, no closing of an order and no closing of a gap is stored without the audit row of the key that confirmed it. An order without an hour is given effect only after it is verified, or by an admin who signs that it is urgent.
4. While a notice is held, for whatever kind of order, nothing reaches the author on any path: the post reads to its author on every screen, in every statement, mail and export exactly as a post that no order touched would read in that state, and what cannot be said truthfully is held with the notice. An order whose author must not be told always keeps the post from its author.
5. The author is told at once, or, when the order forbids it: for a removal order at an end that the database alone computes from the Regulation's period and its one extension, never early anywhere in the EU's European territory; for another order at the time its issuing authority states, and at no time while it has stated none. No hold is shortened from the console but by an admin's signed correction of a tick the order never had, and every staff account is warned {holdWarningDays} days before any release, so that an extension that arrived meanwhile is recorded first. The notice says which authority the order names, and that korbat confirmed it once it has; gives the authority's reasons, remedies and scope in the authority's words, with a mail and a recorded time when they are added later; and never says or implies that korbat judged the content.
6. korbat's appeal is offered for no order, and korbat lifts an order's measure only on a recorded ground that is a fact about the order or korbat's own mistake. What korbat's own rules say about the same post is a second decision with its own statement and appeal, told with the order's notice when that is held.
7. What a removal order disabled is kept for six months from the disabling, and from the new disabling when it was reinstated and disabled again, against its author's deletion, the account's purge and every retention rule; it is opened only from the command line for a recorded purpose, and is erased when the period ends.
8. Every notification to an authority, every production and every hand-over has a record that the API's role cannot read. A hand-over holds exactly the files of one seal, byte for byte, and no program of korbat decodes one.
9. An address that a report to the authorities needs survives its account's deletion for at most seven days; the person is told in the deletion's own mail, in words that say no more than the seal's own statement said and that name the complaint to the authority and the court; the credentials and everything else of the account go at once. No deletion mail says that everything is gone while something is kept.
10. Every alert has a push and a mail; a dead watcher, a dead push channel and an unreadable mailbox are themselves alerted, by mail, and for the night in which the watcher or the database itself stops the on-call phone's own mail app on the contact mailbox is the second leg, set up and checked from the runbook. No push, and no mail of 3f's own, carries content, a link, a handle, an address, an authority's name or a reference. A push is owed for as long as its hour runs, not for five tries.
11. After a restore, the keeper's copy names every order, decision and record lost up to the keeper's last download, read from the files as a keeper holds them, and it says where the files themselves have a hole; a copy is recorded only once the keeper holds the file, under a key; each lost measure is taken again by a person under a newly enrolled key, an order's with its original preservation, hold and clock; and the app's site stays closed until every lost order disabling and seal is back.
12. The yearly figures of TCO Art. 7(3) and 21(1) are printed from rows, and a figure the rows cannot give is printed as not recorded.
13. The console runs under 3a's policy with no violation in the browser check over every new screen, at a width of 360 px.
14. A record that a person entered wrongly is corrected from the console: every member before any effect, by anyone; afterwards towards silence by anyone and otherwise by an admin with a key. A closed order can be reopened. No wrong record stands in the way of a true order.
15. The seven tasks can be built in order by the lighter loop: each names its files, among them every test that pins what it changes, its unit and integration files and what it needs of Docker; nothing a task's tests need is built by a later task; and with the profiles off and none of their settings set, every Compose command of the stack works.

## 15. Changes to the main design and to the 1b, 2b, 3a, 3c and 3e designs

Task 7 writes each as a dated amendment: in the main design's section 9, and as a section "Changed by 3f" in each other design.

**The main design** (`2026-09-03-korbat-design.md`).

- **Line 10** ("Every removal cites a public rule, shows the author exactly what happened, and can be appealed to a different human"): an authority's order is the exception to all three, and the terms say so beforehand. It cites the order, not a rule; its author is told at once unless the order forbids it, and then when the law, or for an order without a period of the law's the issuing authority, allows; korbat's appeal does not apply, and the notice names the authority's remedies. Reason: TCO Art. 3, 9 and 11; DSA Art. 9 and 17(5).
- **Line 191** ("A removed post stays in the author's view ... never silently gone"): holds for every decision of korbat's own. A post disabled under a removal order, or under any order that says its author must not be told, is shown to nobody while it is disabled. It is silently gone only while a non-disclosure decision runs: under a removal order for six weeks, for twelve at most, and up to a day and a few hours more, because korbat counts the end so that it is never early; under another order for as long as its issuing authority states. Then the author is told what happened and since when. During that time a decision of korbat's own about the same post, and the answer to an appeal about it, are told late too, with the notice. Reason: TCO Art. 6(3) and 11(3); DSA Art. 9(5). Flagged for the owner and counsel (section 2, rows 16, 24, 26, 46 and 47).
- **Line 293** (images of withheld and removed posts reach only their author): images of a disabled post reach nobody.
- **Lines 23 and 38 to 42** (the stack and the tunnel): the stack gains the services `notify` and `ntfy`, under a Compose profile that `COMPOSE_PROFILES` in `infra/.env` switches on, and, on demand, `handover`; Caddy gains a third site for the push hostname and the tunnel a third public hostname; the network `push` has no route to the internet and Caddy's two other sites refuse a client from it; PostgreSQL and `notify` meet on a network of their own, `watch`, so the stack's rule that the API alone reaches the data services gains exactly one named exception. The app's site answers 404 with the shell for a post's page that is not public, and 503 to everything while `APP_SITE=closed`.
- **Line 156** (backups): beside the backups, the keeper downloads the audit log's tail each day, under a staff key, and holds it off the server; after a restore it names what was lost, and a person takes each measure again.
- **Line 160** (alerts on moderation queue age): staff alerts reach phones through korbat's own ntfy and stay mails as well; a push carries counts and times only, and so does every mail of 3f's own.
- **Context and section 6, row 3:** korbat is offered in its home state only (section 2, row 6). The order of sub-project 3 is 3c, 3e, 3f, 3d; korbat is not opened to the public before 3f's order path and 3d's handling of sealed images have shipped and the owner's list (W.10) is done.

**The 1b design** (account lifecycle).

- **7.6 and success criterion 3** (`finishPurge` overwrites the address; a half-finished deletion is completed within five minutes): holds, except while a report to the authorities is owed for the account. `finishPurge` becomes two statements: the first, unchanged in its time, clears the password hash, the TOTP secret and the phone hash at once; the second, which overwrites the address and sets `purged_at`, waits until the report is recorded as sent or declined, at most seven days. Everything else runs as before. `REPORT_HOLD=off` (`API_REPORT_HOLD` in `infra/.env`) restores 1b's behaviour.
- **7.6 and the mail `account_deleted`:** its sentence "Your posts, images and profile are gone" becomes, for every account, "Your posts, images and profile are gone, except what the law obliges korbat to keep for a time (Terms, Your data)": a post preserved under an order outlives the account, and a wording for that account alone would tell what a non-disclosure decision forbids. When the address is held the mail gains one paragraph, in one of two variants by the seal that holds the record (W.5).
- **7.5** (the export): a disabled post and its images are left out, with no line that says so; an order's notice is in `moderation` once its author was told, and so is every decision that was held with it, never before (W.7).
- **7.6** (posts become tombstones): a post preserved under an order keeps its row, its text and its images through its author's deletion and the account's purge, until its preservation ends.
- **9** (the deletion screen): gains [delete.hold], which names both things the law can make korbat keep.

**The 2b design** (images).

- **4.5 and 5.5** (serving): no image of a post in the state `disabled` is served to anyone. `serve` needs no change, since it refuses a post state it was not taught; `reach`'s `served` leaves such a post out.
- **4.6 and 4.7** (delete, the sweeper): the image rows and files of a preserved post are kept through `frozen_for`, as a frozen post's are, and erased when the preservation ends.
- **2.6 and 9** (the boundary; infrastructure): the `media-store` volume gains a third reader, the `handover` service, read-only, without network, started by hand. Its program reads files by the names it is given and belongs to no module. The architecture test pins it two ways: `handover.ts` imports nothing under `./`, and no file outside `media/` but `config.ts` and `index.ts` names the store's directory setting. The store's naming rule is exported from the media module as one pure function, the program keeps a copy, and a unit test holds the two equal.
- **2.6** (the boundary): the media module's index gains three read-only functions: `imagePost(id)`, which answers which post an image's address belongs to, for opening an order; and two for the command line, each taking the caller's transaction, as `imageModerationStates` does: `preservedImages(tx, postId)` for `order-access` and `sealFiles(tx, sealActionId)` for `handover-list`. No file outside the module names `post_media`, the command line included.

**The 3a design** (the staff console).

- **Section 10:** `STAFF_STEP_UP_ACTIONS` gains `order_effected`, `order_event_recorded`, `order_corrected`, `audit_exported` and `gap_closed`; `case_decided`'s action entry gains `reinstate_by_order` with `orderGround`. One `order_effected` row confirms up to ten decisions of one transaction, each named in its metadata; every other action still confirms one.
- **Section 9:** the console is usable at a width of 360 px. The router gains `contact`, `orders`, `order` and `restore`; the audit page gains the download, which now asks for the key, and the key filter.
- **Section 7** (staff mails): each staff alert is also pushed, with a fixed text of counts and times; a staff mail still carries no link, and 3c's and 3e's staff mails keep their words. The runbook's kept head gains the daily download on the same visit.
- **Section 8** (origins and hosts): Caddy's third site, `:8091`, serves the push hostname and proxies only the two topics' paths; the console's site and policy are unchanged but for one rule, that the app's and the console's sites refuse a client from the `push` network; the console never talks to ntfy.

**The 3c design.**

- **Section 16** is superseded by this design for orders, alerts, records and recovery. Where it differs: the watcher polls and reads no header, sender or subject (not IDLE with "headers only"); an order is given effect by a decision of the kind `order` that answers only its own item, so "lane 0 for an order's item" and "its outcome phrase for notifiers" have nothing to apply to, and nobody who reported the post is answered by the order; the hold's end is computed from the order's day and is never early, not "42 days", and for an order that is not a removal order it is the time the issuing authority states; the push is added to the staff mails, not put in their place; `reverse-window` and European Production and Preservation Orders are not built.
- **6.1:** `frozen_for` keeps its name and now also holds what is preserved under an order (a fourth arm). `snapshot_withheld` is not changed: it still means that a copy's content was erased, which is what the author's decision view, the export and `access-request` read it for. The staff case page's snapshot read gains a term of its own, `public.post_preserved(post)`, the fourth arm's test and nothing else. **6.2:** `decisions.informed_at` may be null for an `order` decision, for its correction, and for any decision about a post whose order notice is held, and for no other, under the CHECK `decisions_informed` (an untold decision has `author_mail` `none`); the dispatch mails no decision whose `informed_at` is null, and every read of `decisions` on the author's side, `chipFacts` among them, takes the one fragment `toldToAuthor` (`author_mail <> 'none'` and `informed_at is not null`); `decision_holds` has a branch for `order_effected`; a `final` decision that closes its case sets `answered_seq` to `intake_seq`. **6.3:** `appeals_check` refuses an `order` decision.
- **7.12:** "3e replaces the mails with the phone channel" reads "3f adds the phone channel beside the mails"; the last sentence of `moderation_urgent` and `moderation_waiting` is W.4's. **7.15** is not amended: the staff mails keep their words, with the staff handle where they name one; their pushes are W.4's and name nobody. **7.13:** `account-facts` becomes `notification-facts`; `access-request` leaves an order decision its author was not told of, its post and every decision held with it out of what is sent, and marks the case for the operator (`access.noticeHeld`); it prints `access.authorityHold` for an authority's demand (W.8). **7.14** is replaced by section 7 and the runbook (W.9).
- **Section 9:** the terms' "Terrorist content" is W.6's. **Section 10:** redress version 3, `MEASURES`' two kinds, the decision kind `order`.
- **Section 13**, Task 12's runbook: the R2 paragraph's off-server records of a report become the records of 8.11's commands; the hard gate names 3f's order path. Task 9's test of `account-facts` is rewritten for `notification-facts` by 3f's Task 5.
- **Section 17**, What 3d receives: where it lists `frozen_for` and its callers, the predicate now means "frozen or preserved", and 3d's release adds its clause to the seal arm only. Which sub-project owns what: the question whether an account whose images are sealed may be deleted before the report is answered here (the address is held, the deletion is not).

**The 3e design.**

- **Section 2, row 27** ("no hold") and **section 15's line on 1b 7.6**: a deletion is still never refused or delayed; the address alone is held, for at most seven days, while a report is owed (section 2, row 35; flagged for the owner and counsel, with "no hold" as the stated alternative and one setting).
- **Section 6:** the functions 3f replaces are written from the text "as 3e leaves it"; `measures_in_force`'s list of kinds and `append_confirmed_audit`'s list of actions grow; the enum rule binds 3f's six migrations, and `enum-literals.test.ts` covers them.
- **Section 13**, Task 7's runbook bullet (c): "whoever has the server still runs `staff.js account-facts` before the admin answers the report, since korbat never holds an account against its deletion" is replaced by W.9's bullet: `notification-facts` after the seal for good, with the address held meanwhile. Task 4's tests of `PurgeReport.terminated` gain the member `held` (3f's Task 5).
- **Section 16** is superseded by this design. **W.3:** an order's redress is version 3's `order` texts. **W.6:** the two staff mails end as W.4 says.

## 16. What 3f leaves ready for 3d

- **The authority's answer to a report of sealed images.** `seal_report(action)` answers whether the seal was reported, the authority's reference and the day of its answer; it is executable by nobody until 3d grants it to its release. `restore-import` writes those records again, so the replay of releases after a restore (3c 17) has them.
- **The hand-over's reader.** The `handover` service mounts the image store read-only; when 3d moves sealed files to a quarantine volume it mounts that volume instead, and nothing else changes.
- **The purge of edge copies.** `disable_by_order` changes a post's level like any measure, so 3d's purge on every restriction covers it. `ORDER_RULES`' target of 55 minutes exists only for the five minutes an edge copy may live; with the purge it becomes 60 in a new version of the constant and the console's words follow.
- **The public pages.** `tco-counts` gives the yearly figures from rows; 3d prints them on the transparency page and publishes the yearly report (W.9 has the manual form until then). An `order` decision is never exported to the Transparency Database (DSA Art. 17(5)): the filter is the decision's kind.
- **The head's publication.** `copy_marks` and the audit export are the keeper's manual form of it.
- **Alerts.** A new alert is one kind added to `ALERT_KINDS`, which says whether its mail is its push sentence or a mail of its own, with its text in `ALERT_TEXTS`; the outbox, both legs and the channel's own alarm need nothing more. 3d's hash match raises `moderation_urgent`, as a finding citing R2 does.
- **The predicate.** `frozen_for` has four arms; 3d's release adds its clause to the seal arm only (3c 17), and a post that is both sealed and preserved stays frozen by the fourth. `post_preserved` is the staff case page's term alone and `snapshot_withheld` means what it meant in 3c: 3d reads neither for the release.
- **`STATE_FACTS`**, which the public pages read for the contact point's languages and the authorities' names.

## 17. Sub-project 3d (scope only)

3c 17 and 3e 17 stand as written, with 3f in place of 3e in "Which sub-project owns what". 3f adds four things to 3d's scope: the quarantine also moves the reader of section 16's hand-over; the release reads the notification record's answer through `seal_report` and refuses a seal that was never reported; the Cloudflare purge covers an order's disabling and ends the 55-minute target; and the transparency pages print the order figures of `tco-counts` beside the Art. 24 figures, with the sentence that korbat publishes no statement of reasons for an order since it gives none. 3d builds no path that tells an author of an order: the notice and its hold are 3f's alone.

## 18. The words (what sections 7 to 10 refer to by key)

Where each block belongs in the joined design: W.1 to W.3 in section 10 (constants of `@korbat/contracts` and `@korbat/rules`); W.4 and W.5 with section 7's mails and pushes; W.6 in section 9; W.7 with the export; W.8 with the command line; W.9 and W.10 are the README's.

Six rules hold for every text below.

1. **Nothing is told that an order forbids, on any path.** While a notice is held, the post reads to its author on every screen, in every statement, mail and export exactly as a post that no order touched would read in that state, and where that cannot be done truthfully the text is held with the notice. For the order's own texts that is one fragment of the API (section 7): no text of W.2 or W.5's order mails is rendered, sent, listed or exported for a decision that `toldToAuthor` does not hold for, so those words can assume their reader may be told. For every other text it is three more rules, none a property of the words: a decision of korbat's own about the post is not told while the notice is held (section 6), so no statement of 3c's can speak of "another decision"; the chip's read takes the same fragment; and a text that goes to every account alike (the deletion mail, the deletion screen, the terms) is worded once, as a rule, never as a finding about one account.
2. **korbat never says that it judged.** No text to an author says "korbat found", "it breaks" or "illegal" as korbat's own view of a post disabled for an order. Each says that an authority ordered it and that korbat did not assess it.
3. **The authority's words are the authority's.** Its reasons, its remedies and its order's territorial scope are printed whole, in the language they came in, inside a block marked as the authority's; korbat does not translate, shorten or comment on them, and they are the only free text in a notice.
4. **A push, and every staff mail of 3f's own, carries counts and UTC times only**: no content, no link, no handle, no address, no authority's name, no reference. Whoever reads a lock screen or a mail server's log learns that an alert of a kind went out at a time. 3c's and 3e's six staff mails keep their own words, which name a staff handle where they did; their pushes name nobody.
