5. **Every time** in a notice or a mail prints as `utcMinute` does ("2026-10-16 14:05 UTC"); an alert's time prints as "14:05 UTC" with the day only when it is not today's.
6. **A person is told nothing korbat does not know, and no duty the law does not give.** Until an order is verified a text says which authority the order names, not which authority issued it. A text says that the law obliges korbat only where the Regulation does (a removal order), never of another kind of order. No text says that something is shown to nobody, or that everything is gone, while a purpose of the law still opens it or keeps it. And no text promises a moment ("the first time it may", "for twelve weeks at most") that the database's own caution can miss: it prints the times, which are facts.

### W.1 Labels (`packages/contracts`)

- `MEASURES.disable_by_order.label`: "Disabled for an order". `MEASURES.reinstate_by_order.label`: "Reinstate after an order".
- `ORDER_KIND_LABELS`: 8.4's three. `ORDER_GROUND_LABELS`: 8.6's five and "It is not addressed to korbat" for `not_addressed`. `ANNEX3_GROUND_LABELS`: 8.5's three (`force_majeure`, `manifest_error`, `insufficient_information`; three grounds in one spelling everywhere, the database's CHECK included, and "impossibility" is part of the first one's label, not a fourth value). `DISPOSITION_LABELS`: 8.3's six and "An order" for `order`. `HOLD_END_REASON_LABELS`: "The order was not authentic" (`not_authentic`), "A court annulled the order" (`annulled`), "The authority released it" (`released_by_authority`), "korbat's mistake: the order had no non-disclosure decision" (`not_ordered`). `SCRUTINY_OUTCOME_LABELS`: "upheld: the order stands" (`upheld`), "annulled: the order infringes" (`annulled`). `ORDER_ITEM_CLOSE_REASON_LABELS`, for `ORDER_ITEM_CLOSE_REASONS`: "The link names nothing korbat can disable for an order" (`no_post`), "korbat's mistake: the order does not name this link" (`not_named`), "The authority withdrew this item" (`withdrawn`). `ORDER_CHANNEL_LABELS`, for `ORDER_CHANNELS`, the form's "How it arrived" (8.4): "By mail to the contact point: it is in the mailbox and not in this list" (`mailbox`), "Through a portal" (`portal`), "By post or in person" (`post`), "Another way" (`other`). Each of these lists has one spelling in section 6, in section 10 and here: the table test of 12.2 reads the CHECK and fails for a second one.
- `ORDER_EVENT_WORDS`, one phrase an event kind: `opened` "Order opened"; `annex3_sent` "Annex III sent: {ground}"; `grounds_ceased` "The grounds ceased: a fresh hour from {effectiveAt}"; `clarification_received` "Clarification received: a fresh hour from {effectiveAt}"; `annex2_sent` "Annex II sent"; `receipt_confirmed` "Authority informed"; `closed_without_effect` "Closed without effect: {ground}"; `effect_given` "Item {n} disabled"; `reinstated` "Item {n} reinstated: {ground}"; `notice_released` "The author's notice released"; `verified` "The check recorded".
- `ORDER_EVENT_WORDS`, the command line's and the correction's kinds: `issued_at_set` "The order's own date recorded"; `received_set`, `sent_at_set`, `due_set` "Time of receipt recorded", "Time of sending recorded", "Deadline recorded"; `texts_recorded` "The authority's reasons, remedies or scope recorded"; `document_recorded` "The document's digest recorded"; `hold_until_set` "The authority's end of non-disclosure recorded" (or "... recorded: no end named"); `hold_extended` "Non-disclosure extended once" (from the console, by an admin, or from the command line); `hold_ended` "Non-disclosure ended: {reason}"; `corrected` "The record corrected: {rule}" (the event's ground, one of `ORDER_CORRECTION_RULES`, in words: "before any effect" for `entry`, "the author must not be told, set after effect" for `caution`, "by an admin, after effect" for `admin`; which members changed, and an item's closing, are in the audit row, and a closed item shows its reason on its own block); `reopened` "Order reopened"; `tell_person_set` "Whether the person may be told recorded"; `person_told` "The person was told"; `preservation_extended` "Item {n} preserved longer, until {until}"; `preservation_ended` "Item {n}: preservation ended"; `scrutiny_requested` "Scrutiny requested"; `scrutiny_decided` "Scrutiny decided: {outcome}"; `imported` "Written again after a restore".
- **What a reporter reads.** Nothing new. An order answers no report, so no phrase of 3c 10 or 3e W.1 is added, and a reporter of a post that an order disabled keeps the line their own item has.

### W.2 The notice (`ORDER_NOTICE`, `packages/rules/src/statement.ts`)

For a decision of the kind `order`, `statementParagraphs` renders these paragraphs in this order, each only when its condition holds. Unlike a statement, the notice is rendered from the order's row as it stands, so it grows. `{address}` is the post's korbat address as text; `{memberState}` prints the state's name. `{dated}` is "and is dated {orderedOn}" once the order's own date is recorded, and until then "and reached korbat on {orderedOn}". The authority's three texts (its reasons, its remedies and the order's territorial scope) are not part of the statement's data: the screen prints `DecisionView.orderTexts` under their labels, so a text recorded later appears without a new decision, with `orderTexts.textsAt`, the time from which the author holds the newest of them: the time korbat recorded it, or the time the author was told of the notice when that is later. No paragraph prints a mail address: `statementParagraphs` runs in the app, in the console's preview and at the shell, and `@korbat/rules` holds none, so each says "the address under Contact in korbat's Terms".

| Key | When | Paragraph |
|---|---|---|
| `done.disabled` | the measure withholds from the author | "korbat disabled access to your post of {createdAt} ({address}) on {disabledAt}, because it received an order to do so, which this notice describes. Nobody can see the post or its images, you included." |
| `done.removed` | a DSA Article 9 order that does not withhold | "korbat removed your post of {createdAt} ({address}) on {disabledAt}, because it received an order to do so, which this notice describes. Other people can no longer see it; you still can." |
| 3c's `already`, `deletedPost` | the measure's `noEffect` | as 3c 10 |
| `lead.tco_removal` | a removal order | "The order is a removal order under Article 3 of Regulation (EU) 2021/784 on addressing the dissemination of terrorist content online. It names {authority}, {memberState}, as the authority that issued it, under the reference {reference}, {dated}." |
| `lead.dsa_art9` | a DSA Article 9 order | "The order is an order to act against illegal content, as Article 9 of the Digital Services Act (Regulation (EU) 2022/2065) describes it. It names {authority}, {memberState}, as the authority that issued it, under the reference {reference}, {dated}." |
| `lead.confirmed` | after either lead, once the order is verified (`verified`) | "korbat has since confirmed that the order comes from that authority." Until then the lead stands alone: korbat knows only what the order names, and a notice may reach its author before the check is recorded. |
| `notAssessed.tco_removal` | a removal order | "This is not a decision of korbat's. korbat did not assess your post, under its rules or under the law, and takes no view of it: the law obliges korbat to carry out such an order. No automated means were used: a person at korbat carried it out." |
| `notAssessed.dsa_art9` | a DSA Article 9 order | "This is not a decision of korbat's. korbat did not assess your post, under its rules or under the law, and takes no view of it: korbat carries out such an order because the law under which it was issued requires it. No automated means were used: a person at korbat carried it out." |
| `heldUntilNow.tco_removal` | a removal order; the author was told later than the decision | "The order came with a decision that you must not be told for a time (Article 11(3) of the Regulation). korbat was not allowed to tell you until now. The post has been disabled since {disabledAt}; korbat tells you on {informedAt}." The two times are facts; the paragraph promises no "first" moment, since korbat counts the end so that it is never early. |
| `heldUntilNow.dsa_art9` | a DSA Article 9 order; the same | "The order said that you must not be told until a time its authority set. korbat was not allowed to tell you until now. The post has been disabled since {disabledAt}; korbat tells you on {informedAt}. Because you were not to be told, korbat kept the post from you as well, and it stays so for as long as the order's measure stands." |
| `heldUntilNow.notOrdered` | the hold was ended as `not_ordered` | "korbat did not tell you of this until now, and that was korbat's own mistake: its record said that the order forbade telling you, and the order did not. The post has been disabled since {disabledAt}; korbat tells you on {informedAt}, and regrets the delay." It stands in the place of the two above. |
| `textsSince` | the authority's words were recorded after the author was told (`orderTexts.textsAt` is later than the time the author was told) | "korbat added the authority's words below to this notice on {textsAt} and sent you an email that day." |
| `reasonsLabel` | recorded | "The authority's reasons, in its own words:" and the text, in its block |
| `redressLabel` | recorded | "How the order can be challenged, in the authority's words:" and the text, in its block |
| `textsFollow` | the reasons or the remedies are not recorded | "korbat has not yet recorded the authority's reasons and the ways to challenge the order. It adds them to this notice as soon as it has, and sends you an email when it does. You can ask korbat for a copy of the order now: write, from the email address of your korbat account, to the address under Contact in korbat's Terms, and give the reference above." For a DSA Article 9 order this stands only when the order was given effect as urgent: otherwise its reasons, remedies and scope are recorded before the effect (W.9). |
| `copy` | both are recorded | "You can also ask korbat for a copy of the order: write, from the email address of your korbat account, to the address under Contact in korbat's Terms, and give the reference above." |
| `scrutiny` | a removal order from another Member State | "The order comes from another Member State than korbat's. You have the right to ask the competent authority of {homeState} to scrutinise it, with a reasoned request, within 48 hours of receiving the authority's reasons or a copy of the order (Article 4(4) of the Regulation). korbat cannot make that request for you." Then the fact from which the author can count: "This notice has held the authority's reasons since {reasonsAt}." (the time the author was told of the notice, or `textsAt` when the reasons were added after that) or "This notice does not hold the authority's reasons yet." korbat states the fact and leaves the counting of the 48 hours to the author and the authority. |
| `preserved` | a removal order, while preserved | "korbat keeps the post, its images and a copy of its text until {preserveUntil}, six months from the disabling, as Article 6 of the Regulation requires, so that it can be reinstated or examined; longer only if an authority or a court asks. Meanwhile korbat shows them to nobody but the authorities and courts the Regulation names, uses them for nothing else, and leaves them out of your data export. Then korbat erases them, and a later reinstatement cannot bring the post back. If you challenge the order, ask the court or the authority to tell korbat to keep the post until it is decided." |
| `preservedLonger` | the preservation was extended | "An authority or a court has asked korbat to keep them longer: until {preserveUntil}." |
| `erased` | the preservation ended while the measure stood | "The six months ended on {erasedOn}. korbat erased the post's text, its images and its copies of them. The post cannot be reinstated." |
| `scopeLabel` | a DSA Article 9 order whose scope is recorded | "The territory the order covers, in the authority's words:" and the text, in its block, directly before `everywhere`, so that the author can see what the order asked for and what korbat did |
| `everywhere` | a DSA Article 9 order | "korbat cannot restrict content for one country alone, so it gave effect to the order everywhere korbat is available, which may be more than the order asks for." A removal order's notice says instead "This applies everywhere korbat is available." |
| `reapplied` | the decision closed a restore gap | "The post was first disabled on {lostOn}. korbat lost its record of that when it had to restore its database from a backup, and disabled the post again with this decision; the six months above count from the first time." |
| `noAppeal`, `wrongItem` | always | W.3 |

**A reinstatement.** The correction that holds `reinstate_by_order` renders three paragraphs and W.3's `lifted`; on the Cases screen they stand under the notice they end.

- `reinstated.lead`: "korbat lifted the measure it took on {disabledAt} for an authority's order on your post of {createdAt} ({address})."
- `reinstated.<ground>`, which is also the fixed text `ORDER_DRAFTS.reinstate[ground]` that the composer saves as the correction's reason:

| Ground | Text |
|---|---|
| `withdrawn` | "The authority that issued the order withdrew it." |
| `annulled` | "A court annulled the order." |
| `scrutiny` | "The competent authority of korbat's own Member State scrutinised the order and decided that it infringes the Regulation or fundamental rights, so the order no longer has effect (Article 4 of Regulation (EU) 2021/784)." |
| `not_authentic` | "The order was not authentic: it did not come from the authority it named. korbat disabled your post on a forged order, and regrets it." |
| `wrong_item` | "korbat made a mistake: the order did not name this post. korbat disabled it wrongly, and regrets it." |

- `reinstated.now`: "Your post is shown again as before, unless another decision listed under Settings, Cases still restricts it." or, after the erasure, `reinstated.erased`: "korbat had already erased the post's content when its six months ended on {erasedOn}, so nothing can be shown again."

**The sentence a decision taken again adds** (`STATEMENT_WORDS.reapplied`, for every other kind of decision that closes a restore gap, after its lead): "korbat first took this decision on {lostOn}. It lost its record of that when it had to restore its database from a backup, and has taken the decision again here. Your time to appeal counts from this one."

What these paragraphs read. `DecisionStatement.order` holds `kind`, `memberState`, `authority`, `reference`, `orderedOn`, `preserveUntil`, `preserveEndedOn`, `orderGround`, and, each a fact of the order's row that section 10 must list with them: whether `orderedOn` is the order's own date or the day of receipt (`orderedFrom`: `issued` or `received`); `verified`, for `lead.confirmed`; `crossBorder` and `homeState`, for `scrutiny`; `preserveExtended`, for `preservedLonger`; the measure's `withholdAuthor`, for the first paragraph; `disabledAt`, when the measure was taken; `held`, whether the notice was held, which is what "the author was told later than the decision" means in the table above; `informedAt`, the one name for the instant the author was told (the release, for a held notice; section 10's draft called it `heldUntil`, a name that reads as the hold's end and is not used); and `holdEndReason`, for `heldUntilNow.notOrdered`. `DecisionView` holds, read fresh, `orderTexts` (`reasons`, `redress`, `scope`, each the text or null, and `textsAt`) and `erasedOn`; `reapplied` is the statement's.

### W.3 Redress version 3 (`REDRESS_TEXTS[3]`; `REDRESS_VERSION` becomes 3)

Version 2's texts stand word for word. One group joins, `order`, which is `ORDER_NOTICE`: W.2's paragraphs and the four below. So a notice reads under its own version, and a changed sentence is a new version.

- `noAppeal.tco_removal`: "korbat's appeal does not apply: this is not korbat's decision, and korbat cannot lift it on its own review. You have the right to challenge the order before the courts of the Member State whose authority issued it (Article 9 of Regulation (EU) 2021/784). How, where and within what time is for that authority to say; korbat passes on what it said in this notice. If the order is withdrawn or annulled, korbat reinstates your post at once and tells you."
- `noAppeal.dsa_art9`: the same, with "The order's own text says how it can be challenged" in place of the second sentence.
- `wrongItem`, after either: "One thing is korbat's own: whether it disabled the post the order names, and only that. If the order does not name this post, write, from the email address of your korbat account, to the address under Contact in korbat's Terms, with the post's address and the reference above. korbat then compares the post with the order and, if it made that mistake, corrects it and tells you; if not, it answers why." (The paragraph prints no address, as W.2 says; the app's own sentence beside it does, 9.3.)
- `lifted`, for a reinstatement: "Nothing of the order's measure remains on this post. korbat's appeal does not apply to this. Where another decision about the post still stands, its own screen says whether you can appeal it."

### W.4 Pushes and staff mails (`ALERT_TEXTS`)

Two tables of the contracts, side by side: `ALERT_KINDS` holds each kind's priority, scope, legs, the names of its facts, and whether its mail is its push sentence (`mail: "sentence"`), a mail of its own (`mail: "own"`) or none, for a kind that is pushed only; `ALERT_TEXTS` holds each kind's title, sentence and the mail's line of what to do. `notify` imports both.

For the kinds of the first table below one text serves both legs. The push is the title and the sentence, with the kind as its tag. The mail's subject is "korbat: " and the title; its paragraphs are the sentence, the kind's line of what to do, and `staffMail`'s closing sentence, "korbat never puts a link in a staff email." A value in braces is a member of the row's `facts`: a count or a time. These are the only kinds the tick's mail leg mails from a row, whoever wrote the row (the trigger, `alert_steps()`, `staffAlert` after a commit, or a command).

| Kind | Title | Sentence | The mail's line of what to do |
|---|---|---|---|
| `contact_mail` | "Mail at the contact point" | "Mail at the authorities' contact point, received {receivedAt}. If it is a removal order it is due {dueAt}." After a restart of an order's clock, when the row has no `receivedAt`: "A removal order's hour has started again: due {dueAt}." | "Read it in the mail host's app, never in korbat, and with it every other new message there: further mail within {quietMinutes} minutes raises no alarm of its own. Then open Contact point in the staff console. What ends this message's alarms is handling it there: opening an order from it, or closing it." |
| `contact_mail_escalated` | "Nobody has answered: mail at the contact point" | "Nobody has answered the mail that arrived at the authorities' contact point at {receivedAt}. If it is a removal order it is due {dueAt}." | "This went to every staff phone. Whoever reads it first acts, and tells the others." |
| `order_due` | "{minutesLeft} minutes left" | "A removal order, or mail that may be one, is due {dueAt}: {minutesLeft} minutes are left and it is not carried out in full." | "Give effect now, to every post the order names. Only if the order has a manifest error, or lacks what you need to find the content, send Annex III and record it: that is no way to stop this alarm. If nobody can act in time: the README, When nobody can act within the hour." |
| `order_opened` | "An order was opened" | "An order was opened: {items} items. Due {dueAt}." (without a deadline: "No deadline runs." or, opened without a time of receipt, "Its deadline is unknown: act now.") | "If you are not the one on call, you need do nothing. If nobody was on call, ask who opened it." The same alert goes out for an order opened from the command line. |
| `order_corrected` | "An order's record was corrected" | "An order's record was corrected at {at}: {count} of its entries changed." | "Every staff account gets this, because a record can be corrected without a key. If you are on call, read the order again before you act on it. If nobody says it was them, a staff session is in someone else's hands: tell the operator at once." |
| `order_effected` | "An order was given effect" | "An order was given effect at {at}: {items} posts disabled." With `itemsOpen` above zero it adds "{itemsOpen} items of the order are still open." With `urgent` 1 it reads "An order that is not verified was given effect as urgent at {at}: {items} posts disabled." | "Every staff account gets this. If it was confirmed with your key and not by you, someone else holds your key: tell the operator at once." |
| `order_reinstated` | "An order's measure was lifted" | "An admin lifted an order's measure on a post at {at}." | the same |
| `order_unverified` | "Orders with something missing" | "{count} orders are given effect and not verified, or hold a post added after their check, the oldest since {oldestAt}." With the counts above zero it adds "{textsMissing} are given effect and have no reasons of the authority recorded for their authors." and "{holdsOpen} hold a notice with no end recorded." | "Whoever has the server compares each with the order in the mailbox and records what is missing: staff.js order-verify, order-texts, order-hold-until. This repeats daily until then." |
| `hold_ending` | "A held notice is about to be released" | "{count} held notices are released within {holdWarningDays} days, the first on {at}." | "Whoever has the server checks the contact-point mailbox today for an extension or a later time from the authority, and records it before that day. If nothing came, nothing is to be done: korbat tells the author by itself." |
| `report_owed` | "A report to the authorities is owed" | "{count} reports to the authorities have been owed for more than 24 hours, the oldest since {oldestAt}." | "The admin answers the case; whoever has the server reports and records it (staff.js records). This repeats daily until then." |
| `handover_undestroyed` | "A hand-over copy still exists" | "{count} hand-over archives are older than 7 days and not recorded as destroyed." | "Whoever has the server deletes every copy and records it with staff.js handover-destroyed." |
| `copy_overdue` | "The copy is overdue" | "The audit log's tail was last downloaded {hours} hours ago." | "The keeper opens the console's Audit log today and presses Download since my last copy." |
| `copy_taken` | "The audit log's tail was downloaded" | "A staff account that did not take the last copy downloaded the audit log's tail at {at}: {rows} rows." | "If you are the keeper's stand-in, or asked for it, nothing is to be done. If nobody says it was them, a staff session and its key are in someone else's hands: tell the operator at once." |
| `mailbox_unreachable` | "The contact point cannot be read" | "korbat has not been able to read the contact-point mailbox since {since}." | "Mail that arrives there raises no alert now. Check the mailbox in the mail host's app until this is repaired; an order you find there is opened in the console under Orders, with "The message is not listed". The operator checks the password and the mail host." |
| `alert_channel_down` (mail only) | "Phone alerts are down" | "Phone alerts are down since {since}: korbat's watcher or its push server does not answer." | "Until it is repaired, korbat wakes nobody: the mail host's own app on the on-call phone, signed in to the contact-point mailbox and allowed to ring at night, is the only thing that does. Check now that it is. An order you find there is opened in the console under Orders, with "The message is not listed". The operator restarts the notify and ntfy services." |
| `alert_test` (push only) | "Daily test" | "Daily test, {at}. If you read this on your phone, alerts reach it. Nothing to do." | |

**3c's and 3e's kinds** keep their mails word for word (3c 7.15, 3e W.6), with the staff handle where they name one, and gain a push that names nobody. They are the kinds with `mail: "own"`: the outbox row is for the push alone, `staffAlert` mails as today with the caller's full message and then sets `mailed_at` whether or not an address took the mail (an unsent alert stays 3c's case: the throttle's hit is given back and the next occasion sends it again), and the tick never renders one of these mails from a row, whose `facts` could not fill it.

| Kind | Title | Sentence of the push |
|---|---|---|
| `moderation_urgent` | "Urgent lane" | "{count} cases wait in the urgent lane, the oldest since {oldestAt}." |
| `moderation_waiting` | "Cases are waiting" | "{count} cases or replies are waiting longer than their response time." |
| `moderation_burst` | "Many decisions in a short time" | "One staff account confirmed {count} decisions in 15 minutes. The staff mail says which." |
| `moderation_reads` | "A daily case limit was reached" | "One staff account opened {limit} different cases today. The staff mail says which." |
| `moderation_reveal` | "A reporter was revealed" | "One staff account revealed who sent a report, at {at}. The staff mail says which." |
| `moderation_csam_lifted` | "Reported images are no longer protected" | "A decision at {at} ended the protection of reported images. Read the staff mail." |

The last sentence of two staff mails changes: `moderation_urgent` ends "Open the staff console. The same alert went to the phone on call." and `moderation_waiting` ends "Open the staff console. The same reminder went to every staff phone."

### W.5 Mails to a person

The three order mails are members of `mailAccount`'s union, built with `userMail`, so each ends "korbat staff never ask for your password or a code, and korbat does not read replies to this email." None names a post, an authority or an order, and none holds a link or a mail address: a mail passes a relay and a mailbox that korbat does not control.

- **`order_notice`**. Subject: "korbat has a notice for you". "korbat has a notice about one of your posts. Sign in to korbat and read it under Settings, Cases." "This email says no more than that, and korbat's emails never contain a link to act on a notice."
- **`order_reinstated_notice`**. Subject: "korbat has added to a notice for you". "korbat has added to a notice about one of your posts. Sign in to korbat and read it under Settings, Cases." and the same last sentence.
- **`order_notice_added`**: the same subject and the same words as `order_reinstated_notice`, under a key that says what it is for. It is sent when korbat records the authority's reasons, remedies or scope for a notice whose author was already told: a time limit of the author's runs from receiving them, so they do not appear unannounced. The instant is recorded, as the time of the order's event `texts_recorded`, and the notice prints it (`orderTexts.textsAt`; W.2 `textsSince`, `scrutiny`). For a notice that is still held nothing is sent: the release's `order_notice` covers what the notice then holds.
- **`account_deleted`, for every account.** 1b's sentence "Your posts, images and profile are gone, and your handle stays reserved so nobody can take it." becomes "Your posts, images and profile are gone, except what the law obliges korbat to keep for a time (Terms, Your data), and your handle stays reserved so nobody can take it." It is one wording for every account, because it is a rule and not a finding about this one: a post preserved under an order outlives the account, a sentence for that account alone would tell what a non-disclosure decision forbids, and "everything is gone" would be false. The mail never says more than that about a preserved post.
- **`account_deleted`, the paragraph added when the address is held**, after 1b's two, in one of two variants, chosen by the seal that holds the notification record at the moment of the mail (`auth.address_hold_final(account)` answers which, from the hold's `seal_final`, section 6). Neither says more than the seal's own statement told the person, and each gives the reason, the end and the three routes GDPR Art. 12(4) asks for.
  - `account_deleted.held.final`, a seal for good, whose statement already said that korbat reports the matter: "One thing korbat keeps for a short time: your email address, and nothing else of the account. korbat has sealed images of the account, and the law may oblige it to report them to the authorities, for which it needs the address. It uses the address for nothing else and erases it as soon as that is settled, on {heldUntil} at the latest. You can write to korbat about this at the address under Contact in korbat's Terms, complain to a data protection authority, and go to court."
  - `account_deleted.held.deciding`, a seal taken while deciding, whose statement said only that the images are hidden until an admin has answered a report: "One thing korbat keeps for a short time: your email address, and nothing else of the account. korbat has sealed images of the account until an admin has answered a report about them. Until that is settled, and until {heldUntil} at the latest, korbat keeps the address because the law may oblige it to keep it for that time, and uses it for nothing else. You can write to korbat about this at the address under Contact in korbat's Terms, complain to a data protection authority, and go to court."
  - `{heldUntil}` is the day of `auth.address_held_until(account)`. Neither variant offers a right to "object": the hold rests on a legal obligation, and whether GDPR Art. 21 gives that route here is counsel's (section 2, row 35), as is the wording of the reason while nobody has judged the report.

A mail that cannot be sent is given up after `RETENTION.mailGiveUpDays`, as any statement's; the notice stays on the Cases screen. A notice released to an account that was deleted during its hold has no address to go to: it is set pending and given up the same way, without an error.

### W.6 The terms (`apps/app/src/lib/terms.ts`)

Draft, without a new `TERMS_VERSION`, as 3c 9's and 3e W.7's sections; counsel reviews them in sub-project 4. Each `{name}` is printed from `ORDER_RULES`, `RETENTION` or the configuration, and the table test holds each section to it. Every sentence about what an author is told, and about the address that is kept, is flagged `(counsel)` in section 2.

**"Authorities' orders"** (new)

"An authority or a court of the European Union or of one of its countries can order korbat to act against a post, or to give information about an account. korbat carries such an order out within the time the law sets and checks that it comes from the authority it names; if it does not, korbat reinstates the post and tells you. A removal order for terrorist content leaves korbat no choice, and has to be carried out within one hour, so korbat may act on it before that check is finished. Any other order korbat checks first, and carries out because the law under which it was issued requires it. korbat does not assess the post under its own rules when it carries out an order: such an order is not a decision of korbat's.

So korbat gives no reasons of its own for it, and korbat's appeal does not apply. You get a notice instead, under Settings, Cases, announced by an email that says only that there is one. The notice says which authority the order names, under which law, on which day and under which reference, and whether korbat has confirmed that it comes from that authority. It gives the authority's reasons and the ways to challenge the order, in the authority's own words, as soon as korbat has them; when korbat adds them later it sends you an email, and the notice says since when it has held them. The notice also says how to ask korbat for a copy of the order.

An order can forbid korbat to tell you about it for a time. Then korbat keeps the post from you as from everyone, tells you nothing, and can say nothing about the post by email either. For a removal order the law sets that time (Terrorist content); for another order the authority that issued it does, and korbat tells you when that authority allows. While korbat may not tell you of an order, it cannot tell you of a decision it takes about the same post under its own rules, or answer an appeal you made about that post before: you get those together with the notice.

korbat lifts what it did only when the authority withdraws the order, when a court annuls it, when the authority of korbat's own country finds that an order from another country infringes the law, when the order turns out not to be authentic, or when korbat disabled a post that the order did not name. For that last case write to the address under Contact, from the email address of your account. When korbat's own rules apply to the same post, that is a second decision, with its own statement of reasons and its own appeal. korbat cannot act for one country alone, so an order takes effect everywhere korbat is available."

**"Terrorist content"** (3c 9's section, in full as it now reads)

"korbat removes terrorist content as defined in Article 2(7) of Regulation (EU) 2021/784 when it is told about it in a notice, and carries out a removal order of a competent authority within one hour of receiving it. Material for educational, journalistic, artistic or research purposes, or for preventing or countering terrorism, and polemic or controversial views in public debate are not terrorist content. korbat uses no automated tool to find it.

A post disabled under a removal order is shown to nobody, you included, for as long as the order stands. korbat keeps it, with its images and a copy of its text, for {orderPreserveMonths} months from the disabling, as the Regulation requires, so that it can be reinstated or examined, and longer only when an authority or a court asks. Deleting the post or your account does not shorten this. Then korbat erases it, and it cannot be reinstated afterwards.

The law can oblige korbat to tell you nothing about such an order for six weeks, for twelve at most, and up to a day and a few hours more, because korbat counts the end so that it is never early. During that time the post is simply not there, for you as for everyone, and korbat cannot say why. korbat tells you when that period ends, with the day on which the post was disabled. If the order comes from another country of the European Union than korbat's, you can ask the authority of korbat's country to scrutinise it, within 48 hours of receiving its reasons or a copy of it; the notice says since when it has held the reasons. If you challenge an order in court, ask the court or the authority to tell korbat to keep the post until that is decided, since korbat erases it after the {orderPreserveMonths} months otherwise. korbat publishes the figures the Regulation asks for, for every year in which any of them is not zero."

**"Contact point for authorities"** (new; printed only while the web build's `EXPO_PUBLIC_CONTACT_POINT` is set: the app's page is built, so it reads the address and the home state from its build's two arguments, Task 7, and the languages from `STATE_FACTS`)

"Authorities of the Member States, the European Commission and the European Board for Digital Services reach korbat at {contactPoint} (Article 11 of the Digital Services Act; Article 15 of Regulation (EU) 2021/784), in {languages}. This address is for authorities. Everyone else writes to the address under Contact, and reports content with the report form."

**Changed sentences.**

- "Who can use korbat" gains: "korbat is established in {homeState} and offers its service there only."
- "What you can post" gains, after its last sentence: "The one restriction that is not korbat's decision is an authority's order (Authorities' orders)."
- "How moderators decide" lists the measures from `MEASURES`, so the two new labels join it; "Appeals" gains "korbat's appeal does not apply to what korbat does on an authority's order. If such an order forbids korbat to tell you about a post, korbat's answer to an appeal you made about that post reaches you only when it may tell you of the order; your time to appeal a decision you are told of late counts from the day you are told."
- "Your data" gains, as four paragraphs:
  - **What is kept when you delete your account.** "Two things the law can oblige korbat to keep for a time when you delete your account; everything else is deleted at once. First, your email address: when korbat has sealed images of your account, the law may oblige it to report them to the authorities. Until it has done so, or has decided that it need not, and for {reportHoldDays} days at most, korbat keeps the address, and nothing else of the account; it uses it for that report alone, and the email that confirms your deletion tells you. You can write to korbat about this at the address under Contact, complain to a data protection authority, and go to court. Second, a post that an authority's order obliges korbat to preserve: korbat keeps it, with its images, until that time ends (Terrorist content), also after your account is gone."
  - **What korbat records about authorities.** "korbat keeps a record of every report it makes to an authority and of everything it hands to one: which duty, which authority, when, both references, what was asked for and which kinds of data, never a copy of the data. It keeps these records and its records of authorities' orders for {orderRecordYears} years after the matter's last event, an order's record for as long as its measure stands, and a record that says you may not be told of something for as long as that holds. korbat records when a message reaches its contact point for authorities, never who sent it or what it says, and deletes that record {contactMessageDays} days after the message was handled."
  - **What an authority can get, and whether you are told.** "When an authority lawfully asks who holds an account, korbat gives the handle, the email address and the day the account was created. korbat also holds when and from which kind of device an account signed in; it gives those only when an order names them and the law requires it, and its record says so. korbat tells you when it has given an authority information about your account, with the authority's reasons and the ways to challenge its order, unless the order, or the law under which it was issued, forbids telling you or delays it; korbat records which of these applies and on what basis. While korbat may not tell you, its answer to a data request of yours leaves the matter out, and korbat keeps the reason on record."
  - **After a restore.** "If korbat has to restore its database from a backup, it may have to take a decision about you again; the decision then says so."

`RETENTION`'s printed list gains `orderPreserveMonths` ("content disabled under a removal order: 6 months and a day from the disabling, and from the new disabling when it was reinstated and disabled again; longer on an authority's or a court's request; then erased"), `orderRecordYears`, one period for an order's records and for the records of a notification or a production, since `order_rules()` has one `record_keep_years` ("3 years after the matter's last event; an order's never while its measure stands; a record of an authority's demand that a person is not told never while that demand holds"), `contactMessageDays` (30, counted from the day the message was handled; the record of a message nobody handled is kept until a person has closed it), `restoreGapMonths` ("12 months for a gap closed as not needed or imported; a gap that a decision taken again closed is kept as long as that decision") and `ORDER_RULES.reportHoldDays` (7).

### W.7 The export's members

- **Posts and files.** A post in the state `disabled` and its images are left out, and no line says that something was left out. After a reinstatement they are in the export again.
- **`moderation.cases`.** An order's decision is an entry like any other once its author was told, never before: the statement's data with its `order` member (the order's kind, state, authority, reference and day, whether korbat has confirmed it, `held` and `informedAt`, which say whether and until when the notice was held, the preservation's end) and, read at the export's time, `orderTexts` (the authority's reasons, remedies and scope, each or null, and `textsAt`) and `erasedOn`. A reinstatement is the entry of its correction, with its ground. The export's cases take the one fragment `toldToAuthor`, in both its halves: a decision of korbat's own that was held with a notice is an entry only once it was told, and a decision that 3c never tells its author of is never one.
- **An earlier decision about a post that is now preserved** keeps the entry it had, with the content it had: the export reads `snapshot_withheld`, which preservation does not change, so no entry says that content was erased when none was.
- Nothing in the export names a notification to an authority, a production or a held address: the first two are not korbat's to tell where an authority forbade it, and the third exists only after the account is gone.

### W.8 Fixed sentences of the command line

- **What korbat holds of an account**, printed by `notification-facts` and `production-account` as "name: value" lines and nothing else: `handle`, `email`, `created_at` (UTC), and for a notification each post's `id` and `created_at`. The display name, the bio and the posts are public and are not part of a production.
- **`records.notHeld`**, printed after them and stored with the record as the sentence of what korbat does not hold and of what it holds and did not produce: "korbat holds no name, postal address, date of birth, phone number or IP address of this account. It holds IP addresses, and a phone number the account verified, only as keyed hashes that identify nobody outside korbat; a device's hash is deleted 30 days after the device was last seen. korbat also holds, and has not produced here, the times and device types of the account's sign-ins." This is version 1 of the sentence: a notification's record stores which version went with it (`not_kept_version`, section 6), and a changed sentence is a new version. A production for an order also stores what the order asked for, by kind (`production-account --asked <kind,...>`), so the record says what was asked, what was given and what was not. Whether the sign-in times are produced at all is counsel's, order by order; no command prints them.
- **`access.authorityHold`**, printed by `access-request` in place of the lane-0 mark for a case whose notification record carries an event marked "not to the person" whose end is ahead or open: "An authority asked that this person is not told of its request (until {day})." or "... (no end given yet)." then "Do not send this case's part of the draft, and do not say that an authority is the reason, before counsel has read it." The record that carries the mark is kept for as long as the mark holds, whatever its age (section 6), so the mark cannot be erased before its end.
- **`access.noticeHeld`**, printed by `access-request` for a case that holds an `order` decision its author was not told of, in the place where the case's part would stand; the operator reads the draft, the person never does: "An order's notice about this post is held until {holdUntil}." or "... is held, with no end recorded." then "Leave the order, the post and every decision marked below out of the answer, and note the reason in the operator's records, not in the answer." The decisions held with the notice are marked the same way. What the command prints to send holds none of them.
- **The order's printed texts** (`order-access`): "----- preserved content of item <id>: for the recorded purpose only -----" before and "----- end -----" after.
- **A refusal** prints the code's sentence: `already_released` "the author's notice was already released, or its hold was ended; nothing changed" (only for an order with Section C: an order that holds nothing takes `order-issued` at any time); `already_extended` "the non-disclosure period was already extended once; nothing changed"; `tell_rule_missing` "whether the person may be told of this order is not recorded: run staff.js order-tell-rule first; nothing changed"; `order_ambiguous` as 8.11 has it; `nothing_held` "no notice of this order is held; nothing changed"; `not_preserved` "this item's preservation has ended; nothing changed"; `handover_mismatch` "the image rows of this seal's posts are not what the seal recorded: run staff.js text-verify, and hand nothing over; nothing changed"; `tail_not_continuous` as 8.11 has it.

### W.9 The README's runbook

Task 7 writes these as sections of the README, after "Staff console". Task 1 writes the Upgrading lines and Task 6 "Restoring" when their code lands. A number is printed from `ORDER_RULES` or `ALERT_RULES` by the docs test's rule. A setting is written as the operator sets it in `infra/.env`: an API setting carries the prefix Compose maps (`API_NOTIFY`, `API_REPORT_HOLD`, `API_MODERATION_RETENTION`, `API_HOME_STATE`, `API_CONTACT_POINT`, `API_PUSH_ORIGIN`, `API_APP_ORIGIN`, as section 5's table lists them), while `MEDIA_RECONCILE`, `APP_SITE` and `COMPOSE_PROFILES` have none.

**The contact point.**

- The contact point for authorities is a mailbox at an EU mail host, set as `API_CONTACT_POINT`. korbat watches it and reads nothing of a message but that it arrived and when; a person reads every message in the mail host's own app. Remote content is switched off in that app, and no attachment is opened on a device that holds a staff key before the sender is checked against the register.
- Every message there may be a removal order until someone has read it. Junk is watched too: look into the junk folder whenever an alert came and the inbox shows nothing new. The mail host must file nothing anywhere else: no rule, filter or quarantine of the host may move or delete mail before it reaches one of the two folders, and whoever is on call looks through every folder of the mailbox once a day, because a folder korbat does not watch is an order nobody sees.
- Two credentials open the mailbox and they are not the same. `notify` has an application password that can only read. Each person on call has the mailbox's own sign-in, with its second factor, in the mail host's app on the on-call phone: it is the one credential that can answer an authority in korbat's name. The operator keeps a list of who holds it and on which devices, and changes the password the day a person leaves.
- Never answer an authority from another address, and never from korbat.
- korbat never deletes the record of a message that nobody handled. After an outage, a restore or a change at the mail host, Contact point may therefore list old messages as stale: look at them in the mail host's app, then close them together on that screen, so that the home screen's count is true again.
- The address is published (the terms, the app's Contact section) only once `API_CONTACT_POINT` and `API_HOME_STATE` are set and the web build has its two arguments, which is the last step of Before going public.

**On call.**

- Who is on call is whoever's phone is subscribed to the topic `korbat-oncall`; every staff phone is subscribed to `korbat-all`. Handing over means: the next person subscribes, publishes a test, hears it ring, and only then the last person unsubscribes. Both note the time.
- The phone is an Android phone with ntfy from F-Droid. Before a phone goes on call, and after every system update, its owner runs the checks P1 to P8 and notes each result: the build and its version; a message through the public hostname on mobile data; battery optimisation off for ntfy; exact alarms allowed; the highest-priority channel overriding every Do Not Disturb mode the person uses at night, ringing until dismissed; a ring after two hours unplugged and still; a ring after ntfy, Caddy and the tunnel were each restarted; "Connection lost" within ten minutes of ntfy stopping. (The research's ninth check is an iPhone's and does not apply: korbat's phones are Android.)
- **The second leg, check M.** korbat's own alarm has one blind spot: when the watcher or the database itself stops at night, nothing reads the mailbox, so nothing rings; korbat only mails "Phone alerts are down", and a mail wakes nobody. For that night the on-call phone has the mail host's own app, signed in to the contact-point mailbox, with its notification channel set to ring through every Do Not Disturb mode the person uses at night. It is checked like P1 to P8, before a phone goes on call and after every system update, and noted: the operator stops `notify`, sends a test mail to the contact point, and the phone rings from the mail app alone. Its push runs through Google, which is why it is the second leg and not the first.
- The on-call phone carries a staff key the person has used on that phone, over NFC or USB, for a sign-in and two confirmations in a row. A computer with the second key is the fallback. No weaker way in exists for the night.
- Each morning the person on call looks for the daily test. A day without it is a fault: check the console's home screen and tell the operator.
- Once a quarter, at night and unannounced to the person on call, the operator sends a mail to the contact point and times the push, the sign-in and an order opened and closed without effect on a test post. The times go into the operator's notes.
- A phone's ntfy user is a line of `infra/ntfy/server.yml`, which ntfy reads as a mounted file; the operator makes its password hash and token with `docker compose --profile notify run --rm ntfy user hash` and `ntfy token generate`. The publisher's token stands in two places, that file and `NTFY_PUBLISH_TOKEN` for `notify`. A lost or replaced phone: the operator removes its line and restarts ntfy; the other phones reconnect by themselves, and an alert that arrives during the restart is published once ntfy is back, for as long as its hour runs.
- Upgrading ntfy: once a month, and at once on a security release, move the pin in `infra/compose.yml` to the newest release that is at least 7 days old, restart ntfy, publish a test and hear it ring. Its maintainer supports only the newest release.
- What each alert means and what to do is its own second line (W.4). An alert never says who or what: open the console. What ends a message's alarms is handling it on Contact point, not the sign-in alone.

**A removal order, step by step** (the hour runs from the mail host's delivery time, not from when you woke).

1. Read the mail in the mail host's app. Note the authority, the reference, the addresses named, whether Section C is ticked, whether it says emergency.
2. Sign in to the console with your key. Contact point: "Open an order from this message". If Contact point does not list the message (the watcher was down, or the mail host filed it in another folder), open it all the same: Orders, "Open an order", "The message is not listed". The deadline then shows as unknown: act at once, and tell the operator, who records the time of receipt afterwards. Never pick another message in its place. Fill the form exactly as the order reads; if you cannot tell whether Section C is ticked, choose "I cannot tell", which holds the notice.
3. Read the record against the order once. A mistake is corrected on the order's screen, under "Correct what the order shows", before you disable anything; a missed Section C can be set by anyone at any time, also afterwards. Never open the order a second time under a changed reference.
4. Check the authority against your offline copy of the Commission's register. If you cannot finish the check and the hour is running, go on: the measure is reversible and keeps everything.
5. If the order has a manifest error or lacks what you need to find the content, send Annex III from the mailbox to the address in the order's Section F, with a copy to the address the console shows, and record "Annex III sent". The clock stops. When the clarification arrives, record it: a fresh hour starts at its delivery time. A link that names an account, or an image, is not unclear: an image's address is taken as its post; for an account, add the address of each post the order means, call the operator for the rest, and then close that link's item without effect ("The link names nothing korbat can disable for an order"). Annex III is no way to stop an alarm.
6. "Disable for an order", one key touch for up to ten posts, and again for the rest: the order is given effect in full only when every post it names is disabled and every other link is closed with its reason, and its reminders run until then (before the first disabling anyone closes a link; afterwards an admin). Check each address from outside, in a private window: it must say that the post does not exist.
7. Send Annex II from the mailbox with the values the console shows, a line for each address; record "Annex II sent". If a member of korbat's staff wrote a post the order names, say so to the authority in the form: that person reads the contact-point mailbox like everyone on call.
8. By the next working day, on the server: `order-verify`, `order-issued`, `order-texts` (the reasons, then the remedies: each author who was already told gets an email that the notice has grown), `order-document`. For an order from another Member State decide with counsel, inside 48 hours of receipt, whether to ask for scrutiny, and record it with `order-scrutiny`. A link added to the order later makes it unverified again until `order-verify` is run once more.
9. Never tell the author anything yourself: not of the order, and, while its notice is held, not of any decision of korbat's own about the same post. korbat tells them, at once or when the non-disclosure period ends.

**Other messages and orders.**

- **An order to act against content under another law (DSA Article 9).** No hour runs for it, so nothing is done in doubt, and the console gives no effect to it before it is verified. In this order: open it; counsel reads it; whoever has the server verifies it and records `order-verify` (no register lists who may issue such an order: ask korbat's Digital Services Coordinator whether it holds a copy, which it does of a genuine one (Article 9(4)), or call the authority on a number you looked up yourself); then records the authority's reasons, its remedies and the order's territorial scope with `order-texts`, three times, because the author is owed all three with the notice (Article 9(5)); and, when the order says the author must not be told, the time until which, with `order-hold-until` (`none` when it names no time: the notice is then released at no time until one is recorded, and every staff account is reminded daily). Then "Disable for an order". Choose "must not be told" as the order says; the post is then kept from its author too. Tick "withheld from the author" otherwise only if the order's text says so. Afterwards tell the authority from the mailbox what was done and when, and record "Authority informed". Only when the order itself sets a time that cannot wait for all this does an admin give effect first, marking it urgent; the check and the texts then follow the same day, and each author gets an email when the texts arrive.
- **An order to provide information (DSA Article 10).** Open it; counsel reads it. Whoever has the server records with `order-tell-rule` whether and when the person may be told, and on which provision or sentence of the order that rests; the home state's default is in `STATE_FACTS`, and the cautious choice for an order nobody has classified is "never" until counsel has read it. Then `production-account`, with `--asked` for what the order asked for: it prints the handle, the address and the creation day and records the production; the operator sends that from the mailbox and records "Authority informed". When the rule is "at effect", or its time has come, the operator tells the person from the contact address, to the account's address and to no other, with the authority's reasons and the ways to challenge the order, and records it with `order-told`. korbat produces nothing else without counsel: it also holds the times and device types of an account's sign-ins, and whether an order reaches those is counsel's to say, order by order.
- **An order that names an account, not a post.** korbat cannot disable an account for an order from the console (section 2, row 43). Add the address of each post the order means and disable those, and close the account's link without effect ("The link names nothing korbat can disable for an order"). For the display name or the bio, and when the order means the whole account, call the operator at once: the hour for such an order depends on the operator, who decides with counsel what korbat does (a suspension is korbat's own measure and tells its subject, so it is no way to carry out an order that forbids telling). Do not answer such an order with Annex III: it is not unclear.
- A European Production or Preservation Order (e-Evidence): korbat has designated no establishment, since it serves its home state only. Do not act on it; send it to counsel the same day.
- A referral, a first-order notice, a request to preserve longer, a scrutiny decision: close the message with its word; the screen says what follows.
- A withdrawal or an annulment: check it as you check an order; an admin reinstates each item on its case and, once no measure of the order stands, closes the order ("Close without effect", with the ground), which also ends the reminders for an item that was not disabled yet. When the authority withdraws one item that korbat has not disabled and other items of the order stand, that item alone is closed on the order's screen ("Close this item without effect", "The authority withdrew this item"): by whoever is on call before the order's first effect, by an admin with the key after it. A reinstatement while the order's notice is still held tells nobody and leaves the hold as it is: whether the author may now be told is the authority's to say, and the operator ends the hold with `order-hold-end` (`annulled` or `released_by_authority`) when it says so. A forged order found later: `order-hold-end` with `not_authentic` first, then the reinstatement with the same ground, so that the author is told at once. An order that a court restores after it was lifted: an admin reopens it if it was closed, and the post is disabled again; its six months count from the new disabling.
- **An extension of a non-disclosure period, or a later time.** The database releases a held notice by itself at its end, and nobody is asked. So: every staff account is alerted daily in the last {holdWarningDays} days before any release, and whoever has the server then looks through the contact-point mailbox for an extension. When an extension of a removal order's period arrives, an admin records it on the order at once ("Extension received"), also at night and before the operator has read it; the operator checks it afterwards and confirms it with `order-hold-extend`, or, if it was not the authority's, ends the hold once the first period is over. A later time that the authority gives for a DSA order is recorded with `order-hold-until`, which only the operator can do: the person who reads such a mail calls the operator the same day.
- **A record entered wrongly.** Correct it on the order's screen; never close a true order or open it twice. Before any post is disabled anyone corrects anything. Afterwards anyone can still set "the author must not be told", and an admin corrects the rest, the closing of a link among it. Setting it after a notice went out withdraws that notice until the hold ends; what the author already read cannot be taken back, so tell the operator at once. Every correction alerts every staff account. If korbat's record said that the author must not be told and the order did not, an admin unsets it (or the operator ends the hold with `order-hold-end --reason not_ordered`): the author is told at once, and the notice says that the delay was korbat's mistake.

**When nobody can act within the hour.** Act the moment someone can. Never record a time other than the true one, and send Annex II with the true times. "Nobody woke up" is not force majeure: Annex III is for its three grounds only. If korbat's watcher is down or cannot read the mailbox, the console still works: the order is opened with "The message is not listed" and given effect, and the hour does not wait for anyone with the server; its time of receipt is recorded afterwards. If the console itself cannot be reached, the operator takes the app's site off the network (`APP_SITE=closed`) until the order is given effect, and writes down when. Tell counsel the same day; the quarterly drill exists so that this does not happen twice.

**A report to the authorities, and a hand-over.**

- When images of an account are sealed, the home screen says that a report is owed. The admin answers the case. After a seal for good, whoever has the server runs `records`, then `notification-facts`, reports through {notificationIntake}, and records it with `notification-sent`; later `notification-answer` with the authority's reference and answer. A check that ends in "no" is recorded with `notification-declined` and its reason. A seal that a correction or a review lifts closes its record by itself, and the address is no longer held.
- Whether the sealed files go with the report: "only on the authority's demand" until counsel has answered the first question of Before going public; then this line reads what counsel said.
- A hand-over goes to an authority, never to a hotline, a lawyer or a company, by the channel the authority names in writing: `handover-list`, then the `handover` service with the directory given on its command line (`docker compose --profile handover run --rm -T -v <directory>:/handover handover < list > digests`), `handover-record`, and after the authority's receipt delete every copy and run `handover-destroyed`. The directory is on an encrypted volume and owned by uid 1000, the user the service runs as; no setting names it, so a stack on which no hand-over was ever made needs none. Nobody opens the archive. With a seizure, record what was taken.
- A person who deletes their account while a report is owed is told in the deletion's mail that the address is kept for at most {reportHoldDays} days, in words that say no more than the seal's own statement told them. `API_REPORT_HOLD=off` ends the practice for everyone; set it only on counsel's advice.

**A person asks about a missing post.** Whoever answers the contact address gives every such question the same answer, whatever the reason the post is gone: "korbat can tell you about your posts only what the app shows you: your posts are on your profile, and every decision about them that korbat can tell you of is under Settings, Cases. If something is not there, korbat has nothing it can add by email." Never look the post up first: an answer that differs by what you found would tell. This stays the answer to every mail that does not meet the next entry's test.

**An author writes about an order's notice.** The notice sends its author to the contact address for three things: a copy of the order, korbat's own mistake ("the order does not name this post"), and the preserved text for their challenge. A person reads that mailbox every working day and answers within two working days.

- Look the order up only when the mail names a notice the app has shown: it quotes the reference, or the day, that the notice prints. A held notice shows neither, so nobody can pass this test for one, and the fixed answer above stays for everything else.
- Check that the writer is the author before anything is sent: `staff.js address-check --handle <handle> < address`, with the mail's sender on standard input. It answers yes or no, prints no address and writes one audit row. On "no", answer with the fixed sentence above and ask the person to write from the address of their korbat account.
- A copy of the order, or the preserved text of `order-access --purpose complaint`, goes to that address and to no other. The copy is the document in the authorities' mailbox; korbat holds no other.
- "The order does not name this post": an admin compares the item's link with the order in the authorities' mailbox and, within two working days of the mail, reinstates with `wrong_item` or answers why not.

**The daily copy.** On the daily visit the keeper notes the head as before and then presses "Download since my last copy", confirms it with the staff key, waits for the line "The copy is recorded", and keeps the file with the head, off the server, on an encrypted device. A download that broke off records nothing: press again. The file holds no post and no statement, but it names authorities and references, held orders' among them, and a reference may itself name a person (a court's file can carry a party's name): it is kept like the head, not like a log. Every staff account is told when someone who did not take the last copy takes one.

**Restoring** (added to the README's steps, after the staff steps).

1. Before the API starts: `API_MODERATION_RETENTION=off`, `MEDIA_RECONCILE=off`, `APP_SITE=closed`.
2. Feed the import every keeper's file from the one that holds the restored head's place onward, oldest first, concatenated on standard input: `cat <files> | docker compose -f infra/compose.yml run --rm -T migrate node dist/staff.js restore-import <kept places>` (`-T`: without it Compose attaches a terminal and the redirect fails). The files need not start at the head: the import checks the earlier lines and skips them. Read what it prints, a hole in the files among it.
3. Compare the contact-point mailbox since the keeper's last download with the orders it lists; open what is missing with `order-open --received`. An imported item whose link named no post shows `sha256:` and a digest in place of the link, since the copy holds no link's text: read the address from the order in the mailbox. Enter the records of class "record" again from the authority's letters, each with its command and `--gap`. Until the last of them is entered, answer no data request: `access-request` cannot print an authority's "not to the person" mark for a record that is still lost.
4. Before any order's disabling is taken again: compare each held notice the import lists with the mailbox since the keeper's last download. An extension or a later time that was recorded after that download is lost with the restore; record it again first (`order-hold-extend`, `order-hold-until`), or the disabling taken again would tell the author at the old end.
5. Start the API; everyone enrols new keys.
6. On the console's After a restore screen, take every measure again, from the top. An order's disabling first: its hour, its preservation and its hold are the old ones.
7. When the screen says so, set `APP_SITE=open`; when it says so, switch retention and the sweep back on.

**A key in someone else's hands.** Revoke it (`staff.js revoke`). On the Audit log, "What one key confirmed" lists what it did in the period. An admin corrects each decision on its case, under the caps; there is no batch. An order's disabling confirmed with a stolen key is checked against the mailbox: a true order stays in force.

**Upgrading.**

- On a database that exists, run two statements once, as the superuser and against korbat's own database, before the first 3f migration: `docker compose exec postgres psql -U "$POSTGRES_USER" -d "$POSTGRES_DB" -c "create role korbat_notify login password '<KORBAT_NOTIFY_DB_PASSWORD>'" -c "grant usage on schema public to korbat_notify"`. The second cannot be made by the migration: the migrator does not own the schema, and its grant would be a warning and nothing more. The migration checks both and stops with "create the role korbat_notify first" when either is missing, so a half-done upgrade stops there and not at a watcher that cannot read a row.
- `notify` and ntfy run under the Compose profile `notify`. Switch it on in one place, `COMPOSE_PROFILES=notify` in `infra/.env`, which Compose reads for every command: the README's commands stay as they are, and an upgrade then recreates the two services with the rest. Without that line they are never started, or keep running an old image against a migrated database.
- Until the contact-point mailbox exists, leave the profile off and set `API_NOTIFY=off`: korbat then says on the console's home screen that phone alerts are switched off, and mails no "Phone alerts are down". No setting of `notify`, ntfy or the hand-over is needed for the stack to start.

**The yearly counts.** Each January: `staff.js tco-counts <last year>`. When any figure is not zero, publish before 1 March, on the page the terms link (until 3d's transparency page exists, the page that Before going public has the owner create, W.10), a report with those figures under these headings: the measures korbat takes against terrorist content (notices and orders, no automated tool); removal orders received, by issuing state; items disabled, and how fast; orders not carried out in time, with the ground; scrutiny and reinstatements; complaints and court proceedings, from the operator's own notes. Then the state's own filing, where `STATE_FACTS` names one.

### W.10 For the owner before going public

The README's "Before going public" gains this list. korbat is not opened, and `API_CONTACT_POINT` stays empty, until every line is done; the first line of that section now reads "until sub-project 3f's order path and sub-project 3d's handling of sealed images have both shipped and the list below is done". The list holds everything this design assumes the owner has done: a line that is missing here is a thing nobody does.

1. **The Member State.** Set `API_HOME_STATE` and the web build's `WEB_HOME_STATE`; fill the state's entry of `STATE_FACTS` (the authorities and their register entries, the address for the copies of Annex II and III, the intake for notifications, the contact point's languages, the yearly filing, and the state's rule for telling a person of an order to provide information). Germany's entry is the worked example; any member the console prints as "not filled in" is yours.
2. **The mailbox.** A mailbox at an EU mail host that offers IMAP over TLS with an application password that can only read mail, tells you the junk folder's name, stores mail in the EU and forces no other sign-in for IMAP. Two more requirements, each checked with the host and with a test mail: no rule, filter or quarantine of the host moves or deletes mail before it reaches the inbox or the junk folder (a folder korbat does not watch is an order nobody sees), and the date the host stamps on a message is the time of its final delivery. Switch off remote content. Put the four `NOTIFY_IMAP_*` values, `KORBAT_NOTIFY_DB_PASSWORD` and `NTFY_PUBLISH_TOKEN` in `infra/.env`, switch the services on with `COMPOSE_PROFILES=notify`, and set `API_NOTIFY=on`.
3. **The people's own access to the mailbox.** Every person on call reads orders in the mail host's own app and sends Annex II and III from the mailbox, so each needs the mailbox's own sign-in, which is not `notify`'s read-only password: decide who holds it, with which second factor, on which devices; write the list down; and change the password the day a person leaves. It is the one credential that can answer an authority in korbat's name.
4. **Registering.** Register the contact point with the competent authority and ask the eight questions of OL E9.4: the channel and sender, whether other states use the same address, which time counts as receipt, how Annex II and III go back, whether a small host needs a portal account, how removal is checked and whether a 404 is read correctly, a test transmission, and whether referrals come to the same address. Register with the national intake for notifications and ask how files are delivered.
5. **The push hostname and ntfy's users.** The tunnel itself, which is still the Foundation's open task. A third public hostname on it for the push site, set as `API_PUSH_ORIGIN`. `infra/ntfy/server.yml`, made from its example: the publisher's token (the same value as `NTFY_PUBLISH_TOKEN`) and one user a phone, each with a password hash and a token made with ntfy's own commands; no example value left in it.
6. **The phones.** One Android phone a person on call, ntfy from F-Droid, the checks P1 to P8 each noted, and check M: the mail host's app signed in to the contact-point mailbox, ringing through Do Not Disturb, tried with a test mail while `notify` is stopped. The hand-over practised; the daily test looked at for a week; one night drill.
7. **The keys.** A security key with a PIN that works on each on-call phone, over NFC and over USB: sign in and confirm twice at night speed, and note the phone models that work.
8. **The papers, and counsel.** An offline copy of the Commission's register of competent authorities, refreshed each month; the texts of Annex II and III; this runbook, printed. Counsel who has agreed to answer inside two days, and the same day for a removal order from another Member State (the 48 hours for scrutiny), who reads every DSA Article 9 and Article 10 order before korbat acts on it; counsel's number, and a second one for the nights the first does not answer.
9. **The contact address.** The notice, the terms and the deletion mail send people to the address under Contact for a copy of an order, for korbat's own mistake and about a held address. Decide who reads that mailbox every working day and answers within two working days, from the runbook's two entries ("A person asks about a missing post", "An author writes about an order's notice").
10. **The page for the yearly report.** Until 3d's transparency page exists: a page of korbat's own site on which the yearly report stands, linked from the terms' "Terrorist content"; create it before the first January.
11. **The keeper, and the two places files rest.** The daily copy kept off the server with the head, on an encrypted device, since it names every order, held ones included; one restore drill with it, from `restore-import` to the app's site open again. And the directory a hand-over's archive is written into: on an encrypted volume of the server, owned by uid 1000, emptied after every hand-over.
12. **Decide**, each with its cost in section 2: korbat serves its home state only (row 6); a page that is not public answers 404 (17); Android only, no voice call (19, 20); every message at the public contact address rings the on-call phone, so anyone who knows the address can cost a night, bounded only by the quiet period of {quietMinutes} minutes, which is one constant and can be 0 (20); a watcher or a database that stops at night wakes nobody, and the hour then depends on the mail host's app (22); a console tick holds the notice with no shorter cap (25); a DSA order's silence lasts as long as its authority states, with no end while it names none (24, 46); the author's view during a hold, and that korbat's own decisions and appeal answers about the post wait with it (26, 47); account data is not held for an order (30); an order against an account as such is not built, and its hour depends on the operator (43); the hand-over's way and the address hold (34, 35); when the yearly report is published (40); the periods, and that a message nobody handled is never deleted (42); the known limits (45).
13. **Counsel**, with the questions as the research words them: OL E9.4 (receipt under national law; an order in a language not declared), E12.4 questions 1 to 7, E15.4, E20.4 and E21.4; AU E16.4 questions 1 to 7, E17.4 and E22.4 questions 1 to 6. The first to answer: whether sealed files go with a notification. And six that this design's review added: a DSA Article 9 order whose silence has no end ("until further notice"); whether a statement of korbat's own about the same post, sent during a hold, would itself be "information on the removal"; whether an earlier decision's copy of a post's text may still be shown to its author while Article 6 preserves the post; when and how a person is told of an order to provide information (DSA Art. 10(5)) and what the home state's law says instead; whether sign-in times are produced for such an order; and the words of the deletion mail while nobody has judged the report, with whether GDPR Art. 21 gives a right to object there.

## 19. Amendments from the dry run (2026-10-17)

The dry run built this design task by task in a scratch clone (2026-10-13 to 2026-10-17). Where the code had to differ from the text above, or the text left something open that the code settled, the rule the code follows is recorded here, in the order of the tasks that settled it (Tasks 1 to 9); where a bullet and an earlier section disagree, the bullet is the later and verified word. Migration numbers: 3e ended at `0025_standing_review.sql`, so each of this design's six migrations is two higher than section 6 writes: `0026_messages_alerts.sql`, `0027_orders.sql`, `0028_orders_ledger.sql`, `0029_orders_retention.sql`, `0030_authority_records.sql` and `0031_recovery.sql`, and the review's fixes add `0032_orders_review.sql`; `--upgrade-from` names 25. Labs L2, L3 and L5 ran in Task 1, L1 and L4 beside Tasks 1 to 4, and their numbers are in the bullets that cite them.

- **The migration's number (6, 13).** The migration is 0026_messages_alerts, not 0024: 3e ended at 0025_standing_review, and section 13 numbers 3f's migrations from the first free number.
- **A fact for an unknown deadline (10.1, W.4).** ALERT_KINDS gives order_opened a third fact, unknown (1 for an order opened without a time of receipt, else 0), beside items and dueAt: both 'No deadline runs' and 'Its deadline is unknown: act now' are sentences without dueAt, and the push cannot tell them apart from the two facts alone. AlertPart.when takes a list of conditions for that.
- **staffAlert's throttle for the kinds of 3c and 3e (7.2).** 7.2 gives the hit back only when neither leg took the alert. For the six own kinds the hit is given back whenever no address took the mail, even when the row was written: a written row is no proof that a phone got the push, the mail is the leg that carries the full message, and no row can mail it later; 3c's tests pin that an alert nobody received is sent again at the next occasion. For a kind of 3f a written row counts as taken, since the tick mails it. The answer is sent when a row with a push leg was written or an address took the mail.
- **The mail leg takes its row (7.2).** staffAlert's mail of a sentence kind and the tick's step 2 each take the row FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED in a transaction that sends the mail and sets mailed_at, so the two never mail one row twice; a row another transaction holds counts as taken for staffAlert's answer.
- **moderation_waiting's one number (7.2, W.4).** Its push carries one count: the sum of what its mail lists, the cases past their lane's time, the cases holding a measure taken while deciding, and the replies waiting.
