- **The migrator's insert into contact_messages (6.1).** contact_messages_insert forces seen_at, stale, the lesser received_at and a null disposition for every role but the migrator, as 6.1 says; for the migrator (a restore's import, a test fixture) it keeps what was supplied and fills seen_at and stale only when left out, so the integration tests can age a message without waiting.
- **The guard's word for a half-set disposition (6.1).** contact_messages_guard raises invalid_request when a disposition is set without handled_by, or handled_by without a disposition; 6.1 names no word for these, and the route never sends either.
- **alert_outbox_facts in strict mode (6.1).** The CHECK reads the members with the jsonpath 'strict $.*': in the default lax mode an array member is unwrapped and a fact such as a list of numbers would pass as numbers.
- **Helpers of the migration (6.1, 6.7).** alert_outbox_step_key is written by hand after the generated part (drizzle cannot write NULLS NOT DISTINCT with a predicate); a small IMMUTABLE alert_time(timestamptz) prints the facts' UTC form (2026-10-13T07:55:29Z) for the trigger and alert_steps(); minutesLeft is rounded up and never below 0, computed without an interval literal; handled_by references profiles with ON DELETE restrict.
- **alert_steps() pins public alone (6.1).** 6.1 gives it search_path = public, auth, pg_temp; it has public, pg_temp, and names auth.staff_sessions in full, because 3c's moderation schema test pins every function the migrator owns to one schema and pg_temp. That test and its list of what the API role may call (now with alert_steps and order_rules) are changed, and moderation-retention's, whose startJobs now also starts the alert tick, which asks for its lock every 30 seconds in both branches.
- **push_ok_at (7.1 step 6).** It is set when every publish of the round succeeded or nothing was owed, not only the last one: a round in which an earlier row failed and a later one passed still has a row owed.
- **A development-only push address (5, 7.1).** notify publishes to http://ntfy:2586, which a notify on the host cannot reach, and the push site passes only subscriptions. A third development-only setting, NOTIFY_PUSH_URL, refused in production like NOTIFY_IMAP_PORT and NOTIFY_IMAP_CA, points notify:dev at ntfy's own port, which compose.dev.yml publishes on 127.0.0.1 (DEV_NTFY_PORT, 2586) through a non-internal network of its own, since push is internal.
- **notify's start (5, 7.1).** With NODE_ENV unset notify judges as production; an empty password in DATABASE_URL_NOTIFY is refused as KORBAT_NOTIFY_DB_PASSWORD is not set, so a profile switched on before the password is set names the setting and exits 1.
- **ntfy's user and volume (5).** ntfy runs as 1000:1000 with its volume at /var/tmp (cache.db and auth.db), the one directory of the image that user may write to under a read-only root; listen-http is :2586.
- **How the two other sites refuse the push network (5).** On :8090 a first handle @push answers 403. On :8080 the refusal is a respond @push 403 first inside the API proxy's handle and the files' handle, because Caddy sorts a handle whose matcher names no path after the path handles; the two staff-API handles, which answer 404 and reach nothing, are unchanged. Both sites were checked with caddy adapt and from inside the ntfy container.
- **PUSH_SUBNET (5).** The fixed subnet is a setting with the default 172.16.91.0/24, passed to Caddy, which reads it for the refusal. The first default, 172.30.91.0/24, lay inside the pools Docker gives its own networks (172.17.0.0/16 to 172.31.0.0/16, and 192.168.0.0/16) and collided on this machine with a network of another project, so the stack did not start; a test now holds the default outside those pools, and infra/.env.example names the setting.
- **ntfy is told the phone's address behind the tunnel too (5).** Lab L3: behind the tunnel Caddy's client is cloudflared, so the X-Forwarded-For Caddy writes would name cloudflared for every phone, ntfy would count them as one visitor, and 30 wrong tokens from anyone would shut every phone out. The Caddy image now writes push-header.caddy: with TUNNEL=on the push site sets X-Forwarded-For to CF-Connecting-IP's value, with it off Caddy's own value stands, so server.yml's one setting (behind-proxy, X-Forwarded-For) holds in both.
- **The images (5).** The api service names its image ${COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME}-api, which notify runs; migrate keeps the build section 3e left it, the same Dockerfile with cached layers, so a first up that starts with migrate still has its image.
- **The push site's paths (5).** Caddy passes GET of /korbat-oncall, /korbat-all or both joined by a comma, followed by /ws, /json, /sse or /auth, and answers 404 without a Server header to every other path and method, /raw and OPTIONS included.
- **The contact page's cursor (7.3, 10.2).** ContactQuery's cursor is base64url of the last row's received_at in microseconds and its id, so a page of 50 never skips or repeats a message that shares a time.
- **What waits for Task 2 (10.2, 8.2).** Each ContactPage item's orders is [] and StaffOverview.contact.dueAt is the oldest unhandled message's received_at plus hourMinutes, a stale one included: Task 2 adds the orders and their deadlines.
- **The channel's since (7.2, 10.2).** channelHealth answers down since the older of the late beat and the oldest owed row, and the database's now when there has never been a beat; mailbox since mailbox_ok_at, or now when it was never set.
- **The route table's word rule (10.2).** apps/api/test/staff/route-table.test.ts forbade any answer member ending in contact; StaffOverview.contact, the contact point's counts and no person's contact, is the one exemption, and the test also holds that no ContactPage member names a sender, subject, address or body.
- **Home's block (8.2).** The heading Authorities is drawn only while one of its lines applies, so an ordinary night shows no empty block; [home.contact] carries its link to Contact point inside the sentence, laid out as a block of its own under 600 px.
- **Contact point does not read again by itself (8.1, 8.3).** It reads its page on opening, on Load older and after each close; it prints no deadline in this task, so it does not read again every 20 seconds.
- **The phone rules in the browser check (8.10).** The two new failure rules (a horizontal scroll bar, a control under 44 px) run on Task 1's four runs at 360 by 740 px; the 43 older runs keep 1280 by 900 and their own checks.
- **The Upgrading lines (W.9).** The README's command creates the role only when it is missing, with the password the postgres service already holds, then grants USAGE, so it runs unattended and twice without harm; it stops, creating nothing, when the container's password is empty.
- **Proving that a missing grant stops the migration (6.7).** alerts-grants runs the migration's first statement, read from the file, with the role's name replaced by one that does not exist and by korbat_auth (which lacks the grant on public), each in a rolled-back transaction, and expects 'create the role korbat_notify first'; a whole migration run without the grant is not repeated per test.
- **A certificate without a dependency (12).** Node has no certificate generator: fake-imap.ts holds a small DER writer that signs a self-signed P-256 certificate (CA true, names localhost and 127.0.0.1) with node:crypto, and scripts/dev-mailbox.mjs imports the fixture through Node's type stripping, so both callers share one IMAP server.
- **notify's memory (5).** Measured in the image: about 82 MB resident idle, a peak of 93 MB during the first read of 10,000 old messages (21 seconds), back to 81 MB after; under the 128 MB limit the container's peak was 85 MB with no kill, so the limit stays at 128 MB, with the numbers in a comment.
- **Files section 13 does not list.** apps/api: moderation/cases.ts (the overview), decide.ts and reveal.ts (the keyless kinds), context.ts (message_handled), moderation/orders.ts and orders-repo.ts (new), auth/index.ts, and tests architecture, config, moderation-context, mail/templates and mail/moderation-templates (the new sentences), integration notify-fixtures.ts (new), moderation-fixtures.ts, moderation-schema.test.ts and moderation-retention.test.ts, test/lab/README.md; contracts moderation.ts and the appeals, outcomes, staff-cases and moderation tests; the console's App.tsx and Layout.tsx (the nav entry, where nav.tsx's links are not), and tests caddyfile.ts, case-fixtures.ts, cases, fixtures.ts, screens and contact (new). moderation/dispatch.ts and store.ts are unchanged: the tick lives in alerts.ts and starts from index.ts.
- **The migration's number (6, 13).** Task 2's migration is 0027_orders, not section 6's number: 3e ended at 0025_standing_review and Task 1 is 0026_messages_alerts. The later migrations of 3f follow from there: 0028_orders_ledger, 0029_orders_retention, 0030_authority_records, 0031_recovery.
- **The search path of a definer function (6, 6.2).** Every function of 0027 pins `search_path = public, pg_temp`, not 6.2's `public, auth, pg_temp`, and names `auth.users` in full, as every migration from 0016 to 0026 does; each function granted to korbat_api joins moderation-schema's callable list.
- **The two order measures stay out of ACTION_KINDS (10.1, 10.5).** The contracts keep ACTION_KINDS at 22 and MEASURES unchanged; `ORDER_ACTION_KINDS` (`disable_by_order`, `reinstate_by_order`) is named once, in `orders.ts`, and the two enum tests (moderation-schema, standing-ledger) expect the database's action_kind to be ACTION_KINDS followed by ORDER_ACTION_KINDS. So PlainAction, ROUTE_KINDS, the composer and the terms meet neither kind before Task 3, which writes them and decides whether they join the shared list.
- **order_event_record and order_correct are written whole in 0027 (6.2, 6.3).** Task 3's orders_ledger replaces neither, so both functions are complete now, order_correct's step 5 included (untelling, the hold's end as not_ordered, the preservation and the withholding of an order given effect). Until 0028 no order is given effect and step 5 is reached only by a test that sets an item's disabled_at as the migrator in a rolled-back transaction.
- **A correction's forced columns in the same statement (6.2, order_correct step 4).** order_correct sets the corrected columns and the forced ones (the withholding for a removal order and for Section C, the preservation for a removal order) in one UPDATE, each forced column computed from the corrected values. legal_orders_tco is checked at each row's update, so a kind corrected to a removal order would otherwise fail the check before the forced columns follow.
- **The correction's key only when sent (7.4, 10.2).** POST /api/staff/orders/:order/correct runs the step-up of order_corrected only when the body carries `stepUp`, and otherwise validates the body as OrderCorrectRequest without one: requireStaffStepUp refuses a body without `stepUp`, and under the rules entry and caution no key is asked. When the database decides the rule is admin and no key came, the transaction is rolled back and the route answers staff_step_up_required (403), keeping nothing.
- **HOME_STATE is a state whose facts are filled in, and cross_border without it (2 row 41, 10.7).** The API refuses to start with a HOME_STATE that is not a key of the contracts' STATE_FACTS (only DE is filled in), since the console reads the register's entries and Annex II's copy address from those facts; it is still required once CONTACT_POINT is set, and an empty value counts as unset. While HOME_STATE is unset every order is recorded as cross_border (no issuing state can be shown to be korbat's own), so the scrutiny paragraph errs on the side of naming the right. staff.js order-open refuses until the migrate service has HOME_STATE and APP_ORIGIN.
- **Files section 13 does not list (13).** Task 2 also changes `apps/api/src/config.ts` and the two env examples (`HOME_STATE`, `CONTACT_POINT`; `DE` in development), `auth/context.ts` and `auth/staff/sessions.ts` (StaffMe's homeState and contactPoint), `auth/flows/notifiers.ts` (StaffAlertKind), `mail/mailer.ts`, `mail/templates.ts` and `moderation/alerts.ts` (the mails and tick legs of order_opened, order_corrected and order_unverified), `moderation/context.ts` and the contracts' `moderation.ts` (the five codes order_needs_message, order_exists with orderId, order_closed, order_event_refused and already_released; `disabled` in StoredPostState and the snapshot digest's input), `moderation/decide.ts` (RETRY_MARGIN_MS exported), `media/index.ts` and `media/repo.ts` (imagePost and the item's image count), the console's `Layout.tsx`, `App.tsx`, `store.ts`, `api.ts`, `app.css` and `test/orders.test.tsx`, and the API's integration fixtures `orders-fixtures.ts` (new) and `flow-harness.ts`.
- **Open an order from a message, in its block (8.3, 8.4).** On Contact point the button 'Open an order from this message' opens the form of 8.4 in that message's own block, the message picked, in place of the button; the Orders screen opens the same form, nothing picked, above its list. An order's page reads the contact point's messages once when it opens, for the choice of 'Clarification received'.
- **The development cleaner and orders (13).** scripts/clean-dev-fixtures.mjs keeps an account that opened an order or closed a message at the contact point, as one that decided is, and one that wrote an event of an order that stays (legal_order_events.by_staff refers to its profile with on delete restrict), applied in the same loop as the moderation rows. An order whose items name only posts of accounts that go is deleted first, with its alert rows, events and items, and so is the message it came in with unless an order that stays came in with it too; both are counted in the report.
- **The migration's number and its bases (6.3, 13).** Task 3's migration is 0028_orders_ledger: 3e ended at 0025_standing_review, Task 1 is 0026_messages_alerts and Task 2 0027_orders. It replaces each function from its newest built text (decisions_record, decisions_apply and decision_actions_apply from 0021; decision_actions_check and decision_holds from 0025; moderation_apply and posts_guard from 0023; frozen_for from 0015; appeals_check from 0021; alert_steps, legal_order_events_confirmed and order_correct from 0027). The lab's patches of 20-proto.sql no longer applied to six of these bodies, which had moved since the lab, and were rewritten against the built text; the lab's orders rerun on PostgreSQL 18.6 gave the recorded outcomes.
- **A NULL deadline opens no appeal (6.3, 9.2).** appeals_check's step 4 compares coalesce(appeal_deadline(informed_at), ...), so a decision held with an order's notice, whose informed_at is null, has no appeal window until the release. As built, a NULL deadline let the appeal through.
- **Every later writer of the telling holds it too (6.3, 7.10).** Besides decisions_record's step 10, decisions_apply (the ended seals) and decision_actions_check (its step 11) write author_mail; on a post whose order notice is held (order_notice_held) they now leave the decision untold, so no path tells the author during a hold.
- **order_correct withdraws only what was told (7.4, 7.10).** A missed must-not-be-told set after effect untells the order's decisions whose author_mail is not none and whose informed_at is set. A decision that told nobody, such as a 3c no-breach, keeps its row as it is; the built function would have marked it untold and released it later as if it were a notice.
- **The effect's and the reinstatement's events need no order_event_recorded row (6.2, 7.6, 7.9).** legal_order_events_confirmed returns early for effect_given and reinstated: both are written via console inside the effect's (order_effected) or the correction's (case_decided) transaction, whose audit rows decision_holds already ties to them. As built, it would have refused both at commit.
- **A mail gives up from the telling (7.10, W.5).** The dispatch round's give-up of a statement's mail counts from greatest(decided_at, informed_at, the newest texts_recorded), so a notice released after weeks, or owed again by order-texts, is not given up at once.
- **A reinstatement ends only a preservation that exists (6.3, 7.9).** decision_actions_apply sets preserve_ended_at only on an item that was preserved, a removal order's; a DSA order's item keeps it null. A disabling after a reinstatement counts its six months and a day anew.
- **The author's deletion of a disabled post (6.3, 9.4).** posts_guard refuses the tombstone of a disabled post unless its author's profile is already deleted, so purgeProfile now anonymises the profile and its handle history before it writes the tombstones. deletePost filters out disabled rows and its flow answers not_found, which the route walk found was a silent success before.
- **The case page's contracts take an order (8.6, 10.2).** SourceSummary gains the kind authority with SOURCE_LABELS.authority (An authority's order), CaseItem an optional orderId (from legal_order_items.intake_item_id), CaseHistoryEntry the intake kind order and every decision kind, and CaseTarget the level disabled. Without them the console's parse refused every case that held an order's item, decision or disabled post.
- **The row on Cases says which order measure (9.3, 10.2).** AuthorCaseRow.order is disabled, removed or lifted rather than a flag, read from the disable_by_order's withhold_author and from a reinstate_by_order, so the row can say a post disabled, a post removed, or an order lifted; such a row prints no rule and no law line.
- **New members are optional for compatibility (10.2, 10.4).** DecisionStatement.order, DecisionView.orderTexts and erasedOn, PostModeration.order, the export's order members and CaseDecisionAction's orderId, withholdAuthor and orderGround are optional or nullable, and DecisionView.content is nullable (null while an order's measure is in force). CaseView.preserved and noticeHeld and StaffOverview.ordersUnverified are required.
- **statementParagraphs takes the notice's extras (10.4, W.2).** It stays a function of the statement's data, with an optional second argument: the authority's words and the erasure day, read fresh. The notice prints the post's address as a path, /post/<id>, and the decision screen prints the authority's words after the paragraphs in their own labelled block.
- **A decision on a preserved post signs the stored digest (8.6).** Every copy of a post preserved for an order is withheld from staff, a fresh one too, so the composer no longer takes a fresh snapshot in a loop: it signs the newest copy's stored digest, which the database compares as before. This is what lets an admin reinstate, and anyone decide another report, on such a case.
- **[effect.gone] is order_changed (8.5).** The API cannot tell a post purged between the read and the key from facts that changed: both answer order_changed, whose words say that the order, its items or their posts are no longer what the page showed. [effect.cap] is order_cap's words.
- **The effect block's defaults (8.5 block 4).** The first itemsPerAction items that can be disabled are ticked; the button greys above itemsPerAction; [effect.held] for a removal order whose receipt is not recorded says six weeks after the receipt. The variant of [effect.preserve] after a restore waits for Task 6's restore. A disabled item links to its case, and shows its preservation, its reinstatement with its ground, and a measure that changed nothing.
- **The reinstatement's composer (8.6).** It shows the five grounds, the ground's fixed draft in a frame and never a field (saved as the correction's review_reason), [reinstate.what], [reinstate.told], [reinstate.fake] and [case.heldToo], and a preview of the notice's ground, reinstated.now and lifted paragraphs without the lead, whose facts only the API reads. [reinstate.erased] waits for Task 4's erasure: erasedOn is null until then.
- **Home's unverified orders (8.2).** [home.unverified] comes with each order as a link built from its checked id, reading Given effect at its effectAt.
- **The terms name the order's measure (W.6).** The measures paragraph lists every kind of the ledger, so it gains On an authority's order alone, never on korbat's own judgement, with Disabled for an order, and the list of lifts gains Reinstate after an order; the terms' own sections on orders stay with their task.
- **metadata.target on decisions (6.3).** The decide and review routes write the case's target into the audit row's metadata, which decision_holds compares for the correction of an order decision.
- **The hold commands (7.10, 8.11).** order-hold-until refuses dsa_art10 and an order without Section C, and takes an end in the past only with --release; order-texts sets the told order decisions pending again and prints the counts, and its audit metadata names the authors' count.
- **text-verify after a correction (8.11).** It names an order decision by its decisionIds, checks the level disabled and that each item and its measure name each other, counts console events without an audit row, and skips the comparison of the signed facts when an order_corrected row came after the effect.
- **A data request's held case (W.8).** The access draft replaces the part of a case whose notice is held by its id, noticeHeld and the held decisions, for the operator alone.
- **The account_deleted mail is unchanged (W.5).** Its built text is already one wording for every account, so it needed no change.
- **The effect's lock waits (7.6).** ORDER_EFFECT_WAITS(n) = 5 + 9n bounded lock waits for n items; the effect is tried again once, while its confirmation leaves time, after a deadlock, a bound reached, a serialization failure or case_closed, and every staff account hears of it (order_effected) after the commit.
- **audit-verify takes a keyless correction of an order's record (7.4, 8.11).** A correction under the rule entry or caution writes its order_corrected row without a step-up, as 7.4 designs it, and audit-verify refused every such row as a step-up action without one; it now accepts order_corrected without a step-up when the row's metadata names the rule entry or caution, which the database chose and the chain covers, and still refuses one under admin. Task 2's batch did not run the whole suite, so this first showed here, in moderation-races' and moderation-appeals-flow's audit-verify over the record that http-staff-orders committed.
- **The route kinds (7.1, 10.3).** ROUTE_KINDS takes reinstate_by_order and every kind of the ledger but disable_by_order, which only the effect of an order writes; standing-handles' pin says so.
- **Known limits (7.10).** An order decision on a post whose notice another order already holds is not held by that hold; and the statement text of a decision held with a notice still prints an appeal deadline counted from decided_at, while the database counts it from the release.
- **The migration's number (6.4, 13).** Task 4's migration is 0029_orders_retention, written by hand through generate:custom: 3e's dry run ended at 0025 and Tasks 1 to 3 took 0026_messages_alerts, 0027_orders and 0028_orders_ledger, so it is the next free number. It changes no table and replaces no function; it adds order_preservation_end, order_records_due, erase_order_records and delete_contact_messages, each SECURITY DEFINER with its search path pinned to public and pg_temp, EXECUTE taken from PUBLIC and granted to korbat_api alone, and every number read from order_rules().
- **The page status is built (row 17, 5, 7.12).** Task 4's first step ran the pinned image caddy:2.11.4-alpine (sha256:6aeddd44...) on ports of its own against a stand-in for the API: the app's shell with 200 after a 204, the same bytes with 404 after a 404, 200 after a 429 and with the API stopped, the security headers equal in all four, with and without gzip. So the status is built and row 17 stands. The /post/* handle proxies to the API with method GET and rewrite /api/posts/{http.request.uri.path.1}/status, and three handle_response routes serve the shell (file_server with status 404 for a 404, the plain shell for a 204 and for any other answer); handle_errors serves the same shell with 200 when the API cannot be asked. The page's security headers are one snippet, app_page_headers, which the app's files, the post's page and the error route import, and the post's page gets Cache-Control no-cache as the app's page does.
- **APP_SITE fails closed (5, 7.17, 10.7).** The app's site is the snippet APP_SITE names: :8080 holds only import app_site_{$APP_SITE:open}, with the snippets app_site_open (the site as before, with the status) and app_site_closed (503 with the fixed text 'korbat is closed for maintenance. Please come back later.', text/plain and no-store, to every path, the API's included). Any other value names no snippet, and Caddy refuses to start ('File to import not found', tried on the pinned image), so a mistyped value never leaves the site open during a restore. A matcher inside each handle, the form @push uses, was tried too; the snippet was chosen because a handle with a non-path matcher sorts after the path handles, and the refusals of /api/staff carry a JSON media type the closed answer must not take. Compose passes APP_SITE: ${APP_SITE:-open} as the caddy service's second environment entry, after Task 1's PUSH_SUBNET (the design says first), and infra/.env.example names APP_SITE=open. The console's Caddyfile reader puts an import's {$NAME:default} in, as Caddy does, and its tests read the site open, closed and mistyped.
- **What the end of a preservation locks (5, the lock order item 3; 6.4 step 1).** order_preservation_end takes, in one statement in id order, the case of the item's decision and every case that holds an unerased snapshot of the post, then the item, the post and its image rows, as redact_r4_body locks the cases of the snapshots it erases: step 5 erases the post's snapshots in every case, and a decision on one of those cases that had read its snapshot unerased must wait for the erasure or be waited for. It reads the item before the locks (an unknown item, or one that names no post, is not_found) and again after them, and answers not_due when the item's decision changed in between (a new disabling), when preserve_until is null or ahead, and when the preservation has ended.
- **How an erasure is told from an end without one (6.4 step 5, 8.5, 8.6, 10.2).** order_preservation_end sets posts.erased_at and the item's preserve_ended_at to the same instant, its transaction's start, and no other writer does; a reinstatement, a correction to another kind and an item whose measure no longer stands end the preservation without touching the post. Every reader tells the erasure by that equality: OrderItemView gains erasedAt (null unless the end erased the post; the order's page prints [item.erased] with its UTC day and no longer Preserved until), CaseView gains orderErasedOn (the composer prints [reinstate.erased] and its preview the notice's reinstated.erased paragraph), and orderNoticeOf reads erasedOn for DecisionView and the export's decision, whose content is null once the content was erased. 10.2 lists neither new member.
- **The notice once an order's records are deleted (6.4, 6.8, W.2).** erase_order_records leaves the ledger's decisions without an order's row, items or events to read, and statementParagraphs threw for a decision of the kind order with no order facts. ORDER_NOTICE gains recordsDeleted: done ('korbat restricted your post of {createdAt} ({address}) on {decidedAt}, because it received an authority's order to do so.') and gone ('korbat has since deleted its record of that order, as its Terms say it does {recordKeepYears} years after an order's last event once nothing of the order still applies, so this notice can say no more about it.'). An order decision without facts renders the two; a correction of one renders reinstated.lead, gone and lifted. The author's screen, the export and the case page read such decisions without an error.
- **What order_records_due reads (6.4).** An order is due when its newest event is older than record_keep_years, no measure of any decision naming one of its items is in force, no item of it has preserve_until set with no preserve_ended_at, and no decision in a case whose target is one of its items' posts has informed_at null. So korbat's own decisions held with a notice, and the corrections of the order's decisions, keep it as the order's own decisions do. An order nothing acted on is due three years after its opening.
- **What delete_contact_messages keeps (6.4, 7.18).** It raises not_due when older_than is null or later than now() less message_keep_days, deletes the messages with a disposition handled before older_than that no order names, and the outbox rows created before it except those of a message without a disposition and those of an order that is not closed and has an item neither disabled nor closed (an order of any kind; the clock view covers removal orders only). The retention job passes now() less message_keep_days computed in the same statement, so no clock of the API's is compared with the database's. A test shows that alert_steps() writes no new order_due for a deadline long past while those rows are kept, and writes one at once if they go.
- **The retention job's three steps (7.11, 7.18).** order_preservation, order_records and contact_messages run after access_log and before the image disposal, so the image rows an erasure marked go in the same round and their files are requested after its commit. order_preservation runs one transaction an item, from the items with preserve_until passed and no end; an item whose post is still frozen answers false, writes nothing and is named again next round; its audit row's metadata holds the step, itemId, erased (whether the end erased content: the item's measure still stands after the call) and the count, beside 3c's three null ids. order_records runs one transaction an order, and its row holds orderId and the number of rows deleted. contact_messages is one call whose row names no id. RetentionReport gains preservations, orderRecords and contactMessages. A not_due raised by a race (an extension committed between the read and the call) is counted as a failed row and logged, as for 3c's steps.
- **RETENTION's entries move to Task 7 (10.1, W.6, 13).** Section 13 has Task 4 add contactMessageDays, orderPreserveMonths, orderRecordYears and restoreGapMonths to RETENTION, but the app's terms table test requires every number of RETENTION to be printed by the terms, which is Task 7's printed list (W.6). Nothing of Task 4 reads them in TypeScript (the functions read order_rules() and the job lets the database compute the cut-off), so RETENTION is unchanged and Task 7 adds the four with their sentences. The notice's recordsDeleted reads ORDER_RULES.recordKeepYears.
- **order-preserve-extend (7.11, 8.11, 10.5).** It takes the item's row FOR UPDATE and refuses not_preserved ('item <id> is not preserved, or its preservation has ended') for an unknown item, one never preserved and one whose preservation ended; an item whose preserve_until has passed while the retention job has not yet ended it can still be extended. --until must be later than the current preserve_until; --received may not be in the future. The event preservation_extended names the item, with effective_at the time received; the audit row order_recorded on the order holds event, itemId, until, was, received, by and reference as given, and the proceedings only as proceedingsSha256.
- **order-access (7.11, 7.14, 8.11, 10.5).** It takes the item's row FOR SHARE and opens only an item whose preservation runs (post_preserved's test: preserve_until ahead and no end), refusing not_preserved otherwise. It prints 'order-access: recorded, purpose <purpose>.', then the text of the copy the item's decision names (else the post's newest unerased copy) between two marker lines that start with the command's name, then each alt text and each image's id, pixel size and creation time through preservedImages; never an image. The audit row preserved_accessed (target order_item) holds purpose, productionId null, forSha256, images, altTexts and textLength. --purpose authority is refused ('an access for an authority is recorded with its production, which this server cannot record yet') until Task 5 builds the production record, so no access leaves without its record.
- **preservedImages and its test (5, 13).** The media module's index exports preservedImages(q, postId), the image rows' id, width, height and creation time in position order, read through the media repository's new method of the same name. It reads the database, so its test is an integration test (cli-orders' order-access), not a unit test; media-gates' table of repository methods classifies it as neither a deleter nor a reader of an image, since it gives no file and no state.
- **The status route (7.12, 10.2).** social.statusRoutes is built by postStatusRoutes(deps) in social/routes.ts over the repository's findPosts, and app.ts mounts it at /api between the global limit and the session's resolution. A malformed id, parsed with the contracts' PostId, is 404 with no body. A test counts the session's resolution and finds it never reached, whatever cookie or bearer token the request carries, and the status agrees with a signed-out GET /api/posts/:id for every stored state. The 301st request of a client in a minute is the global limit's 429.
- **A reinstatement after the erasure (7.9, 8.6).** The erasure erases the case's copies of the post, so the admin's reinstatement first takes a fresh copy of the erased post, as the console does for any decision whose copy was erased (the decide route answers that the snapshot was erased). The post stays removed for good and the notice says that nothing can be shown again.
- **The smoke test (12.2, 13).** scripts/smoke-auth-stack.mjs gains step 17: a post's page answers the shell with 200 while public and the same bytes and security headers with 404 once its author deleted it, 404 for a post that does not exist and for a malformed id, and the status route 404 with no body; and step 18 with --closed, for a stack started with APP_SITE=closed: every path of the app's site, the API's included, answers 503 with the fixed sentence, the console's site still serves its page, and nothing else runs. The closed run ends by setting the process's exit code rather than calling process.exit: a few requests long, it otherwise ended while fetch's idle connections were closing, and Node 24 on Windows then aborted with a libuv assertion (exit 127) after printing its result.
- **The contention lab (12.1 invariant 23, 13).** contention.lab.ts gains the test '3f, Task 4': one order of eleven posts by one author, its first item aged past its six months, the end of that item's preservation against the ten-item effect of the other ten, 40 rounds with each side first in half of them, both rolled back: 71 ms alone, 882 ms for the effect alone, 40 ok on each side with no deadlock and no lock timeout, the longest side 4,823 ms, on the scratch stack at 19 percent busy. Task 3's part of the lab (the ten-item transaction against intakes on the same posts, a seal on one author and the purge of another) is still not built.
- **Migration number (sections 6 and 13).** The design numbers the records to authorities 0028. 3e's dry run ended at 0025, and Tasks 1 to 4 took 0026 to 0029, so the migration is `0030_authority_records`, the next free number. Its tables are generated from `packages/db/src/schema/safety.ts` and `auth.ts`; its functions, triggers and grants are written by hand in the same file. 0031 `recovery` follows.
- **Search path (6.5).** The design gives `report_open()` the search path `public, auth, pg_temp`. Every function of 0030, `report_open()`, `auth.address_held`, `auth.address_held_until` and `auth.address_hold_final` among them, pins `public, pg_temp` and names `auth.report_holds` and `auth.users` in full, as 0016 to 0029 do. 3c's pin on every function the migrator owns holds this.
- **seal_report (6.5, 3d ruling O1).** `seal_report(action)` returns `(reported, their_reference, answer, answered_at, sent_at)`: 3d's amendment adds the answer's word and the time the report was sent. It is executable by nobody until 3d grants it to its release. It always answers one row: `reported` false and nulls when the seal has no record. A seal's record is the one it opened. A seal that found its account's record already owed (report_open's first case) reads the record a seal opened for the account that was open when this seal was taken.
- **report_open's first case (6.5).** Only an owed record that a seal opened (`opened_via = 'system'`) counts as the account's record. A record the command line opened for a check (`notification-open`) holds no address, so it cannot stand for a seal. A seal on an account that has only such a record opens its own record and sets the hold. A partial unique index allows one owed record a seal opened per account. The hold's row is inserted released when there is none, so it can be locked, with `released_at` set and no `release_reason`: the CHECK allows a missing reason, never a reason without its time. Nothing writes `expired`. A hold older than `reportHoldDays` simply reads as not held.
- **What a later event records (6.5, 8.11).** `notification-event` takes `--how` (a free text about the act, at most 500 characters) and `--reference`. 6.5 gives the events table no column for `--how`, so `authority_notification_events` gains `how text not null`, which the CHECK bounds. Only the migrator reads it, and the audit row holds only the record's digest.
- **Who sent and who checked (6.5, 8.11).** `notification-sent` and `notification-declined` take `--by <name>`, which 8.11 does not list. It fills `sent_by` and `checked_by` ('who sent it', 'who checked'). notification-sent records the check as yes; notification-declined records it as no, with its reason as both the check's reason and the record's `declined_reason`. Both commands take the account's row of `auth.report_holds` first and the record after it. They release the hold only when no record a seal opened stays owed for the account, so declining a record the command line opened never releases a seal's hold.
- **The closed lists (10.1).** Besides `NOTIFICATION_DUTIES`, `NOTIFICATION_SENT_KINDS`, `NOTIFICATION_ANSWERS` and `PRODUCTION_INSTRUMENTS`, the contracts' `orders.ts` holds the other closed lists of 6.5, each the one spelling of its CHECK, with a database test that holds the two equal: `NOTIFICATION_STATES`, `NOTIFICATION_EVENT_KINDS`, `NOTIFICATION_OPENED_VIA`, `HANDOVER_INSTRUMENTS`, `ACCESS_PURPOSES`, `REPORT_HOLD_RELEASES`, `PRODUCED_KINDS` (the sent kinds and `content`: the text, alt texts and image facts a preserved access prints) and `NOT_HELD_KINDS` (name, postal address, date of birth, phone number, IP address: W.8's sentence by kind, which a production stores in `not_held`). Also there: `RECORDS_NOT_HELD` (W.8's sentence by version) and `ReportsOwed` with `REPORT_OWED_KINDS`. `StaffOverview` and `CaseView` live in the contracts' `moderation.ts`, not in `staff.ts`. The CHECK on `productions.purpose` compares `purpose::text`: `authority` is also a value 3f added to an enum, and the enum-literal rule reads the migrations' words.
- **The digest (10.5, 8.11).** The digest a later step holds is the SHA-256, in hex, of the canonical JSON (`recordDigestOf` in the contracts' `orders.ts`, a third caller of `canonical.ts`). For a notification it covers `{ record, events }`: the record's row and its events in time order. For a production it covers `{ production }`, its row. The database prints each row with `to_jsonb` in a transaction whose time zone is UTC. Each later step changes the row, so `records --verify` holds each record to its newest audit row alone: the digest where there is one, and otherwise the facts of an opened, sent or declined step (case, seal, duties and kinds). A record that a seal opened and that has no step of the command line yet has no audit row of its own (the decision's covers it), and is not checked.
- **access-request and the authority's mark (7.13 step 6, W.8, section 2 row 31).** The design has `access-request` print `access.authorityHold` but keeps the records from the API's role, and 3c runs `access-request` in the api service on that role. A handle's draft now runs in the migrate service as the migrator: it reads the migrator's URL and `APP_ORIGIN` and nothing else, the draft's other reads are unchanged, and only the migrator reads the records (`authorityHoldsFor` in `cli/records.ts`). Run in the api service, it refuses with one sentence and drafts nothing. An address's draft stays in the api service, since it needs the API's keys and has no lane-0 mark. The mark is the case's member `authorityHold`, with `authoritiesCheck` null on such a case. A case whose order notice is held carries the mark beside `noticeHeld`.
- **The deletion mail's wording (W.5, 7.19).** W.5 quotes 1b's sentence 'Your posts, images and profile are gone, and your handle stays reserved'. The built mail says instead 3c's `ACCOUNT_DELETION_WORDS`, which the deletion screen and the code mail share. W.5's change is applied to that one sentence, for every account: 'Your posts, images and profile are removed from korbat for everyone, except what korbat keeps for a time: where a post or your profile was reported or restricted, its copy for that case, and what the law obliges korbat to keep, each for the periods in its terms (Terms, Your data).' The hold's paragraph, in its two variants (`ACCOUNT_DELETED_HELD` in `mail/templates.ts`, with `{heldUntil}` as a UTC day), follows it and comes before 'This cannot be undone.'
- **A termination's mail while the address is held (7.15 step 8).** A terminated account's take-down is held as a deletion is, but its mail is 3e's `termination_final`, not `account_deleted`. It gains the same paragraph in the variant of the seal that holds the record, after its own words, so a terminated person learns what is kept and until when, with the routes GDPR Art. 12(4) asks for, as a deleting person does.
- **finishPurge and REPORT_HOLD (6.5, 7.15).** `finishPurge` runs its two statements in one transaction. The first clears the credentials and personal facts but the address. The second overwrites the address and sets `purged_at` only where `auth.address_held(id)` is false, and only while `REPORT_HOLD` is on (`AuthConfig.reportHold`). It answers `purged`, `held` or `none`. `pendingPurges` orders held rows last. The account module's purge passes the answer on, and `purgePending` counts `PurgeReport.held`; a round that met only held addresses is not logged. The deletion mail reads the hold through the auth repository's `addressHold`, which reads the hold's end and variant from the two definer functions. It reads them after the take-down and, for a terminated account's mail too, best effort: if the read fails, the mail goes in its one wording for every account.
- **The console's lines (8.2, 8.6).** The design gives the case page's `reportOwed` line no words. It reads 'A report to the authorities is owed for this case since {since}. Whoever has the server records it as sent or declined; nothing more of it is shown here.' (`ORDER_WORDS.caseOwed`). The console adds [home.owed]'s 24-hour sentence once `since` is more than `ORDER_RULES.reportAlertHours` old, by its own clock. The [home.owed] lines stand before [home.unverified], which Task 3 placed before the channel's lines.
- **order-access for an authority (8.11, 7.14).** `order-access` takes only `--purpose` and `--for`. With `--purpose authority` it also requires `--authority <name>` and `--reference <text>`, under an order's bounds, and opens a production of the instrument `preserved_access` (purpose `authority`, the order and its item, the post's author, the authority, their reference, produced `content`). It prints 'order-access: recorded, purpose authority, production <id>.' and names `productionId` in its one audit row, `preserved_accessed`. No `production_recorded` row is written for it.
- **The hand-over's list and record (7.14, 8.11).** `handover-list` stores the listed names in the production's `files` (size and digest null until `handover-record`), so the record can refuse digests whose names are not exactly that list, in its order. Its standard output's first line is `production <id>`, which the hand-over's program requires. The production names the seal's own record as `notification_id` when the seal opened one. The image rows are read through the media module's repository read `sealImageRows` (classified neither a deleter nor a reader of a file in media-gates' table). `sealFiles` composes the names of every stored size of every row with the exported `storedImageName`, and the command compares each post's row count with `seal_posts.images` itself. For a seizure, `handover-record --taken` records the delivery and the destruction ('taken by the authority') at once and takes no digests; `--taken` is refused for any other instrument. `handover_undestroyed` counts productions with files whose `delivered_at` (set by `handover-record`) is older than `handoverAlertDays` and that are not destroyed.
- **The hand-over's program and service (sections 5 and 7.14).** `apps/api/src/handover.ts` imports only Node's own modules, none that opens a socket or a process; the architecture test pins this and that nothing imports it. It reads every listed file (opened without following a link, a plain file only) before it writes anything, refuses a name the rule does not make and a name listed twice, and writes the archive as a new file readable by its owner alone, never over an earlier one. The archive has stored entries without ZIP64; a seal's files beyond its limits are refused. The Compose service also has `restart: 'no'`, `stdin_open`, a `/tmp` tmpfs and `no-new-privileges`.
- **production-account (7.14, 6.5).** The production's `not_to_person_until` comes from the order's tell rule: `never` gives `infinity`, `at_instant` gives its instant, and `at_effect` gives none. A production that the person must never be told of is therefore kept for as long as that runs. The command is refused for an order that is not `dsa_art10`, and for an account whose address the deletion has already overwritten: it prints nothing it does not hold.
- **The migration's number (6, 13).** The design's 0029 recovery is `0031_recovery`, the next free number after Task 5's `0030_authority_records`: 3e ended at 0025, and 3f's migrations are section 6's numbers plus 2.
- **Search path (6.6).** `gap_close`, `restore_gaps_confirmed` and `delete_restore_gaps` pin `search_path = public, pg_temp` and name `auth.users` in full, as every function of 0016 to 0030 does, where the design writes `public, auth, pg_temp` for `gap_close`.
- **A gap records when its import ran (6.6, 10.2).** `restore_gaps` gains `imported_at timestamptz not null`, which the import sets to its own `now()`. `RestoreView.importedAt` and `StaffOverview.restore.importedAt` read the newest one; without it the time of the newest import could be found only in the audit log, which has no index by action. An import that found nothing lost writes no gap, and the console then shows no After a restore, which has nothing to show.
- **A gap names its closing decision without a foreign key (6.6).** `restore_gaps.closed_by` is a plain uuid: the ledger keeps every decision for good, so the key would never be needed, and the test fixtures that remove decisions would be held by it. Its CHECK `restore_gaps_closed` holds that a gap is open or closed with a disposition, and names a decision exactly when the disposition is `reapplied`; `place > 0` and a hex hash are CHECKs too.
- **What `gap_close` refuses (6.6).** An unknown gap is `not_found` (404 on the close route). A decision fits only when this transaction inserted it (the `xact` and `decided_at` pair) and the acting account decided it. `restriction` takes a decision that holds a measure (a row that reverses nothing) other than `seal_media`, `suspend`, `terminate` and `disable_by_order`; `record` takes no decision, since a record is entered again by its command; the target is compared, for every class but `order_effect`, only when the gap's metadata names one. A refusal is `gap_mismatch` and leaves the gap open.
- **A copy mark's time is forced (6.6).** The trigger `copy_marks_insert` sets `at` to `clock_timestamp()` for every role, the migrator included, so the copy's age that `alert_steps()` and the home screen read is never a time someone supplied; the API role may insert `staff_id` and `place` alone, and `place > 0` is a CHECK.
- **`delete_restore_gaps` refuses an early cut-off as `not_due` (6.6, 7.18).** As `delete_contact_messages` does; the newest mark it keeps is the newest by `at` and then by id. The retention step `restore_gaps` computes the cut-off in the database's own SQL from `order_rules()`, and runs after `authority_records`, before `images`.
- **An imported order's events pass the commit's check (6.2, 7.17 step 2).** `legal_order_events_confirmed` is replaced from 0028's text with one exemption: an event of an order whose `imported_at` is set, dated before that import, is one the lost database had confirmed, whose audit row is in the keeper's file and not in this transaction. Every event written afterwards is dated by `clock_timestamp()` after the import committed, so it is checked as before. The import sets `imported_at` (if it is not set) on every order a lost row of it is written into, the orders the restored database holds included.
- **What the import writes again, and what it notes (7.17 step 2, 10.5).** An `order_opened` row through `insertOrder`, which gains an `imported` member (the order's own id, `opened_at`, `opened_via`, receipt and sending times, each item's id, position and time, and `imported_at` now), and an `imported` event; `order_items_added`; `order_corrected`, whose new values are written in one UPDATE with the withholding and the preservation the kind forces, and whose closed items are closed at the row's time; `order_event_recorded`, with what a closing, a reopening and the console's extension set; `order_recorded`, with the columns each command sets (receipt, sending time, deadline, verification time, the order's date, the document's digest, the tell rule, the authority's end, the extension, the end of a hold, an item's preservation), a text the command recorded staying lost; each with a gap closed as `imported`. `order_effected` writes each item's first disabling and preservation from the row's time (LAB F) and one open `order_effect` gap an item. `case_decided` and `appeals_reviewed` write one open gap of their class. A row that is neither (a sign-in, a key's or a session's row, a retention round, a copy, a closed message, a reveal) writes nothing.
- **A notification's later steps are record gaps (7.17 step 2).** The import writes again only the step `opened` of a notification record: owed, with its original opening, its case, its seal, its duties, `imported_at`, and its account as the restored case names it. The steps `sent` and `declined` cannot be written from their rows, which hold no authority, channel or reason, while the CHECKs of `authority_notifications` require them; so, like `answered` and `event`, they are open gaps of the class `record`, closed when the operator enters them again with `notification-sent`, `notification-declined` (or the other command) and `--gap`. A production, and an access for an authority (`preserved_accessed` naming a production), are open record gaps too.
- **The files' digest (7.17 step 2.6).** `restore_imported` holds one SHA-256 in `files`: the files reach the command concatenated on standard input, so where one ends and the next begins cannot be read, and the digest names the input exactly as given.
- **A hole, a changed file and a repeated line (7.17 step 2.1).** A line at the next place that names another previous hash, a line at a place not after the line before, a line whose hash does not recompute, a line at the head's place with another hash, and a kept place given as an argument that the input holds with another hash refuse the input whole. A place missing from the input above the head is the hole that ends the run. A line repeated exactly, as two downloads that overlap give it, is read once, and a place read twice with two hashes refuses the input. Kept places are checked against the input's lines, which is where the keeper's hashes are.
- **What restore-import prints and reads (8.11).** It reads `APP_ORIGIN` for an imported item's address and refuses only when an item that names a post must be written without it. With no gap it prints `restore-import: none.`; with no lost disabling or seal it says that the app's site may open once the operator has read the rest; the time of the input's last line stands for the keeper's last download.
- **The download's target and its refusals (7.16 steps 2 and 3).** The route reads `through` from the body the validator parsed and confirms it as the target `audit` with `through` as digits; a `through` after the head is refused 422 `invalid_request` once the key confirmed it, since the file would end short. The lines are streamed a page of 1,000 rows at a time and a line at a time to the reader.
- **Who is the last downloader (7.16 step 3).** `copy_taken` is raised when the newest copy mark of a staff account belongs to another staff account than the caller; the marks of `staff.js audit-export` (the system actor) are left out, so a download from the server does not make the keeper's next one an alert, and before the first staff mark nobody is alerted.
- **`audit-export` without `--after` (8.11).** It starts after the command line's own newest mark, as the console starts after the caller's, runs on the API's role as `audit-head` does, prints nothing newer as `audit-export: nothing is newer than the last copy.`, and writes its mark with `copy_marked` only after every line is printed.
- **A row's key on the audit page (10.2, 8.7).** `AuditEntry.keyId` is the key of a step-up, and null for a row that carries a key without a step-up (a sign-in, a key's own rows), which no key confirmed. `AuditPageQuery` takes `from` and `to` only with `key`, and refuses a period that ends before it begins. The key filter lists the rows with that key and a step-up.
- **What `RestoreView` holds (10.2, 8.8).** It gains `retentionMay` (no gap of the classes 1 to 5 is open, [restore.retention]) and `holds` (the orders with Section C whose hold has not ended and runs past now, about which an untold decision stands or an open gap names an item, each with `order_hold_until`, null for no end), which [restore.holds] lists; its gaps are every open gap and the newest import's closed ones, at most 1,000, in the classes' order and then by the lost rows' time.
- **The console learns of an import for its navigation (8.1).** The page reads `GET /api/staff/restore` once a session, without the activity mark, and shows After a restore once `importedAt` is set; `StaffOverview.restore` carries `importedAt` too, for the home screen.
- **`--gap` on the record commands (8.11).** Every record command that writes a record takes it (`notification-open`, `-facts`, `-sent`, `-declined`, `-answer`, `-event`, `production-account`, `handover-list`, `-record`, `-destroyed`); `records` and `address-check`, which enter no record, refuse it. The gap is taken FOR UPDATE and closed first, in the command's transaction, so a refusal of the command leaves it open; the command's last line says the gap is closed.
- **The effect's audit row names its gaps (7.6 step 7).** `order_effected` gains `gapIds`, each item's closed gap by the item's id, when the effect took a lost disabling again, as a decision's row names its `gapId`.
- **text-verify after a restore (8.11).** An item recorded as disabled with no measure is no difference while an `order_effect` gap names it: the import wrote its first disabling and nobody has taken it again, or an admin found it not needed. Every gap closed as not needed must have a confirmed `gap_closed` audit row on it.
- **[home.copy] before the first copy (8.2).** With no copy mark at all, the line says that no copy of the audit log's tail has been taken yet and that the keeper downloads it on the daily visit (`RECOVERY_WORDS.homeCopyNone`), since the design's sentence has no age to print; the copy is overdue then once the audit log's first row is older than `copyOverdueHours`, as `alert_steps()` judges it.
- **The console's words beyond 8.7 and 8.8.** `RECOVERY_WORDS` also holds the key filter's labels and its rule for a refused entry, the line of a closed gap ('Taken again', 'Not needed' or 'Entered again' at its time), 'Closed as not needed.', the newest import's time, 'Every gap is closed.', a gap whose case or order the restored database no longer holds, and the order disablings' hint; `{measure}` prints the lost decision's MEASURES labels, each quoted.
- **The mails of the two copy alerts (7.19, W.4).** `copy_overdue` and `copy_taken` join `StaffAlertKind`, the mailer's union (57 templates) and the tick's mailed kinds, each mailed as its sentence. Task 5's `report_owed` and `handover_undestroyed` are still in none of the three, so the tick pushes them and never mails them: a finding for the review.
- **The README's Restoring (W.9).** The seven steps are a block of their own after the staff steps, which says that where they and step 5 differ they hold; step 5's sentence on what was missing before 3f's copy is left for Task 7, which corrects the lines 3f makes false.
- **Where the tests stand (13, Task 6's integration files).** The decide route's `gap` is tested in `http-staff-restore`, the effect's closing in `recovery-import` (LAB F on the real code, with the disabling taken again), and the audit page's key and copy in `recovery-export`, `staff-audit` and `http-staff-sessions`; `http-staff-decisions` and `orders-effect` are unchanged and pass. `snapshotForTest` copies every table of `public` and `auth` into a schema of its own; `restoreForTest` fills every table in one statement (a data-modifying WITH) with the user triggers off, so that the foreign keys that run in a circle hold at the statement's end, and moves each sequence past the copied ids. The HTTP files share `recovery-harness.ts`, and each new file restores the database it found.
- **Task 7 adds no migration (6, 13).** The counts, the terms and the record need no table, function or grant: tco-counts reads as the migrator what Tasks 1 to 6 built, and the newest migration stays 0031_recovery.
- **Which time puts a row in a year (8.11, 7.18; section 2, row 40).** tco-counts counts, for the calendar year in UTC, a removal order in the year of its receipt, or of its opening when nobody recorded a receipt; an item in the year it was disabled; an order's event (Annex III, scrutiny asked and decided, a reinstatement) at coalesce(effective_at, at); a production at its opened_at; a decision at its decided_at; a closed message at the time of its audit row. Only removal orders (tco_removal) enter the order figures, since the counts are those of the TCO Regulation; the reinstatements are the events reinstated of their items, by ground.
- **Referrals are counted from the audit log (8.11).** delete_contact_messages deletes a handled message 30 days after it was handled, so in January the year's messages are mostly gone. "referrals closed" counts the audit rows contact_closed whose metadata names the disposition referral, which the audit log keeps for good.
- **When a figure is not recorded (8.11, 11.3; invariant 20).** The order figures (received, items disabled, the minutes, Annex III, scrutiny, reinstatements) print "not recorded" for a year in which the retention job erased the records of a removal order that could have held a row of it: a moderation_retention row of the step order_records names the order, whose order_opened audit row (its kind, receipt or time) falls before the year's end, and the erasure came at most record_keep_years after the year's start, since an order's records go three years after its last event. The authorities' accesses print "not recorded" when a preserved_accessed audit row of the year, for an authority, names a production that is gone. A finished order whose receipt nobody recorded is "not recorded" in the minutes list, and "outside the hour" then prints "not recorded (n orders without a recorded receipt)" instead of a number. The terrorist-content decisions and the referrals rest on rows that stay (the ledger, the audit log) and always print a number. An order lost with a restore and never entered again is counted nowhere, and nothing can say so: a figure is as complete as the records.
- **What tco-counts prints (8.11).** One line a figure, each starting "tco-counts: ", in 8.11's words: the issuing states in code order ("(DE: 2, FR: 1; from another Member State: 1)"); the grounds by their ids ("force_majeure 0, manifest_error 0, insufficient_information 1"; the five of ORDER_GROUNDS for reinstatements); the minutes in ascending order, an order without a receipt after them, "none" when no order is finished. The year is four digits, else the usage (exit 2); a year that has not begun is refused with "tco-counts: the year <y> has not begun; nothing changed" (exit 1). It runs in one transaction at one snapshot (repeatable read, read only) with the 10-second lock timeout, on DATABASE_URL_MIGRATE alone, and writes no audit row. The code is apps/api/src/cli/counts.ts (COUNT_COMMANDS, COUNT_USAGE, parseCountArgs, tcoCounts, describeTcoCounts, countCommand).
- **What korbat restricted as terrorist content without an order (section 2, row 40).** The final decisions of korbat's own (kind final, so no order decision, review or correction) with the outcome restricted and the keyword KEYWORD_TERRORIST_CONTENT, decided in the year.
- **The Regulation's own periods in the terms (W.6, 12.2).** The terms print "within one hour", "for six weeks, for twelve at most" and "within 48 hours" (TCO Art. 3(3), 11(3), 4(4)) as the Regulation's words, not from ORDER_RULES, whose holdDays and holdExtensionDays are korbat's own numbers built on them (a day more, so as never to be early). The terms' table test lists the scrutiny's 48 among the typed digits beside the emergency switch's 48, and its digit rule takes "Article 11 of" and "Article 15 of" as citations.
- **RETENTION's printed list (W.6).** The terms have no list of periods: RETENTION's four new entries and ORDER_RULES.reportHoldDays are printed as sentences, in W.6's words, in a fifth paragraph that "Your data" gains after W.6's four, "The periods for what concerns authorities: ...", with orderPreserveMonths and ORDER_RULES.preserveMarginDays together ("six months and one day from the disabling"); "Terrorist content" prints orderPreserveMonths twice and "What korbat records about authorities" orderRecordYears and contactMessageDays. The five paragraphs stand before the export's paragraph. The two numbers of ORDER_RULES that the terms print by themselves join the table tests' constants and their changed-constants mock; the test that refused /preserv/i now requires it, since 3f preserves.
- **The terms without the build's arguments (W.6).** "Contact point for authorities" stands only while EXPO_PUBLIC_CONTACT_POINT is set, after "Terrorist content", which "Authorities' orders" precedes. While EXPO_PUBLIC_HOME_STATE is empty "Who can use korbat" names no state; a state without an entry in STATE_FACTS is named by its code; and while the state's entry declares no languages (Germany's languages are null) the contact point's sentence leaves out ", in {languages}" rather than print that they are not filled in.
- **The web build's two arguments (section 5's settings, 13).** infra/caddy/Dockerfile takes ARG EXPO_PUBLIC_CONTACT_POINT= and ARG EXPO_PUBLIC_HOME_STATE=, both empty by default, and refuses a point that is no address, a state that is not two capitals, a point without a state, and in production a point at a placeholder domain, as it refuses the contact address's; compose passes ${WEB_CONTACT_POINT:-} and ${WEB_HOME_STATE:-}. infra/.env.example sets WEB_HOME_STATE=DE beside API_HOME_STATE=DE and leaves WEB_CONTACT_POINT empty as API_CONTACT_POINT is; apps/app/.env.example the same. ci.yml is unchanged: its export names only the Turnstile key, and its image build reads infra/.env.example.
- **The README's place for the runbook (W.9).** W.9's sections stand as one section, "## Authorities' orders", after Moderation rather than straight after Staff console, since the runbook leans on Moderation's cases and terms; its parts are bold-titled: The contact point, On call, A removal order step by step, Other messages and orders, When nobody can act within the hour, Notifications and hand-overs, A person asks about a missing post, An author writes about an order's notice, The daily copy, A key in someone else's hands, The yearly counts. Restoring stays in "## Restoring" (Task 6's block) and Upgrading in "## Upgrading", which gains the hand-over's profile, API_REPORT_HOLD and the two web arguments. Before going public gains "The owner's list (sub-project 3f)", W.10's thirteen lines, after the hard gate.
- **What the README says beyond W.9 (W.9, W.10).** The section opens with the design and a block of the main commands; it adds that the watcher reads both folders every 20 seconds and that a message rings every staff phone 15 minutes after its arrival when nobody has signed in or acted since; order-preserve-extend and order-access; notification-open for an Art. 18 check that no seal opened; records --verify; audit-export; what tco-counts prints. W.9's {notificationIntake} reads "the home state's intake for notifications (its entry of STATE_FACTS names it)", since the README cannot print a state's entry; W.10's references to section 2 are written "(row 6)" and so on, and "a page that is not public answers not found".
- **The docs test (12.2, The tables).** apps/admin/test/readme.test.ts holds 3f's text (the section Authorities' orders, Upgrading from its 3f paragraph, Restoring's block with the keeper's copy, the owner's list) to ORDER_RULES and ALERT_RULES: eight phrases with their constants (pollSeconds, escalateMinutes, itemsPerAction, holdWarningDays, reportHoldDays twice, copyOverdueHours, quietMinutes), and every other run of digits refused outside references (paths, sub-project names, P1 to P8, a list's numbering, articles, research findings, section 2's rows, steps, an instant, 1 March, uid 1000, sha256, the status 503), fenced code left out. It also holds the parts' titles and order, an API setting's prefix, the hand-over's and the counts' commands, and the absence of the lines 3f made false.
- **The lines 3f made false (13).** account-facts is notification-facts in Moderation's commands, its bullet, the R2 and protective-suspension bullets and the Art. 18 bullet, whose records are now the record commands'; access-request is listed in the migrate service for a handle and in the api service for an address; "Authorities' orders before 3f" and "The yearly count" point to the new section and tco-counts; the records kept off the server lose what the server now records; the kept head gains the daily download; restore step 5 closes the app's site with APP_SITE=closed where it took the app's hostname off the tunnel; the hard gate reads as W.10 words it; the contact address's line names WEB_CONTACT_POINT and WEB_HOME_STATE.
- **The browser check once more (8.10, 13).** One run more (63 in all) visits every screen 3f gave the console in one pass at 360 by 740 under the phone's rules: Home with its 3f lines, Contact point, Orders, an order, its case, the audit log with its download and key filter, After a restore. Section 13's Files for Task 7 do not list apps/admin; the run and the docs test change it.
- **Section 15's dated amendments (15).** Written as the main design's entry "2026-10-17, sub-project 3f" (section 15's bullets, Delivered, the state of sub-project 3, and Status and order with 3f done) and sections "Changed by 3f (2026-10-17)" in the 1b (20), 2b (19), 3a (17), 3c (20) and 3e (20) designs, with what was built where it differs from section 15's words: finishPurge's two statements in one transaction; the hold's paragraph before "This cannot be undone."; storedImageName; access-request for a handle in the migrate service; the console's two phone rules; 3f's migrations 0026 to 0031.
- **Files section 13 does not list (13).** packages/contracts/src/moderation.ts and test/moderation.test.ts (RETENTION's entries), apps/api/test/cli-staff.test.ts (the command's words), apps/app/test/terms.test.tsx and report.test.tsx (the export line's pin), apps/admin/test/readme.test.ts and test/browser/console.browser.ts.
- **When the mail that a notice has grown goes (7.7, W.5; the dry run's walk-through).** order-texts sets a told order decision's author_mail to pending again, and the dispatch round mails order_notice_added; but the round mails a pending statement only once its decision is 15 minutes old (DUE_AFTER_MS, the margin that leaves a decision's first mail to its own send after the commit), counted from the decision, not from the texts. So texts recorded within 15 minutes of the effect are mailed by the first round after that: in the walk-through the effect was at 03:51:55Z, the reasons were recorded a minute later, and the three mails went at 04:13:52Z. A notice's addition is no new decision, so no other clock applies; whether the addition should be mailed at once is left to the review.

Tasks 8 and 9 (2026-10-17 and 2026-10-18) are the fixes that one adversarial review of the built code upheld (five finders: who can see or do what, the database, what people are told, the proofs, the channel and the services); their bullets follow.

- **The review's migration (6, 13).** The fixes the review of 3f as built upheld in the database are migration 0032_orders_review, the next free number after 0031_recovery, written by hand but for one CHECK that drizzle wrote from the schema: it replaces legal_order_events_ground, the view active_strikes, and decision_actions_check, order_correct, erase_authority_records, decisions_record and order_notices_release in full from their newest text, and adds auth.address_hold_told. No table changes.
- **A held decision counts nowhere (7.10; 3e's standing).** A decision held with an order's notice (informed_at null) counts nowhere its author can see and supports no measure: active_strikes counts a strike only once its decision is told, so Cases' standing, /api/me and the ladder leave it out until the release, and exportedStrikes leaves it out of the export; decision_actions_check's bases of a measure at once (protective, terrorist, threat and threat_repeat) and the console's preview (standingFacts) take only a told decision. A suspension resting on a held decision would tell the author at once, on the account's case, what the hold keeps from them; with no other basis it fails with the errors it had.
- **"Must not be told" set again (6, order_correct; 7.10).** When a correction makes Section C true again, order_correct clears a hold ended as not_ordered (hold_ended_at and hold_end_reason null), whether or not the order was given effect: the hold runs again, the release tells nothing, and a later disabling of the order is held. An end of any other reason (the authority's, a court's) stands.
- **The preservation owed again (6, order_correct; 7.11).** When a correction makes the preservation owed again (a removal order corrected to another kind and back), every item whose measure stands and whose preservation ended without an erasure (posts.erased_at null) gets preserve_until = order_preserve_until(its decision's decided_at) and preserve_ended_at null again, as a first preservation does; a reinstated item and an erased post stay as they are.
- **A record kept while its seal stands (7.13, 7.18).** erase_authority_records keeps a record while any seal that seal_report attributes to it is in force: the seal whose id it holds, or a seal of its account taken while the record was open (a record opened by the system), the window seal_report reads. As built only the first seal kept it, and a seal for good still in force lost its record and seal_report its answer.
- **A correction and an effect take one lock order (5; 6, order_correct; 7.6).** order_correct takes the order's items in post order FOR NO KEY UPDATE before the order's row, and the row FOR NO KEY UPDATE, the effect's order; an event's insert (order_preservation_end) takes the row FOR KEY SHARE and never waits for it. correctOrderRecord runs its transaction once more on a deadlock or a bound reached, while a key's challenge has more than a minute left, and then answers late under a key or service_unavailable without one, never a 500. The effect reads, once every item's row is held, which items are closed now, and refuses order_changed when one was closed after the order was read: a correction that closed it committed while the effect waited.
- **A second order on a held post (7.10).** An order decision is held when its own order holds the notice or when order_notice_held(post) is true; order_notices_release tells an untold order decision only when no other untold order decision about the same post, its measure in force, is still held by its own order, so both are told in the same call at the first order's release. This closes the first of the known limits that Task 3 recorded in this section (Known limits, 7.10); the second, the appeal deadline in a held decision's statement, is Task 9's.
- **The deletion mail under an untold seal (7.15).** The address stays held; the hold's paragraph of account_deleted (heldUntil, holdFinal) and imagesSealed in termination_final go only when every seal behind them was told. auth.address_hold_told(account), a definer read the auth role alone may call, answers false while a seal_media of the account, in force or named by one of its records, has an untold decision; the mail then goes in the one wording for every account.
- **Since when the reasons are held (10.4, W.2).** A texts_recorded event names the text it wrote as its ground (reasons, redress or scope: the contracts' ORDER_TEXT_KINDS; legal_order_events_ground allows them, and an event written before has none). OrderTexts gains reasonsAt: the newest record of the reasons, or the telling when that is later, the telling when no event names them, and null without reasons. The scrutiny paragraph's "has held the authority's reasons since" prints it; textsAt stays the newest record of any text.
- **A sibling decision's content (10.4).** A decision about a post that is disabled for an order now, told after the post was first disabled (held and released with the notice), shows no content, as the notice's screen does not; a decision told before the order keeps the content it had.
- **The mail leg, a job of its own (7.2, 7.18).** The tick's step 2 is alertMailLeg, run as the job alert-mail under its own lock (job:alert-mail) at the tick's interval; the tick keeps alert_steps() and the channel's health, and its report loses mailed. A relay that answers slowly or never then holds only the mail leg, and the pushes alert_steps() writes (the escalation, the deadline's reminders) are never late behind a mail.
- **ntfy's request limit (5, 7.1 steps 5 and 6, 7.2 step 3).** notify has a fixed address on the push network, NOTIFY_PUSH_IP (172.16.91.254 by default, inside PUSH_SUBNET), which infra/ntfy/server.yml lists in visitor-request-limit-exempt-hosts: ntfy v2.28.0 checks that list against the address a request comes from, the publisher's token or not, and a phone's request through Caddy carries the phone's address. The owed rows are published priority 5 first, then within a priority the rows of an order, then each subject's first ring (step 0), then oldest first. A 429 is throttled: the row stays owed with no attempt counted, the round publishes nothing more, and the beat sets push_ok_at as for a working push; a row still owed past channel_down_minutes reads as down only while push_ok_at is that old too, so a flood never makes Home say that alerts arrive by mail only. Lab L1's burst again, short, through the scratch stack with the profile notify on 2026-10-17: 1,000 publishes with the publisher's token from notify's fixed address were all answered 200 (7,790 ms); 100 from another address (the host, through a port of ntfy's own) were answered 60 times 200 and 40 times 429, so the limit still holds for everyone else.
- **The contention lab's Task 3 part (12.1 invariant 23, 13).** contention.lab.ts gains the test '3f, Task 3': one order of ten posts by two authors given effect in one transaction against a report on one of its posts, a notice naming two of them and the first author's account, an interim seal of the first author's images and the purge of the second author's account, 40 rounds each with each side first in half of them, all rolled back: the effect alone 1,293 ms; 40 ok on each side against each of the four, no deadlock, no lock timeout and no refusal; the longest side 978 to 1,105 ms; on the scratch stack at 5 percent busy, over the migrations to 0032.
- **copy_taken with no staff mark (7.16, 8.7).** The audit download alerts every staff account when the newest copy mark of any actor, staff.js audit-export's (the system's) included, is not the caller's, or when there is no mark: as built only staff members' marks were read, and no mark alerted nobody, so a stolen key's download went unseen while the keeper copied with the command line.
- **The hand-over's file (7.14).** The handover program writes the archive to <name>.part, opened as a new file only (wx, mode 0600), syncs it and gives it its name with a hard link, which never replaces a file (a rename would replace one), then deletes the .part. On any error it deletes the .part and says whether that worked, never leaving part of the sealed files unrecorded; an archive or a .part of an earlier run is refused with words that say it may be incomplete and must be deleted first.
- **A test push from the command line (8.11, 10.5, W.9).** staff.js alert-test [--on-call], as the migrator in the migrate service, writes one alert_test row (priority 3; scope all, or on_call with --on-call; facts at) and the audit row alert_test_sent naming the scope, which the watcher publishes at its next round. The runbook's hand-over of the on-call phone and its upgrade of ntfy name it, since nobody on call can publish: the phones only subscribe.
- **The switches the runbook sets (8.8, W.9).** Every sentence of Restoring and of the orders' runbook that sets APP_SITE names `docker compose -f infra/compose.yml up -d --no-deps caddy` (Caddy reads it only when it is created, and without --no-deps Compose would start the API) and a check from outside that the app's hostname answers 503, or no longer does; every sentence that sets an API_* switch names `up -d api`, since the API reads its switches when it starts; a docs test holds both. The console's After a restore and its Home line say "Keep the app's site closed (APP_SITE=closed, Caddy recreated) until ...": korbat cannot see the operator's step.
