- **A message moved between the folders (W.9).** The runbook says never to move a message between the inbox and the junk folder: the mail host gives the moved copy a new number and korbat counts it as a second message; if it happened, the second row is closed on Contact point with the first row's word.
- **A held decision's appeal in its statement (7.10, 9.3, 10.4).** `DecisionStatement` gains `informedAt`, the decision's informed_at: its own time for a decision told at once, the release for one held with an order's notice, null while it is held, absent from data read before the review. `statementParagraphs` counts the appeal's six months from `informedAt` when it is set, else from `decidedAt`, as the database's `appeal_deadline(appeal_informed_at)` counts them, so the paragraph and the screen's `appealUntil` name the same deadline: a decision released with a notice weeks after it was taken printed a deadline that many weeks short, or one already past. With Task 8's held order on a held post, neither of section 19's 'Known limits (7.10)' holds any more.
- **The staff mails with phone alerts off (W.4, 7.2).** `moderation_urgent` and `moderation_waiting` take `phones`, 'on' or 'off' from NOTIFY, as their data (a required member: the template refuses a mail that does not say), and with 'off' end 'Open the staff console. Phone alerts are switched off on this server, so this email is the only alert.' and '... so this email is the only reminder.' in the place of W.4's 'The same alert went to the phone on call.' and 'The same reminder went to every staff phone.': the README has a server run with NOTIFY=off until the contact-point mailbox exists, and the mail, then the only alert, said that a phone had it.
- **What an erasure tells the yearly counts (8.11, 7.18; invariant 20).** The `order_records` step's `moderation_retention` audit row names, beside the order and the count, the order's `kind`, `receivedAt` and `openedAt` as they stand when it is erased, read first in the erasure's own transaction. tco-counts judges an erasure by that row alone: the order figures of a year are 'not recorded' when a removal order received (or, without a receipt, opened) before the year's end was erased within record_keep_years of the year. The order's `order_opened` row is not read: a correction may have changed the kind since (opened as dsa_art9, corrected to tco_removal before its effect), and restore-import writes an order again without one. An erasure row written before this names no kind and makes every year it could reach 'not recorded', whatever the order was, so that a published zero stays a true one. This replaces the rule of section 19's 'When a figure is not recorded' that read the opening row.
- **A DSA order that does not withhold, on the notice and in the terms (9.3, W.6).** Under the notice of an order whose measure does not withhold from its author, the screen prints [notice.noContentRemoved] 'korbat does not show the post here. You can still see it on your profile; other people cannot, for as long as the order's measure stands.' in the place of [notice.noContent], which says that only authorities and courts see it. The terms' meaning of the measure 'Disable for an order' reads 'the post and its images are shown to nobody, or to nobody but you when the order does not ask that they be kept from you, because an authority ordered it', where it said 'shown to nobody, you included'.
- **An extended preservation in the notice (W.2, 7.11).** `OrderNoticeFacts` gains `preserveFirstUntil`, the preservation's end six months and a day from the disabling it counts from (the decision's own time, or for a disabling taken again after a restore the item's first disabling, from which the import counted it), which no extension moves; without an extension it is `preserveUntil` itself. `preserved` prints it as six months from the disabling and `preservedLonger` prints the extended `preserveUntil`, where both printed the extended end. `erased` reads 'The preservation ended on {erasedOn}. korbat erased the post's text, its images and its copies of them. The post cannot be reinstated.' and `reinstated.erased` 'korbat had already erased the post's content when its preservation ended on {erasedOn}, so nothing can be shown again.', since an extension, or a freeze that delays the erasure, makes the preservation longer than six months.
- **A disabling taken again whose notice shows no preservation (W.2).** `reapplied` keeps its last clause, '; the six months above count from the first time.', only when the notice prints the `preserved` paragraph above it. Otherwise (a DSA Article 9 order, which preserves nothing, or a removal order's notice once the preservation has ended or erased the post) the notice prints `reappliedNoPreservation`: 'The post was first disabled on {lostOn}. korbat lost its record of that when it had to restore its database from a backup, and disabled the post again with this decision.'
- **A message an order came with, in the terms (W.6, 6.4).** Both sentences of 'Your data' on the records of the contact point say that a message an order came with is kept with that order, as delete_contact_messages keeps it while the order's row exists: '... and deletes that record 30 days after the message was handled, or, when an order came with it, with that order's records.' and 'The record of a message at the contact point for authorities is kept 30 days, counted from the day the message was handled, or, when an order came with it, as long as that order's records; ...'. Both are held word for word in the terms test.
- **The day on an order's row on Cases (9.3, 10.4).** `AuthorCaseRow` gains `toldOn`, on an order decision's row the UTC day its author was told; the app prints it as 'Told on {day}' (`ORDER_TEXTS.row.toldOn`) in the place of 'Decided on {day}'. The list, and with it the export's paging, places an order decision by its informed_at and every other decision by its decided_at, so that each row is sorted by the day it shows: a notice released after a hold stands at the top, where its mail sends the author, and no longer under the decisions taken during the hold. A decision of korbat's own that was held with the notice keeps its own day and place: korbat did decide it then.
- **A gap closed as not needed under another account (6.6; 12.1, invariant 24).** The commit's check `restore_gaps_confirmed` refuses a closing as not needed whose confirmed `gap_closed` row is not a live admin's, whatever account `gap_close` was given, and a test now proves it alone (an admin named, a moderator's confirmed row: refused at commit with the detail 'actor: ...'). It does not compare the account `gap_close` was given with the row's actor, as invariant 24's 'under another account' asks for the order's events: the gap stores no closer for this disposition (6.6), so the account named serves only the call's admin_required and is kept nowhere, and the audit row's actor, which a key confirmed, is the closing's only attribution. A closing named for one admin and confirmed by another admin's key stands, attributed to the key's holder; refusing it would need a migration that stores the closer, which this review's fixes do not add.
